# Rendering

The replay on Metal (`dm/gfx/nvn/nvn_mtl.m`); the Vulkan and OpenGL layers were removed in 0.5 ([Graphics APIs: NVN only](#graphics-apis-nvn-only)). Each entry: symptom, cause, fix, the games it was found on. The [symptom index](../agents/symptoms.md) lists every symptom.

## Textures and formats

### Null textures in diagnostic logs

- **Symptom:** clang reports null dereferences in `nvnWindowInitialize` and `clear_tex`; clearing a null texture crashes with `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP=1`, and so did a texture-to-texture copy with no source or destination texture (in `copy_t2t_enc`).
- **Cause:** window initialization checked the texture count but logged the first texture's dimensions without checking its pointer. Clear replay tolerated a null texture but its pass dump unconditionally read the texture serial. Texture copy replay (`copy_t2t_enc`) printed both textures' serials in its pass-dump `T` line before its null return, and either can be NULL there (`tex_snap` in `hle_nvn.c` can record one).
- **Fix:** check the first texture before logging its dimensions (`hle_nvn.c`) and log serial 0 for a null clear texture, and for a null source or destination of a texture copy (`nvn_mtl.m`). The copy's guard is from the earlier Nintanium line (its analyzer pass after a merge), taken again in NX 18 release 1; `t_clear_tex` runs both with the pass dump on.
- **Found on:** clang static analysis ([issue #424](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/424)); synthetic null-texture tests, with no gameplay failure reported.

### Texture objects copied into command records

- **Symptom:** Metal aborts on a texture descriptor with an absurd size (`height (1869367126) greater than … 16384`).
- **Cause:** a command record kept a pointer to the game's `NVNtexture` and read it at replay. NVN commands do not depend on the API object once recorded.
- **Fix:** the host now copies the texture object into the record (`REC_SNAP` / `tex_snap` in `hle_nvn.c`: render targets, texture copies and clears). Replay rejects objects without the texture magic and sizes beyond Metal's limits, with a red line instead of an abort.
- **Note:** the first red lines name the call site and the object's first bytes. In Engage those bytes were 32-bit triangle indices, i.e. another write had already overrun into the texture object; that stopped once the alias region had address space left ([Size-aligned regions exhaust the alias address space](memory.md#size-aligned-regions-exhaust-the-alias-address-space)).
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Engage](../../dm/games/0100A6301214E000/README.md).

### New textures start as zero

- **Symptom:** a decoded movie stays black even though its YUV textures contain the picture; disabling its depth test makes it visible.
- **Symptom:** a scene background is green and retains silhouettes of moving characters; pixels a character covers can recover their normal colour. The resolved scene is already wrong before postprocessing.
- **Cause:** Metal leaves a new texture's content undefined; a freshly mapped Switch memory pool reads as 0, and feedback effects (such as Mario's fountain wave simulation, [Metal 4 does not order operations inside one encoder](#metal-4-does-not-order-operations-inside-one-encoder)) never clear their state texture.
- **Fix:** non-packaged colour textures are zero-filled from a shared zero buffer when created (`tex_zero_fill`, batched with the uploads; in bands of rows that fit the buffer's 4 MiB: [A full colour texture grew the shared zero buffer](memory.md#a-full-colour-texture-grew-the-shared-zero-buffer)). Depth and stencil textures, and multisample colour textures (a buffer copy cannot write samples), are cleared to zero in render passes in the same upload batch (with the profile key `depth_zero_copy`, Ocarina of Time's, single-sample Depth16Unorm and Depth32Float textures are copied into from the zero buffer instead, in pieces of rows: [A clear pass on a large depth texture fails with `Insufficient Memory`](memory.md#a-clear-pass-on-a-large-depth-texture-fails-with-insufficient-memory)), for every level and slice, once when the Metal texture is created; the game's own clears, loads and blending are unchanged. Upload-failure recovery tracks these destinations too.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md); [Pokémon Brilliant Diamond](../../dm/games/0100000011D90000/README.md): the opening movie's YUV conversion uses GEQUAL against a new 1920×1080 depth texture that the game never clears. Metal supplied depth 1, discarding the movie; zero initialization restores it without a shader override. Rune Factory 5 (`01007B4014086000`, no profile in the repository; PR #1760): its opening forest loads a new 1920×1080 two-sample RG11B10Float scene target without a colour clear and blends into it; initializing the target alone restores the picture.
- **Check:** `t_zero_depth` poisons Depth16 and Depth32F textures (levels, layers, a cube) and reads every texel back as zero after the copies, and after the clear pass with `depth_zero_copy` off; `t_clear_ds` poisons depth and stencil before calling the real initializer and checks both become zero; `t_clear_msaa` fills a multisample colour texture with a colour, calls the initializer and reads every sample on the GPU (RGBA8, RG11B10Float and RGBA16Float at two and four samples); depth clear, stencil-view and upload-budget tests cover the related paths.

### Texture swizzle through swizzled Metal views

- **Symptom:** glyphs render as white boxes (the font atlas is `(1, 1, 1, R)`).
- **Fix:** `TextureBuilderSetSwizzle` / `TextureViewSetSwizzle` are applied with Metal swizzled texture views (NVN and Metal channel enums have the same values). The swizzle names components of the NVN format, which the format's own mapping finds in Metal's (`nvn_fmt_to_mtl`: alpha 1 for RGBX-type formats; red and blue swapped for the RGB565 family, all four reversed for RGBA4, but only in linear and buffer textures, which read guest memory in place: every other host texture holds Metal's order, [RGB565-family textures in Metal's channel order](#rgb565-family-textures-in-metals-channel-order)).
- **Note:** Metal makes a swizzled view read-only (usage 0x1), so an image slot gets the same view without the mapping (`img_fit`), and the on-chip read merge leaves a swizzled sample alone.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md) (the font atlas); The Legend of Zelda: Echoes of Wisdom's hearts (GitHub PR #66, the RGB565 red / blue swap).

### RGB565-family textures in Metal's channel order

- **Symptom:** RGB565, RGB5A1 or RGB5 textures sample with red and blue exchanged: smeared, banded rock, the shapes right and the colours wrong; or what the GPU cleared, drew or copied into such a texture reads back to the game, or samples, with red and blue exchanged.
- **Cause:** Metal has these formats only with red and blue exchanged (`B5G6R5Unorm`, `BGR5A1Unorm`; red at bit 11 or 10, the guest's at bit 0), and the host kept them in two orders: packaged textures (`NvPkg`) in the guest's, swapped when sampled, plain ones in Metal's, as Metal renders and clears into them. Every path that moved bytes from one to the other without converting exchanged the colours: the one-time fill of a plain texture (`tex_init_fill`), buffer → texture and texture → buffer copies, `ReadTexels`, a clear or a draw into a packaged texture, a texture copy between a plain and a packaged one, the readback of a copy into a packaged texture's storage.
- **Fix:** one order. Every host texture of these formats holds Metal's order; only guest memory holds the guest's, and bytes are converted where they cross (`rb_packed_shift`, from `nvn_fmt_to_mtl`'s swap): every upload is repacked on the CPU (`pkg_upload_`; these formats leave the GPU deswizzle), buffer ↔ texture copies exchange the fields on the GPU in the copy encoder (`rb_swap_gpu`: through a staging area of the frame arena into the texture, in place in the buffer out of it; the GPU may have written the buffer earlier in the submission), and `ReadTexels` and the readback into a packaged texture's storage exchange them on the CPU (`rb_swap_rows`). Clears, draws and texture copies then need nothing. Linear and buffer textures read guest memory in place: they keep the guest's order and the swap when sampled (`tex_by_id`), and copies into or out of them stay raw (`rb_copy_shift`).
- **Note:** a view of one of these textures in another format of the same size (RG8, R16) now sees Metal's order where it saw the guest's in a packaged texture; no game is known to make one. A linear render target of these formats is drawn in Metal's order into guest memory; not seen.
- **Check:** `t_view_swizzle` fills RGB565, RGB5A1, RGB5 and BGR565 textures from `CPU_UNCACHED` and `CPU_NO_ACCESS` pools through the real `tex_init_fill`, reads them on the GPU, draws into one texel and reads both again, and checks the guest storage is unchanged (379 checks). `t_readback` records the real commands for RGB565, RGB5A1 and RGB5: a buffer → texture copy sampled, copied back and read with `ReadTexels`; a Metal clear to red copied out and read; a packaged texture sampled, one with a texel cleared, one copied into from the cleared plain texture and read from its storage (78 checks, none failed). With the host before the fix, 18 of `t_readback`'s fail.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md#rendering) (GitHub issue #183, the Depths' cave walls: their initial-fill RGB565 / RGB5A1 textures), found and checked in the game by the issue's reporters (M1 with 1.2.1, M4 with 1.4.3); the copy paths found reviewing that fix, checked by the tests only.

### RGBA4 textures in Metal's channel order

- **Symptom:** outdoor battle ground and lower foliage are nearly black while the characters and the UI stay lit: a baked shadowmask takes the sunlight away. RGBA4 textures sample with their four channels reversed.
- **Cause:** NVN's RGBA4 holds R, G, B, A from bit 0 up, Metal's `ABGR4Unorm` A, B, G, R; the host kept the guest's bits, so every channel read another's nibble. Super Mario RPG's shadowmask texels are mostly `0x0f00` (blue 1), sampled through a (B, G, R, A) mapping: red should read 1, and read 0, with green 1.
- **Fix:** RGBA4 takes the [RGB565 family's](#rgb565-family-textures-in-metals-channel-order) conversion, all four nibbles reversed instead of red and blue (`rb_packed_shift` 4 in `rb_swap_rows` and `rb_swap.metal`), at the same crossings between guest memory and host textures (uploads are repacked on the CPU and leave the GPU deswizzle, as that family's do); linear and buffer textures, read in place, take `nvn_fmt_to_mtl`'s reversed mapping when sampled. That entry's note holds here too: a same-size view in another format (R16) sees Metal's order; no game is known to make one.
- **Check:** `t_view_swizzle` samples the texels `0x8421` and `0x0f00` (every nibble different) uploaded and on a buffer, with and without the (B, G, R, A) mapping, cleared by Metal and filled from `CPU_UNCACHED` and `CPU_NO_ACCESS` pools; `t_readback` runs its RGB565-family copy, clear and `ReadTexels` cases for RGBA4. With the host before the fix, 40 of `t_view_swizzle`'s 554 checks and 6 of `t_readback`'s 154 cases fail.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario RPG](../../dm/games/0100BC0018138000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (Mushroom Way's battles), checked in the game before and after.

### `nvnTextureCompare` compares the channel mapping

- **Symptom:** dark boxes and cream cloud rectangles behind HUD prompts, a black stamina wheel and a black System menu background (layout captures).
- **Cause:** agl's layouts rebuild a capture texture on the same storage with a new channel mapping, and replace the registered descriptor only if `nvnTextureCompare` reports the two textures different. The host compared the description and the storage but not the mapping, so every rebuild was dropped.
- **Fix:** `nvnTextureCompare` compares the mapping too, an unset mapping counting as (R, G, B, A). `t_nvn` covers the comparison. From GitHub PR #6.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): panes tagged `CaptureOn` (about 140 layouts, e.g. `PaGuideKeySmoke_00`; user data `A8`, `CaptureScale` 0.5, `MultiFilter`) are drawn into a target, blurred at half size and copied into a capture texture that the layout's materials sample in place of the placeholder `FBLayout_00`. The game creates it with its per-format default mapping (one call site, `main+0xecb0bc`: R8 → (R, 0, 0, 1), RG8 → (R, G, 0, 1), and so on), then rebuilds it with the capture's own mapping: `main+0xf597dc` picks (1, 1, 1, R) for an `A8` capture (the material colour kept, the mask as alpha) and alpha 1 when the pane says `CaptureOutputAlpha` 255 (read at `main+0xf58e98`; an RGB10A2 capture then reads (R, G, B, 1)); `main+0xecacf0` stores the mapping and rebuilds the texture (`main+0xecabe8`), and `main+0xec33f0` replaces the descriptor only on a difference. The captures kept (R, 0, 0, 1), whose alpha is always 1: hard-edged dark boxes behind every prompt (the shadow pane `e6a88e12dbae9743`: black, alpha = capture alpha × vertex alpha 180/255) and rectangles filled with cream cloud (the glow `aa209f57b133e862`: alpha = min(cloud alpha, capture alpha)). Checked on the Mac by the PR's author (M4 Pro, unchanged shader libraries): green stamina, the purple noise meter and the game's own blurred System menu background. [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md): its layouts rebuild their captures through the same agl code (`main+0x982320` replaces the registered descriptor only if `nvnTextureCompare` reports a difference), so the fix covers them too.
- **Note:** before the cause was known, a profile key, `alpha_from_red`, listed the layout library's pixel shaders and bound such render targets through a view mapped (R, 0, 0, R). In BotW (all 26 pixel-shader variants, `main`'s BNSH at 0x1d83000) that fixed the alpha but zeroed green and blue, so tinted panes such as the stamina wheel came out black, and the System menu background (a capture with alpha forced to 1) stayed black. In Odyssey (all 26 layout pixel shaders, `main`'s BNSH at 0x132c000) it had turned the coin counters' solid white cards into the right shapes. With the comparison fixed it found no (R, 0, 0, 1) target on either game (the mapping a capture is rebuilt with replaces it, on the Mac and the iPhone alike), so the key and its host code (`tex_alpha_red`, DM_NVN_ALPHA_RED) were removed (c76f6798); Odyssey's note has the A/B runs.

### Sampling a depth attachment through a red texture view

- **Symptom:** Metal validation aborts in `tex_by_id` with `MTLPixelFormatDepth32Float` not compatible with `MTLPixelFormatR32Float`.
- **Cause:** an NVN sampled view requests the single-channel colour format of a depth attachment. Metal forbids that format cast even with pixel-format-view usage. Reading the unmodified depth texture as colour can also repeat depth in the green and blue channels.
- **Fix:** for D32F viewed as R32F, or D16 viewed as R16 UNORM, keep the depth format and map its value to `(depth, 0, 0, 1)` before applying the descriptor's channel swizzle. The view selects the requested mips and layers of the original attachment, so later depth writes stay visible.
- **Found on:** [Burnout Paradise Remastered (#985)](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/985) (D32F as R32F).

### Texture views cached by texture and view parameters

- **Symptom:** `newTextureViewWithPixelFormat` takes a large share of the replay thread (45% in Age of Calamity) when a game registers textures into a ring of IDs before every bind.
- **Cause:** KTGL registers a texture into the next ID of a ring before every bind (`RegisterTexture`, `GetTextureHandle` and `BindTexture` counts are equal: 8,000 a frame in battle, up to 28,000 after the cutscene). Only 1 in 6 registrations repeated what the ID held, so the per-ID view (`treg_t.view`) was rebuilt for half of all binds.
- **Fix:** views are now also kept by texture serial and view parameters (4 per texture), checked against the current base texture on each hit, and dropped when the texture or its pool is finalized; a registration identical to what the ID holds keeps its view and the registration generation (`vc_get` in `nvn_mtl.m`).
- **Found on:** [Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity](../../dm/games/01002B00111A2000/README.md): 2,036 views were made in a 15-minute Mac battle run, 211 million reused; the replay before the cutscene went from 14.3 to 10.8 ms a frame.

### Compatible views for a mismatched declared texture type

- **Symptom:** daylight scenes washed out to white (every read of a texture returned one fixed value); `texture type mismatch` red lines, among them `declares slot … as Metal type 6, texture ID … is type 5` (a cube in a cube array slot).
- **Cause:** on Maxwell the sample instruction carries its own dimension, and a non-array sample of an array texture reads layer 0. On Metal a 2D array bound to a `texture2d` argument is a type mismatch, and every read returned one fixed value. 3D World creates its deferred pre-lighting buffer (the G-buffer's fourth target, R11G11B10F) as a *one-layer 2D array* texture, and the lighting shader samples it as 2D, so the whole picture got one pale ambient colour (bloom then blew it out further). Measured on an M4 Pro, a cube view bound as `texturecube_array` reads face 0 in every direction, and bound as `texture2d_array` reads layer 0 at every index; neither faulted the GPU, over 2,000 dispatches of 262,144 reads at random array indices.
- **Fix:** the host compares each texture binding's declared type (the reflection's `dtt`) with the texture it resolves to and binds a compatible view (`tex_fit` in `nvn_mtl.m`, cached per texture-pool entry). 2D, 2D array, cube and cube array are all layers, and the declared type says how many the view takes: 2D the first (for a cube, the face a non-array sample of its six layers reads), 2D array all of them, cube the first six, cube array all whole cubes. Any other mismatch is reported once. `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_tex_fit_root` reads every face and layer through these views under Metal API validation.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario 3D World + Bowser's Fury](../../dm/games/010028600EBDA000/README.md). The cube → cube array view on [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md). The cube → 2D view on [Atelier Ryza DX](../../dm/games/0100199023B2E000/README.md), whose draws leave cube textures in slots they read as 2D (`texture type mismatch … declares slot … as Metal type 2, texture ID … is type 5 (NVN target 8 …); bound as is`, up to five over the opening, the textures different in each run): the lines are gone and the field looks the same (0.3 % of pixels differ between the two hosts at the same frame, scattered particles and foliage). A Metal view of one cube face as 2D passes the API validation layer. What the Switch reads there was not measured; [ROMANCE OF THE THREE KINGDOMS 8 REMAKE](../../dm/games/0100C2A01A730000/README.md) has the same case in two vertex shaders, not run since.
- **Note:** how it was found: a blit-based pass snapshot of that target showed a correct, colourful image while a lighting shader edited to output only that input showed a uniform colour, and both resolved to the same Metal texture. Earlier suspects that turned out correct: fog, bindings, the 1D lookup tables, clear colours, the depth convention, the final sRGB view, blend states, GPU luminance readback. There is no tone-mapping pass in this game: the final composite writes `scene + bloom` into an RGB10A2 target.

### Compatible views made from the texture under a view

- **Symptom:** with `MTL_SHADER_VALIDATION=1`, `Invalid Metal usage: Texture is not resident` for a texture allocated in `tex_fit`; without validation those reads are undefined.
- **Cause:** Metal takes a view's residency from the texture it was made from. `tex_fit` made its view of the texture the binding resolved to, which is often a view itself (the view cache's, a level and layer range or a swizzle), so the new view hung under a texture that is not in the residency set although the texture under both is.
- **Fix:** `tex_fit` makes the view from the texture at the bottom of the chain, with the bound view's level and layer offsets, level count and swizzle (`t_tex_fit_root`).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): a 64x64 environment cube read from a 2D-array view, 7 draws in the outdoors frame replayed with `nvn_replay` under shader validation.

### 1D textures as one-row 2D textures

- **Symptom:** a type mismatch when a Metal 1D texture is bound to a shader's `texture2d` argument.
- **Cause:** translated shaders never declare `texture1d` (none of the five games' libraries did at the time).
- **Fix:** NVN 1D and 1D-array textures are created as one-row 2D / 2D-array Metal textures.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario 3D World + Bowser's Fury](../../dm/games/010028600EBDA000/README.md): the material shaders sample 64×1 R32F lookup tables created as NVN 1D textures (and filled with `WriteTexels`).

### A 1D array texture uploaded as its layer count of rows per layer

- **Symptom:** with Metal API validation, an abort at a texture upload: `(destinationOrigin.y + sourceSize.height)(64) must be <= height(1)`. Without it, the upload writes past each layer into the next ones, and the texture holds junk rows: a light that flashes across a surface as the camera crosses a distance band.
- **Cause:** a 1D array is a one-row 2D array whose layer count is the NVN height ([1D textures as one-row 2D textures](#1d-textures-as-one-row-2d-textures)), and the storage layout (`nvn_tex.c`) and the deswizzler lay it out so, but the upload paths took the NVN height as each layer's row count: every layer went up as that many rows into a one-row slice, and the GPU wrote past each slice.
- **Fix:** `tex_lsize` (`nvn_mtl.m`) gives a level's size as its Metal texture holds it (width, rows, depth planes; one row for a 1D or 1D array texture). `pkg_upload_` (its byte counts `total` and `total_up`, the GPU and CPU deswizzles, and the copies of the images a partial re-upload keeps), the sparse texture's tiles and `scissor_full` take it, so no path keeps a rule of its own. The direct upload path takes only cube and 2D arrays and is not reached. Ported from upstream NXNative 1f35a05b.
- **Open:** region-based operations on a 1D array (`WriteTexels`, `ReadTexels`, texture copies) still take the region's y and height as rows, not layers. No game is known to reach them.
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md): its light-profile lookup table, a 512-wide R16F 1D array of 64 layers read by the deferred lighting, went up as 64 copies of 64 rows.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_partial_upload` (`R16F 1D array` and `RGBA8 1D array with levels`, uploaded whole and partly through the GPU deswizzle and whole through the CPU's, under Metal API validation; without the fix the first aborts, and without validation the whole uploads count 4,194,304 bytes for a 65,536-byte texture).

### Cube textures held as 6-layer 2D arrays

- **Symptom:** shaders that sample a cube texture as `texturecube_array` get a type mismatch.
- **Cause:** Metal can make a cube-array view only of a 2D-array base texture.
- **Fix:** NVN cube textures are created as 6-layer 2D arrays and bound through cube / cube-array views by `tex_fit`, which also maps a cube array read as a cube to its first cube.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): 1,512 shaders sample the environment probes as `texturecube_array` while a single cube texture is bound.

### Cube map arrays count layer-faces

- **Symptom:** an abort in `MTLTextureDescriptor` validation, `arrayLength (510) … greater than the max allowed size (341)`, when the prologue movie ends.
- **Cause:** as in OpenGL, an NVN cube map array's depth is its number of layer-faces (a multiple of 6: 510 for 85 cubes), while a Metal cube array's `arrayLength` counts cubes. The host took the depth as a cube count in three places: the Metal texture, the upload's layer count and the guest layout (`nvn_tex.c` `shape`), so `GetStorageSize` reported six times the storage.
- **Fix:** a cube array is now created like a cube, as a 2D array of all its faces, and `tex_fit` makes the cube-array view a shader asks for (`t_nvn` checks the storage size against a 2D array's).
- **Found on:** [DRAGON QUEST XI S](../../dm/games/01006C300E9F0000/README.md).

### Cube views count their layers in cubes

- **Symptom:** an abort in Metal's validation: `newSliceRange.length(1) must be a multiple of (6)`.
- **Cause:** environment maps are 6-layer textures (target 2D array, depth 6) sampled through views with target CUBEMAP and layers 0 + 1. As in the hardware's texture header, a cube view counts its layers in cubes; Metal counts faces.
- **Fix:** a cube view whose layer count is not a multiple of 6 now takes its layers as cubes when the texture has that many faces (`tex_by_id`; else it is read as a 2D array with a red line).
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Sword](../../dm/games/0100ABF008968000/README.md) (as the player first walks out of the house).

### Texture views with a range past their source

- **Symptom:** an abort in Metal's validation on the replay thread: `(newSliceRange.location + newSliceRange.length)(3) must be <= (2)` (or the same for levels). Metal aborts the whole process for it, with or without the debug layer. S2Arm had three such reports from one game on an iPhone (Pokémon Sword / Shield's title IDs); not seen here.
- **Cause:** about 20 places in `nvn_mtl.m` make a texture view with a level / layer range (registered texture IDs, `tex_fit`, image and float views, same-pass feedback snapshots, sparse level clamps, draw texture copies, pass-as-compute targets, upgrades, uploads, the texture dump). Only the draw texture copy checked the range first ([`copy_t2t_draw`](#copies-a-blit-cannot-do-drawn), f36edf8c6); a bad range from any other one ended the process.
- **Fix:** every such view goes through `mtl_view` / `mtl_view_sw` (after S2Arm a6ba2b665), for every game. A range past the source, or an empty one, makes no view: the caller gets nil, which each one takes as no view (an empty slot, no draw, the texture as it is, the copy not drawn; a copy whose stencil view fails now stops there too). The first one writes `[RED] texture view outside its source (<function>): levels …, slices … (source …)`, later ones `texture views outside their source, not made: N so far` at each power of two. Which caller gives the bad range is still open: the red line names it.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_view_range`: ranges in and past a 2-layer, 3-level array and a cube, with and without a swizzle, give a view or nil as counted by hand, and the run ends; `T_VIEW_RANGE_RAW=1 ./t_view_range` makes the same view straight through Metal and aborts (exit 134, with and without `MTL_DEBUG_LAYER`).

### A 2D texture in a cube slot binds the zero cube

- **Symptom:** `texture type mismatch … declares slot 10 as Metal type 5, texture … is type 3` (undefined reads); the later `[RED] texture type mismatch: shader declares a cube, texture is 2D` could also report an unused conditional input as an error.
- **Cause:** a 2D texture bound where the shader declares a cube (a placeholder for an environment map) has no cube view. The same for a 2D array of fewer than six layers, which is how the host holds some NVN 2D textures. A uniform-controlled branch may choose a different cube input while the unused slot retains a 2D descriptor. Binding all statically declared Metal resources does not establish that this input is sampled.
- **Fix:** the zero cube is bound instead of a type mismatch with undefined reads (`tex_fit`). This supported binding fallback is informational (`cube binding fallback`), rather than a red error: it cannot diagnose which branch the shader executes. Other unsupported texture mismatches still report errors.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md); [Mario Kart 8 Deluxe](../../dm/games/0100152000022000/README.md) binds a 2D array of fewer than six layers (format 0x44) at slot 10 of two pixel shaders that declare a cube there as a race loads, and it was bound as is; [Echoes of Wisdom](../../dm/games/01008CF01BAAC000/README.md#rendering-and-shaders) exposed the false error for an unused conditional cube input.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_cube_placeholder` uses production `tex_fit` and GPU sampling under Metal API validation. For 2D descriptors and one- and five-layer arrays, an unselected fallback preserves the other cube's blue output, a selected fallback reads zero, and binding alone raises no error; both cube and cube-array arguments are covered.

### A 2D texture in a 3D slot binds the zero 3D texture

- **Symptom:** `[RED] texture type mismatch: shader … declares slot … as Metal type 7, texture ID … is type 2 … bound as is` (or type 3, a 2D array); the reads are undefined.
- **Cause:** the game leaves a 2D texture bound in a slot its shader samples as a 3D texture, and Metal makes a 3D view only of a 3D texture. Batman: Arkham Knight's shaders sample a volume at world coordinates through separate texture slots that hold 2D textures, mostly half-resolution render targets, from the first frames of New Story on (`553c51835a24a36a`, separate slot 2: a 720x406 RGBA16F target); the pass dump shows the game binding them there, so no bind was lost, and the game does create 3D textures (2x2x2, 32x32x32, 71x40x64). What the Switch's texture unit reads from a 2D texture header through a 3D instruction is not known.
- **Fix:** as for a cube slot, `tex_fit` binds the zero 3D texture, a defined read, for a 2D texture or 2D array in a 3D slot, and logs the first case (`3D binding fallback`); other mismatches no view bridges are still reported and bound as is.
- **Found on:** [Batman: Arkham Knight](../../dm/games/0100ACD0163D0000/README.md#files-and-shaders) (four shaders, 3,813 draws in a run to the diner). The same red line is recorded on [FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md), [ROMANCE OF THE THREE KINGDOMS 8 REMAKE](../../dm/games/0100C2A01A730000/README.md), [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md) and [Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity](../../dm/games/01002B00111A2000/README.md), none of them run with this rule.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_cube_placeholder` also covers a 2D texture and one- and five-layer 2D arrays in a `texture3d` argument: an unselected fallback leaves the real volume's output alone, a selected one reads zero.

### A 3D texture in an array or cube slot binds the zero texture

- **Symptom:** `[RED] texture type mismatch: shader … declares slot … as Metal type 3, texture ID … is type 7 (NVN target 2, …); bound as is` (or Metal type 5 or 6: a cube or cube array slot); the reads are undefined.
- **Cause:** the reverse of [the case above](#a-2d-texture-in-a-3d-slot-binds-the-zero-3d-texture): a 3D texture (NVN target 2, Metal type 7) left in a slot the shader declares as a 2D array (Metal type 3), cube (5) or cube array (6). Metal makes a view of a 3D texture only as a 3D texture, so no view bridges it. A copy of its depth planes into a 2D array would be a guess, not a bridge: a 3D texture's block-linear tiles span several depth planes where an array's layers lie apart, and what the Switch's texture unit reads from a 3D texture header through an array or cube instruction is not known; such a copy would also have to follow every write to a lookup table that some games draw each frame. Where it was looked into, the shader does not read the slot. Ocarina of Time on the Switch 2 leaves an RGB10A2 colour-grading lookup table (format 0x3d, Metal format 90) in slot 7 of its Layout2 `MergeShader` (`9b310b3349825e9a`, variant 4), which samples that slot as a 2D array, at a layer from a flat input, only when its uniform `c3[0].x` is set.
- **Fix:** as for a cube or 3D slot, `tex_fit` binds the zero texture of the declared type, a defined read, and logs the first case (`layered binding fallback`) instead of a red line. It cannot tell whether the shader reads the slot; if one does, it reads 0 where the old binding read undefined values. A 3D texture in a 2D slot (Metal type 2) is still bound as is and reported: a size query carries no texture type and is translated as 2D when nothing else uses its slot ([A size query on a 3D texture gets a 2D slot](shaders.md#a-size-query-on-a-3d-texture-gets-a-2d-slot)), and through that binding Metal returns the 3D texture's real width, where the zero texture would return 1. Its report is a plain `texture type mismatch` line, not a red one (`tex_mis_3d_in_2d`, from S2Arm 1b229450c): it names the case, and Ryujinx binds nothing there either (`IsSameDimensionsTarget` is false for 3D against 2D), so the slot reads 0 on it.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time (Switch 2)](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (iPhone, 30 fps runs; not run with this rule yet). The 2D slot case, left as it was: [Kirby and the Forgotten Land](../../dm/games/01004D300C5AE000/README.md) (a 16x16x16 RGBA8 lookup table in slots 1 and 2 of eight layout shaders that `c3[0].y` switches off), [Subnautica](../../dm/games/0100429011144000/README.md) and [Subnautica: Below Zero](../../dm/games/010014C011146000/README.md) (a 3D RGBA8 texture in slots 135 and 136 of two pixel shaders, not identified).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_cube_placeholder` also binds a 4x4x4 RGB10A2 3D texture in `texture2d_array`, `texturecube` and `texturecube_array` arguments under Metal API validation: the fallback has the declared type, raises no red line, reads zero when selected, and leaves the real texture's output alone when not; in a 2D slot `tex_fit` makes no fallback.

### Multisample texture slots

- **Symptom:** `[RED] texture type mismatch: shader … declares slot … as Metal type 2, texture ID … is type 4 (NVN target 5, …); bound as is`, for a shader whose MSL declares the slot `texture2d_ms`.
- **Cause:** the reflection reader (`nvn_refl.m` `dummy_kind`) had no rule for `texture2d_ms`, `depth2d_ms` and their arrays, so it recorded such a slot as 2D. The multisample texture the game binds there was reported as a mismatch although the binding was right; an unbound slot got a 2D zero texture, which a multisample argument does not take; and a handle pool for such a slot would be built of 2D textures. The dummy tables were also indexed by the Metal type masked with 7, where the 2D multisample array (8) shared the 1D entry.
- **Fix:** the multisample types are read as `MTLTextureType2DMultisample` and `2DMultisampleArray`. Their zero textures are 4-sample private textures cleared by a render pass (`dummy_tex`; the integer classes as RGBA16, as the 128-bit formats have no multisample form), and the dummy tables have an entry for each Metal texture type.
- **Found on:** [Super Monkey Ball Banana Rumble](../../dm/games/010031F019294000/README.md#files-and-shaders): URP's `CopyDepth` (`a3d53d20b89e6015`) reads the multisample depth buffer at the title.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_ms_slot`: the reflection of each multisample type, the multisample dummies of every pixel class, the 1D dummy beside the multisample array's, and a kernel reading the float, uint and depth dummies through multisample arguments under Metal API validation (all samples 0).

### Integer views for images a shader declares uint or int

- **Symptom:** translucent UI (frosted text bands, button guides, dialog headers) yellow and white.
- **Cause:** the composite shader loads the window texture's texel as `r32ui`, unpacks the four bytes and stores the blend through a float view of the same image. The host bound the RGBA8Unorm texture to the `texture2d<uint>` argument as it was, whose reads are undefined (Metal's validation layer does not check textures in argument tables).
- **Fix:** an image slot the shader declares as uint / int now gets a view of the same texel size in that class (`img_fit` in `nvn_mtl.m`). The format-view usage costs lossless compression, so a texture gets it only when a shader needs such a view: it is upgraded (copied into a texture with the usage) before the next draw pass opens, as the copy cannot run inside the draw's own pass; until then that one draw reads it as before.
- **Found on:** [FINAL FANTASY TACTICS - The Ivalice Chronicles](../../dm/games/010038B015560000/README.md): the last passes have no render target; fragment shaders composite each UI layer into the window texture with image stores, blending in software (`uirender.tec`, `4cf1975ed1e36590`).

### A dispatch upgrades its own images for another class's view

- **Symptom:** a compute kernel's output through an image slot it declares uint / int is missing or garbage when the texture is another class (an RG32F volume written as `rg32ui`); a table such a kernel writes once at load stays wrong for good.
- **Cause:** a texture made without the format-view usage is upgraded for [the slot's class view](#integer-views-for-images-a-shader-declares-uint-or-int) only when the next draw pass opens (`img_fit_upgrade` in `rp_begin_ex_`). A dispatch has no such point once its slots resolve, so the dispatch itself ran with the texture bound as is, its reads and writes through the mismatched type undefined.
- **Fix:** `img_fit_prescan` in `nvn_mtl.m`: before its encoder opens, a dispatch looks at its image slots, queues each whose texture needs a class view and lacks the format-view usage, and runs the upgrade then, so the slot gets the view at once. Draws keep the upgrade at their pass's start.
- **Found on:** ported from S2Arm 2e9a6ce7 (Apache-2.0, its issue #768, a black 3D scene not confirmed as this cause); no game here was run with it.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_image_usage` (`class_view_case`: a uint slot over an RG32F texture without the usage is upgraded by the prescan, gets the RG32Uint view at once, and a kernel's writes through it reach the texture another name sees; the first two checks fail without the fix, the write-back check passes either way on an M4 Max, whose GPU happens to store the bits).

### Float views for integer buffer images

- **Symptom:** a buffer image appears in both integer atomic and float load/store arguments. The float argument's format differs from the shader declaration.
- **Cause:** `img_binding` adapted only slots declared as integers. Metal leaves reads and writes through a mismatched texture declaration undefined. The game uses the same histogram bytes through `texture_buffer<uint>` atomics and `texture_buffer<float>` loads and stores.
- **Fix:** `img_fit` takes a same-width float view of 32-bit integer buffer images (`img_needs_class_view`, `tbuf_view_format`); the view shares the buffer and offset. Such image buffers are made with the format-view usage (a view on a pool's buffer has no lossless compression for it to cost), so the first bind gets the view. `check_t_buffer_image_float` checks the view and the bits through both views under Metal validation.
- **Found on:** LEGO Star Wars: The Skywalker Saga upstream (no profile in NX 18): clear `9a6b21facc9b346d`, accumulation `fd31a0e4ff650684` and reduction `823aeb9661fb80eb` share a 36-word R32Uint histogram. Ported from upstream NXNative `d43a37905`; not measured in NX 18.

### Image views of the type a shader declares

- **Symptom:** a compute pass's image stores are lost with no red line: the levels or layers it writes stay as they were. With `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP`, its `W` lines read `target=3 shader wants target 2` (a 2D array in a slot the shader declares 2D).
- **Cause:** an image store or load carries its own dimension, as a sample does, and a non-array access of an array image reaches layer 0 of its view. Sampled slots get a view of the declared type ([Compatible views for a mismatched declared texture type](#compatible-views-for-a-mismatched-declared-texture-type)), but image slots adapted only the pixel class (`img_fit`), so a one-layer 2D-array view went to a `texture2d` argument as it was, and Metal dropped the stores.
- **Fix:** `img_fit` makes the image view with the shader's pixel class and its declared type together, with the layers `fit_layers` gives (the rule `tex_fit` uses), from the texture under the bound view and without its channel mapping. A type no view bridges (a 2D array in a 3D slot, a multisample texture in a 2D one) is bound as is and logged once: `image slot declared as Metal type … holds texture ID … of type …: no view bridges them`. `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_image_type` stores through a 2D slot into one layer and level of a 2D array and checks nothing else changed.
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md) (GitHub issue #1648): the blob shadows are two R8 2D arrays (64x64x128 and 128x128x64, one layer per character or vehicle); a compute pass writes each layer's mips through one-layer array views in slots its shader declares 2D. Levels 1 and 2 stayed zero, which showed once the depth downsample was right (473c9026): the decal `8206d20d0055515d` read the zero mips at a distance and drew a dark box around each kart's shadow.

### Buffer textures behind handles in uniform buffers, and rows of finalized descriptors

- **Symptom:** flat-coloured objects with no texture detail in an otherwise correct scene: the UVs and colours a vertex factory fetches from buffers read 0. Then `texture buffers bound as 2D rows … N times, about N made` in the log, a Metal texture for nearly every bind.
- **Cause:** with a handle in a uniform buffer the build has no descriptor to ask, so a buffer texture there is declared 2D and read [in rows](#buffer-textures-read-by-1d-integer-fetches-row-wrap); only slots the translator recorded got the row form. And the rows were kept with the texture object, while Unreal Engine 5.4 finalizes its buffer texture objects and keeps using the registered IDs, as the API allows. Comparing the texture's address in the cache missed as well, as new descriptors view the same memory.
- **Fix:** any `NVN_RB_TEXU` declared 2D takes the row form for a buffer texture. Rows of a finalized object are kept by what they view (`g_tb2_gone`: buffer, offset, length, format), emptied with the pools.
- **Found on:** [Yoshi and the Mysterious Book](../../dm/games/0400245021D2C000/README.md): 13.1 million views made for 13.2 million binds became 1.08 million, with frame times unchanged at 16.7 ms.

### sRGB textures loaded and stored raw through an image

- **Symptom:** the whole picture pale and washed out, its blacks lifted to grey (a frame's median luminance 0.46 against 0.16 in console footage, its darkest percent 0.18 against 0.01): bloom reads as a haze over everything and the dither of fine detail (Star Fox's fur) as grain. A pass dump shows a compute shader writing an `RGBA8_SRGB` texture (Metal format 71) through an image.
- **Cause:** NVN's image loads and stores convert no sRGB, as OpenGL's image units do not (no sRGB format is an image format there), so a shader that stores an sRGB-encoded value into an sRGB texture's image leaves those bytes as they are; sampling decodes them later. Metal decodes a read and encodes a write through an sRGB format, and the host bound the texture's own Metal format to the image slot, so each stored value was encoded a second time. Star Fox relies on it: after DLSS (MetalFX here) its copy pass encodes the linear result itself (`35ebccc529cc58a9`, flag 0x80) into its `RGBA8_SRGB` target, so that the upscaler network (`ddc729bcdb4c8df9`), which samples that target, gets encoded colour, and the network stores its encoded output into the `RGBA8_SRGB` image it fills (768x432 → 1536x864). Measured on one frame: MetalFX's output equals the decoded input (identity), the stored bytes are the twice-encoded output, and the displayed picture decoded once matches the console footage's percentiles within 0.02.
- **Fix:** `img_binding` / `img_fit` (`nvn_mtl.m`), for every game: an image slot holding an sRGB texture gets a view of its linear twin (`pf_srgb_linear`, the `g_pfv_srgb` pairs, which Metal views without the format-view usage), so loads and stores go raw; the sampled descriptor keeps the sRGB format. Log line `sRGB texture bound as an image`. The MetalFX stand-in for a game's upscaler ([A game's own upscaler drawn by MetalFX](#a-games-own-upscaler-drawn-by-metalfx)) takes its output through the same binding; no game seen gives it an sRGB image (Donkey Kong Bananza's is R11G11B10F).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_image_srgb` (Metal validation): RGBA8_SRGB and BGRA8_SRGB textures; the image view is the linear twin over the same writable storage, an image load returns the stored byte (0x80 → 0.502), a sampled read decodes it (0.216), and an image store of 0.5 keeps 0x80 (0xbc before).
- **Found on:** [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md) (update 131072), measured on a build of our fork: every frame after its DLSS pass; with it fixed, General Pepper's close-up in the intro matches console footage (luminance percentiles 1 / 50 / 90 / 99: 0.024 / 0.174 / 0.529 / 0.851 against 0.012 / 0.158 / 0.513 / 0.861), where before it was 0.175 / 0.456 / 0.747 / 0.933. Its bloom chain (separate textures 384x216 to 12x6, additive upsampling as the game sets it, 1/30 in the composite) and MetalFX's DLSS stand-in were measured unchanged by this.

### Buffer textures read by 1D integer fetches (row wrap)

- **Symptom:** `texture type mismatch: shader declares 2, texture is 9`; the slot read 0.
- **Cause:** Maxwell fetches a texture buffer and a 1D texture with the same instructions (TLD with integer coordinates only, TLDS `Texture1DLodZero`), and the bound descriptor decides which it is. Without PC reflection the translator has no type answer and reads such a slot as a 2D texture at `(x, 0)`, and the host could only bind a dummy when the game put a buffer texture there.
- **Fix:** the vendored translator (`InstEmitTexture.cs`, marked `nxnative:`) now addresses every such fetch as `(x & 0x3FFF, x >> 14)` and reports the slot (`fetch1d` in the reflection). The host binds a buffer texture in a flagged slot as a linear 2D texture of 16,384-texel rows over the same pool memory (`tbuf_as_2d` in `nvn_mtl.m`, `nvn_tbuf_rows` in `nvn_tex.c`), cached by texture serial beside the views. A real 1D texture, which the host creates as a single row at most 16,384 wide, reads as before. A mismatch no view can bridge is logged once per shader and slot, with its texture ID, NVN target and formats (`tex_mis_note`).
- **Note:** limits: a size query on a buffer of more than 16,384 elements returns 16,384 (no shader both size-queries and fetches such a slot in Age of Calamity; the build summary counts them), and the rows stop at the end of the Metal buffer.
- **Found on:** [Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity](../../dm/games/01002B00111A2000/README.md): 16,438 such shaders (98,970 bindings; slots 16–21 hold KTGL's light and decal lists).
- **Check:** Age of Calamity's 15-minute Mac battle bound 15.7 million of them through 320 row textures, none failed; the scene is darker and more saturated than with the zeros (mean luma 163 against 178 at the start of the battle), GPU time unchanged.

### Integer buffer textures read through float slots

- **Symptom:** none of its own seen here. Pull request #984 reports GPU hangs from huge light counts on its build of the same game.
- **Cause:** with no type answer the translator declares a fetched texture float. KTGL keeps its light and decal lists in 32-bit integer buffers and takes the fetched bits back with `as_type<int>`. Reading an integer texture through a float declaration is undefined in Metal.
- **Fix:** `tbuf_as_2d` (`nvn_mtl.m`) views a 32-bit integer buffer in a slot declared float as the float format of the same size (`tbuf_view_format`), so the shader reads the stored bits.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Pokopia](../../dm/games/0400A3A01EE4C000/README.md). No visible change in its tutorial area.

### Integer textures read through float slots

- **Symptom:** none visible on an M6, where at most 0.23 % of pixels differ between paired runs. GitHub issue #783 reports wrong light offsets on an M2.
- **Cause:** as for [buffer textures](#integer-buffer-textures-read-through-float-slots), but for a 2D texture: with no type answer the translator declares it float, and the shader takes the fetched bits back with `as_type<int>`. The answer table cannot say otherwise, because the format is known only at bind time.
- **Fix:** `tex_float_fit` (`nvn_mtl.m`) binds the float view of the same size (`tbuf_view_format`) in a slot declared float. A texture made without the format-view usage is upgraded before the next draw pass (`img_fit_upgrade`). 32-bit formats only: a smaller integer format has no float view that keeps its bits.
- **Found on:** [Metroid Prime Remastered](../../dm/games/010012101468C000/README.md): the clustered lighting's R32Uint light grid (`fde1a3e769219a77` and the other world materials). Reported and fixed by nobz226.
- **Check:** `t_int_float_view` reads small integers (denormal floats), NaN patterns and negatives through the R32Float view in a compute shader and takes back every bit.

### Private buffer texture fallbacks must not query CPU contents

- **Symptom:** Metal API validation aborts in `validateCPUWriteable` before an unsupported buffer-texture copy can report its fallback.
- **Cause:** `tbuf_shadow` and `tbuf_rows_copy` tested `!buffer.contents` to detect CPU-inaccessible storage. Calling `contents` on a private `MTLBuffer` is itself invalid under API validation; it is not a safe capability query there.
- **Fix:** check `storageMode` before querying `contents`. Keep the existing warning and unsupported result: the shadow leaves the original view unchanged, while the row copy returns nil. This avoids the invalid API call; it does not make GPU-written snapshots coherent or make private misaligned textures render correctly.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_tbuf_access` uses real private Metal allocations and aligned buffer textures under API validation, with both encoder flags. The row case requests a larger row alignment; in NX 18 the shadow case raises the alignment with `DM_TBUF_ALIGN` so the aligned view sits below it and reaches the guard (upstream registers an owning view for its `tbuf_lead`). Both check that the fallback result and resource ID stay as they were and that no CPU snapshot or staging copy is made. Ported from upstream NXNative `f67a9e88d`.
- **Found on:** synthetic tests during [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) source review; no Ocarina crash or rendering artifact is attributed to this path.

### Transient buffer textures shared by storage

- **Symptom:** the residency line adds and removes thousands of allocations every 300 frames, nearly all from one line, the `res_add` of a buffer texture's view in `tex_mtl_`; the memory line's `finalized textures still referenced` stays at the ghost table's 512 while its puts in total climb by about a hundred or more a frame. No red line and no visible effect: the cost is submit-thread time in residency commits.
- **Cause:** a buffer texture (`NVN_TEXTURE_TARGET_BUFFER`) got a Metal view of its own for each NVN texture object, made before the storage-sharing table was looked at, while every other texture is shared by `(pool, offset, description)` ([Transient render targets shared by storage](#transient-render-targets-shared-by-storage)). Unreal Engine makes its transient buffer textures again every frame over the same pool ranges, so each frame added new views to the residency set and finalized objects to the ghost table, which they wrapped, pushing out finalized textures that are still in use.
- **Fix:** a buffer texture's view (`tex_buffer` in `nvn_mtl.m`) is shared through the table like any other texture (`the sharing table (`g_alias`)`, ``alias_put``): one view per pool range and description, kept resident by the table until its pool is finalized or a new texture takes its storage over. The view starts at the aligned boundary below its offset as before ([Buffer textures at offsets below 16 bytes](#buffer-textures-at-offsets-below-16-bytes)); textures sharing it have the same offset and width, so the same `tbuf_lead`. Since the table leaves `IMAGE` out of its key ([IMAGE usage does not split texture storage](#image-usage-does-not-split-texture-storage)), the view is made with every usage it can take, whatever the texture's flags (`tbuf_usage`): shader write, atomics for R32 integers, and format views. Made with read usage alone, a view shared with an IMAGE texture object would be rebuilt (`tex_upgrade`) at its first image write, and every one viewed in another format at its first such view, and an image write that `image_prepare` does not see (an image slot past 32) would reach a view that cannot be written. A view on its pool's buffer has no lossless compression for these usages to cost: on an M6, reads of R32F, R32UI, RGBA8, RGBA16F and RGBA32F views over 64 MB took the same time with and without them, within the ±13% the runs differed by. Metal makes no writable BGRA8Unorm texture buffer (its validation refuses one), so that format's view has no write usage; every other format the host maps takes it (checked under validation).
- **Check:** `t_upgrade_alias` under Metal validation: live, finalized and IMAGE objects over one range share its view; each registered ID of it moves its coordinates by the same lead; an atomic image write and an R32UI view rebuild nothing; 64 objects made again over the range add nothing to the residency set; another width over the same range is a view of its own; a GPU write through the shared view lands on the texture's own bytes in the pool and reads back through another object. Without the fix 5 of its checks fail.
- **Found on:** [Mario & Luigi: Brothership](../../dm/games/01006D0017F7A000/README.md) (GitHub issue #1061: about 18 transient buffer textures a frame, 5,350 views added every 300 frames), [FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md) (GitHub issue #2423, an iPhone 15 Pro Max: about 79,000 added and removed every 300 frames of play, 235 ms of residency commits per 300 frames, the ghost table at about 175 puts a frame). Measured on the Mac (M6, macOS 27.2, `regress.py check` headless at 60 fps, 2026-11-14): Pikmin 4, two runs a side, from about 15,000 views added every 300 frames of its route to 86–232, residency commits from about 1,500 to 114–190 per 300 frames, and the submit thread's wall time a frame from 1.79–1.86 ms to 1.56–1.67 ms at the median (2.26 to 1.92–2.08 ms at p90); OCTOPATH TRAVELER II, one run a side, from 22,898 views added every 300 frames to none by the end of its route, 72,541 residency commits in the run to 1,467 (293 to 70 ms), and the submit thread's wall time from 2.15 to 1.85 ms at the median (3.02 to 2.67 at p90). Frame rate held at 60 in both, and GPU memory was unchanged.
- **Open:** a view no texture object names any more stays in the table, and resident, until its pool is finalized or a new texture takes its range over. The table holds more entries for it: Pikmin 4's levelled off at about 6,300 (2,400 before, 3,600 of them unreferenced) from frame 3,900 of its route; OCTOPATH TRAVELER II's grew as areas loaded, from about 1,900 to 7,100 over 24,000 frames (2,070 before), and stayed flat within an area. Each costs a Metal object and a place in the residency set, not memory (the set's bytes were the same or lower). No game has shown growth without bound.
- **NX 18:** ported by hand from upstream NXNative 0929dd69e (MIT) on 2026-11-22, for the owner's FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE: the view is made by `tex_buffer` (unchanged construction: created at its offset, read through the aligned copy `tbuf_shadow` below Metal's alignment, as NX 18 has no offset table) and shared through `g_alias` (`alias_take_over`, `alias_put`; one another thread shared meanwhile is used). Metal does not keep the atomic usage on a texture-buffer view (0x13 on an M4 Max), as before. `t_tbuf_share`: 64 objects remade over one range, image ones among them, resolve to one view and add nothing to the residency set; another range or format makes its own; BGRA8 stays read only. Not yet run on a device.

### Buffer textures at offsets below 16 bytes

- **Symptom:** particle effects drawn at the wrong scale or place, or flickering, where the particle data is a buffer texture at a pool offset that is not a multiple of 16; log line `buffer texture at pool offset …, below Metal's 16-byte alignment`.
- **Cause:** Metal requires 16-byte alignment for texture buffer views (`minimumTextureBufferAlignmentForPixelFormat`), and an Apple GPU addresses a view created at a smaller offset from the 16-byte boundary below it: on an M4 Pro, R32Float views at offsets 4, 8 and 12 read element 0 where elements 1, 2 and 3 are expected, for every format and width tried, shared and private buffers, bound directly or in an argument buffer (`t_tbuf_align`). The host used to leave such a view as a dummy, then created it at the offset on the strength of a measurement on an M6 that the M4 Pro contradicts. NVN places buffer textures at any element-aligned offset, and no Metal view can start there.
- **Fix (since chain 76fc8168da61, below: [read in place through the offset table](#buffer-textures-read-in-place-through-the-offset-table)):** the view starts at the aligned boundary below the offset and the shader moves its reads; the copies described here remain for bindings with no table entry. **Before:** the view was created at the offset, and a shader read it through a copy of its bytes at an aligned offset; aligned views were untouched. The copy is made on the GPU timeline before the draw or dispatch that reads it (`tba_prepare` in `nvn_mtl.m`, see [the copies on the GPU timeline](#aligned-copies-of-buffer-textures-on-the-gpu-timeline)); a copy of its bytes into a shared staging block at its first binding in each submission (`tbuf_shadow`) is the fallback. The alignment is the device's linear-texture alignment for the format (`tbuf_al`): 16 bytes for every format on an M4 Max, 64 on an iPhone. The check used to be a fixed 16 bytes, so on an iPhone a view 16, 32 or 48 bytes past a 64-byte boundary was bound in place. Gaps still **open**: shader writes through an image view at such an offset land at the boundary below (`[RED]` once at creation), and a binding the GPU copy does not reach (more than 32 such buffers before one draw, an element size below 4 bytes at an offset that is not a multiple of 4, a geometry-shader or mesh-task draw) takes the CPU copy, which holds what the CPU wrote before the submission was encoded and binds a pool the CPU cannot access as is (`[RED]` once). A shader-side element offset (the view at the boundary below, the shader adding the skew) would close all of them, at the cost of a chain change and a per-binding value in the argument buffer.
- **Found on:** [Octopath Traveler 0](../../dm/games/01005270232F2000/README.md): one at pool offset `0x168`, read by its particle shaders ([Texture slots picked at run time](shaders.md#texture-slots-picked-at-run-time), `shaders.md`). [FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE](../../dm/games/0100606026F12000/README.md): particle data at offsets 4, 8 and 12 past the boundary (village fires, smoke).

### Buffer images below the 16-byte alignment

- **Symptom:** walls, floors and props vanish and come back from frame to frame in Assassin's Creed II (the Abstergo elevator corridor, the background of Ezio's birth scene); before the fix the log had `[RED] image buffer texture at pool offset …, below Metal's 16-byte alignment: shader writes to it land at the 16-byte boundary below`. Forcing occlusion queries visible (`DM_NVN_VIS_ALL=1`) does not help.
- **Cause:** the game's occlusion culling draws each object's bounding box, and fragment shader `f17624f1613d0321` marks "object k visible" in a 4096-entry R32 image buffer, one of three it rotates through, two of them at pool offsets 8 mod 16. A buffer texture view below Metal's alignment is addressed from the 16-byte boundary below its offset on an M4 Pro ([Buffer textures at offsets below 16 bytes](#buffer-textures-at-offsets-below-16-bytes)), so in those frames every mark landed two objects early and the game culled visible geometry. Reads through texture slots already went through an aligned copy; image loads, stores and atomics did not. An M6 (macOS 27.2) writes through such a view at the offset itself (`t_buffer_image_bias` prints where), so the symptom depends on the GPU.
- **Fix:** the shader moves each coordinate of a buffer image by an element offset the host gives it. The translator (`TranslationFlags.MetalBufferImageBias`, `Instructions.cs` `BiasBufferImageCoordinate`) gives each buffer image of a shader an index in an offset table, bank `DMSH_TBIAS_BANK` (`dmsh_abi.h`), adds that entry to every load, store and atomic coordinate of the image, and leaves a coordinate from minus the offset to -1 as it is, outside the image as on the GPU; `Program.cs` exports the index (`tbx`), which `dmsh_reflect.py` gives the image's binding. The host binds such an image as a view from the aligned boundary below its offset, wider by the elements in between (`tbuf_lead` in `nvn_mtl.m`, kept with the texture's views), and fills the table (`NVN_RB_TBIAS`) in `encode_args`. The shader works on the pool's own bytes, so the rest of the pass sees its writes, with no copies and no limit. A buffer image in an array of images, or past a shader's 16th, keeps the old addressing (`[RED]` once at the binding). A shadow copied in before the pass and back after it (the first version of PR #1399) cured the flicker too, but held at most 8 such images a pass, copied back over other writes to the same bytes in the pass, and split a render pass at a shadow's first use. Checked: `t_buffer_image_bias` (the view, the offset, writes at elements 0 to 15 landing on their own bytes, nothing written before or after, coordinates -1, -2 and 16 dropped), `test_image_atomic_bounds.py` with the rebuilt translator on the GPU (buffer image atomics at element offsets 0 and 2, the words before the image untouched; load and store coordinates through the table), `test_dmsh_reflect.py`.
- **Found on:** Assassin's Creed: The Ezio Collection (0100670014482000, update 1.3; not in the games table), brought up in NX2Apple by the author of GitHub PR #1399, who found the cause and confirmed the shadow fix in the game. This fix has not been run in the game.

### Buffer texture rows at a pool offset the device will not view

- **Symptom:** lighting that alternates between lit and unlit from one frame to the next on an iPhone, the same scene steady on a Mac; log lines `texture buffer (texture ID …, … at pool offset …) has no 2D-row form for a wrapped 1D fetch: the slot reads 0` and `texture type mismatch: … a buffer texture: the slot reads 0 (dummy)`.
- **Cause:** [row wrap](#buffer-textures-read-by-1d-integer-fetches-row-wrap) views a buffer texture at its pool offset, and Metal views only at a multiple of `minimumLinearTextureAlignmentForPixelFormat`, which is per device: 16 bytes for 32-bit formats on an M6 Mac, 32 on an M1 Pro, larger on an A17 Pro. Three Houses keeps two copies of its light list 32 bytes apart, so on the phone one copy was bridged and the other read zeros, and the picture alternated with the list (the two copies alternate per rendered frame, which is why no present-interval or frame-rate setting changed it).
- **Fix:** `tbuf_as_2d` (`nvn_mtl.m`) reads the 2D rows of a buffer texture at an unaligned offset from a copy in the submission's arena (256-byte offsets, already resident, recycled once the submission is done); rows at an aligned offset are untouched. The copy is made on the GPU timeline before the draw or dispatch ([below](#aligned-copies-of-buffer-textures-on-the-gpu-timeline)); the CPU copy (`tbuf_rows_copy`, one per texture per submission, counted in the periodic `tbuf_as_2d` line as `… copied into aligned rows (N MB)`) is the fallback, with the gaps of the case above. The shader-side element offset named there would close them here too.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem: Three Houses](../../dm/games/010055D009F78000/README.md): its lighting flickered on an iPhone 15 Pro class device, its two light-list copies 32 bytes apart.
- **Check:** on an M1 Pro with `DM_TBUF_ALIGN=64`, a Mac that cannot see the problem otherwise: `[RED]` lines naming the same shaders and slots as the phone's log on the base this was found on (18 of them, `094a2d11253f126c_b` slots 18/19/20, `f54830a75e07e14a_b` slot 20, `6797af67f88a4ddd_b` slot 0), and a static terrain patch that stops being static — `YAVG` two values 0.008 apart alternating frame to frame, against one value for 12 frames with the alignment the Mac reports. With the copy in, the same forced alignment fails nothing, copies 5,058 times over the run (4,592 MB, about 2 copies and 2 MB a frame) and leaves the single value the aligned path gives; with the Mac's own alignment no texture takes the copy. Three Houses' own frames on the phone then hold 60 fps with the flicker gone.
- **Not checked:** another device's alignment (the value is read, not assumed). A buffer the GPU writes in the same submission is now copied after that write ([below](#aligned-copies-of-buffer-textures-on-the-gpu-timeline)); no game here is known to have one.

### Aligned copies of buffer textures on the GPU timeline

- **Symptom:** the two cases above, on a buffer the GPU writes: lighting that still flickers, or particles still wrong, while the log shows the aligned copies being made.
- **Cause:** the copies were made on the CPU when the binding was encoded, so they held what the CPU wrote before the submission: a compute pass or a draw that wrote the buffer earlier in the same submission, or in one still running, was not seen. The S2Arm project met it on an iPhone with a light list of 1,052,640 R32UI words (4 MB) a compute pass writes, at a pool offset 32 bytes off the 64 the phone needs: its first copy, which refused buffers over 64 KB or written by the GPU, left the slot reading zeros and the lighting flickering again.
- **Fix:** `tba_prepare` (`nvn_mtl.m`; ported from S2Arm 8aa6f66c, and 2c7f74c5 for buffer textures read as texture buffers) runs before each draw and dispatch whose shaders read buffer textures (decided once per shader, `tb_reads`), and only once a buffer texture below the alignment has been made. For each such texture the program's bindings reach (texture buffers, handles in uniform buffers and handle arrays, and 2D rows of wrapped 1D fetches), it ends the open render pass and copies the bytes into the submission's arena in the copy encoder, behind the barriers that order it after every earlier GPU write; the bindings then view the copy (`tba_view`: the texture buffer with its swizzle, or the 2D rows). A copy holds while nothing could have written its bytes: the same submission, no copy, fill or dispatch since (`g_wr_epoch`, and `g_tba_gen` after each dispatch is encoded), no storage write noted over its range (`sbw_hits`) and no inline update queued over it (`pend_last`). Not seen: an image store into such a buffer during a render pass. Not taken: the geometry-shader draw path and mesh-task draws (they keep the CPU copy). Otherwise the next draw that reads it copies again, which splits the render pass it falls in. Costs: one copy of the bytes per change (counted in the periodic `tbuf_as_2d` line as `… copied on the GPU N times (N MB, render passes split N), on the CPU N times`), and nothing at all in a game that has no buffer texture below the alignment. `DM_NVN_TBUF_GPU_COPY=0` goes back to the CPU copies alone.
- **Check:** `t_tbuf_gpu_copy`: a dispatch writes 70,000 words at a pool offset 8 bytes past the alignment, and two dispatches of the same submission read them through a buffer texture over them, as a texture buffer and as 2D rows: both read what the first wrote. With `DM_NVN_TBUF_GPU_COPY=0` both read the bytes from before the submission (the gap), and with `DM_TBUF_ALIGN=64` at offset 32 (an iPhone's case the old 16-byte check left in place) the copies are made. A second submission reads the words the CPU wrote between them. The test runs without Metal's validation layer, which refuses the view the host makes in place at such an offset. Not checked: a draw (the same `tba_prepare` from `draw_`), a render pass split by a copy, and any game; not run on an iPhone.
- **Found on:** no game here. Reported by the S2Arm project on an iPhone.

### Buffer textures read in place through the offset table

- **Symptom:** FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE on the owner's iPhone 18 Pro Max (75fb5543c): 2.6 million GPU copies of buffer textures below the alignment (388 MB) and 950,775 render passes split for them in 22 minutes, and `[RED] buffer textures below the alignment: more than 32 copied before one draw; the rest are copied on the CPU`.
- **Cause:** every read of a buffer texture below the device's view alignment (64 bytes on an iPhone) went through a copy ([above](#aligned-copies-of-buffer-textures-on-the-gpu-timeline)); Unreal's particle and vertex-factory data sit at such offsets all the time.
- **Fix:** ported from upstream NXNative `596d719c8` (PR #1399's buffer images) and `3c4de03c7` (MIT), adapted to NX 18's GPU copies. Every buffer texture's view starts at the aligned boundary below its offset (`tex_buffer`, `tbuf_view_al`: the larger of the linear-texture and texture-buffer alignments), wider by the elements in between (`tbuf_lead`). The translator (`TranslationFlags.MetalBufferImageBias`) gives each buffer image, texture buffer slot (`TBUF`, `TBUFU`) and each element of an array of buffer textures (`TEXD`, `TEXA`) an entry in the shader's offset table (bank `DMSH_TBIAS_BANK`, up to `DMSH_TBIAS_SLOTS` entries), moves every fetch, load, store and atomic by it, and gives a size query the view's width less it. `encode_args` fills the table in the arena as it binds (`NVN_RB_TBIAS`); a shader with a table is always encoded whole (its kind is on no patch or reuse list). A binding with no entry (a texture slot declared as a texture, a handle array in a uniform buffer, past the table) still reads the texture's own bytes through an aligned copy (`tba_prepare` on the GPU, `tbuf_shadow` on the CPU), now copied from the texture's own offset; `tba_collect` leaves out the bindings read in place, so they no longer split passes. Reads through a texture pool or a handle table carry no entry and read early: `[RED] buffer texture (texture ID …) whose view starts … elements before it …, read through a texture pool or handle table` (key 182). A buffer image with no entry: `[RED] buffer image whose view starts …` (key 119). Every game's shaders rebuild (chain `6d434ffbc316` → `76fc8168da61`).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_buffer_image_bias` (Metal validation, `DM_TBUF_ALIGN=16`): 18 checks (views, image writes, texture-slot reads in place and through the copy, an array read by per-element entries, sizes). `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=… DMSH_TEST_METAL=1 DMSH_TEST_GPU=1 test_image_atomic_bounds.py`: 14 tests, buffer image atomics and buffer texture fetches with size queries at element offsets on the GPU. `test_dmsh_reflect.py`. Not checked yet: a game on the iPhone (Resonance's next run should show the splits gone and no `[RED]` 182).

### Automatic block height truncates

- **Symptom:** torn digits and a background in blue steps (Rhythm Heaven Groove's latency calibration).
- **Cause:** NVN chooses the automatic block height by truncating `height_in_blocks * 1.5 / 8`, while `nvn_tex.c` rounded the division up, so some sizes (a 160×160 BC3 texture: H3 instead of H2) were read with the wrong layout; Rhythm Heaven Groove's BC7 textures without a packaged layout were read with the wrong block height.
- **Fix:** truncating height in blocks × 1.5 / 8, as MacNativeGames found: `nvn_tex.c` now truncates for every game (`pick_tile(ah * 3 / 16)`). It changes layouts at 6–10, 17–21, 38–42 and 81–85 block rows, which matters only for data the game lays out itself (no packaged layout: Tears of the Kingdom's flushed textures, Monster Hunter Rise's sparse ones); those were not re-checked.
- **Found on:** found by MacNativeGames on [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md) (listed there as open); confirmed by [Rhythm Heaven Groove](../../dm/games/0100D9F01D474000/README.md) on its two 40-block-row textures (two BC7 textures): the rule matches every size checked by eye: 672×160 and 1232×160 (40 block rows: 4 GOBs, where rounding up gives 8), and 112×112 (4), 288×208 (8) and 880×432 (16), where both roundings agree and `floor(rows / 8)`, the other rule that fits 40 rows, does not; and by Touchable (a d2r-mobile port of Tears of the Kingdom) on 16 of its texture files.
- **Check:** a dump of the guest bytes deswizzled in Python with 1 to 16 GOBs and decoded with `texture2ddecoder` shows the right one by eye; a smoothness score does not, because a block height too tall reads padding and looks smooth.
- **Not checked:** the games whose textures the game lays out itself (above).

### Sparse textures laid out in 64 KB tiles

- **Symptom:** magenta (the ASTC error colour) on streamed textures in a sparse pool; `DM_NVN_ZBLOCK` finds such textures (all-zero blocks).
- **Cause:** sparse textures have a layout of their own: they are mapped in 64 KB tiles, one row of blocks 128 GOBs / (block height × block depth) wide. The game's files hold its streamed textures pre-swizzled that way, and it places each level at the offset the host reports; the host laid them out unpadded.
- **Fix:** `nvn_tex.c` `level_walk`: a level at least one tile wide, high and deep is laid out with its width padded to whole tiles, the levels from the first smaller one on form the mip tail (packed as usual), and the storage is whole tiles, 64 KB aligned.
- **Found on:** [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md): laid out unpadded, the 1024×1024 ASTC 6×6 clothes of Hinoa and Minoto (43 GOBs wide instead of 48, level 1 at 0x81000 instead of 0x90000) read 2,183 all-zero blocks in level 0 and 3,043 in level 1, which ASTC decodes as its error colour: their trousers, sleeves and gloves were magenta, worst in Kamura Village.

### Packaged textures carry width alignment as well as height and depth

- **Symptom:** a fixed magenta rectangle over part of a packaged ASTC texture (Bayonetta Origins' tutorial-card background), the same in every frame.
- **Cause:** `SetPackagedTextureLayout` was kept only as byte 0 (block height and depth) in the texture description. Byte 1's low nibble is log2 of a width alignment in GOBs: the levels at least one tile (that many GOBs wide, one block high and deep) in size are padded to whole tiles, as a sparse texture's are to its 64 KB tiles, the levels from the first smaller one on form the mip tail, and a layered texture's layers align to the whole block times that width. It applies whether or not the texture is `SPARSE`. Without it a texture whose width is not a whole number of tiles was laid out too narrow: its rows from the second block row on, its later levels and layers read from the wrong place, and the padding they read, all-zero ASTC blocks, decodes as magenta.
- **Fix:** `nvn_tex.c` (`packaged_width_log2`, `level_walk`, `layer_bytes`): `nvn_texdesc.pkg_layout` holds byte 0 and the width nibble, and the width alignment pads the tiled levels and aligns the layers. A layout with a width nibble of 0 is laid out as before; flags and the sparse tile layout the game reads are unchanged. The `[nvn] packaged texture` log line prints the width alignment.
- **Found on:** Bayonetta Origins: Cereza and the Lost Demon (`0100CF5010FEC000`, 1.0.0, no profile in the repository): the 2052x1026 ASTC 6x6 tutorial-card background (byte 0 3, width 4) is 96 GOBs wide and 0x120000 bytes; laid out 86 GOBs wide (0x102000) its rows from y 384 on read ten GOB columns of padding, the 240-pixel-wide magenta rectangle. Checked on an iPhone (PR #2268). The packaged layouts logged in nine other games' runs all have a width nibble of 0.
- **Check:** `t_tex_layout` (width 0 and 4, a mip tail, an array and a cube; the width-4 shapes fail without the fix) and `t_nvn` (the builder keeps the nibble, the flags and sparse tile layout are unchanged). The author compared the layout with the SDK's own arithmetic on 37,296 shapes and replayed the game's texture on the GPU; neither is in the repository (they need the game's files).

### Sparse textures take the block height of their 64 KB tile

- **Symptom:** a CPU-filled texture shuffled into a patchwork of rectangles (OCTOPATH TRAVELER's world map on the traveler selection).
- **Cause:** a sparse 2D texture without a packaged layout is laid out with the block height of its 64 KB tile, whose shape in blocks is the standard one: 256×256, 256×128, 128×128, 128×64 and 64×64 blocks for 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 bytes per block, i.e. 32, 16, 16, 8 and 8 GOBs. The host used the [automatic block height](#automatic-block-height-truncates): 16 GOBs for the 4096×2048 BC1 map (512 block rows), whose tiles then landed in the wrong places.
- **Fix:** `nvn_tex.c` `sparse_tile_h` gives such a texture its tile's block height; a packaged layout still wins.
- **Found on:** [OCTOPATH TRAVELER](../../dm/games/010057D006492000/README.md) (Unreal Engine 4.18 flags its streamed and CPU-loaded textures sparse).
- **Check:** the map's guest bytes deswizzled in Python at 1 to 32 GOBs read right only at 8; a 512×512 RGBA8 sprite sheet only at 16; [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md)'s packaged ASTC 6×6 textures (16 bytes per block) carry 8 in their layout. The 1- and 2-byte heights follow the same tile shapes and were not seen.

### Sparse 3D textures take the standard 3D tile

- **Symptom:** none visible on an M6 on the Frigate Orpheon's deck. Laid out 1 GOB high, the mapped tiles of a streamed 3D texture fall in scattered places, so lookups read zero in the holes; GitHub issue #783 reports a black gunship hull in the opening on an M2.
- **Cause:** a sparse 3D texture without a packaged layout took the automatic layout (1 GOB high, 32 GOBs deep). Its 64 KB tile is the standard 3D shape: 64x32x32, 32x32x32, 32x32x16, 32x16x16 and 16x16x16 blocks for 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16 bytes per block.
- **Fix:** `nvn_tex.c` `sparse_tile_h3`, `sparse_tile_d3` give such a texture its tile's block height and depth when level 0 is at least one tile in each direction; a packaged layout still wins, and a smaller texture keeps the automatic layout. `nvnTextureBuilderGetSparseTileLayout` answers the same shape.
- **Found on:** [Metroid Prime Remastered](../../dm/games/010012101468C000/README.md): its six 512x256x368 lighting volumes map the same tile indices whichever shape the host reports (256x32x16 or 128x64x16 texels for BC1), so the game's layout is fixed. With this shape BC1's tiles 5-7 and 9-11 of a 16-slice slab and BC6H's 10-15 and 18-23 are the same texels, 128..512 x 64..192. With 1-GOB blocks they were two different patchworks. Reported and fixed by nobz226.
