- **Check:** `t_sparse_layout` fills those tiles of both formats and decodes exactly that box, in exactly that slab, plus the tile shapes of 1, 2, 4 and 8 bytes per block. Only 8 and 16 bytes per block were seen in a game; the others follow the same table. Metal cannot confirm the shape: `sparseTileSizeWithTextureType:` gives a 3D texture the 2D tile with depth 1.

### Sparse textures of 8- and 16-byte blocks: blocks at most 8 GOBs high

- **Symptom:** crashes that move between runs (30–70% of boots, in the first minute): in the allocator, on bogus pointers, in unrelated threads. Unreal's purgatory proxy ([05-debugging](../agents/05-debugging.md)) finds freed 64 KB-aligned blocks modified; with the parked blocks read-only, the faulting store is a `memcpy` of texture mip data from the texture streamer into the buffer `RHILockTexture2D` returned.
- **Cause:** with the automatic block height the host gave sparse textures the same block height as plain ones (16 GOBs for 96 or more block rows), so a 64 KB tile was 8 GOBs wide. For 8- and 16-byte blocks (DXT1, DXT5 and the like) the real layout uses blocks of at most 8 GOBs and so tiles 16 GOBs (1,024 bytes) wide: the game's pre-swizzled data for a 384×384 DXT5 level is 32 GOBs × two 8-GOB block rows, 0x40000 bytes, where the host's layout said 0x30000 (the padding sits at the end of each 8-GOB block in the dump). 4-byte texels keep 16-GOB blocks (a 1350×270 BGRA8 level is 88 GOBs × three 16-GOB block rows, 0x210000 bytes). Unreal's NVN RHI sizes its lock buffer from the host's level layout and copies the cooked level in at its own size, past the end of the buffer.
- **Fix:** `nvn_tex.c` `sparse_tile_h` (see [Sparse textures take the block height of their 64 KB tile](#sparse-textures-take-the-block-height-of-their-64-kb-tile)): a sparse texture without a packaged layout and with 8 or 16 bytes per block takes 8-GOB blocks. `dm/gfx/tests/t_sparse_layout.c` checks 15 measured textures and Monster Hunter Rise's level offsets.
- **Found on:** [Yoshi's Crafted World](../../dm/games/01006000040C2000/README.md): 9 streamed DXT5 textures (384×384 to 1920×912) overran their buffers by 0x8000–0x20000 bytes; after the fix none of 10,578 level copies did, and 10 of 10 boots ran clean.

### Sparse textures smaller than a tile keep the automatic block height

- **Symptom:** a small CPU-filled sparse texture shows part of its image beside fragments, cut off at the bottom (Yoshi's Crafted World's loading icon: the left half of a chalk drawing and two stray strokes).
- **Cause:** a sparse texture smaller than one 64 KB tile in width or height is all mip tail, laid out as a plain texture with the [automatic block height](#automatic-block-height-truncates), but the host gave every sparse texture its tile's block height. The 192×160 DXT5 icon (12 × 5 GOBs, under the 16 × 8 GOBs of a 16-byte tile) is laid out in 4 GOBs and was read with 8; a 384×384 DXT1 (12 GOBs wide, under the 16-GOB tile width, 96 block rows) in 16, also read with 8. The storage sizes stay the same, so only the picture shows it.
- **Fix:** `nvn_tex.c` `nvn_tex_layout` gives a sparse texture its tile's block height only when level 0 is at least one tile wide and high, the test `level_walk` uses for padding; the tile's shape for that test and for `nvn_tex_sparse_tiles` comes from the block size (`tile_shape_h`), not from the block height the texture got. `t_tex_layout.c` checks both block heights and the icon's tile shape.
- **Found on:** [Yoshi's Crafted World](../../dm/games/01006000040C2000/README.md): the guest bytes of the icon and of the DXT1 deswizzled in Python read right only at 4 and 16 GOBs; its 384×384 DXT5 (24 GOBs wide, at least one tile) still reads right only padded to 32 GOBs at 8.

### Sparse textures bound from their first mapped level

- **Symptom:** streamed textures read 0 (magenta on Monster Hunter Rise's title screen's clothes).
- **Cause:** the translator drops TEX's LOD clamp and its residency predicate, so streamed textures were sampled at levels the game had not mapped yet.
- **Fix:** a sparse texture is bound as a view starting at its first level whose pages, and those of every smaller level, are all mapped (`sparse_view` in `nvn_mtl.m`). That samples exactly as a minimum LOD would; the view follows each upload.
- **Found on:** [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md).

### Sampler reduction (min / max)

- **Cause:** a sampler built with `SamplerBuilderSetReductionFilter` (min / max) was averaged by the host. Metal 26 has the same thing (`MTLSamplerDescriptor.reductionMode`), but ignores it with a nearest or no mip filter.
- **Fix:** reduction samplers filter mips linearly (`samp_by_id`): at the integer LODs reductions are used with, that is the same level.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md): its depth pyramid is built with a min-reduction sampler (`SamplerBuilderSetReductionFilter 1`; `151607c7a92386cc` samples the level above at an explicit LOD).

### Sampler LOD bias

- **Note:** the Vulkan layer was removed in 0.5 (in git at `93c34371`); its half of this entry (`vkCreateSampler`, the red line) is history, the NVN half still applies.
- **Symptom:** textures a mip level sharper or blurrier than on the Switch where the game biases its sampler; in the Vulkan layer (removed in 0.5; history), a red line `sampler LOD bias … not implemented`.
- **Cause:** the host kept NVN's `SamplerBuilderSetLodBias` and Vulkan's `mipLodBias` but never gave them to Metal, taking them for something Metal samplers lack. Metal 26 has `MTLSamplerDescriptor.lodBias` (range −16 to 15.99).
- **Fix:** `samp_by_id` (NVN) sets it, clamped to that range; the Vulkan layer's `vkCreateSampler` did too, until 0.5 removed it (clamped to the ±15 the device reported as `maxSamplerLodBias`).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Galaxy](../../dm/games/010099C022B96000/README.md) (the Vulkan red line). MacNativeGames uses the property for its own MetalFX bias, which pointed to it.

### Border colours Metal has no sampler state for

- **Symptom:** whatever a game keeps in a texture that it samples past its edge with clamp-to-border reads the wrong value there: grass flattened along Link's path in Tears of the Kingdom, its bend map filled with "bent" wherever the window scrolled.
- **Cause:** NVN's `SamplerBuilderSetBorderColor` takes any RGBA colour; `MTLSamplerBorderColor` has transparent black, opaque black and opaque white only, and `samp_by_id` took the nearest of them. Tears of the Kingdom's border (0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 0.5) became (0, 0, 0, 0).
- **Fix:** a filtered sample is linear in the border colour, so two samples through samplers that differ only in their Metal border colour give the exact one: r = s_n + (s_w − s_n)·k, k = (B − Bn) / (Bw − Bn) per channel. The build (`dmsh_rewrite.py border_emu`) wraps every sample through a sampler member of the argument buffer in `dm_bs(…)`, adds a twin sampler member `<member>_w` for each, and one block `dm_border` (a mask word, then k per sampler; `DMSH_BORDER_*` in `dmsh_abi.h`). The host (`nvn_border.h`) keeps the nearest Metal colour for the sampler and makes a twin only for a colour Metal lacks when a wrap mode reaches the border and no min / max reduction is set; `encode_args` binds the twin and sets the sampler's bit and k, and binds the shared zero bank as the block otherwise. The twin sample runs only for those samplers. A pipeline whose draw or dispatch binds no sampler with a twin is built without the corrections ([details](#unused-border-corrections-retain-sample-registers)). The readable-tile variants (`dmsh_fetch.py`) replace a wrapped sample whole, so a tile read is never corrected.
- **Not covered:** samples through sampler arrays, `sample_compare` and `gather`, and samples of integer textures (not filtered; their border colour is an integer one) keep the nearest Metal colour.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md): the grass bend map, a 256×256 RG8 window that follows Link, advanced by `ff9def5b3c418851` with wrap 5 and border 0.5.
- **Check:** `t_border` (nvn_border's choices for opaque, translucent and Metal's own colours, and the registry's twins) and `test_dmsh_border.py` (the rewrite, the reflection, the readable-tile variant, the compile for macOS and iOS, and with `make check_t_border` the GPU: corrected samples against Maxwell's bilinear filter with the border colour, and Metal's nearest colour with the mask clear).

### Unused border corrections retain sample registers

- **Symptom:** material pipelines spill registers even when every sampler uses a native Metal border colour.
- **Cause:** the uniform mask branch in `dm_bs` keeps the twin sample's operands live even when no draw needs the correction.
- **Fix:** every shader that corrects a sample exposes `dm_border_off` (`DMSH_BORDER_FC`, 216). An undefined constant preserves the corrections. `border_need` checks registered sampler parameters before lazy Metal sampler creation; `pso_key.border` distinguishes draws that need corrections. Binding a sampler, or changing a handle a uniform buffer holds, does not mark the pipeline dirty, so each draw compares `prog_border` with its last pipeline's and fetches again when it changes (found by the iPhone port, PR #1073); with no twinned sampler registered that is one atomic load a stage. Vertex, fragment, task and mesh stages, including the vertex-pulling replacement, and compute dispatches (`cps_new_b`) specialize the constant to true only when none of their corrected samples needs a twin. Metal hands out one `MTLFunction` per function content, device-wide (`newFunctionWithName:` on the same library, or on another library loaded from the same data, returns the same object on macOS 27.2), and the host keys a function's Metal 4 descriptor by that object (`fn4_note`); noting the new constants on the shared object would have taken the corrections from every draw of that shader. So the variant is not a function object: whoever builds a pipeline says which one it builds (the `off` argument of `pso_build`, `pso_build_ex`, `pso_desc4`, `cps_new_b` and the mesh pipelines, always `!pso_key.border`), a descriptor always holds the shader's own function, and the base key carries the variant (`pbase_key.off`). `fn_off` makes the variant's descriptor, copying the constants the function was noted with (alpha test, logic operation, merged outputs) and cached by that descriptor; `fn4_of_b` gives the Metal 4 descriptor of either variant, which the one Metal 4 compiler specializes with the pipeline (NX 18 has no MTLDevice path, so there is no device function to specialize). A function whose entry point does not declare the constant has no variant. Generated geometry mesh entries that declare constants are noted specialized with the empty set, as `shader_load` notes a shader (`geo_mesh_entry`), so they have a variant too. Pair prebuilds use the same border-off specialization (`pre_fn`), and so does the offline pipeline archive (`pso_offline.py` `HOST_CONSTANTS`: metal-tt specializes a `<key>_b` or `<key>_bp` function, and the host finds the base by its specialized descriptor), so the pairs stay in the app's archive; NX 18's offline task / mesh pipelines specialize it the same way where the row's key leaves the corrections off and the mesh shader's own object entry does not declare it. Older serialized rows read as border-off and live draws needing corrections have a different key. `DM_NVN_BORDER_KEEP=1` keeps the corrections in every pipeline (diagnostic; `ab` alternates it with `DM_AB`). A compute shader with constants is loaded like the other stages (`fn_spec`): `cps_new` looks its pipeline up by the specialized descriptor, in the bundle and the device's archives, compiles it through the Metal 4 compiler on a miss and captures it; compute pipelines are keyed by shader and variant (`cps_key`), and the prewarm builds the border-off one. Geometry kernels keep the corrections (`cps_new`). Sampler arrays, gathers and comparisons retain the existing limitations described above.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#unused-border-corrections): its 1080p material passes spilled 300-400 bytes a thread; in-process A/B on a MacBook Air M4, 1.8 ms of a 26.5 ms frame (1.5 ms in the render stages, the rest in its kernels, NDSR's network among them). [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md) and [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) on an M1, measured with the corrections taken out of the source at build time (PR #1203), not yet with the constant: Splatoon's `c0ae76e0ca76b574` spilled 592 bytes a thread and none without them, a 236-draw 1920x1080 pass 20-21 → 9.3 ms and the frame 31.6 → 19.8 ms; Bananza's `6f9fd9cb5fbc873b` 336 → 160 bytes, its heavy area's scene pass 5.1 → 3.2 ms.
- **Check:** `t_pso_skey` covers old row lengths; `t_border --cpu` covers first use before sampler creation, per-stage bindings and sampler re-registration. The GPU cases in `t_border` check preservation of existing constants, the variant's own descriptor, the original function, a generated mesh entry and the prewarm specialization, each on a kernel of its own content compiled through the Metal 4 compiler, alongside the existing filtered-border samples. The rewritten kernel of `test_dmsh_border.py` runs on the GPU through `cps_new`, with the corrections and without them (`cps_new_b`). `test_pso_offline.py` checks the constant names and the specialized bases, compute and mesh pipelines.

### Texture fields Metal refuses

- **Symptom:** the process ends at once (the window vanishes), with `-[MTLTextureDescriptorInternal validateWithDevice:]` failing an assertion such as `requests 9 mipmap levels, but the dimensions (1, 1, 1) can only support 1`, or `sampleCount must be > 1 for multisample textures`.
- **Cause:** NVN takes texture fields Metal refuses, and Metal aborts instead of returning nil: more mip levels than the size holds (on NVN the extra levels are 1×1), an array or 3D texture of no layers, more than 2048 layers (a 1D array's layer count is its height, which the size check let through), and sample counts the GPU lacks (an M4 Pro has 1, 2 and 4; NVN also has 8 and 16). A blit at a level the Metal texture lacks is not checked either.
- **Fix:** `tex_desc` makes such textures with the levels their size holds and one layer for none. Textures of more than 2048 layers, and multisample textures of one sample, are not made, as textures past Metal's size limits are. A multisample texture of a sample count the GPU lacks is made with the largest count the GPU has below it (ported from S2Arm a6f28581, which met a game's 8-sample targets on an A15 iPhone): before, it was not made, and the target and every pass drawing into it were lost. Passes, pipelines and resolves take the count from the Metal texture (`g_pass.samples`), so they follow; a shader that reads a sample at or past the new count (sample 4 to 7 of an 8-sample target made with 4) reads undefined texels, and the red line (`made with 4`, first 4 cases) says the count was lowered. A red line names each case once. Copies and `ReadTexels` at a level the host texture lacks are skipped (`tex_level_ok`). Retail data is within every bound except, possibly, 8- or 16-sample targets.
- **Found on:** reported by MacNativeGames (`f864eb0`): a mod's 1×1 texture in Tears of the Kingdom declaring 9 levels. No game here is known to reach it; the sample counts (an M4 Pro has 1, 2 and 4, and a multisample texture of one sample aborts) were checked on that Mac while porting the fix.
- **Check:** `t_view_levels` makes each case with Metal's validation layer on: a 1×1 texture of 9 levels, a 3D texture whose depth holds the levels, an array of no layers, a 1D array of 4,096 layers, an 8-sample and a 1-sample multisample texture, and a copy at a missing level. `t_ms_fit` makes 2-, 4-, 8- and 16-sample 2D and array targets with the validation layer on and checks each count against the largest the device answers it has (an M4 Max has 2 and 4, not 8 or 16), and clears each in a render pass. Not run on an iPhone: an A15 or older iPhone and the iPhone 18 Pro Max answer for themselves at run time; no game here is known to ask for 8 or 16 samples.

### Unreal's texture streaming pool

- **Symptom:** in an Unreal Engine game, textures and lighting turn blurry and muddy about a second after an area loads and stay that way, whatever the frame rate, render scale or upscaling; no red line.
- **Cause:** not the host: the game's own budget. Unreal's texture streamer keeps at most `r.Streaming.PoolSize` megabytes of streamed mips resident and drops the top mips of what is in view once it is past them; lightmaps stream too, so the lighting goes flat with the textures. The game's Switch device profile sets the pool (DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined: 200, and 1000 on Windows), so the Switch looks the same. The host's texture sizes, which the streamer counts, follow the NVN driver's layout (`nvn_tex_layout`).
- **Fix (an improvement over the Switch):** the profile key `ue_texture_pool <MB>` (`DM_UE_TEXTURE_POOL` overrides) raises the variable to that size whenever the game sets a smaller one; a larger value, or −1 (no pool size), stays (`hle_uecvar.c`, on every present). Unreal's streaming manager reads the variable again when it changes (DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined `main+0x342230`: game thread copy, MB → bytes, into the pool size that `main+0x4d98b00` reports to the streamer), so a write at run time takes effect. The variable is found by its name, with no address per build: the rewriter names it in main's fixups ([Unreal's frame limit and present interval, found by name](frame-rate.md#unreals-frame-limit-and-present-interval-found-by-name)); until 2026-10-30 the host searched for it on the first present (`dm_ue_cvar`, 35 ms). It applies on the Mac; on iOS only `DM_UE_TEXTURE_POOL` (`Documents/env.txt`) turns it on, until an iPhone run shows the memory fits.
- **Found on:** [DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined](../../dm/games/0100A9D01C446000/README.md) (GitHub issue #589). The rewriter finds the pool in every Unreal main on the drive: OCTOPATH TRAVELER and Yoshi's Crafted World (UE 4.1x, UTF-32), Pikmin 4 (4.26), OCTOPATH TRAVELER II and DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined (4.27), Octopath Traveler 0 (UE 5) and FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE (5.6).
- **Check:** `t_uecvar` (the variable named in main's table, or not; the value raised, kept and left); the search itself, `test_ue_cvar.py`. On a Mac run, `DM_CTL` `cvari <main offset> <MB>` sets the pool while the game runs, for an A/B at one spot.

### Models black for one frame when a streaming step replaces their textures

- **Symptom:** large static and mesh-shader models (Ocarina of Time's castle town wall, rocks, trees, Lon Lon Ranch's barn) draw black for exactly one frame, with the fog in front and the terrain untouched, in pairs four or five frames apart; a few times a minute in play, at fixed places of a route.
- **Cause:** the models' shaders read their textures through the bindless pool table (`pool_gpu`). When a walk writes an ID's entry (`pool_entry`) it makes the ID's Metal texture if it fits the walk's room (`pool_room`, within `pool_budget()`), or, whatever the room, if a shader has read the ID (`pool_seen`, under `POOL_REQ_BYTES`); otherwise the entry is the dummy (black), and `pool_seen_scan` makes the texture after the first read. In Ocarina of Time the room is always 0: `pool_budget()` is a twentieth of Metal's working set (909 MB on a 24 GB Mac) against 15-18 GB of `currentAllocatedSize`, so only read IDs are made ahead. `mtl_reg_tex` cleared the mark whenever the game registered another texture into the ID, so when the game's streaming replaced the 17 textures of a material set at once (3-7 MB each, finalizing the old texture first; each step registered twice, 4-5 frames apart), the replacements counted as unread and their first reads found the dummy, one frame per registration.
- **Fix:** the read mark is the ID's, not its texture's: it stays while the ID holds a texture and is cleared only when the ID is emptied, so the next walk makes a replacement under the read budget. A render target no pass has written stays the dummy all the same (`pool_unwritten_target`), which is what kept transient targets from being made ahead. A texture streamed into an ID no shader has read yet is not covered by this: its first read finds the ID's stand-in ([next entry](#a-streamed-textures-first-read-drew-its-surface-dark-for-one-frame)).
- **Check:** a replay of a route past the castle town wall, counting single-frame dips of a per-frame luminance grid, each checked by picture: 4-5 flagged per run before (2-4 at the wall's streaming steps), 0 after (PR #2022); `t_pool_chunks` checks which registrations keep the mark, `t_pool_seen_replace` the replacement's pixels in the first table with the walk's room at 0 (a live replacement, one after Finalize, one for an ID whose texture waited for the read budget).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (a player's recording, M4 Pro, macOS 27.0.1).

## Render targets, passes and clears

### A streamed texture's first read drew its surface dark for one frame

- **Symptom:** a patch of scenery (Ocarina of Time's cliff beside the castle town wall) draws darker for exactly one frame, once, as the game gives it a sharper texture; a few times a minute while walking through Hyrule Field. Single frames, not the pairs of the entry above; no red line.
- **Cause:** the surface's shader reads its textures through the bindless pool table (`pool_gpu`). Once `pool_budget()` is spent a walk makes only the textures a shader has read (`pool_seen`), so an ID no shader has read holds the dummy, which reads 0; the first read sets the ID's bit, and `pool_seen_scan` makes the texture for the next frame. Ocarina of Time keeps a texture set of its own for each piece of distant scenery and resolution (one level each: a BC5 texture, and two three-layer arrays, BC6H and BC7, of half its size), registers a new set 12 to 969 frames before the material names it, and so the first frame of each such step read the dummy. Which registrations a shader will read is not known ahead: in 58 s of play the game registered 14,455 textures that stayed unread, 9.8 GB, against 10 first reads, and making textures ahead by age and size cost 0.7-1.9 GB for one hit in three (issue #1969).
- **Fix:** a texture the walk leaves out for good holds a stand-in in the tables until its first read (`pool_stand`): the texture's own smallest levels, from the first of at most 1 KB on, or, when it has no level that small, its last level cut down to at most 4 KB a layer, every n-th block of every n-th block row (`nvn_deswizzle_pick`; the blocks stay as they are, compressed or not). The host reads them from the storage on the CPU into a small shared texture cut from a heap that is resident from the start, so making one takes no upload batch and no residency commit, and the first read samples the texture's own colours, coarse, for the one frame before the texture is there. 2D textures and 2D arrays of up to 8 layers get one, with the format and channel swizzle of their view (`treg_swizzle`); other types, textures in sparse pools, depth formats and render targets no pass has written hold the dummy as before. At most 512 are made a frame (an area load registers thousands at once); the rest wait and are written into the tables in place (`pool_stand_scan`). A table walked while the budget had room is walked once more when the budget has filled. `DM_NVN_POOL_STANDIN=0` keeps the dummy.
- **Check:** `t_pool_standin` (which registrations get a stand-in and what it holds, read back from the GPU; the first read; a frame's share), `t_tex` P (the picked blocks against the level decoded whole, in both GOB orders). `DM_NVN_POOL_FIRST=1` logs each first read of a texture not made yet and what it found; the totals go to the log every 300 frames (`pool stand-ins up to frame`). Replays of a player's route past the cliff, 10 with `DM_NVN_POOL_STANDIN=0` and 8 without: after the area load, 31 first reads of a texture not made found the dummy in the 10 runs and none in the 8, where 49 found a stand-in (none of them made from storage the game wrote afterwards). On a 32x18 luminance grid of every present, the cliff's first read is a connected group of 9-12 cells at 0.70-0.79 of the presents before and after in each of the 4 baseline runs in which its set streamed, and at most 2 cells, the grid's noise, in the 5 runs with stand-ins; by picture the cliff is dark for one frame in the one and a little soft in the other. At the area load 39 first reads a run found the dummy, and 5-11 with stand-ins (the frame's share). A run makes about 57,000 stand-ins in 355 ms (6 µs each on an M4 Pro) and holds about 6,000 at a time, in 16 MB of heaps. Resident memory, GPU allocated, the residency set, frame times and submit+present stay within the run-to-run range (medians: resident 10,830 and 10,883 MB; frame p95 16.85 and 17.19 ms, with runs of both kinds between 16.8 and 19.4).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (a player's recording, M4 Pro, macOS 27.0.1).

### Transient render targets shared by storage

- **Symptom:** ≈13 MB/frame leak.
- **Cause:** the game builds new texture objects every frame over the same storage and never finalizes them; each new object allocated a new private Metal texture.
- **Fix:** host textures are shared by `(pool, offset, description)`.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md).

### IMAGE usage does not split texture storage

- **Symptom:** a model has correct geometry, base colour and normals but appears as a black silhouette; deferred lighting's material-ID depth texture stays zero.
- **Cause:** render and stencil-sampling descriptors name the same pool, offset and layout, differing only by `NVN_TEXTURE_FLAGS_IMAGE`. Including that usage flag in the storage key made two independent Metal textures; sampling read the empty one's stencil.
- **Fix:** the storage key excludes `IMAGE`. Sampling shares the original texture. A writable image binding prepares any needed `ShaderWrite` usage before opening its encoder; the upgrade copies the pixels and replaces every name of the old texture. Upgrading while encoding arguments would end the active encoder underneath its caller.
- **Check:** `t_stencil_view` writes both planes, reads through an IMAGE stencil descriptor and copies to separate storage under Metal validation; `t_upgrade_alias` checks image-write preparation and every live/finalized alias.
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 3](../../dm/games/010074F013262000/README.md).

### Sample count 0 and 1 do not split texture storage

- **Symptom:** a 3D scene renders black on iOS while the HUD draws: small tables a compute pass writes (exposure, volumes, sky lookups) read back as zero where the render pass samples them.
- **Cause:** the game builds the object its compute pass writes as an image and the object its render pass samples over the same storage, differing only in sample count: 0 in one, 1 in the other. Both mean single-sampled with the same layout, but the storage key compared the count raw, so they were two Metal textures and the reads saw the one nothing wrote.
- **Fix:** `tex_alias_key` in `nvn_mtl.m` takes 1 for a sample count of 0, so both objects resolve to one texture (and one packaged texture through `g_pkg_share`).
- **Found on:** ported from S2Arm 5863a69c (Apache-2.0), cause found by an outside contributor on 01001E9023920000; no game here was run with it.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_alias_takeover` (an IMAGE object with 0 and a sampled one with 1 over one storage share one texture; another format stays its own; fails without the fix).

### Copies into an uncompressed alias of a compressed texture

- **Symptom:** a surface that Unreal draws from a runtime virtual texture (a landscape floor) stays black; its BC tile caches read all zero in a texture dump, and nothing writes them on the GPU or the CPU; no red line.
- **Cause:** the game compresses its virtual texture tiles in a compute shader into 16-byte RGBA32UI texels, one per BC3 block, and copies them with a texture copy into an RGBA32UI texture it made over the same storage as its BC3 tile cache, one texel per compression block (the cache's size in blocks). On the Switch the copy writes the cache's bytes. Here every NVN texture object has a Metal texture of its own, so the bytes went to the alias and the compressed texture never saw them; the game also makes the alias again for each batch.
- **Fix:** `bc_alias_copy` in `nvn_mtl.m`, called from `copy_t2t`: a plain copy (no scaling or mirroring) into an uncompressed 2D texture of 8 or 16 bytes per texel whose storage is that of a registered, live block-compressed 2D texture of the same block size, and whose size is that texture's size in 4x4 blocks, goes into the compressed texture at four times the block position. It passes through a private staging buffer, kept resident and reused from copy to copy (a blit does not copy between an uncompressed and a compressed format, while buffer copies to and from both are allowed). The check runs before `copy_t2t` makes the destination's Metal texture, so the alias, which the copy no longer writes, gets none of its own (31 MB for each of the game's caches). The copies use the forms without `options:`, which deferred encoding (`DM_NVN_ENC_THREAD`, on by default on iOS) records; the `options:` forms stopped the host with `FATAL deferred encoding: DmRecEnc has no stand-in`. `DM_NVN_BC_ALIAS=0` turns it off, for an A/B test.
- **Found on:** Monster Jam Steel Titans 2 (`010051B0131F0000`, update 1.0.2): the landscape's runtime virtual texture renders 264x264 tiles, compresses them to 66x66 RGBA32UI blocks and copies them into a 1386x1386 RGBA32UI texture at the storage of each of its two 5544x5544 BC3 tile caches; the arena floor was black. With the fix, on the Mac the caches went from all zero to about 530,000 of their 1.92 million blocks with colour and the floor shows its dirt, tracks and ramps, with no new red line; on an iPhone (deferred encoding on) the copy runs at the arena with no stop, crash or GPU stall, at 30 fps.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/t_bc_alias.m` under Metal validation: a copy into an RGBA32UI alias over a BC3 texture's storage lands in the BC3 texture's blocks and is counted, and a copy into a texture over other storage leaves the BC3 texture as it was; the alias gets no Metal texture of its own, and a second copy into the alias, through the reused staging buffer, lands too; with `DM_NVN_BC_ALIAS=0` six of its checks fail.
- **Open:** only copies into such an alias are sent to the compressed texture: a draw into it (an uncompressed texture object, not a view: views go through [the shadow](#compressed-textures-as-render-targets-through-an-uncompressed-shadow)) or an image store into it would still write the alias alone, and nothing reads the copy's bytes back into guest storage when the compressed texture is uploaded from a CPU-visible pool (`tex_storage_dst`; the plain copy path does). No game has shown either.

### Shared-storage entries taken over by a new texture

- **Symptom:** GPU memory grows with every image shown; the memory line's `shared textures` count keeps rising.
- **Cause:** the game decodes each picture it shows into one scratch region of a pool that lives for good, finalizes that texture when it is done with it and builds the next one in the same region with another size or format; during some transitions it builds a new render target every frame, one pixel wider each time (614x720, 615x720, …). The storage-sharing table (`g_alias`, keyed by pool, offset and description; [Transient render targets shared by storage](#transient-render-targets-shared-by-storage)) kept every one of them until the pool was finalized.
- **Fix:** a new shared texture takes over the entries of its pool whose storage it overlaps under another description (`alias_take_over` in `nvn_mtl.m`), unless a live texture object still resolves to one (Wonder's transient render targets, rebuilt on new objects every frame and never finalized) or a lookup found it in the last 16 submissions (Breath of the Wild finalizes its transient render targets before every submit and builds them again the next frame; targets sharing storage would otherwise evict each other every frame). An entry that only a finalized serial in `g_ghost` still holds loses the table's reference and is retired with that serial. The memory line reports `shared textures N (M kept by the sharing table alone, X MB, Y MB of it drawn into or shader-written, the oldest idle Z submissions; evicted with pools …, on storage takeover …)`: the ones the table alone keeps (no live or finalized serial, view or pin holds them; `alias_unheld_locked`, upstream NXNative fb4da077a) wait for their storage to be reused or their pool to go, and the line gives their size (each texture once) and the longest any has gone without a lookup. Whether they are part of [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md)'s climb from about 11 to 15 GB resident over a long session on an M3 Pro (upstream issue #2629) is open.
- **Found on:** [STEINS;GATE RE:BOOT](../../dm/games/01008BC024FDE000/README.md) (ASTC 4x4 images up to 4096 wide, RGBA8 render targets, R8 masks): from a quick save, 297 entries and 1.29 GB of GPU memory at frame 6,600; 468 entries and 1.58 GB 24,000 frames into a new game. With the fix: 97 entries and 0.93 GB at frame 6,600; 15 minutes of story from the quick save (53,700 frames) stayed at 1.08–1.15 GB of GPU memory and 1.02–1.13 GB resident, and a new game from boot stays at 0.97–1.03 GB from its first story scene to frame 27,300 (with the 16-submission guard).
- **Check:** Captain Toad: Treasure Tracker's opening course, rendered with agl's transient targets, keeps its 26 entries and takes none over, with the same memory and frame times as before.

### A storage takeover walked the serial table for each entry

- **Symptom:** while the game makes many textures (Ocarina of Time's cutscenes), frames of hundreds of milliseconds up to a second, all of it in `submit+present` (the `[longframe]` lines); `sample` of the process names `alias_take_over` the submit thread's hottest function.
- **Cause:** for each entry a takeover could remove, `alias_take_over` asked whether a live texture object still resolves to it through `serial_holds(&g_by_serial, …)`, a walk over the serial table's whole capacity. With about 7,500 serials and thousands of shared textures, every new shared texture walked the table once per entry of its pool that overlapped its storage, and the texture pool's walks (`pool_gpu`, which makes a texture for every registered ID of the table's type) make hundreds of textures in one submission.
- **Fix:** general, in `nvn_mtl.m`: a count per object of the serial-table entries holding it (`g_by_serial_held`), kept by every writer of the serial table (`serial_put`, `serial_del`, `tex_put` and the upgrade's in-place replacement); `alias_take_over` asks it in one lookup. The count answers only what it counts: a change that lets a live object hold a shared texture some other way (a view's parent, a packaged texture's members, as PR #1404 proposes) must count those holds too, or a takeover retires a texture still read.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/t_alias_takeover.m`: an entry a live object resolves to stays and one nothing resolves to goes after `ALIAS_IDLE` submissions, two serials naming one object keep it held until both go, and a takeover past 1,000 held entries with 20,000 serials in the table takes 0.025 ms instead of 4.3 ms (M5 Max). In the game, a host that asked both the count and the walk (Ocarina of Time, loading a Kokiri Forest save, Saria's dialogue and a walk through the village, M4 Pro) compared them 393,216 times and they never differed, so the takeover makes the same choices as before; on the same route the `[longframe]` lines went from 332 to 212.
- **Found on:** The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time (Switch 2, `0400486024DEC000`, update v131072, with PR #1299), Saria's cutscene in Kokiri Forest, M5 Max. In 60 s `sample` captures of the submit thread over the cutscene, `alias_take_over` took 2,277 of the 3,711 samples spent making textures (`tex_mtl_`), 61 %; with the fix 38 of 618, 6 %. Game runs on the wall clock (the game does not run under `DM_DETCLOCK`) put the cutscene's heaviest part in or out of the run, so frame counts moved within their noise.
- **Open:** the same captures and the memory line show the textures themselves being made again and again: 84,000 to 103,000 storage takeovers in about 200 s of play with 1,400 to 1,900 shared textures alive.

### A storage takeover walked the sharing table for each texture made

- **Symptom:** a scene that makes tens of thousands of textures never finishes loading: no frame is presented, the submit thread stays at 100 % of a core, and `sample` names `alias_take_over` (under `tex_mtl_` ← `tex_by_id` ← `encode_args`).
- **Cause:** `alias_take_over` looked for the entries a new shared texture's storage overlaps by walking the sharing table's whole capacity (`g_alias`), with a layout and a `g_alias_seen` lookup for each entry of the same pool. The cost of each texture made grew with the textures already shared, so a load that shares about 90,000 textures walked 131,072 slots for each of them.
- **Fix:** general, in `nvn_mtl.m`: an index of the shared textures by where their storage lies (`alias_index`, `g_alias_cells`): {pool, 64 KB cell of the pool} → the entries whose bytes reach into that cell, each with its end. `alias_take_over` reads the cells of the new texture only. The image families' owner lookup (`image_texture`: the entries of the same pool, offset and format), which walked the table for each image made in a GPU-only pool, reads the one cell of its offset. The entries it chooses from are the same (same pool, another description, overlapping bytes, not looked up lately, held by no serial); when more than 16 qualify, which 16 go first follows the cells instead of the table's order, and the rest go at later takeovers as before. The table's keys have two writers, `alias_put` and `alias_del`, which keep the index, `g_alias_set` and `g_alias_seen` together; code that writes `g_alias` any other way hides its entries from the takeover. Walks of the whole table remain where they run once per event, not per texture: a pool's release, a texture's upgrade (which walks the serial tables as well), the memory report and the census. An entry costs one lookup per 64 KB of its storage when it is put or removed (16 for a 1 MB texture).
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/t_alias_takeover.m`: an entry that starts in an earlier cell is found, one in the same cell that ends before the new texture stays, one spanning several of the new texture's cells goes once, another pool's entries are not met, a removal and a pool drop leave no cell behind, and a takeover with 100,000 entries in the table takes 0.0003 ms instead of 0.65 ms (M6). `t_image_alias.m`: two families whose offsets lie in one cell keep a heap each.
- **Found on:** Mario Golf: Super Rush (`0100C9C00E25C000`, 4.0.0, issue #814; no profile in the repository), All-Star Summit, MacBook Pro M1 Pro, the PR author's runs: the course registers 134,400 textures and shares about 90,000 as it loads; on main the load showed no frame in 5 minutes (88,513 entries after 12), with the index the first frame comes about 3 s after the start (PR #2229).
- **Open:** Whether 90,000 live Metal textures for one course is what the game needs, and what it costs in memory on an iPhone, is not examined.

### A texture ID with no texture kept its cached view

- **Symptom:** a command buffer fails with a GPU page fault on the same frames in every run, in a compute or draw command buffer whose shaders sample through the texture pool. Ocarina of Time with `DM_NVN_RT_RECLAIM=1` (PR #1588): f3,677 and f4,851, compute shader 9e6b00b050e30801, each run.
- **Cause:** `tex_by_id` (`nvn_mtl.m`) keeps an ID's cached view when the slow path finds the same texture behind it: `cur == v || v.parentTexture == cur`. When the ID has no texture now, `treg_tex` returns nil as `cur`. That happens for a texture that went and was not made again, a texture that could not be made, or a reclaimed render target. A cached view that is the base texture itself has no parent either, so nil == nil kept it and marked it valid again. The pool tables' fast path (`tex_fast_rid`) then wrote its resource ID after the host had released the texture and dropped its residency. With the reclaim it happened about 60 times in a 5,000-frame run.
- **Fix:** general, in `tex_by_id`: the cached view is kept only when `cur` is non-nil. An ID with no texture drops its view, and its slot takes the typed zero texture an empty slot of that type gets.
- **Check:** Ocarina of Time on an M2 Pro 16 GB with `DM_NVN_RT_RECLAIM=1`, to f5,241 and f7,750. The f3,677 and f4,851 faults are gone, with no trimmed texture handed back (checked by marking every texture the reclaim's trim released). Pace is the same (7,750 frames in 202.5 s against 7,731 in 202.75 s), and reclaim counts are alike. A fault at f786 has another cause and is there without the reclaim too. The picture there was not compared: a dead ID now reads the typed zero texture instead of the released one.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (Switch 2, `0400486024DEC000`, v131072).

### Live resolution variants share physical image storage

- **Symptom:** GPU memory grows as resolution changes or rooms load, although almost every retained texture still has a live NVN descriptor; reclaiming unused sharing-table entries cannot recover it.
- **Cause:** an NVN texture object describes storage in a pool. Several live descriptions at the same pool offset can describe the same physical memory at different resolutions, but `(pool, offset, description)` sharing allocated a separate private Metal texture for every size. The live-object guard in `alias_take_over` correctly kept those descriptors, and consequently kept all their independent allocations.
- **Fix:** `image_texture` in `nvn_mtl.m` gives a declared resolution family an exact-sized placement heap. Eligibility is GPU-only, mapped, non-sparse storage, `COMPRESSIBLE` without `DISPLAY`, `VIDEO_DECODE`, `LINEAR`, `SPARSE` or `LINEAR_RENDER_TARGET` (with or without `IMAGE`: a depth target carries only `COMPRESSIBLE`), single-level, single-sample 2D images. Sharing stays within one NVN pool, offset and format. Live descriptions are recorded at initialization so the first used resolution can allocate the largest declared requirement; a single-description address retains its ordinary allocation. No power-of-two capacity is imposed. `image_texture` adds the format-view usage only to 32-bit integer families ([below](#a-32-bit-integer-family-member-sampled-as-float-left-its-familys-heap)); depth-stencil textures already have it from `tex_desc`. Pass-as-compute's shader-write usage is the family's: every member gets it when the largest size is within `DM_NVN_PCM_MAXPX`, none when it is over (`fam_usage`; [one-draw passes as compute dispatches](#one-draw-passes-as-compute-dispatches)), as a heap is shared only among members of one usage. A fresh heap is zeroed through its largest description; creating a later alias never queues a zero fill over another alias's content. Metal 4 residency names the heap, and encoder boundaries include resource-alias visibility while these heaps exist. Each texture holds its heap owner; normal sharing-table eviction and deferred texture release retire the allocation after its last texture/view goes, without a pool-lifetime heap cache.
- **Limits:** this handles initialized resolution families, not arbitrary overlapping offsets, cross-format inheritance, CPU-visible or sparse storage. If a larger description is declared later and cannot fit an existing heap, it gets a new heap from the recycled heaps ([the entry below](#render-targets-that-move-with-the-dynamic-resolution-get-new-metal-memory-each-time)); an old heap follows its textures' lifetimes and then waits there. Inherited texels are not translated between incompatible texture layouts; compressed families clear cross-size loads to 0 and mask cross-size reads ([the entry below](#a-resolution-family-member-reads-another-sizes-compressed-blocks-as-garbage)). No game-specific profile switch is used.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=0100DCA0064A6000 check_t_image_alias` runs under Metal validation with direct and deferred encoding: 128 live descriptions retain their individual dimensions while sharing one allocation; small-first fresh storage, literal pixels across resolution changes, descriptor creation after a writer, indirect Metal 4 argument-buffer access, native integer-image atomics, depth/stencil clears and stencil views, pool/offset/format separation, excluded CPU-visible/sparse/linear images, a depth family with `COMPRESSIBLE` alone, 32-bit integer families sampled through float views (a third run with `DM_NVN_FAMILY_PFV=0`), the family's pass-as-compute usage, cross-size safety (a fourth run with `DM_NVN_FAMILY_NOCOMPRESS=1`) and repeated destruction are checked. Integer-image matching restores the `ShaderAtomic` usage that Metal omits from its queried usage flags.
- **Found on:** [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md#texture-memory-2026-10-27), investigated from [#257](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/257) and [#100](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/100). The game's note records the live-allocation census and Mac comparison; other games and long iOS sessions remain unvalidated. [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): its dynamic-resolution depth buffer, Depth32Float with `COMPRESSIBLE` alone in a virtual pool, had 15–16 live sizes of 1,882–1,920 pixels at one offset, 14.5 MB each, outside the family until the flags rule took any compressible image; in Kokiri Forest on an M3 Pro (18 GB, measured before main's Ocarina fixes) Depth32Float textures went from 95 (458 MB) to 60 (191 MB) plus 21 family heaps (82 MB), and GPU allocation from 7,710 to 7,520 MB.

### A resolution family member reads another size's compressed blocks as garbage

- **Symptom:** after the game renders at another resolution for a while (a pause menu, a resolution switch) and comes back, a render target it loads instead of clearing holds non-finite or random pixels, in a screen-shaped region that opaque draws wipe away and transparent ones keep; bloom spreads them into a strobing glow. A non-finite scan (`DM_NVN_MAGSCAN`) counts about a million of them in the first pass back.
- **Cause:** [a resolution family](#live-resolution-variants-share-physical-image-storage) places every size in one Metal heap. Metal compresses a private texture losslessly by default (`allowGPUOptimizedContents`), and each texture reads the shared bytes through its own compression layout. When one size reads what another size wrote, it decodes the other's compressed blocks as garbage. On the console the same bytes are texels of the same format in other places, which stay finite.
- **Fix:** general (`host`-bound), `NvImageHeap.valid_wh` in `nvn_mtl.m`, ported from upstream NXNative 4015ce2c0 (MIT): `image_texture` keeps lossless compression on by default and each family heap tracks the geometry whose compressed blocks it holds now (`valid_wh`, `fam_set_valid`; NX 18's families are single-sample, so upstream's 4-sample storage-pair geometry has no case here). When another size attaches with `MTLLoadActionLoad` (`rp_begin_ex_`), the load is rewritten to `MTLLoadActionClear` (0) before the render-area shrink and the standalone oversized clears, and a pass drawn every other frame does not turn it back into a load; a read-only pass can still drop its store without disturbing the live size, and a combined depth-stencil half-clear stores both planes when either stores (`sd_finish`). A stored attachment makes its size the live one. Partial cross-size writes (`copy_b2t`, `copy_t2t`, `clear_tex`, `clear_rect_zero`, `image_prepare_`, `dlss_eval` and its bilinear stand-in) clear the member to 0 first; whole overwrites take the heap over directly. Cross-size reads (`copy_t2b`, `copy_t2t`, `mtl_read_texels`, `draw_tex`, `fb_prepare`, `tex_upgrade`, direct bindings in `tex_resolve` and `encode_args_full`, `htab_gpu`, and the pool tables' `pool_entry` and `pool_seen_scan`, whose entries are taken again through `ser_note` on a size switch) give zeros without decoding another size's compressed blocks or disturbing the live size; pass-as-compute leaves such a pass to its render pass (`pcm_run`, declined at 16). Storage with one description keeps its ordinary allocation. `DM_NVN_FAMILY_NOCOMPRESS=1` restores the uncompressed path as before the port (raw reads see misplaced texels of the same format, as on the console; no tracking), also from a player build's `env.txt` for the phone A/B. A family keeps its compression only without the shader-write usage, which takes it away (the family's pass-as-compute usage, [above](#live-resolution-variants-share-physical-image-storage)); 32-bit integer families keep the format-view usage, which costs theirs ([below](#a-32-bit-integer-family-member-sampled-as-float-left-its-familys-heap)).
- **Cost:** upstream measured keeping resolution families compressed against uncompressed on Ocarina of Time: about 0.85 ms a frame of GPU time on an M1 (PR #2573), 0.43–1.02 ms and 3.6–3.9 mJ a frame on a base M2 Air, about 1.4 ms on a base M4 Mac mini, and about 0.3 ms on an M4 Pro (within the runs' spread; NX 18's earlier `kokiri-deck` A/B there: 22.07 ms compressed, 4 runs, against 22.38 ms uncompressed, 5 runs). Not yet measured on an iPhone: `DM_NVN_FAMILY_NOCOMPRESS=1` is the A/B's other side.
- **Check:** `check_t_image_alias` (above) checks the compressed default (a cross-size `OP_COPY_T2B` gives zeros and leaves the live size's pixels intact; a pool table masks the other size and takes it after the size switch through `ser_note`; a cross-size render-pass load with a 16x16 scissored clear leaves all other 1,439,744 pixels exactly 0, no garbage; a combined `Depth32Float_Stencil8` half-clear across sizes stores a clean 0 stencil and the cleared depth; a family over `DM_NVN_PCM_MAXPX` has no shader-write usage) and, in its `DM_NVN_FAMILY_NOCOMPRESS=1` run, the old path (a 1440x810 clear read back as 1600x900 holds only the clear colour or zero). Before the uncompressed fix, a compressed family read 6,144 of 1,440,000 other texels on an M4 Pro.
- **Found on:** [ASTRAL CHAIN](../../dm/games/01007300020FA000/README.md) (1.0.1): the 1600x900 HDR targets (RG11B10Float) share their storage with the pause menu's 1440x810 targets; the first pass back from the menu loaded 1,115,523 of 1,440,000 non-finite pixels, which the game never clears (issue [#1935](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1935), PR [#1936](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1936)). [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (v131072), upstream's 6,600-frame diagnostic in Kakariko Village: 11,315 cross-size pass loads on the RG11B10Float bloom chain (960x540 → 480x270, 30x16 → 240x135 → … → 30x16), 0 of 322,650 cross-size direct shader bindings, and 0 pool table reads of family textures (PR [#2573](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2573)).

### A 32-bit integer family member sampled as float left its family's heap

- **Symptom:** the log repeats `texture upgraded to allow format views ser=… WxH base format 53 → view format …` (or base format 103) dozens to hundreds of times a run, each on a texture of a [resolution family](#live-resolution-variants-share-physical-image-storage), and GPU memory grows by about the texture's size each time; the frame of each upgrade can take a GPU spike. Luigi's Mansion 3 on an iPhone 18 Pro Max: 80 to 170 such lines a run, about 8 MB each. Builds before the fix printed no size (`ser=… base format 53 → view format …`) and gave the view format as 53 whatever the slot wanted; the line now ends `…, out of its resolution family's shared heap` for a family member.
- **Cause:** the game binds R32Uint (and two RG32Uint) dynamic-resolution images in shader slots declared float. `tex_float_fit` (`nvn_mtl.m`) gives such a slot a float view of the same size, which Metal makes only of a texture with `MTLTextureUsagePixelFormatView`. Textures get that usage only when needed, as it costs lossless compression, so each size was upgraded the first time it was sampled (`img_fit_upgrade`, `tex_upgrade`): a copy of the whole texture into a new standalone allocation, outside the family's shared heap. Each upgraded size added its own allocation back, up to about 0.66 GB in one run, which is what physical image sharing exists to avoid; the old heap stayed for the other sizes. The log's view format was a placeholder that `img_fit_upgrade` passed in.
- **Fix:** general, `family_pfv` in `nvn_mtl.m`: `image_texture` makes a family member whose Metal format `tex_float_fit` reads through a float view (R32, RG32 and RGBA32 Uint / Sint, `tbuf_view_format`) with the format-view usage from the start. That is wider than the formats in Luigi's Mansion 3's logs (R32Uint and RG32Uint, base formats 53 and 103): R32Sint, RG32Sint and RGBA32Uint / Sint families get it too, at no cost for the same reason. When this was written family members were all uncompressed, so the usage cost no compression; since families keep compression again ([the entry above](#a-resolution-family-member-reads-another-sizes-compressed-blocks-as-garbage)) it costs these 32-bit integer members theirs (what they had before), still far less than the upgrade's copy out of the heap, so it stays on with compression too. The size gets any format view at once and stays in the heap. As the usage is there from creation, these members also skip the host's other fallbacks for a texture without it, not only `tex_float_fit` and `img_fit`: a render-target view of another format is attached in that format (`attach`, `rt_view_fmt`), where before it drew in the base format with `[RED] render target view format … cannot be attached` until something upgraded the texture, and a texture view of another format (`tex_by_id`) no longer runs a synchronous upgrade. That is the intended behaviour, but a pass's attachment formats, and so its pipelines and their archive and prewarm keys, can differ from a build without the fix. The heap is sized with the descriptors as they are made, usage included, and the largest description that zeroes it gets the same usage. Other formats, and storage with one description, are made as before. `DM_NVN_FAMILY_PFV=0` makes the family as before, so the upgrade comes back. The upgrade's log line now gives the size, the format the view was wanted in (55 for R32Float, 105 for RG32Float) and, when the texture was a family member, `out of its resolution family's shared heap`.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=0100DCA0064A6000 check_t_image_alias` (Metal validation, Mac only): an R32Uint and an RG32Uint family of two sizes share a heap sized with the format-view usage; the smaller size, sampled through `tex_float_fit`, gets its float view at once with no upgrade queued and stays in the heap, keeps every texel, and the float view hands over the stored bits (denormals, NaN patterns, negatives) unchanged. Its third run, with `DM_NVN_FAMILY_PFV=0`, checks the old path: the view waits for the upgrade, which is queued with the float format, moves the size out of the heap and keeps every texel. On the Mac (Xcode-beta) all three runs pass; a family heap there may be a recycled one up to half as large again as asked, so the size check allows that window. Not yet measured on a device. On a device, compare `DM_NVN_FAMILY_PFV=0` with the default: the number of `texture upgraded to allow format views` lines and GPU memory, and also the `[RED] render target view format … cannot be attached` lines and the pipelines compiled while playing (`scripts/devlog.py`, bases compiled while playing), as attachment formats can change.
- **Found on:** [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md#texture-memory-2026-10-27) (iPhone 18 Pro Max logs, 2026-11-11).

### Render targets that move with the dynamic resolution get new Metal memory each time

- **Symptom:** while a game's dynamic resolution moves, the residency set's `added` count in the `residency set up to frame` lines stays at hundreds of MB per 300 frames from the shared-texture site, and frames stutter. On a 16 GB Mac, wired memory and swap follow, with p95 frame times near 100 ms. Ocarina of Time after 04419bc2, which freed its dynamic resolution from its floor (GitHub issue #1831).
- **Cause:** the engine keeps its render targets in a GPU-only pool and gives them new offsets and sizes as its resolution changes. Each `(pool, offset, description)` is a Metal texture of its own (the sharing table), and the storage takeover (`alias_take_over`) lets as many go. On an M6, 3,468 textures were made in about 1,000 frames of Ocarina's village: 97% were in its 305 MB virtual pool, at 1,965 offsets and 289 sizes, and they added 450-575 MB to the residency set every 300 frames. The live total stayed bounded at about 2.1 GB: it is churn, not a leak. Metal does not lay a texture out as NVN does, so these targets cannot share the pool's memory as on the console. A placement heap that mirrors the pool (PR #1453) and a reclaim of targets dead by overwrite (PR #1588) both corrupted the picture: the game keeps live targets in the part of the full-size scene target that its current resolution leaves unused.
- **Fix:** general, `rh_heap`, `rh_place` and `rh_texture` in `nvn_mtl.m`. Such a target (a texture in a `CPU_NO_ACCESS` pool that the GPU can render or write to, so not of a block-compressed format, private storage, not display, video, linear or sparse) is made in a placement heap of its own, an eighth larger than the texture. When its texture is freed, which happens after the submissions that used it complete (`defer_drain`), the heap stays resident in a free list. The smallest waiting heap that fits, up to half as large again, takes the next texture. Metal's allocation sizes come in steps of about 10% near 2 MB (#1583's `rt_sizes`: 855 resolutions at 2048x1152 fall into 21), so the eighth of headroom lets a target that grows by one step fit the heap it freed the frame before. The same free list serves the heaps of resolution families (`image_texture`, the entry above: a new family heap is taken from it, and a family heap whose last texture goes waits in it) and same-pass feedback snapshots (`fb_snap`), which are made again at each new size of their attachment; a memory trim (`mtl_trim_at`) frees a snapshot's heap with it rather than handing it back to the list it has just emptied (`rh_tex_bytes`). The new texture is filled as a new allocation is (`tex_init_fill` or zeros), so nothing it reads depends on the reuse. Its memory was the old texture's only up to that texture's completed last use, so no alias flush is added. Heaps that wait 3 s, and the oldest past the cap waiting, leave the residency set when a submission begins (`rh_trim`). The cap is 256 MB on the Mac; on iOS it follows the headroom to the memory limit, 256 MB down to 32 MB ([Render-target heaps kept by a flat cap on iOS](memory.md#render-target-heaps-kept-by-a-flat-cap-on-ios); `DM_NVN_RT_RECYCLE_CAP`). The memory line counts the heaps made, reused, freed and waiting, and gives the cap. `DM_NVN_RT_RECYCLE=0` turns it off (each texture its own allocation, family heaps exact and freed with their last texture), and `ab` alternates it with the `DM_AB` phases. This does not lower the steady total of the live targets (`DM_NVN_RT_DEAD=1`, off by default, releases those another target drew over: [Render targets drawn over stay while their texture objects live](memory.md#render-targets-drawn-over-stay-while-their-texture-objects-live)), and it does not remove the rest of a resize's cost: each new size is still a new Metal texture, filled, and the takeover of the old one.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_rt_recycle`: placement with an eighth of headroom, the heap kept resident while it waits, reuse at a smaller size with zeroed contents and at a size one of Metal's steps larger (a texture without `COMPRESSIBLE`), a new heap for a size the waiting ones cannot hold, CPU-visible pools and block-compressed textures kept apart, the trim, a feedback snapshot in a recycled heap, and the switch off. `check_t_image_alias` checks that a family heap holds its largest size and waits, resident, after its last texture. It runs with direct and deferred encoding under Metal validation. In the game: Ocarina of Time on an M6 (16 GB), the village walk with dynamic resolution on and `DM_NVN_RT_RECYCLE=ab DM_AB=300`, one run. Over the phases while its resolution moved, the shared-texture site added 455, 457 and 521 MB per 300 frames off, and 317, 37 and 27 MB on. Frame p90 was 33.3 and 49.8 ms off, 16.8 and 17.6 ms on. Phases after the resolution settled measured the same either way. Screenshots of the village were clean, with no GPU errors or red lines. Measured with the shaders of chain f9f01e4f9994, which were on disk. Mario Kart World on an M6 (16 GB), its race with two outside GPU loads so that its dynamic resolution moved every frame, one run with the free list as it was before (exact heaps taken up to an eighth larger, only `COMPRESSIBLE` targets, family heaps and snapshots allocated apart) and as above alternating every 600 frames: per 300 frames, 970 residency commits (137 ms) and 1.8 GB added before, 272 commits (26 ms) and 0.34 GB after; the replay's CPU time a frame (encoding and commits) 6.94 ms before, 6.36 ms after. Frame time (p50 16.7 ms, p95 about 24 ms) and GPU time (13.2-13.4 ms) were the same: on the M6 the gain is CPU headroom. Measured on main a77c0ee8 with the shaders of chain cb2e91c138e3.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#memory) (Switch 2, `0400486024DEC000`, v131072), [#1831](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1831). [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md#frame-rate) (Switch 2, `0400C3F00006E000`): its dynamic resolution moves since it reads real GPU times ([#1823](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1823)).

### Render-target views that change the format

- **Symptom:** the whole picture came out darker and more saturated on the Mac and the iPhone: comparing with a Switch screenshot, every channel at every level equalled the Switch value decoded from sRGB once more (0.55 → 0.26, 0.75 → 0.52).
- **Cause:** Mario's last pass writes the `RGBA8` swapchain texture through an `RGBA8_SRGB` view (`TextureViewSetFormat` 0x38 on a 0x25 texture), so the GPU encodes the linear shader output to sRGB on write. The host used to attach the base texture, which stored the linear values unencoded.
- **Fix:** `attach` now attaches a Metal view of the view's format, so the pipelines get that format too; the view is kept until the submission completes. A format change that is not an sRGB / linear pair needs `MTLTextureUsagePixelFormatView` on the base texture; without it the host draws in the base format and reports `render target view format … cannot be attached`.
- **Present step:** the matching rule: the Switch scans out the stored bytes, so an sRGB-typed swapchain texture (`RGBA8_SRGB` / `BGRA8_SRGB`) is sampled through a non-sRGB view (`present_raw_src`); sampling it directly would decode it once more on screen only, which a `DM_NVN_SHOT` capture (raw bytes) does not show. Mario's swapchain is `RGBA8`, so this path is for other games (`t_present_crop` covers it).
- **Check:** `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` prints these views as `V <pass> c<i> view format …`; Mario uses only this one (1 of 80 passes in a course frame).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md).

### Textures of an sRGB / linear pair over the same storage

- **Symptom:** a movie (or another picture the game converts each frame) does not show: the screen keeps the last 3D frame while the decoder delivers every picture. A pass dump shows a pass writing one serial and the composite sampling another serial of the same size, the two Metal formats `RGBA8Unorm` (70) and `RGBA8Unorm_sRGB` (71).
- **Cause:** the game makes two NVN texture objects over the same pool and offset with the same description but for the format, `RGBA8` (0x25) and `RGBA8_SRGB` (0x38): it renders into the linear one and samples the sRGB one, which on NVN share their bytes. The storage-sharing table is keyed by pool, offset and the whole description ([Transient render targets shared by storage](#transient-render-targets-shared-by-storage)), so each got its own Metal texture and the sampled one never changed.
- **Fix:** `tex_mtl_` (`nvn_mtl.m`): a texture that misses the table, but whose description differs from a shared texture's only by the other format of an sRGB / linear pair (`g_pfv_srgb`, the pairs Metal views without `PixelFormatView` usage), is a Metal view of that texture in its own format (2D and 2D array, single-sample). Other format changes keep their own texture. A usage upgrade (`tex_upgrade`) asked through the view upgrades its parent and makes the view again on it; a view whose parent left the sharing table (upgraded through its own serial, taken over, evicted) is dropped at its next lookup and made again on what holds the storage now (log line `stale sRGB / linear view dropped`).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_srgb_alias` (Metal validation): the sRGB texture is a view of the linear one, pixels written through the linear texture read back through the sRGB one, an `R32F` texture over the same storage keeps its own; after the linear texture is upgraded the view reads the upgraded storage, and an upgrade through the view upgrades the parent.
- **Found on:** Mario Kart World (`0400C3F00006E000`; its profile is a separate pull request): the Grand Prix's cup opening movie (2560x1440: the YUV pass writes ser 31381 `RGBA8`, the composite samples ser 31382 `RGBA8_SRGB`, both at offset 0 of a 15 MB pool).

### Render-target use added on demand

- **Symptom:** a clear of, or a format-converting copy into, a texture made without render-target use is declined.
- **Cause:** a whole-level clear and `copy_t2t_draw` (which renders into the destination, [Copies a blit cannot do, drawn](#copies-a-blit-cannot-do-drawn)) need a texture with render-target use.
- **Fix:** the texture is upgraded to render-target use first, as format views are upgraded (`tex_upgrade`; [Integer views for images a shader declares uint or int](#integer-views-for-images-a-shader-declares-uint-or-int)).
- **Found on:** [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md) (the clear of a 32x80 packaged texture); [Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door](../../dm/games/0100ECD018EBE000/README.md) (a format-converting copy of a 128x128 RGBA8 render target into a packaged RG8 texture, mirrored in Y, as the boat arrives in Rogueport, about frame 10,950: `copy_t2t_draw` declined a destination made without render-target use).

### A texture upgrade while a draw binds its arguments ends the pass

- **Symptom:** `CRASH sig=11 addr=0x794` in `-[AGXG16GFamilyRenderContext_mtlnext drawIndexedPrimitives:…]+0x50`, called from `draw_`, right after the log line `texture upgraded to allow format views` (Metal API validation reports nothing first).
- **Cause:** `draw_` takes its render encoder from `rp_begin_ex`, then binds the program's arguments (`encode_args`). Binding a texture view of another format calls `tex_by_id`, which upgrades a texture made without `PixelFormatView` (`tex_upgrade`); the upgrade copies the old texture through `blit()`, which ends the render pass (`rp_end`). The draw then encoded on that ended encoder: the driver reads the context it released at end of encoding (`ldr x21, [x0, ivar]`, then `[x21 + 0x794]`).
- **Fix:** after `encode_args`, a draw whose pass ended (`g_enc != en`) is encoded again from the start in a new pass; the texture is already upgraded, so the second binding does not end it (a second end would be logged red and the draw skipped).
- **Found on:** [Need for Speed: Hot Pursuit Remastered](../../dm/games/010029B0118E8000/README.md) (a 4096x1024 texture sampled through a view of another format at its first loading screen).

### Compressed textures as render targets through an uncompressed shadow

- **Symptom:** `render target is a compressed-format texture …`; the pass's draws did nothing (the attachment was left out), or the pipeline-descriptor validation aborts the process.
- **Cause:** a GPU block encoder writes a BC texture through an uncompressed integer view (one texel per block). Metal can neither attach a compressed format nor make an uncompressed view of one.
- **Fix:** such a pass draws into an uncompressed shadow of blocks × blocks in the view's format: the level's (or layer's, or depth plane's) blocks are copied into it through a buffer before the pass and back after it (`bcrt_attach` / `bcrt_flush` in `nvn_mtl.m`; the method comes from PR #11). The shadows stay resident and are reused by format and size ([Render-target shadows made anew at every size change](memory.md#render-target-shadows-made-anew-at-every-size-change)), and they are kept out of the load-skip and store-elision bookkeeping. `attach`, the pipeline prediction and the sampling views go by the NVN format, so BC textures are never attached or viewed raw. The encoder's output must be declared `uint` ([Integer fragment outputs declared by hand](shaders.md#integer-fragment-outputs-declared-by-hand)).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): agl's BC4 block encoder (`8e2cc2671cae6020`) writes a 32×32 RGTC1 (BC4) texture through an `RGBA16UI` view (one texel per 8-byte block); its twin for a 2D source (`93aa5e22a8202409`) failed in an iPhone run; GitHub issue #4 (reported from an iPhone 14 Pro / A16). [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md): agl's BC4 block encoder (`0544282c162305db`, the same shader Mario Wonder ships) encodes a 32×32×32 BC4 3D texture one depth plane per pass through an `RGBA16UI` view; a raw dump at frame 1500 of the new-game route: the texture was all zeros before, now all 32 planes hold encoded blocks.
- **Note:** the A16 has no BC formats; the host used to store BC textures decompressed on such GPUs (about 3.9 GB instead of 2.8 GB for BotW, over iOS's limit on a 6 GB iPhone), and that path has since been removed: only GPUs with BC are supported ([GPUs without BC textures are refused at start](#gpus-without-bc-textures-are-refused-at-start)).

### GPUs without BC textures are refused at start

- **Symptom:** on an iPhone before the A17 Pro, the game runs until its first BC texture, then `failed assertion 'Texture Descriptor Validation' … MTLTextureDescriptor has invalid pixelFormat (134).` (or another number from 130 to 153, Metal's BC formats; 134 is `BC3_RGBA`) and `CRASH sig=6`, the backtrace through `tex_mtl`, `tex_by_id` and `encode_args`.
- **Cause:** BC textures are created and uploaded as BC, with no decompression path ([Compressed textures as render targets](#compressed-textures-as-render-targets-through-an-uncompressed-shadow), note), and the A16 and older iPhone GPUs have no BC formats (`MTLDevice.supportsBCTextureCompression` false). The format mapping is right; the device cannot make the texture.
- **Fix:** the device is checked before the game starts. The iOS app checks it when Play is pressed (`front_ios_device_check` in `dm/ios/Sources/main.m`, called by `startGame` in `front_ios.m`, or at once in an app without a launcher) and does not start the host on such a GPU: the screen says the device is not supported and names the GPU and the devices that are (iPhone 15 Pro or newer, M1 iPads or newer), and the log has a `[RED] boot:` line. The check makes the process's first Metal device, and Metal reads `MTL_HUD_ENABLED` then, so it runs after the launcher has applied its settings; at launch (`boot_env`, before the launcher), it kept the launcher's **Performance HUD** switch from working ([GitHub issue #840](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/840)). The host's early loading, which makes no device, still starts at launch on such a GPU and is left unused. `mtl_init` (`nvn_mtl.m`) stops every other route the same way, with a `FATAL … has no BC texture formats` line (then for the NVN, Vulkan and GL layers alike; the GL and Vulkan layers are gone since). A GPU without Metal 4 is refused the same way: [GPUs without Metal 4 are refused at start](#gpus-without-metal-4-are-refused-at-start).

  On an M4 iPad Air even Play was too late for the **Performance HUD** (ported from upstream `3d8fc824c`, [PR #1966](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1966)): `boot_env` (`main.m`) now applies the saved choice (`launcher.DM_METAL_HUD`) before `UIApplicationMain`, unless `DM_METAL_HUD` or `MTL_HUD_ENABLED` is set or `DM_SAVED_CHOICES=0`. If that turned the process HUD on and the player switches it off before Play, `startGame` sets the game layer's HUD to disabled. A first switch on takes effect at the next launch.
- **Found on:** [FINAL FANTASY X/X-2 HD Remaster](../../dm/games/0100BC300CB48000/README.md): [GitHub issue #704](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/704), an iPhone 15 (A16) with another region's copy (`0100B3600AE9A000`): the launcher ran (no BC textures), FFX aborted at its first BC3 texture.

### GPUs without Metal 4 are refused at start

- **Symptom:** none in a game: a requirement of the Metal 4-only release. Before it, a GPU without Metal 4 (a virtual Mac's paravirtualized GPU) still ran a classic Metal queue and `MTLDevice` pipelines, which no longer exist.
- **Cause:** the host draws through Metal 4 only: one `MTL4CommandQueue` (`g_q4`) and pipelines built through one `MTL4Compiler` (`g_comp4`). The classic queue, the `MTLDevice` pipeline path and the switch that chose it (`DM_PSO_MTL4`) were removed.
- **Fix:** `mtl_init` stops with one message that names the GPU and the requirement: `the Metal device <name> has no Metal 4 (…), the only way the host draws: it needs an iPhone 15 Pro or newer (A17 Pro chip or later), an iPad with an M1 chip or later, or a Mac with Apple silicon on macOS 27.2 or later; a virtual Mac's GPU has none`. There is no classic part. The iOS boot screen (`front_ios_device_check`) refuses such a GPU before the host starts, as it refuses one without BC textures, and names the reason. The Mac app shows an alert before its window or the host ([Front ends](../frontends.md)). The Mac host's minimum is macOS 27.3 (`MACOS_MIN` in `dm/mac/min_os.mk`).
- **Found on:** no game: the Metal 4-only release (2026-11-10).
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_mtl4_only.py` fails on any Metal 3 queue, command buffer, encoder or `MTLDevice` pipeline in the production code, and on a classic queue in the graphics test fixtures. `test_nsp_mac_app.py` runs the Mac app with `DM_TEST_METAL4=0` and checks the refusal.

### RGB9E5 render targets written through R32UI views

- **Symptom:** the whole picture (a main menu) red, with `[RED] render target view format 0xb cannot be attached (base texture Metal format 93 was created without the format-view usage): drawing in the base format`.
- **Cause:** the game packs its HDR colour itself and writes the packed words into an `RGB9E5F` render target through an `R32UI` view. `RGB9E5` to `R32Uint` is not an sRGB / linear pair, so the view needs `MTLTextureUsagePixelFormatView` on the base texture (see [Render-target views that change the format](#render-target-views-that-change-the-format)); colour textures are made without it to keep lossless compression, so `rt_view_fmt` fell back to `RGB9E5` and the packed words were written as floats.
- **Fix:** `tex_desc` gives every non-packaged `RGB9E5Float` texture the format-view usage at creation, as it does for `Depth32Float_Stencil8`. Upgrading at attach time (`tex_upgrade`) would recreate a render target in use. Packaged ones are never drawn into and keep compression. The cost is lossless compression on `RGB9E5` render targets, which few games use; not measured.
- **Found on:** the S2Arm project reports it on 04008E001F098000's main menu (ported from S2Arm 03a63fa2); not run here.
- **Check:** `t_pfv` (`RGB9E5 render target through an R32UI view`: the descriptor has the usage and a packaged one does not, the view is attached as `R32Uint`, and the word `0x7e020080` drawn through it reads back bit for bit from the base texture; without the usage it is attached as format 93 and reads 0).

### A depth texture drawn through a colour view

- **Symptom:** the boot aborts in Metal's pipeline validation: `MTLPixelFormatDepth16Unorm is not color renderable`.
- **Cause:** a depth texture drawn through a colour view (a 1024×1024 D16 texture through an R16 view); Metal has no colour view of a depth texture.
- **Fix:** such a pass draws into a colour shadow of the same pixel size, copied in before and back after the pass through a buffer, as a compressed render target is ([Compressed textures as render targets through an uncompressed shadow](#compressed-textures-as-render-targets-through-an-uncompressed-shadow); `shrt_block`; D16 and D32F, whose Metal pixel is the NVN pixel; not D24S8). The shadows are kept by format and size, so passes of several sizes do not make them again ([Render-target shadows made anew at every size change](memory.md#render-target-shadows-made-anew-at-every-size-change)).
- **Found on:** [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md).

### The same texture attached at two colour slots

- **Symptom:** a G-buffer target holds only scattered squares (a different order for each 32×32 tile).
- **Cause:** on Maxwell the ROP writes only masked channels, so two slots holding the same texture with one at write mask 0 do not interfere. On Apple's TBDR each attachment has its own tile memory and the whole tile is stored at the end of the pass, so the copy stored later (loaded, never written) overwrote the other one's writes.
- **Fix:** a slot whose texture, level and layer repeat an earlier slot is left unattached (`rt_dup_slot`), also in pipeline prediction; a draw that really writes such a slot is reported once (`color slot … holds the same texture as an earlier slot`). Records hold copies of texture objects (`tex_snap`), so slots are compared by serial, not by pointer.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): the G-buffer passes attach the albedo and another buffer twice each (c1 = c2, c3 = c4), the duplicate slots with write mask 0; the albedo held only scattered squares.

### Passes with no render target

- **Symptom:** `no render target could get an MTLTexture` (the pass skipped).
- **Cause:** a fragment shader that only stores to a storage buffer draws into a viewport with no colour or depth target set.
- **Fix:** the host opens an attachment-less pass at the viewport's size (`bare_dims` in `nvn_mtl.m`, from PR #11).
