  - Red Dead Redemption (`01007820196A6000`, profile not in this repository; [PR 1866](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1866)): its cloud fog `rdr2_clouds_fog` (`c0ab3c051753e36d`) fades the clouds against the depth it samples through a uniform-buffer handle, in the pass where the billboards (`rdr2_billboard`), the low-detail terrain and other geometry have just written depth: beige blocks above the ridge behind Armadillo, in both 1.0.0 and 1.0.4. With the pass ended before it (Apple M4, macOS 27.0.1, the route out of the saloon in Armadillo): the blocks gone, about one more pass end a frame, GPU p50 14.3 → 14.7 ms, frame time unchanged at the game's 30 fps.

### Mip-chain pass sampling the level it renders

- **Symptom:** NaN texels in the upper levels of a texture the game fills level by level (each level drawn from the one above), and from there NaN wherever the texture is read: black speckles in whole 2×2 quads, white blotches once bloom spreads them, and a dark frame from the eye adaptation.
- **Cause:** a mip-chain pass renders level n while its fragment shader samples level n − 1 through a view that covers the whole chain (explicit level, linear mip filter). On NVIDIA that reads level n − 1 only. On Metal the fetch can also touch level n, at weight 0 but still fetched, and an attached level read through a texture holds no defined data on Apple's GPUs; 0 × NaN is NaN. The same-pass feedback rule ([above](#same-pass-feedback-read-through-a-snapshot)) left such passes as they were.
- **Fix:** `fb_prepare` in `nvn_mtl.m`, for every game, by the levels the slot's sampler lets the draw fetch (`fb_reach`: the combined handle's sampler, the shader's `SAMPT` binding of the same slot; its minimum and maximum LOD clamp whatever LOD the shader asks for, an explicit one too, and a linear mip filter also fetches the level above an integer LOD at weight 0). A sampler that clamps from below the attachment's level, or one not known here, gives the draw a view of the same texture limited to the levels below the attachment (same base, so the shader's level numbers keep their meaning): no copy, and exact whenever the clamp ends below the attachment's level. A sampler that clamps to one other level gives a view of that level alone ([below](#upsample-pass-reading-the-level-above-the-one-it-renders)); a range wholly above the attachment's level reads the view as it is. A clamp that starts at the attachment's level (a pin there, or a range or a fractional LOD from there up), and a pass that renders the view's base level with no level pinned, or one layer of a layered view, take a snapshot of the whole view: its type, sizes, layers and levels, so the shader's LODs and the sampler's clamp keep their meaning, with the levels the sampler can reach copied (every level when it is not known), in the type the shader declares (the host holds cube maps as 2D arrays). The prefix view stood in for those before: a pin at the attachment's level read the level below, and a range or fractional LOD from it up was clamped to the level below too; a base-level or layer pass read the attachment it rendered. The snapshot cache keys a view by serial, base level and layer and its shape (type, size, layers, levels; also what the idle release remembers for kept snapshots) and copies a level a later draw reaches that an earlier one did not, until the next barrier. The view of the attachment's level and layer alone keeps the single-level 2D snapshot. From upstream NXNative 0def324d5 (PR #1004, by ComicBit; its full copy for every non-pinned mip-chain pass was not taken); NX 18 asks the sampler only for a view of several levels, after the check for an attachment read directly.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_feedback`: `mip_chain_case` checks the view or snapshot each sampler gets; `mip_pixels_case` samples a 4×4 chain of red, green and blue levels at LOD 2 through registered samplers after the pass overwrites the attachment's level, and reads the pre-pass level each clamp names (blue, red, green, blue for the pins away from the attachment and a range above it, with no snapshot; green, blue and a green-blue blend for a pin at the attachment, a range from it and a fractional pin, with one); `base_level_case` renders the base level of a 2D view, a view whose base is level 1, a 2D-array view of layers 1–2 and a cube, overwrites every level and layer, and reads the level the shader asks for as it was. With the earlier rule 18 of 478 checks fail (the three clamps from the attachment's level and the four base-level passes); all pass with Metal API validation and direct, threaded and idle-feedback encoding (Apple M4 Max). The clamps from the attachment's level were PR #1004's.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md#rendering): its environment cube (128×128, 8 levels, R11G11B10F, levels 1–7 drawn by `d1a506c1a94ffdfa` and `89dfaeb53f85920a`) got NaN texels; copied into the sources of the prefiltered cube (`62dc9b15931e0290`) and fed back through reflections and the cube capture every frame, they reached 59,592 NaN pixels of the first gameplay frame's scene (0 with the fix).

### Upsample pass reading the level above the one it renders

- **Symptom:** bloom wide and hard around bright edges: neon letters swell into fat, filled shapes. With `DM_NVN_FB_SNAP=0` they are thin again, but the white blotches of the entry above come back.
- **Cause:** the fix in [Mip-chain pass sampling the level it renders](#mip-chain-pass-sampling-the-level-it-renders) gives a mip-chain pass a view of the levels below its attachment, which assumes the pass downsamples. An upsample pass renders level n and reads level n + 1, chosen by its sampler's LOD clamp (n + 1..n + 1). In a view that ends at level n − 1 Metal clamps that read to level n − 1, a sharper level, and the chain adds it back up to the top. An upsample into the view's base level got no view at all and read the attached level.
- **Fix:** `fb_prepare` in `nvn_mtl.m` (`fb_reach`), for every game: when the slot's sampler (the combined handle's, the shader's `SAMPT` binding of the same slot) has a mip filter and a minimum and maximum LOD that are the same whole number, every fetch reads that level, so the draw samples a view of that level alone, whichever side of the attachment it lies on. A pin at the attachment's own level takes the snapshot of the [entry above](#mip-chain-pass-sampling-the-level-it-renders). `t_feedback` (`mip_chain_case`, `mip_pixels_case`) checks the view each sampler gets and the pixels it reads.
- **Open:** an upsample that picks its level in the shader (an explicit LOD through a sampler whose clamp starts below the attachment's level, such as one that clamps nothing) still gets the view of the levels below, and reads its last level; no game is known to do it. Telling it from a downsample would need the shader's LOD.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md#rendering), found by the author of PR #1073: its two bloom chains (576×324 R11G11B10F, 5 levels) downsample with `ef18c6eca9b2f991` (sampler LOD n − 1..n − 1) and upsample with `ad1a5991aaad135e` (n + 1..n + 1); a pass dump showed the upsample drawing level 3 through view `0+3` with LOD 4..4. With the rule switched off and on through the Ingot Isle stage intro, the neon letters went fat and filled, then thin, with it.

### Lower-left window origin

- **Symptom:** text a game renders into textures for the title and the menus comes out upside down, while the scene looks right.
- **Cause:** a game that never calls `nvnDeviceSetWindowOriginMode` keeps NVN's default, lower-left (OpenGL), where NDC y = -1 lands on a render target's first memory row. Read as upper-left, every texture the game renders and samples came out flipped and cancelled out, but not the text, and the game copies the frame with `MIRROR_Y`.
- **Fix:** viewport and scissor rectangles, fragment coordinates, copies and texture coordinates address the same memory rows in both conventions, so the host flips the direction inside the viewport (negative viewport height, using its [recorded device origin](#viewport-origin-belongs-to-the-recording-device)) and flips the window image at present and in raw screenshots (`g_origin_ll` in `nvn_mtl.m`). Facing also follows the [raster flip captured at queue initialization](#queue-initialization-captures-the-raster-origin).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md); the six games before it all switch to upper-left.
- **Note:** the window's Y-direction system register under this origin is open ([Open: the window Y-direction register `SR_Y_DIRECTION`](shaders.md#open-the-window-y-direction-register-sr_y_direction)); the OpenGL layer (removed in 0.5) rendered through this origin, and Absolum combines it with a viewport swizzle ([Viewport swizzles applied as a flipped viewport](#viewport-swizzles-applied-as-a-flipped-viewport)).

### Queue initialization captures the raster origin

- **Symptom:** geometry appears inside out and almost black; a fullscreen indirect-lighting triangle is entirely culled even though its vertex data and polygon state match the game.
- **Cause:** NVN emits `SET_WINDOW_ORIGIN.FLIP_Y` into a queue's initial GPU state. `DeviceSetWindowOriginMode` only changes the device field that later viewport construction reads; it does not rewrite an existing queue's raster flip. Deriving Metal winding from the current device origin loses this distinction when the game creates a queue before changing the origin. Reversing winding globally fixes that sequence but breaks games that set their origin first.
- **Fix:** `queue_t.origin_ll` captures the device origin in `nvnQueueInitialize`. Each submission selects that queue's raster origin under the submission lock. Metal winding uses this captured origin; viewport direction uses the [origin recorded with the viewport](#viewport-origin-belongs-to-the-recording-device), while presentation keeps using the device origin.
- **Original validation:** `t_queue_origin` alternated two queues created with different origins on one device: 389 checks passed with direct and deferred Metal encoding; restoring the previous winding expression failed 48. The complete Metal suite and Mario Wonder's full smoke route passed then. The current fixture also checks the [recording device's viewport origin](#viewport-origin-belongs-to-the-recording-device).
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 2](../../dm/games/0100E95004038000/README.md#rendering), with native SDK initialization evidence and the comparison with Mario Wonder recorded there.

### Viewport origin belongs to the recording device

- **Symptom:** a movie pass clears its target but its back-face-culled YUV-to-RGB quad disappears when a second NVN device records commands for the game's queue.
- **Cause:** `SetViewports` constructs the viewport using the command buffer's device origin. The recorder retained only the rectangle, and replay applied the current global device origin instead. A later origin change, including one on another device, could flip an already recorded viewport and reverse its facing relative to the queue's independent raster origin.
- **Fix:** the full viewport payload and compact viewport header carry the recording device's origin. Replay retains it with each viewport in the queue state and uses it for the Metal viewport and projective sample-coordinate proof. Updating another viewport does not replace viewport 0's origin. Queue winding remains fixed by queue initialization. The unused depth slot in the existing viewport payload holds the origin without changing command sizes. Complete captures made before this field keep their previous device-origin interpretation because the slot was zero. Ported from upstream (`8f51289db`).
- **Check:** `t_queue_origin` tests full and compact records, later origin changes, a second recording device, independent viewport updates, retained queue state, implicit and legacy viewports, culling, facing, asymmetric pixels, negative-Y swizzles and the projective sample proof. On Apple M6 / macOS 27.2 / Xcode 27.2, 3,072 of 13,839 checks fail on unchanged main; all 13,839 pass with the correction in each encoder mode. `t_capture` checks the origin's layout and relocation and accepts the complete earlier layouts. (Upstream's numbers.)
- **Found on:** Deathsmiles II's `NvnVideoRenderer`, reported by hada0127 in [issue #593](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/593#issuecomment-6628114255). Its movie device keeps the default lower-left origin while submitting to the game's upper-left device queue.
- **Open:** Deathsmiles has no repository profile; its current-build movie and iOS confirmation remain open. The issue's separate idle VideoToolbox session failure and the remaining game bring-up are not resolved by this change.

### The residency set committed before a present

- **Symptom:** a pink window with every command buffer ignored while headless runs look right: the first presents fail on the display command buffer with `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault`, and after a few of those macOS ignores every submission of the process ("Ignored (for causing prior/excessive GPU errors)").
- **Cause:** the game presents its window texture in its first frames before drawing into it; the texture was made on that first resolution in the present path and added to the residency set (`res_add`), but only a submission commits the set, so the display command buffer sampled a texture outside the committed set. Headless runs never present, which is why they looked right.
- **Fix:** `present_now` commits the residency set after resolving the presented texture (`res_commit`, free when nothing changed), for every game.
- **Found on:** [Super Smash Bros. Ultimate](../../dm/games/01006A800016E000/README.md) (13.0.5, `DM_WINDOW=1`).
- **Check:** Breath of the Wild windowed with the change: its title, 60 fps, no GPU error.

### Naming the shaders of a GPU fault

- **Symptom:** `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault` or `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorHang` on a command buffer, and nothing says which draw or dispatch caused it; `DM_RES_CHECK=1` reports no `missing residency`.
- **Cause:** Metal 4's commit feedback gives only the error and two times, so the log can name a submission but no pass or shader. `MTL_SHADER_VALIDATION=1` can name one, at about a fifth of the frame rate, which is little use for a fault several minutes into a game.
- **Fix:** each command buffer keeps the list of the game shaders it encoded (`cbs_note` in `encode_args`, one comparison per binding once a shader is listed). On an error that command buffer caused, the log has `[RED] game shaders of failed command buffer #N (fence F, segment K): …`, then `[RED] game shaders by failed/ran over N failed of M command buffers: key failed/ran, …`, most often failed first: in how many failed command buffers the shader was, of how many that encoded it. Read the second line of the last report: a shader in most failures with a high ratio is the suspect; one that runs everywhere has a low ratio. A victim of another's fault and a submission refused after one do not count. The first 16 failures are printed. The lists leave out the host's own passes and geometry emulation's kernels, so no shader standing out points there. Nothing here shows a shader is wrong, only that it was present: check it with validation or by reading what it addresses. A validation run must not share a `pso_archive` with normal runs: they cannot use the files it writes.
- **What can fault:** measured on an M4 Pro, with a Metal 4 queue: loads and atomics through a buffer address that is unmapped, freed, not resident or null, from compute or fragment shaders, raise no error (reads give 0 or whatever is mapped there, so an overrun can silently write into another allocation). A fragment shader sampling a texture not in the residency set, or one freed whose resource ID nothing holds, raises `PageFault`; after two of these macOS refused every later submission. A freed texture's resource ID goes to the next texture made, and a stale binding then reads that texture with no error. Other chips may differ.
- **Early release:** `DM_RES_CHECK=1` also reports `[RED] released too early`: an object retired behind an earlier fence than a binding of it. Its `missing residency` line cannot see this, as the object is still in the set at that binding.
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md) ([issue #1289](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1289)), whose faults recur every minute or so before a fatal one.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_cbs_fault`.

### A present snapshot's copy needs the same residency commit

- **Symptom:** with present snapshots on (`DM_PRESENT_SNAP`, the iOS launcher's **Frame buffering**), a frozen, corrupted picture from the first presents; the first error is already `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorSubmissionsIgnored` ("Ignored (for causing prior/excessive GPU errors)") on the display command buffer, then on every upload, segment and submission command buffer, and `[RED] present snapshots: the previous copy into a ring slot did not finish within 2 s`. With snapshots off the same game runs.
- **Cause:** the case of [the entry above](#the-residency-set-committed-before-a-present), on the snapshot path: `snap_take` resolves the presented texture and copies it into a ring slot on the game's queue before `present_now` runs, and only `present_now` committed the residency set. A window texture presented before any submission had used it was outside the committed set when the copy read it. The copy's command buffer has no error handler, so its fault shows only as the ignored command buffers after it.
- **Fix:** `snap_take` commits the residency set after resolving the source (`res_commit`, free when nothing changed), for every game.
- **Found on:** [DRAGON QUEST XI S](../../dm/games/01006C300E9F0000/README.md) 1.0.4 on an iPad mini 7 (A17 Pro), which presents three times before its first submission ([GitHub issue #626](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/626)). The reporter's Apple M4 test did not fault without the fix.
- **Not checked:** the iPad with the fix.

### Innocent-victim GPU errors said nothing of what ran beside them

- **Symptom:** a few command buffers come back `Discarded (victim of GPU error/recovery) (00000005:kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorInnocentVictim)`, one detailed red line and a red line each, and the game goes on. Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave lost 7 so at about 1,665 s of a run in the 2026-11-15 round, as an AV1 movie stopped (`movie paced (stopped or flushed)` right after), about 20 s before the app left the screen; an older Fortune's Weave log has the same kind mid-play, and Ocarina of Time had one about 1 s before it left the screen. Nothing else was seen; the cost was those frames.
- **Cause:** open. A victim is discarded in the GPU's recovery from another command buffer's error, so its own error names neither the faulting work nor what ran beside it, and the log had nothing of the host's state at that moment. The first suspect is the movie's end (Fortune's Weave's pictures go to the GPU as native images: a linear texture over a memory pool of the decoder pool's memory, `h_ni_texture` in `hle_nvn.c`); the drawn-over render targets' release (`DM_NVN_RT_DEAD`) freed only 3 textures in that run, so it is unlikely, though it is the A/B to pair with ([Render targets drawn over stay while their texture objects live](memory.md#render-targets-drawn-over-stay-while-their-texture-objects-live)).
- **Fix:** logging only (`victim_note` in `nvn_mtl.m`, from every command buffer's feedback handler: submission, segment, upload batch, one-off, offscreen present, display). The run's first innocent victim writes, once, `[RED] GPU error: the run's first innocent-victim command buffer (<which>, fence F) …`, then `in flight:` the fences submitted and completed, the main command buffers not reported back, upload batches pending, the present queue and what of it is on the GPU, the residency commit worker and a commit in the kernel; `movies:` the movie signal's last picture, each nn::codec decoder (codec, size, pictures handed out, those waiting, its clock, pictures to the GPU as native images), the last stop or flush and the last image pool finalized (`dm_movie_state` in `hle_codec_video.c`), and the native images' memory pools live and finalized (`nvn_native_state` in `hle_nvn.c`); `GPU probe:` its window so far without ending it, the thermal state and Low Power Mode, the drawn-over render targets released and when the last were, and the presents. Every read is an atomic or a lock only tried, as the stall dump's are.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_gpu_lost`: no line for no error or a page fault; the first innocent victim writes the four lines, the second nothing. `make -C dm/host/tests GAME=<id> run` (`t_codec_video`): the movies' part with two decoders, a busy decoder or table not waited for, a short buffer cut, and after both end. Not yet in a device log.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (an iPhone 18 Pro Max, the 2026-11-15 round) and [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game).

### A GPU whose access macOS revoked stops the game

- **Symptom:** after a GPU hang or GPU page faults, every upload, segment, submission and display command buffer comes back `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorSubmissionsIgnored` ("Ignored (for causing prior/excessive GPU errors)"). Nothing is drawn again while the game's sound and logic go on, until the game aborts itself (`game called nn::diag abort`, about 70 s later); in full screen, WindowServer froze for 40 s and its watchdog killed it. With the fix the log has `[RED] GPU access revoked: the … command buffer was ignored`, and a Mac app shows an alert and quits.
- **Cause:** macOS revokes a process's GPU access after GPU errors it caused, and from then on runs none of its command buffers: they come back at once with `MTL4CommandQueueErrorAccessRevoked`. The host logged them and went on. Their feedback still came, but the GPU no longer signalled the shared event a game's SyncWait waits on (for up to an hour, `mtl_wait_at`), and each present took a drawable that no command buffer drew. Metal 4 reports a commit's error as `MTL4CommandQueueErrorTimeout` with no text, with the command buffer's own error under `NSUnderlyingErrorKey` and `NSMultipleUnderlyingErrorsKey`, so the top-level code alone never shows it.
- **Fix:** `gpu_lost_check` (`nvn_mtl.m`) looks for `MTL4CommandQueueErrorAccessRevoked` in each command buffer's error and the errors under it; the first one stops the game, for every game:
  - presents stop (`present_now`);
  - the shared event is raised from the CPU to the last fence whose feedback has come back, then again at each later submission's feedback (`gpu_lost_release`), so no SyncWait waits for a signal that will not come, and no command allocator is reset before Metal hands its command buffer back;
  - the front end is told once (`front_gpu_lost`, `dm/host/front.h`). A Mac app leaves full screen, says why in an alert that points to the log and `scripts/bug_report.py`, and quits with exit status 70 (⌘Q's is 0). Without a window the run ends with a FATAL line and an abort; the iPhone saves and quits ([A GPU that finishes no work is taken as lost](nvn-objects.md#a-gpu-that-finishes-no-work-is-taken-as-lost)).

  A hang or a page fault alone does not stop the game: the GPU recovers from those, and command buffers discarded as innocent victims (`kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorInnocentVictim`, also a timeout) come back while the game goes on. `DM_TEST_GPU_LOST=<n>` imitates the revoked access from the nth submission on, and `t_gpu_lost` checks the error's shape, the single call, the released waits and the stopped presents.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (v131072) on a Mac ([GitHub issue #1326](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1326)). Its first run hung in full screen and every command buffer after the hang was ignored. Its second run, in a window, recovered from several innocent-victim timeouts, page-faulted at fences 110102 and 110108, and had every command buffer ignored from fence 110114, two frames later.
- **Open:** the hang and the page faults that made macOS revoke the access.
- **Not checked:** a real revoked access with the fix; only the imitation (`t_gpu_lost`).

### A CPU-written window texture resolved where the present is queued

- **Symptom:** an abort at the first present: `[RED] upload batches belong to the submit thread only: up_begin called on thread 'nvn-present-link'`, with `pkg_upload`, `tex_mtl` and `present_now` in the backtrace.
- **Cause:** the game fills its window textures with `nvnTextureWriteTexels` at boot and presents one before drawing into it. A texture whose content comes from its storage (CPU-written, packaged) is uploaded when it is first resolved, and again when its pages have changed since, in an upload batch only the submit thread may open; the first resolution of this one was `present_now` on the present thread. Two cases were left after the first fix: a texture first used by the present, whose first use decides whether it is taken from its storage ([storage the CPU wrote before its first use](nvn-objects.md#block-compressed-storage-the-cpu-wrote-before-first-use), `tex_cpu_content`), so it did not count as one before; and an uploaded one whose pages changed between the queue and the present thread, which the present thread uploaded again.
- **Fix:** `present_enqueue`, on the thread that queues the present, resolves the texture when that is the submit thread, and flushes any upload batch it opened (`up_flush`; outside a submission every upload path flushes at once anyway), so the display command buffer comes after the upload on the queue. With Metal encoding on its own thread (`DM_NVN_ENC_THREAD`, the default on iOS) that flush between submissions is only recorded and published, and nothing drains the encode thread until the next submission: when an upload batch began since the last submission's drain and since the last note, a `RO_NOTE` behind it is what the present thread waits for (`pup_commit_wait`) before it commits the display command buffer straight to the queue; every queued present carries the newest note, so one the display link shows in place of a dropped present still waits for that present's upload. Off the submit thread `tex_mtl` hands back an uploaded storage texture as it is, without looking at its pages (the submit thread's next look still finds a write since): a present shows the content as of when the game queued it. For every game.
- **Found on:** [Bastion](../../dm/games/010038600B27E000/README.md) (1.0.0, SDK 5.4.0: both 1920x1080 window textures written by `main+0x9374`). The two later cases in `t_nvn`, whose window textures nothing draws into: they were GPU textures while a sliver of another GPU-written span counted, and came up as storage textures once it did not (c06d1e79).
- **Check:** `t_nvn` (present pacing presents its never-drawn window textures); `t_present_fill` (a window texture made and zero-filled by its present, with the encode thread held behind a sleeping block: the present thread commits only after it).

### The present upscales through MetalFX

- **Symptom:** a game that renders below the screen's resolution looks soft on a phone, in a large Mac window or on a 4K screen.
- **Cause:** the drawable has the layer's native pixel size (`front_ios.m`, `front_mac.m`; on the iPhone and iPad only where MetalFX can run: [The iPhone's drawable at the picture's render size](#the-iphones-drawable-at-the-pictures-render-size)), and the present pass (`present_encode_m4`) scaled the presented texture into it bilinearly.
- **Fix:** `DM_PRESENT_UPSCALE=metalfx` (the iOS launcher's **Upscaling**; off by default on every platform, no profile key): where the picture's rectangle on the canvas (`present_viewport`) is larger than the content (`fx_wanted`), the present copies the content to the origin of the scaler's input texture (MetalFX reads from the origin, so a crop with an offset works too; the input is the window texture's size, see [The MetalFX scaler under a moving crop](#the-metalfx-scaler-under-a-moving-crop)), MetalFX's spatial scaler (`MTL4FXSpatialScaler`, perceptual colour processing: the input is the raw gamma-encoded bytes the present samples) scales it into a texture of the rectangle's size in the present's Metal 4 command buffer, and the present pass draws that 1:1 (`fx_encode`). Letterbox, the lower-left origin and the drawable format stay the present pass's. The copy waits for the queue's earlier work and ends with a producer barrier that the scaler's encoders wait behind. A new texture or output size or format makes a new scaler after the presents on the GPU finish, at most once every 30 presents (a window being resized shows bilinear frames meanwhile); a size MetalFX refuses stays bilinear. Whole-window captures and `DM_NVN_SHOT_OUT` screenshots draw the picture the same way (`present_offscreen_m4`), with separate scaler state and resources retained until GPU completion; `DM_NVN_SHOT` and `DM_CTL` "shot" still save the game's own frame. Tests: `t_present_crop` (sizing, offset crops, letterbox, sRGB bytes, lower-left origin, standard fallback, resize throttling, a moving crop and asynchronous whole-window capture). [Sharpening in the present](#sharpening-in-the-present) is not drawn over MetalFX's picture.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild — upscaling and measurements](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#upscaling-current).
- **Limit:** the scaler takes the presented frame, including any filtering and HUD drawing the game already performed. Feeding it an earlier scene target would mean replacing a draw of the game's own, and nothing general identifies which draw is a plain upscale. Scaler support is checked at runtime; unsupported devices, sizes and formats use standard scaling.

### The MetalFX scaler under a moving crop

- **Symptom:** with Upscaling MetalFX, a game whose dynamic resolution moves its crop inside its window textures shows bilinear frames between MetalFX ones, and its present thread stalls: `present upscaling: MetalFX spatial scaler … (scaler #N …)` climbs every 30 or more presents, and the 600-present GPU lines say `through the bilinear pass` as well as `through MetalFX`.
- **Cause:** `fx_encode` keyed the scaler on the crop's size. Each new crop size returned the bilinear pass until 30 presents had passed since the last scaler (`FX_REMAKE_PRESENTS`), then waited on the present thread for every present on the GPU, made a new scaler and new textures. MetalFX's `inputContentWidth` / `inputContentHeight` (the part of the input it scales) were set, but always to the input's own size.
- **Fix:** the scaler's input is the window texture's size wherever the texture is no larger than the picture's rectangle in either direction and smaller in one (`fx_input_size`); the crop is copied to its origin as before and is the scaler's content each frame. A scaler is made again only for a new texture size, output size or format. MetalFX's filter reads past the content's last column and row, into texels an earlier, larger crop left there: on the M4 Max, without anything there, a 48x48 content scaled to 96x96 took a third of the magenta beyond it into its last two output columns, and at 1080p 1,600 to 7,300 output pixels were more than 8/255 off a scaler made for the crop's size (content 0.95 to 0.5 of 1920x1080 scaled to 2346x1320). So the crop's last two columns and rows are repeated past it (`FX_PAD`, eight small copies), as an input of the content's own size clamps to them; with them the picture is byte-identical to that scaler's at every one of those sizes (one texel of padding still left two pixels off). Where the texture is larger than the picture's rectangle (a narrow layout: an iPad or a portrait split, about 1320 pixels across for a 1920x1080 texture with a 960x540 crop), a texture-sized input would be larger than the output, which the spatial scaler does not scale down to: the input is the crop's size there, as before, and a new crop size still waits 30 presents. `DM_NVN_PRESENT_FX_EXACT=1` keys every scaler on the crop's size, as before, for an A/B on a GPU whose MetalFX might treat a small content differently. The 600-present GPU line (`present_gpu_note`) ends with `crop moved N times so far`: the live presents whose crop differed from the one before in a texture of the same size (`present_crop_note`). Every 300 live presents, once a present this session has shown less than its whole window texture or the crop has moved, `[nvn-mtl] present crop over 300 presents: …` gives the crop's mean, smallest and largest share of the window texture (with the side length they come to and the smallest and largest crop in pixels), the moves among them, the thermal state and the seconds since launch, so a heat A/B shows how far a game's dynamic resolution went at each thermal state (`nvn_crop_area.h`; logging only, written from the present's feedback handler).
- **Check:** `t_present_crop`: a crop moving inside a 64x64 texture (64, 48, 56, 64) is drawn through MetalFX at every present with no new scaler, an input of the texture's size and three moves counted; texels a whole-texture crop left in the input stay out of the next, smaller crop's picture, which is byte-identical to that of a scaler made for the crop's size; in a narrow layout the input is the crop's size and a new crop size waits 30 presents. The 1080p comparison was a scratch harness on the M4 Max's Metal 4 queue (the study's, with the padding added).
- **Found by:** a study of the present (2026-11-09). Pokémon Scarlet calls `nvnWindowSetCrop` about once a frame (5,354 calls in its usage profile), which does not show that its size changes; Super Mario Odyssey's dynamic resolution is documented to move its crop. The `crop moved` counter is there to show which games do. The crop-size line beside it shows how far (Mario Kart World's resolution at each thermal state, for the X2.0 heat ladder's M1 runs).
- **Not checked:** on a device: whether the A20's MetalFX matches the M4 Max's for a content well below its input size (`DM_NVN_PRESENT_FX_EXACT=1` is the A/B), and the frames and stalls saved in a game that moves its crop.

### MetalFX copied a crop that is its whole texture

- **Symptom:** none on screen. With MetalFX in the present (the Quality preset, or Upscaling MetalFX) and edge smoothing off, every present copied the whole picture into the scaler's own input texture first, even when the game's crop was the whole window texture: a full-frame copy each present (about 0.44 GB/s read and written at 720p60 RGBA8, more with repeats) and an input texture as large as the picture (3.5 MB at 720p, 7.9 MB at 1080p).
- **Cause:** `fx_encode` copies the crop into `fx->in` so a crop smaller than its texture can be padded past its edge ([the scaler under a moving crop](#the-metalfx-scaler-under-a-moving-crop)); edge smoothing's output was already read in place (`ready`), the game's texture never was.
- **Fix:** general, `nvn_mtl.m` `fx_encode` (`DM_PRESENT_FX_DIRECT`, on by default; `=0` copies always, as before). When the crop starts at the origin and fills the texture, the scaler's input is that texture's size and the texture has the scaler's usage, the scaler reads the texture itself: the same texels in the same format and no edge to pad, so the same bytes out. Only a plain texture qualifies (`fx_direct_plain`: 2D, one level, one sample, no channel swizzle): the copy takes slice 0, level 0 and the stored bytes, and read in place a swizzled texture came out with its channels swapped and a 2D array of one slice came out wrong too (found in review; both still copy). The copy's ordering is kept: MetalFX's encoders take no barrier, so `fx_order` puts a one-word copy behind the same queue barrier first (as the replay's `mfx_order`); the present pass after the scaler waits for it, as it did. `fx->in` is made only for a crop that needs the copy. A moving crop (a dynamic resolution) still copies.
- **Measured (Mac, M4 Max, a bench of `present_encode_m4` through MetalFX, median of 60 presents, best of 5):** the present command buffer's GPU time does not move measurably on the Mac: 1280x720 → 2868x1320 0.911 → 0.918 ms, 1920x1080 → 3840x2160 0.860 → 0.845 ms (the copy is about 10 µs at its bandwidth; the scaler is the cost). The gain is the copy's memory traffic and the input texture, which count for power on the phone more than for time.
- **Log:** the present GPU time line through MetalFX ends with `MetalFX read the picture in place N times so far`.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_present_crop` (all four modes): a shared and a private sRGB picture, an RGBA16Float one and a 50x38 one, each at two output sizes through the live present, read in place once each (counted) with the same bytes as through the copy, and a capture the same; a crop inside the texture still copies and is not counted; a swizzled texture, a 2D array of one slice and a texture with mips go through the copy with the same bytes as `=0` (without `fx_direct_plain` the first two differ from the copy's bytes). An input content two texels short in the in-place path fails every byte check (the negative control, tried by hand).
- **Phone check:** a game with the Quality preset (MetalFX) at a fixed resolution, on battery with the same controller setup: the present GPU time line says `read the picture in place` with a count that rises with the presents; the picture is the same; watts against `DM_PRESENT_FX_DIRECT=0` in env.txt over a long session (expected: a small difference, if any).

### Sharpening in the present

- **Symptom:** a game's picture looks soft where the app shows it larger than the game renders it: Breath of the Wild handheld (1280x720), docked after its own 900p to 1080p scale, and every game in Picture Stretch or Ultrawide, where MetalFX does not run.
- **Cause:** apart from MetalFX's opt-in scaler, the present scales the picture bilinearly (`fs` in `present.metal`), and on the iPhone Core Animation then scales the picture-sized drawable to the screen ([The iPhone's drawable at the picture's render size](#the-iphones-drawable-at-the-pictures-render-size)).
- **Fix:** the setting **Sharpening** (`DM_PRESENT_SHARPEN`: `0` Off, `1` Low, `2` Medium, `3` High; a game's default from its profile's `present_sharpen`, Off without it) draws the present with `fs_rcas`, `fs` with FidelityFX FSR1's Robust Contrast Adaptive Sharpening fused in (MIT, ported from `ffx_fsr1.h` with its notice). Its four neighbours are one source texel away, clamped to the crop like the centre, so a 1:1 present is RCAS on the picture's own grid and a crop smaller than the drawable is RCAS of the bilinear scale, still one draw (five bilinear taps). Low, Medium and High are 2, 1 and 0.5 stops (RCAS's sharpness `exp2(-stops)`); FSR's default of 0.2 stops is not offered. Alpha passes through as `fs` passes it. The present's constants grew from 8 to 24 floats (Sharpening's strength and tap step at 8-11, edge smoothing's at `AA_UV`, 12-21); Off draws `fs` with the same 8, byte-identical to before. Both pipelines are made together at the first present (`present_pipeline`), so a pick from the in-game menu (`mtl_present_sharpen_set`, taken by the live present at its next present as Upscaling's is, `nvn_upscale_pick.h`) compiles nothing. Not drawn, with one log line each: over a picture MetalFX scaled (Upscaling MetalFX, `fx_encode`) or the game's upscaler drawn by MetalFX inside the frame (`DM_UPSCALER=metalfx`, `up_mfx_encode`), whose perceptual mode overshoots text by about 10/255 already and RCAS on top by 15-26/255 in the study; and over a source that is not a UNORM format, whose values RCAS's limiter does not hold for (the drawable is BGRA8Unorm). It applies in every graphics API and picture mode, on the Metal 4 present; the classic-queue present (a Mac without a Metal 4 queue, screenshots only) stays bilinear. Screenshots and whole-window captures (`present_offscreen_m4`) include it. The 600-present GPU line has a third bucket, `through the bilinear pass with sharpening`.
- **Measured** (M4 Max, synthetic: a 3840x2160 photo with aliased lines, drawn at the output size as the reference and area-scaled to the game's size with a HUD of outlined white text and 1-pixel ticks drawn at the game's size; the HUD's overshoot past the range of the source texels a pixel is made from, the 2x2 a bilinear scale reads, the 3x3 around it at 1:1, in /255; PSNR of the rest against the reference):

  | Case | Sharpening | Scene PSNR | HUD overshoot p99 | HUD max |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1280x720 crop of a 1920x1080 texture into a 1920x1080 drawable (Breath of the Wild handheld): fused, as shipped | Off | 34.03 dB | 0.00 | 0.43 |
  | | Low | 34.57 dB | 0.22 | 2.79 |
  | | Medium | 35.15 dB | 2.07 | 7.93 |
  | | High | 35.33 dB | 5.15 | 14.15 |
  | the same, RCAS at 1280x720 then the bilinear scale (the study's variant) | Low / Medium / High | 34.48 / 34.98 / 35.22 dB | 0.14 / 1.65 / 4.14 | 2.21 / 4.86 / 12.86 |
  | 1600x900 crop (a dynamic resolution) into 1920x1080: fused | Low / Medium / High | 37.20 / 38.00 / 38.11 dB (Off 36.44) | 0.43 / 2.28 / 5.35 | 2.43 / 8.00 / 16.78 |
  | 1920x1080 at 1:1, then a bilinear scale to 2347x1320 (Core Animation's, Fit on the iPhone 18 Pro Max) | Low / Medium / High | 38.98 / 39.89 / 40.06 dB (Off 38.15) | 0.65 / 2.00 / 4.35 | 3.43 / 12.43 / 24.49 |
  | 1920x1080 at 1:1, the drawable itself (against the 3x3 range) | Low / Medium / High | | 1.93 / 4.78 / 8.63 | 6.21 / 15.64 / 28.42 |

  The fused scaled crop, which the study had measured only at FSR's 0.2 stops (HUD p99 10.34/255), overshoots a little more than sharpening before the scale, about 0.1-0.4/255 at p99, and Medium stays near 2/255 there: not bad enough to keep Medium from the scaled case. High's 4-5/255 at p99, with single pixels up to 14-28/255, can show as light rims on text. The scale between picture and screen (the scaled crop's, or Core Animation's) softens what RCAS adds at 1:1, so the drawable's own numbers are larger than what reaches the screen. In the same draw RCAS costs about +0.01 ms at 1920x1080 on the M4 Max (the study's timing); scaled by the GPUs' sizes that is about +0.05-0.1 ms on the A20.
- **Check:** `t_present_crop`: Off within 1 of a CPU computation of `present.metal`'s `fs` at 1:1 and scaled (it was byte-identical to the classic queue's `fs`, which is gone); on a grey step each level moves the two pixels beside the edge further apart (64 | 192 becomes 61 | 195, 58 | 198, 54 | 202) and leaves every other pixel, grey and alpha as they were, also scaled 2x; magenta around a crop never reaches the picture, with either origin; a float source, a frame of the game's upscaler drawn by MetalFX and a picture MetalFX scaled are drawn as with Off; the in-game menu's pick reaches the live present alone. `make -C dm/gfx/tests live-menu` (`t_upscale_pick`: the levels, also under ThreadSanitizer and AddressSanitizer); `dm/ios/tests/test_live_menu_wiring.py` (the live row, the harness's stub, the copy under the menu's lock).
- **Found by:** a study of the present (2026-11-09; CAS was measured too, with 2-3 times RCAS's HUD overshoot).
- **Not checked:** on a device: the A20's cost and heat, and whether players prefer Low or Medium on real frames (the numbers come from a photo with synthetic HUD text). Games that sharpen already (Tears of the Kingdom is reported to use FSR1) would sharpen twice: that is why the default is Off. RCAS sharpens grain and dither too; FSR's noise detection (`FSR_RCAS_DENOISE`) was not tried.

### Edge smoothing in the present

- **Symptom:** a game that draws without anti-aliasing of its own shows stair-stepped, crawling edges: Mario Kart 8 Deluxe at its native 1080p docked, Super Mario Odyssey's dynamic 900p (Digital Foundry and Nintendo Life report both; not checked on a device here).
- **Cause:** nothing in the host smoothed edges. The game's picture reaches the present as the game drew it, and MetalFX and the bilinear scale enlarge its steps.
- **Fix:** the setting **Anti-aliasing** (`DM_POST_AA`: `off`, `edge`, `edge-sharp`; a game's default from its profile's `post_aa`, off without it; no profile sets it) runs Intel's Conservative Morphological Anti-Aliasing 2.3 (Apache-2.0) on the game's crop in the present, before MetalFX, the bilinear scale and [Sharpening](#sharpening-in-the-present) (`aa_encode`, called by `present_encode_m4` after `present_crop_rect`). `dm/gfx/msl/cmaa2.metal` is a hand port of `CMAA2.hlsl` for one configuration: one sample per pixel, the HIGH preset (edge threshold 0.07), luma edges computed in place, packed half-width edges. `edge-sharp` is CMAA2's own `CMAA2_EXTRA_SHARPNESS`, a function constant.
  - **The pass.** The crop is copied to the origin of a texture of the window texture's size (so a dynamic resolution's crop moves inside it with no new allocation), with the crop's last column and row repeated two texels past it. Then five dispatches run in one Metal 4 compute encoder with a barrier between each: edges, the first dispatch arguments, shape candidates (indirect), the second arguments, the deferred colour apply (indirect). The apply writes only the pixels it smooths, in place. The present's draw samples that texture, or MetalFX takes it as its input: with both on, the present makes one copy of the picture, as MetalFX alone made (`fx_encode`'s input texture is then not made at all). The drawable stays framebufferOnly. It applies in every graphics API and picture mode, on the Metal 4 present; the classic-queue present (a Mac without a Metal 4 queue, screenshots only) draws without it.
  - **The crop's size is a constant** (the present's constants buffer, `AA_UV`), not the texture's: colour reads are clamped to the crop's edge and an edge read past it is "no edge", so nothing outside the crop is read and a line stops at the crop's edge as upstream's stops at the screen's. Upstream relies on Direct3D returning 0 past the texture; this port therefore never takes the picture's border for an edge against black. Nothing is written outside the crop but the two padding columns and rows, which the apply keeps equal to the smoothed edge (MetalFX reads up to two texels past its content: [The MetalFX scaler under a moving crop](#the-metalfx-scaler-under-a-moving-crop)).
  - **Colour.** An sRGB swapchain is smoothed in linear light, as CMAA2 does on Direct3D: the texture is the `_sRGB` format, read decoded, and written through its UNORM view with the encode in the shader (CMAA2's `CMAA2_UAV_STORE_CONVERT_TO_SRGB`). A capture is handed the UNORM view (`present_offscreen_m4`), so the sRGB parent is found through `parentTexture`. An RGBA8 swapchain (Super Mario Odyssey's) is smoothed as stored. Other formats (10-bit, float) are shown without it, one `[RED]` line. Alpha is kept (upstream writes 0).
  - **Buffers.** The list heads and edges are buffers with device atomics rather than CMAA2's R32Uint and R8Uint textures: no texture atomics, which no device run here has exercised. Shape candidates are sized w*h/2 (a one-pixel checkerboard would reach w*h) and blend locations one per 2x2 quad, the most there can be. Deferred blend items start at a quarter of the pixels (CMAA2's reference sizing is w*h/2; the bench's dense foliage at 1080p wants 11.4 %), and a run that fills them makes them one per pixel from the next present on ([Edge smoothing's blend item list sized for every pixel](memory.md#edge-smoothings-blend-item-list-sized-for-every-pixel); `DM_PRESENT_AA_ITEMS=1` starts at one per pixel). Every append is clamped all the same, since Metal does not drop an out-of-bounds write as Direct3D drops one; a run that ran past a list says so once (`aa_note`), and what did not fit keeps its pixels. About 21 MB for 1920x1080 window textures (12.9 MB of lists, 7.9 MB of texture; 33 MB once the blend items are one per pixel), made at the first present that smooths and released once it has been off for 120 presents ([Present effects kept after they are turned off](memory.md#present-effects-kept-after-they-are-turned-off)); the first time, nothing on the GPU can still use older ones, so the present makes them without waiting for the presents in flight.
  - **Z shapes' blend items** are collected in threadgroup memory (768 per threadgroup) and applied after a barrier, as upstream does. A shape whose items do not fit is blended in place instead, and the slots it reserved below the store's end are marked empty, which the items' pass skips: upstream leaves them unwritten, and Metal's threadgroup memory holds whatever an earlier threadgroup or kernel left there, which would decode to a blend of some pixel in the crop with a colour far from it. Shapes blended in place are counted in the control buffer (`CTL_RAW_INPLACE`) for the tests.
  - **The setting** follows Sharpening's rule (`nvn_upscale_pick.h`): the first present reads `DM_POST_AA` (a copy under the in-game menu's lock, `front_env_copy`), else the profile's; the in-game menu's pick (`mtl_present_aa_set`, front_ios.m `dm_setting_live`) is taken by the live present at its next present. The seven pipelines are made together on a background queue (`aa_pipelines_start`) the first time anything asks for them, the in-game menu's pick or a present that smooths: compiling seven specialised kernels on the present thread, while it holds a drawable, would show as a hitch. The live present shows the picture without edge smoothing until they are made, a few presents later; a capture, which waits for the GPU anyway, waits for them. A capture has its own buffers, so screenshots and `DM_NVN_SHOT_OUT` captures show it as the screen does. `DM_POST_AA=ab` with `DM_AB=<frames>` alternates off and edge with the phases (read through `sw_mode_of`, so `edge` and `edge-sharp` are not taken for numbers). The 600-present GPU lines (`present_gpu_note`) have a bucket after edge smoothing for each of the three: the `[ab]` lines time the game's submissions and leave the present's command buffer out, so the alternation's cost is read there, as a difference within one run (in-game present times run under contention and overstate an isolated pass).
- **Cost** (M4 Max, `t_post_aa bench`: the copy and five dispatches, back to back, p50 of 40 over three runs; the study's synthetic scenes, typical: 14 large triangles and 3,000 1-2 pixel white strokes; foliage: 40,000 small triangles and the same strokes):

  | Size | Typical | Foliage |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1600x900 | 0.07-0.11 ms | 0.13-0.16 ms |
  | 1920x1080 | 0.07-0.12 ms | 0.16-0.17 ms |
  | 2868x1320 | 0.11-0.16 ms | 0.20-0.26 ms |

  The study's spirv-cross port measured 0.091 / 0.179 ms at 1080p. The present's command buffer runs on the game's queue after the frame's work (every present encoder opens with a barrier after all of the queue's stages), so in a GPU-bound frame, such as Breath of the Wild outdoors on the phone, this adds to the frame time; it does not only shrink the margin before the display. Scaled by the iPhone 17 Pro Max's MetalFX present times, the A20 is estimated at 0.2-0.6 ms at 1080p, up to about 1.2 ms on dense foliage: unmeasured.
- **The HUD:** the pass runs after the game drew its HUD, so the HUD and text are smoothed with the scene. On `t_post_aa`'s white 1-2 pixel strokes on dark, which cross one another, `edge` changed 45 % of the strokes' pixels by 10.3/255 on average, `edge-sharp` 45 % by 5.1/255; the study's strokes over a scene gave 3.0/255 (CMAA2), 4.4 (SMAA 1x) and 7.1 (an FXAA-class filter). That is why no profile turns it on: games heavy in UI (Persona 5 Royal's bold 1080p UI over 810p 3D, Persona 4 Golden, 13 Sentinels) lose most. A hook before the HUD (a game's last scene draw, found per game with `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP`) is the planned second phase. Breath of the Wild very likely smooths with FXAA already (Cemu's Wii U pack), and Mario Kart 8 Deluxe, Super Mario Odyssey and Breath of the Wild ship agl's `agl_technique_pfx`, which may hold an AA filter: a profile default waits for a capture showing none. Temporal shimmer (grass, fences) is not touched by a one-frame filter.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests check_t_post_aa` (under `MTL_DEBUG_LAYER=1`, then with `MTL_SHADER_VALIDATION=1` too), on a synthetic aliased picture: off byte-identical to the source; edge and edge-sharp change no pixel whose 3x3 neighbourhood is one colour, keep alpha, and bring a 1:5 half-plane's edge from 0.28 to 0.04-0.05 pixels RMS off its line; the HUD bounds above (under 12/255 and 7/255 on average, under half the pixels); a 201x147 crop at 13,7 of a 256x192 texture byte-identical to the crop alone, whatever lies around it; the live present's picture byte-identical to the capture's, its texture kept when the crop moves; on black and white the sRGB swapchain's result is the RGBA8 one encoded (never darker, within 3/255); a flat picture gives no candidates and no items; capacities of 64 / 16 and of 48 overflow, are said, and still touch only edges; a threadgroup store of Z shapes' items cut to 1, 20 and 40 (`g_aa_test_slm`) blends shapes in place, with the store filled with a stray item first (`g_aa_test_stray`), and gives the whole store's blend item count (a slot read before anything wrote it would add the stray one) and its picture within 1/255; the first live present that smooths does not wait for the kernels; a float swapchain is skipped; MetalFX after edge smoothing byte-identical to MetalFX of the smoothed picture handed in as a plain source, for the whole texture and for a crop (its padding smoothed too), and the live scaler makes no input texture of its own; the in-game menu's pick reaches the live present alone; `DM_POST_AA`, the profile's default and `ab` with the phases. Against the study's spirv-cross port of the same HLSL (scratch harness, RGBA8 at 256x192 and 1920x1080, typical and foliage) the picture is byte-identical except within 7 pixels of the border, where the border handling differs. `make -C dm/gfx/tests live-menu` (`aa_parse`), `dm/ios/tests/test_live_menu_wiring.py`, `test_launcher_settings.py`, `test_game_profile.py` (`post_aa` takes off, edge and edge-sharp; no profile sets it).
- **Found by:** a study of anti-aliasing for every game (2026-11-09), phase 1: the present path.
- **Not checked:** on a device: the A20's cost, heat and missed vsyncs (with `DM_POST_AA=ab` and `DM_AB=600`, the present's GPU lines give the difference in one run), whether texture-free device atomics and indirect dispatches behave as on the M4 Max, and the quality on real game frames (the numbers above are synthetic). The swapchain formats of the owned games were not surveyed; only 8-bit ones are smoothed.

### Frame generation

- **Symptom:** a game held at 30 fps (the 30 fps cap, a phone's heat) shows 30 pictures a second on a 60 Hz screen, though NX 18 already runs the game's temporal upscaler, where depth and motion exist.
- **Cause:** nothing generated frames; the present showed each game frame for its two ticks.
- **Fix (X2.0 item C6, `DM_FRAMEGEN=1` in env.txt, off by default, no launcher row):** `nvn_framegen.h` (the decision and the tick order, pure code) and `nvn_framegen.inc` (the Metal side, included by `nvn_mtl.m`, whose hooks each ask `fg_on()` first). At the 30 fps cap on the 60 Hz display link, the tick that takes frame n shows G(n-1, n), the midpoint from MetalFX's frame interpolator (`MTL4FXFrameInterpolator`), through the same present pass as every frame (edge smoothing, MetalFX or bilinear scaling, Sharpening, picture fit, the touch controls drawn into the frame); the next tick, the one the 30 fps hold kept before, shows F(n). Depth and motion are copied into a ring of host textures where the game's upscaler runs, so the game may reuse its own:
  - NVN2's DLSS on MetalFX's temporal scaler (`dlss_eval`): its depth, its motion with the motion scale (input pixels pointing to the previous frame, the interpolator's convention at scale 1), the depth direction and the reset. Motion at the output's size (no `MVLowRes`) and the bilinear stand-in are left out. This is the path that generates with real motion, and the one to judge first (Star Fox).
  - The NDSR networks (`DM_GAME_UPSCALER_KEYS`, `up_mfx_named`: every NDSR pack's networks since the port of upstream 9e0ddeebd, agl's `agl_ndsr`, `agl_ndsr_vr`, `agl_ndsr_vr_light` and the others; the game's shader or MetalFX with `DM_UPSCALER=metalfx`): of a network's dispatches only the upscale has an input and an output rectangle in its push constants (the others hold floats there), and only that one is taken. Ocarina of Time plays with `agl_ndsr_vr_light` (`fa3033d05c0a41d6`, 1536x864 into 2048x1152). The scene depth is the newest depth attachment of the frame that covers the input rectangle in its shape, reversed when cleared to 0 (a lazily backed depth texture, `DM_NVN_DEPTH_LAZY`, is copied as it is: its unmapped tiles read 0). The network gives no motion, so this path generates only with `DM_FRAMEGEN_MOTION=zero`. The interpolator does not estimate motion of its own: with zero motion the probe's square stays near one of the two frames (38.6 of 40 px off the midpoint), and G is close to a blend of the two pictures. Host-built motion for Ocarina of Time is the next step.
  - The colour is the presented frame (the HUD included: H0), from the present snapshot ring, which `DM_FRAMEGEN` turns on. A crop that is part of the window texture is copied out, interpolated at its size, and G is copied back to the crop's place in a texture of the window texture's size, so the present pass sees G as it sees F (the MetalFX present scaler's input is the window texture's size, and the crop line counts no move). The interpolator keeps the previous colour itself (`requiresPrevColorTexture` NO) and is made off the display link's thread (0.1-0.5 s).
  - Generation is off, with a reason a status line every 600 frames counts, for: a movie (`dm_movie_playing`: the SDK's movie library or an nn::codec video decoder handed out a picture in the last half second; Ocarina of Time's opening is an nn::codec video), the front end's menu (`dm_front_menu_now`: the iPhone's in-game menu and the Mac's menu-bar menus, half a second past the close), a period other than two ticks (a 60 fps frame, a hitch at three), a link other than 60 Hz or its rescue, a screenshot, no upscale event in the frame, a capture overwritten before its tick, a change of sizes or formats, missing depth or motion. The first generated frame after any of them resets the interpolator's history; so does a frame whose ticket does not follow the last one decided on (frames dropped as old, shown by the present thread in a rescue, or a pending F dropped), which is first shown real ("no history").
  - **Power first.** It steps aside, real frames only, at thermal state serious or worse and in Low Power Mode, whatever its setting, both until they have been gone 30 s (the thermal cap's own calm, so a thermal state at the edge does not turn it on and off), on any rung of the heat ladder (`nvn_heat_rung` in `hle_nvn.c`: the rungs on, which the ladder publishes after each 1 s sample and frame generation reads with one atomic load; 85%, 70%, effects, 40 fps and the 30 fps heat hold all count, each through its restore wait, so it stays aside until the heat hold's backed-off calm of 30-240 s has passed, not only its own 30 s, and also in a game the host does not pace; Low Power Mode's 30 fps cause is not a rung, as frame generation reads Low Power Mode itself whatever the Low Power Mode at 30 fps setting), and in the Battery Saver preset (`DM_PRESET=battery`). It reads them itself (`fg_power_why`), the thermal state, Low Power Mode and `DM_PRESET` once a second, with no side effects: it never samples the thermal cap's hold (`thermal_capped`, which logs and reports a hold even for a game the host does not pace). On the Mac, whose host reports neither heat nor Low Power Mode, it reads the system's own (`NSProcessInfo`), so a MacBook in Low Power Mode or hot steps aside as a phone does. With frame generation on, the launcher sets `DM_PRESET` (the preset the settings are: `battery`, `balanced`, `quality`, `performance`, or `custom`; never with `DM_FRAMEGEN` unset, when it is nobody's to read, so the Mac's start without its window builds no settings model) at every start: Play, a start without the screen (Skip this screen next time, `DM_LAUNCHER=0`, a home screen shortcut, a program handing over) and the Mac's start without its window (`LauncherModel.exportPreset`, `dm_mac_preset_env`); the iPhone's in-game menu sets it again after a preset change (a chip, L / R, X, Back, Undo) or a hand change to a preset row, under the menu's lock (`gameMenuPresetEnv`, `dm_setting_env`), so it steps aside or comes back within a second; an env file's `DM_PRESET` stays. The status line counts these as "heat or Low Power Mode" and "Battery Saver". While it steps aside it copies no depth or motion at the upscale (`fg_cap_wanted`; the first frame back captures again and its generated frame resets the interpolator's history), and the 30 fps cap gets its 30 Hz power-saving link back (`fg_keeps_60hz`).
  - **The 60 Hz link only where it can generate.** The 30 fps cap keeps its 30 Hz link until the game's upscaler has given a capture (a game whose upscaler gives none, DLSS without low-resolution motion or NDSR without `DM_FRAMEGEN_MOTION=zero`, never leaves it), on a device without MetalFX's frame interpolator, and while MetalFX made none for the capture's sizes and formats. Fullscreen on a variable-refresh screen with no fixed-rate 60 Hz mode would turn the 60 Hz link into the 120 Hz one, where nothing is generated: the screen is asked for 60 Hz first, and without such a mode the cap keeps its 30 Hz link and no depth or motion is copied (`link_sync` clears `g_fg_fps30` there; it comes to that fallback only after a capture, so a window or a fixed-rate 60 Hz mode generates again at the next frame). Movies, the menu and frames without an upscale keep the 60 Hz link once captures came, so generation comes back on the next frame with no change of rate.
  - **Memory.** What it made goes again when it is not in use, and is made again on demand (the next capture, the next generated frame; the interpolator off the link's thread, its frames real meanwhile, and only for a frame that would be generated once it is there, so a long menu, movie or 30 Hz stretch with captures coming does not make and let go one over and over): after 300 frames without a generated frame (the display link's side: G's output, crop and window textures, command allocators and the interpolator) or without a capture (the capture ring and the zero-motion texture), at a memory pressure warning or critical while it is not generating (`fg_trim` from the trim: the ring at once, the link's side at its next real frame; a generated frame before it takes the request back, as generation is using it all again), and all of it going to the background, where the link calls back nothing. Each waits for the generated frames on the GPU: going to the background, where the fence has passed, a generated frame a display-link callback committed after the drain looked is waited for up to 50 ms (under `g_fg_res_mu`, so none is encoded meanwhile), and one still there keeps the ring until the next submission's fence and the link's side until its next real frame. The live present's MetalFX scaler and edge smoothing that a generated frame's present pass ran are kept until that frame has completed (`fg_effect_busy` in `present_fx_idle`), as for a real present. Present snapshots stay on for the session.
  - **`DM_PRESENT_REPEAT`.** Frame generation turns off when `DM_PRESENT_REPEAT=1` (only `1` counts; a log line says so): [the present repeat](#the-present-repeat-on-every-tick-dm_present_repeat), a change of its own, fills the same empty ticks.
  - Every per-ticket record (`g_pq_done`, the present allocators, `g_pres`, the link's tick record behind `@link_target_ms`, the frame step's screen readings) follows F; G has command allocators of its own. Where generation starts or stops, the real frames' on-screen intervals read three ticks and then one, though a picture was on screen at every tick: the one-tick shift of each F shown after its G is recorded per ticket (`mtl_presented_shift`) and taken out where `hle_nvn.c` judges them (`pres_shifted_ms`: the frame step's screen verdict, the dropped frames of the window line, Vitals' gap, `@presented_ms`). A pending F is shown before the hold and before a new frame, by the present thread after a rescue, and is dropped (its ticket completed) on a suspend or a silent link, also when the stall drops the frames queued after it (counted with them, in the `fallback dropped` count too).
  - **Vitals.** The fps field counts the frames the game rendered; generated pictures the system shows (`mtl_framegen_frames`, counted from their presented handlers) are added beside them, so while frame generation produces frames it reads `60 fps, 30 rendered` (`nvn_vitals_fps_text`, also the in-game menu's header), never a bare 60. The total is at most one generated picture per frame rendered and the link's ticks (`nvn_vitals_shown10`: the generated pictures' window edge falls apart from the rendered frames', so a steady second holds 29 or 31 of them), and the reader holds it with the frame rate (`nvn_vitals_shown_step`: it moves when the frame rate shown moves; while that holds it stays within ±2, the frame rate's ±1 for a frame and its generated picture; and it stays between one more than the frame rate shown and twice it): a steady scene reads 60 every second, never 59 or 61, and a window that loses one frame to a hitch still reads `60 fps, 30 rendered`, never `58 fps, 30 rendered`. GPU busy counts the generated frames' command buffers too.
- **Cost:** off (the default), nothing: each hook is one cached load of `fg_on()`; no texture, interpolator or command buffer is made and no per-frame work runs; the link, the snapshots and the 30 Hz link are as before. On: F reaches the screen one tick (16.7 ms) later, or none, by the game's phase against the link (below); per generated frame the interpolation and a second present pass (1.3-1.9 ms of GPU in Ocarina of Time's 1536x864 → 2048x1152 on an M4 Max, 4-7 ms p50-p95 at 960x540 → 1920x1080 in the probe), a depth and a motion copy at each upscale (none while it steps aside), present snapshots (two window-sized textures, for the session), the ring's depth and motion copies (4 of each), the interpolator and its output (and, for a crop that is part of its texture, a crop-sized input and a window-sized output), each let go when not in use (Memory, above). G's GPU time must fit the link's lead (16 ms at latency 1 in fullscreen on a fixed-rate screen). No telemetry: everything it measures goes to log.txt.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> t_framegen` (the decision, the power reasons, the tick order and the real frames' intervals less the shift), `t_framegen_probe` (what MetalFX's interpolator takes), `check_t_framegen_dlss` (DLSS end to end through the display-link callback with and without the encoding thread and with a crop; the link record and the shift of F, and `hle_nvn.c`'s screen intervals less it (`pres_shifted_ms`: three ticks to a shifted F and one after it read two, an unshifted pair and frame generation off read `a - b` exactly), the menu, a one-tick period, a suspend, the display link's stall with a real frame pending and a newer one queued (both dropped and counted), Battery Saver and heat with its calm (real frames, no captures, the 30 Hz link), a rung of the heat ladder alone (`DM_HEAT_FORCE` for the ladder, its own reads calm: aside through the heat hold's calm wait, on the ladder's clock moved ahead, and back once no rung is left; it fails with a hook returning 0), the Mac's heat and Low Power Mode from `NSProcessInfo` (its getters replaced in the test), the 30 Hz link before any capture and in variable-refresh fullscreen (no captures there), edge smoothing kept for a generated frame on the GPU, what it made let go after a long step-aside, at a memory trim and going to the background, and made again; a long menu with captures coming lets it go once and makes no interpolator meanwhile (the log lines counted); a trim's request taken back by a generated frame, and one made before a generated frame lapsing; going to the background with a generated frame on the GPU (the ring behind the next submission's fence) and with one that completes within the wait (all at once); `repeat`: off with `DM_PRESENT_REPEAT=1`, on with another value; `DM_PRESENT_SNAP=0` throughout, so a profile's `present_snap` decides nothing), `t_vitals` (the fps field, the cap and the smoothing; the shown count held through a one-frame dip and never above twice the rendered count), `test_launcher_storage.py` and the Duo harness's preset chooser (`DM_PRESET` at Play, at a start without the screen and after an in-game change).
- **Measured** (the prototype: `t_framegen_probe`, M4 Max, macOS 27.3; another agent's game host prewarming on the same Mac): every colour format a window texture has (RGBA16F, RGBA8, RGBA8 sRGB, BGRA8, RGB10A2, RG11B10F), depth D32F, D32F_S8, D16, R32F and motion RG16F, RG32F make an interpolator. On a moving square the midpoint is exact with DLSS's motion convention, also with the content rectangle offset inside larger depth and motion textures (dynamic resolution); zero motion lands 38.6, 20.2 px off the midpoint for 80, 40 px of motion. After a history reset the reset encode and the two after it give the current picture, not a midpoint. A static HUD box over the motion keeps a mean error of 0.057 interpolated with the scene (H0) and 0 with a UI texture (the full picture composited, or a UI layer with alpha): the case for capturing the picture before the HUD (H1), not done yet. GPU time for 960x540 → 1920x1080: p50 4.0-5.1 ms, p95 6.9-11.3 ms over 300 encodes in two sessions with other hosts running, on top of the second present pass. `t_framegen_dlss` (and `crop`) drives `dlss_eval` with synthetic inputs through the display-link callback: G then F on consecutive ticks, the midpoint within 2 px after the warm-up, the menu, a one-tick period and a suspend.
- **Measured in a game** (the prototype, before it came onto X2.0: Ocarina of Time, M4 Max, 2026-11-11, the `kokiri-deck` route of the cloud save, window 85-130 s, `DM_FPS_TARGET=30`; the screen was locked, so the link was `DM_LINK_SIM=1` and presented times are not the screen's; one or two other game hosts and Metal compiles ran on the Mac): the game held 30.0 fps with and without generation (frame p99 34.3-40.1 ms), its own GPU time p50 10.2-11.7 ms either way. With `DM_FRAMEGEN=1 DM_FRAMEGEN_MOTION=zero` every frame of play got a generated frame (2,146 of 3,600 by frame 3,600, the rest the title and loading: "no upscale"); the targets of all pictures came every 16.67 ms (p99 and max 16.67, none over 25 ms) against 33.33 ms without; each picture's GPU work ended 14.4-14.8 ms (p50) and at least 10.1 ms before its target. The generated frame's command buffer (crop copy, interpolation 1536x864 → 2048x1152, the 0.04 ms bilinear present pass) took p50 1.25-1.67 ms, p95 1.31-1.94 ms per 33.3 ms game frame; the interpolator is made in 3-7 ms when its pipelines are cached. Game enqueue to target display rose from 42-43 ms (30 Hz link) to 58-60 ms in the last minute of each run, but stayed at 42 ms for the 1,200-1,500 presents before it with every frame generated (G1a, G1b, M1): the cost is one tick or none depending on whether the game's enqueue falls just before the tick that takes it or just before the tick that shows the pending F, a phase nothing sets yet. `DM_UPSCALER=metalfx` gave the same numbers (on the prototype's base it replaced nothing in this game's play). Pictures (`DM_FRAMEGEN_SHOT`): standing still, G is within 0.2 of the average of its neighbours (mean absolute error, 0-255); the HUD (hearts, minimap) stays sharp and in place with the scene moving behind it. In fast camera turns (neighbours 17 apart on average) and walking, zero motion gives a soft G with ghosts: a double of the house and missing signposts in the background, Navi and the sparkle trail doubled, a smeared strip at the screen edge the camera turns towards. The pause menu has no upscale, so its frames stay real; the opening movie counts as "movie".
- **Open:** a DLSS game on a real screen (Star Fox on the Mac first: no Mac build of it was at hand when this came onto X2.0), then the phone on battery: generation adds GPU work, so judge W as well as smoothness. On a locked Mac the system's link calls back nothing (`DM_LINK_SIM=1` measures pacing on the link's targets instead). Host-built motion for the NDSR path (Ocarina of Time: zero motion ghosts in camera turns, so it is not ready to show with it). The phase that decides whether F costs a tick. The pre-HUD picture and H1 / a difference mask (H2), the 120 Hz link, generation at hold 2 under a 60 cap.

### The present repeat on every tick (`DM_PRESENT_REPEAT`)

- **Symptom:** in Breath of the Wild's heavy scenes on the iPhone the GPU clock drops and stays low while frames miss the screen; at 40 fps on the 60 Hz display link about a third of the ticks present nothing ([the GPU clock stuck at a third of its speed](frame-rate.md#the-gpu-clock-stuck-at-a-third-of-its-speed-with-the-phone-cool-the-clock-rescue); whether iOS's performance controller reads the present cadence or the frames' completion is unknown).
- **Cause (to test):** a tick without a new frame presents nothing, so iOS sees an uneven present cadence; upstream's idea is that a present on every tick keeps iOS's frame pacing and the GPU clock steady.
- **Fix (an X2.0 rider, off by default, no launcher row: `DM_PRESENT_REPEAT=1` in env.txt, kept in player builds):** `nvn_present_repeat.inc`, included by `nvn_mtl.m`. On a display-link tick that takes no frame (an empty tick, or one held for a long present interval) the last real frame is presented again on that tick's drawable, through the same present pass (edge smoothing, MetalFX or bilinear scaling, Sharpening, picture fit, the touch controls).
  - **The snapshot, never the game's texture.** The source is the last frame's present snapshot (`DM_PRESENT_REPEAT=1` turns snapshots on, a ring of 2, as frame generation does), a host texture the game never draws into. The slot's next copy is the frame a ring's length later, which can start only once the queue holds that many frames less one, so a repeat is made only while `g_pq_in + 2 <= ticket + ring`, and `present_enqueue` waits while one is being encoded (`g_rep_busy`); the copy's queue barrier then orders it after the repeat. With the ring of 2 that is every empty tick; a held tick with the next frame already waiting is repeated only with `DM_PRESENT_SNAP=3` or more.
  - **Not a present.** `g_present_n`, `g_pq_done`, the presented times (`g_pres`), the hold and the display link's line (the tick still counts as empty or held) are the real frames'. A repeat has three command allocators of its own (one still on the GPU passes the tick over) and counts in `g_pq_inflight`, so a suspend's drain waits for it. None while presents are discarded (going to the background), after a GPU loss, in the rescue, before the link has shown a frame with a snapshot, or once the present thread or a drop took a newer ticket. Its touch-control feedback acknowledges as a present's does.
  - **The 30 Hz link.** It repeats on the link's own ticks: on the 30 Hz link (or the virtual ticks of `link_range`) that is 30 a second.
  - **Log.** One line when it is on; a status line every 600 repeats counts them and the ticks passed over (the ring rule, a busy allocator, anything else). Frame generation is off with it.
- **Cost:** off (the default), nothing: the link asks `rep_on()`, one cached load, only on an empty or held tick and after a real present; `present_fx_idle` asks only while an effect's textures exist; `present_enqueue`'s wait reads `g_rep_busy`, always 0. On: the snapshot copy per frame, and one present pass per repeated tick (about 0.2 ms of GPU at the phone's sizes, estimated, not measured).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_present_repeat` (the display link's callback called by hand: a tick before any frame presents nothing; a tick without a new frame shows the last frame again from its snapshot after the game drew into its texture again; not counted as a present; a held tick passed over with a ring of 2 and repeated with 3; nothing while discarded, in the rescue or after a dropped newer ticket; off, unset and with another value, nothing changes and no snapshots; both encode-thread modes, under Metal API validation), `t_framegen_dlss repeat` (frame generation off with it).
- **Open:** the A/B on the phone (Breath of the Wild's heavy area, on battery, same route): the GPU clock probe, fps and watts with and without it.

### The iPhone's drawable at the picture's render size

- **Symptom:** the iPhone's and iPad's drawables are larger than the picture they show: 2868x1320 in landscape Picture Stretch on an iPhone 18 Pro Max (15 MB each, three of them) for a 1920x1080 picture, and larger than the screen with Picture Fill.
- **Cause:** `DmGameView` (`front_ios.m`) gave the drawable the view's pixels, which only MetalFX's scaler fills with more than a bilinear scale ([The present upscales through MetalFX](#the-present-upscales-through-metalfx)). With Upscaling Standard, and in Picture Stretch and Ultrawide, where `fx_wanted` leaves MetalFX out (its spatial scaler is made for a uniform scale), the present pass drew the picture into those pixels bilinearly.
- **Fix:** the present reports the picture's render size (`front_picture_size` in `front.h`, `present_render_size` in `nvn_mtl.m`): the crop's pixels in the picture's shape (`present_shape`), as many as the crop has across or down (`present_size_step`). It used to be the window texture's, which gave the Switch 2 titles that present a 1920x1080 crop of a 3840x2160 texture (Fortune's Weave, Bananza, Star Fox, Mario Kart World, Ocarina of Time) a 3840x2160 drawable, twice the picture's pixels each way. A dynamic resolution moves the crop every few frames, so the size is the largest crop seen since the window texture last took a new size (a mode change, itself counted once it has held for 30 presents; a game presenting two texture sizes in turn keeps the larger of their crops): smaller crops resize nothing. The first present reports it at once, a new size once it has held for 30 presents. Where MetalFX cannot run, the front end gives the drawable no more pixels than that (`dm_drawable_size` in `DmLayout.h`): in Stretch each direction on its own (1920x1080), otherwise the view's shape at the scale that keeps every pixel of the picture. Core Animation scales the layer to the view (its default resize gravity and linear filter), as the present's bilinear pass did, and the present pass draws the picture 1:1.
