Upscaling MetalFX keeps the view's pixels in Fit and Fill, and in Stretch to a shape within `fx_wanted`'s 1 % of the picture's (`dm_layout_fx_shape`, `DM_FX_SHAPE_TOLERANCE`), which a resizable window (Stage Manager, an iPad app on a Mac) can reach: the layout counts 0.1 % off as stretched, and a drawable at the picture's pixels there would have left MetalFX nothing to scale. A pick of the in-game menu's Upscaling or Picture rows, and a new render size, lay the view out again. The view's points stay, and with them the layout and the touches (`layout.game`); a TV's 1920 cap (`tv_attach`) still applies. The whole-window capture (`grab_present`) is made at the drawable's size, so it is now the picture's size there. `front end: drawable WxH, ...` logs each change. `DM_DRAWABLE_NATIVE=1` keeps the view's pixels everywhere, as before.
- **Note:** while the touch controls are drawn into the frame the drawable keeps the screen's pixels, so their pictures land 1:1: [Touch controls over the game's layer cost a refresh of latency](frame-rate.md#touch-controls-over-the-games-layer-cost-a-refresh-of-latency). The picture's render size applies again while a controller is in use, in the Tap layout, on a TV, while an offline HTML page shows and with `DM_TOUCH_DRAW=layer`.
- **Check:** `t_present_crop` (the render size of textures and crops in a shape, `present_size_step`'s hold through smaller crops, a larger one, two texture sizes in turn and a mode change, and that a present of a 40x32 crop of a 64x36 texture reports 57x32); the Duo harness's `testGameViewDrawableTakesThePictureSizeWhereMetalFXDoesNotRun` (Upscaling Standard: the picture's size; MetalFX: the view's pixels; back at once; the view's frame unchanged; MetalFX's shape test at 0.5 % and 2 % off) in the Simulator.
- **Found on:** [Persona 5 Royal](../../dm/games/01005CA01580E000/README.md) and [Super Mario Odyssey](../../dm/games/0100000000010000/README.md) (iPhone 18 Pro Max logs in Stretch, where `metalfx` did nothing).
- **Not checked:** on a device: the memory and present time saved, and the picture against the old drawable (Core Animation's filter in place of the present's); for the crop-sized drawable, a picture check on a Switch 2 title (a hot Mario Kart World for dynamic resolution) and whether iOS still sends the layer straight to the display rather than compositing it.

### The drawable at the picture's render size with the touch controls in the frame (`DM_PRESENT_DRAWABLE`)

- **Symptom:** while the touch controls show, the drawable keeps the screen's pixels whatever the picture renders at: `front end: drawable 2868x1320, the view's pixels` with `present: picture render size 1920x1080 …` on an iPhone 18 Pro Max in landscape Fit, three drawables of 15 MB and a present pass that scales the picture up bilinearly into them.
- **Cause:** [The iPhone's drawable at the picture's render size](#the-iphones-drawable-at-the-pictures-render-size) leaves the touch-controls canvas at the screen's pixels so the controls' pictures land 1:1, and a canvas of the picture's pixels would need the controls laid out at another scale.
- **Fix:** `DM_PRESENT_DRAWABLE=game` (`front_ios.m`; `touch_drawable_game`, `touch_drawable_scale` in `touch_draw.h`) gives the canvas one scale, just enough that the picture's rectangle on it gets every pixel of the picture in the direction that needs more, never above the screen's: 2347x1080 at 2.455 pixels a point for a 1920x1080 picture in landscape Fit or Stretch (a portrait split's picture needs more than the screen has across, so it keeps the screen's). `DmGameView` sizes the drawable at the canvas's points times that scale (`drawableScale`), rounded as the gamepad rounds its frame's canvas, and the gamepad pictures and lays out the controls at it (`dm_gamepad_touch_draw`), so the frame fits the drawable (`touch_frame_fits`) and each quad, normalized on the canvas, lands where its control's points are. Core Animation scales the layer to the screen, controls and all. Touches are unchanged: the gamepad view takes them in points. Edge smoothing (CMAA2) and sharpening work on the picture before the present, as before. Where MetalFX upscales to the screen's pixels (`DM_PRESENT_UPSCALE`, the in-game menu's Upscaling, in a shape MetalFX takes) the switch stands down and the drawable keeps the screen's pixels for the scaler to fill, as it does under `DM_DRAWABLE_NATIVE=1` and on a TV; without touch controls in the frame the drawable takes the picture's render size by default. `front end: DM_PRESENT_DRAWABLE=game …` says which, once per change, and `front end: drawable WxH at S pixels a point, … (DM_PRESENT_DRAWABLE=game) …` the size chosen. Unset or `screen`: as before.
- **Note:** the controls are pictured at the drawable's pixels and scaled up with the picture (about 1.22 times in landscape), so their edges are a little softer than at 1:1. Screenshots and captures read the game's picture, not the drawable, and the launcher (the Gate) has no game view: neither changes.
- **Check:** `t_touch_draw` (`dm/host/tests`): the switch's values, the scale in Fit, Stretch, a portrait split and before a render size, a 2347x1080 drawable that the frame fits (and the screen's 2868x1320 that it does not), the picture 1:1 on it, and a control's quad at its points times the scale in its own shape.
- **Not checked:** a device A/B, which is the switch's purpose: whether iOS still sends a scaled layer's drawables straight to the display (the Metal HUD with `MTL_HUD_ELEMENTS=layersize,presentdelay,…`: the layer size and the present delay against unset), the power and memory saved, and the controls' look at 1.22 times.

### The NVN present crops by the vi layer crop

- **Symptom:** in handheld mode the picture fills only two thirds of the screen's width and height, in the bottom-left corner (the top-left with the upper-left origin), the rest black.
- **Cause:** the game keeps its 1920x1080 window textures in handheld mode, draws 1280x720 into them and crops through `vi::SetLayerCrop`, never `nvnWindowSetCrop`. The host recorded the layer crop (`hle_vi.c`) only for the Vulkan present (Swapchain pre-transform and layer crop); the NVN present read only the window's crop and showed the whole texture.
- **Fix:** the NVN present shows the window's own crop when the game set one, else the vi layer crop (`present_crop` in `hle_nvn.c`); on the Switch both become the crop of the buffer queued to the layer. A layer crop that does not fit the window texture is reported once in red and the whole texture is shown, as before.
- **Check:** `t_present_crop`: the window's crop wins, the layer crop is used without one, one larger than the texture is ignored, and a real `nvnQueuePresentTexture` queues the layer crop (that check fails with the old call). On an iPhone 15 Pro in handheld mode Link's Awakening's picture fills the screen's height.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md#rendering) (1.1.0, handheld mode on the iPhone).

### A crop only picks texels; the picture has the layer's shape

- **Symptom:** a scene turns narrow, with black bars at its sides, while the swapchain or window textures keep their size; circles come out as tall ovals. The log has `vi::SetLayerCrop 0,0 1200x960` and `present upscaling: ... 1200x960 → 1800x1440` in a 2560x1440 window.
- **Cause:** the present used one rectangle for two things: which texels to read and the shape to show them in. On the Switch the compositor stretches a buffer's crop to the layer, so a game can draw an anamorphic picture into a crop of another shape: 1200x960 of a 1920x1080 swapchain docked, 800x640 handheld. The host fitted the crop's own 5:4 into the canvas, and reported 5:4 to the front ends.
- **Fix:** the crop still selects the source texels, for every graphics API, and `present_shape` (`nvn_mtl.m`) gives the shape: the display's 16:9, or a sized layer's (`vi::CreateLayer` with a size, `mtl_present_layer`). A screen taller than 16:9 (`DM_SCREEN_ASPECT`, `mtl_present_screen`) counts only for a texture of its shape, since only builds with screen-shape stubs render it. Both encoders, MetalFX, the screen-sized captures and the shape reported to the front ends (`front_picture_shape`) use it, so a canvas of another shape still gets bars around a 16:9 picture and clicks map onto the picture as drawn. `vi::SetLayerScalingMode` stays ignored: the games seen set mode 2 or none.
- **Check:** `t_present_crop`: a 5:4 crop fills a 16:9 canvas and keeps bars on a 16:10 one on the Metal 4 present, a sized layer's shape, both `DM_SCREEN_ASPECT` cases and the shape a real present reports.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Galaxy 2](../../dm/games/0100FD8022DAA000/README.md#rendering) ([issue #949](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/949)).

### A picture shown smaller than it is drawn: stair-stepped edges from the present's single tap

- **Symptom:** a game whose output is larger than the rectangle it is shown in (a Switch 2 game's 2560x1440 docked output on a 1080p screen, or in a window smaller than its output) has stair-stepped, irregular edges on lines and outlines that crawl as they move, more than the game's own frame shows at full size.
- **Cause:** the present pass took one bilinear sample of the swapchain texture per pixel of the drawable (`present.metal`). Shown smaller, each pixel covers more than one texel, and one tap weighs the texels by where the pixel's centre falls: at 2560 → 1920 a texel counts fully in one pixel and a quarter in the next, so edge steps come out uneven, and past 2:1 texels are skipped entirely. One-texel-wide stripes at 3:2 came out as columns of 64 and 191 (a coarser pattern of their own) instead of an even grey.
- **Fix:** general, `fs` in `dm/gfx/msl/present.metal`: a picture shown at its size or larger still takes one bilinear tap (the bytes as before, and MetalFX's upscale is drawn 1:1). When minified, each minified axis is filtered over the pixel's footprint with a non-negative radius-1 tent kernel stretched by `min(step, 3)` (texels per pixel from `dfdx` / `dfdy` of the linear texture coordinate), while any non-minified axis keeps one bilinear tap. Adjacent texels are paired into one hardware bilinear fetch at their weighted centroid, held in registers (`float3`, avoiding stack spills on Apple GPUs), clamped to the crop's first and last texel centres: at most 4 fetches per pixel up to 2:1 and at most 9 up to 3:1, with zero negative-lobe sign-crossing error or undershoot. Both presents (`present_encode`, `present_encode_m4`), `DM_NVN_SHOT_OUT` screenshots and whole-window captures use it.
- **Cost:** standalone batched pass on Apple M6 (hot GPU clock, median of 25 batches): 2560x1440 → 2560x1440 unchanged (0.046 ms), 2560x1440 → 1920x1080 0.137–0.293 ms (one tap 0.027–0.072 ms), 2560x1440 → 1280x720 0.061–0.083 ms (one tap 0.013–0.016 ms), 3840x2160 → 1920x1080 0.138–0.311 ms, 3840x2160 → 1280x720 0.388–0.500 ms.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_present_crop` holds the 1:1 and upscaled presents byte-identical to before (the single bilinear tap). Upstream NXNative fb3ca11af checks the minified path against a per-texel CPU tent model within 1 of 255 (3:2, 4:3, 2:1, 5:1, lower-left origin, anisotropic, cropped, Metal 4 present); those checks need test helpers NX 18 does not have and are not ported yet.
- **Found on:** Nintendo Switch Sports Resort (upstream; no NX 18 profile), docked output 2560x1440 shown fullscreen on a 2560x1080 screen (a 1920x1080 picture) and in a 1280x720 window ([PR #2492](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2492)).

### Setting the same targets again continues the pass

- **Symptom:** one-draw passes on one target, hundreds of times a frame, with the same attachments each time (a `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` frame: `load` and `store` of the whole target in every line); a low frame rate that follows the target's size.
- **Cause:** `nvnCommandBufferSetRenderTargets` ended the host's pass even when it named the targets of the open pass. On the Switch the call costs nothing; on a tile-based GPU each pass stores its attachments, and the next one starts again.
- **Fix:** the call goes on in the open pass when the targets are the same (`rt_same`, `rtc_on` in `nvn_mtl.m`; `DM_NVN_RT_CONTINUE=0` ends every pass as before). What a pass end gave the draws around it is covered: a colour attachment sampled by [the same-pass feedback rule](#same-pass-feedback-read-through-a-snapshot), a game barrier by `barrier_game`. Depth has two rules of its own: a draw that samples the depth attachment after the call ends the pass there, as its memory holds nothing of this pass until then, and a draw writing depth or stencil after a draw of the pass read that attachment ends it too, as the GPU stores the pass tile by tile, so the earlier reads would see the later writes in the tiles already done. A pass with no colour attachment ends as before.
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md): its ink map (6400x6400 RG8 with a Depth32F-S8) got about 150 one-draw occlusion queries a frame, each after setting the map again: 150 passes of about 1 ms, 42 GB of attachment loads a frame, about 6 fps. Without the depth rules its water came out in grey blocks of tiles: the water reads the scene's depth, then the next draw writes the water's own.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_feedback` (the depth attachment read, then written).

### A leading part of the pass's targets goes on in the pass

- **Symptom:** in `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP`, a G-buffer pass ends whenever the game sets the first few of its colour targets with the same depth (`new targets`), then starts again on all of them: each switch stores and reloads every attachment.
- **Cause:** only the same set of targets continued the pass ([Setting the same targets again continues the pass](#setting-the-same-targets-again-continues-the-pass)).
- **Fix:** SetRenderTargets naming the open pass's first colour targets (the same textures and views, in the same slots), the same depth and stencil target, and the bounds and sample count the pass already has, goes on in the pass with the other targets still attached (`rt_prefix`, `g_rt_sub`, `g_rt_super` in `nvn_mtl.m`; `DM_NVN_RT_SUBSET`, on by default; `0` begins a pass of its own as before, `ab` alternates with `DM_AB`). Pipelines are keyed by the pass's attachments, so an output the fragment shader does not write gets a write mask of zero. The pass ends before a draw (`rt_sub_check`) that writes past the game's targets or whose outputs are unknown, uses a logic operation, samples one of the other targets, or has a geometry, tessellation or mesh stage; a clear ends it as in any continued pass. Setting all the targets again returns to the pass's own. Not for slice-merged or face-split passes, nor while blocks are drawn into compressed-texture shadows. The unwritten-load look-ahead (`ldc_scan`) reads through such a switch.
- **Found on:** upstream, Xenoblade Chronicles: Definitive Edition, Switch 2 Edition (PR #3374): its 8-target G-buffer bound for each character's skinning draw, then the first 5 with the same depth for the character's draws. Ported 2026-11-27 from upstream `dc9c2374b`; NX 18 has no `can_continue`, so the rule sits in its own `OP_SET_RT` continuation. Nothing was measured on a game here yet.
- **Check:** `t_perf_order` (`leading_subset`, adapted from upstream's `t_rt_unbind` cases, which NX 18 does not have), also with `DM_NVN_RT_SUBSET=0`: two targets, then the first, then both again in one pass with the trailing target untouched; a draw writing past the game's targets or reading the trailing target ends the pass and keeps the right pixels; a smaller trailing target refuses; a clear during the continuation lands only on its target. The `[nvn] pass merges` line counts these continuations per frame.

### Passes ended across independent depth and stencil use

- **Symptom:** one-draw passes loading and storing the same depth/stencil attachment: a draw that reads one plane (a stencil view, say) ended the pass because an earlier draw wrote the other, and an identical depth-only `SetRenderTargets` (a shadow map bound again) never continued its open pass.
- **Cause:** the same-pass feedback rules (`fb_depth_rule`, `fb_prepare`) kept one bit for "depth or stencil read" and one for "written", so reads and writes of different planes were taken as dependent. The same-target continuation in `OP_SET_RT` needed a colour target (`r->n`).
- **Fix:** ported from upstream (`a739027a6`, issue #2806) in `nvn_mtl.m`, for every game. `g_fb_dread` and `g_fb_dwrote` hold planes (`FB_DS_DEPTH`, `FB_DS_STENCIL`); a resolved texture binding names the plane of its Metal view (`fb_depth_planes`), and unknown views, images and handle tables keep both. Clears count as writes of their planes: a pass that opens with a depth or stencil clear (`rp_begin_ex_`), a mid-pass depth/stencil clear triangle (`clear_ds_tri`) and a clear still waiting for an encoder, which a read of its plane flushes first (`rp_flush`). Vertex-only draws note their writes too. An identical depth-only binding now continues the open pass as a colour one does. Upstream's third rule (vertex-stage uniform handles in `draw_samples_rt`) and its deferred empty bindings (`DM_NVN_UNBIND_DEFER`) are not in NX 18: its `draw_samples_rt` still ends a continued pass at any bindless shader.
- **Measured upstream:** on the reporter's M4 Mac mini, Orbitals' split-screen workload went from 283 to 150 passes a frame and GPU p50/p95 from 57/59 to 38/40 ms with all three rules and the deferred bindings; NX 18 has only part of that. Not measured on NX 18 or the iPhone.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_ds_planes` (upstream's `t_rt_unbind` depth-only and plane cases, without its deferred-unbind and vertex-handle ones): pass counts and GPU readback of both planes for reads and writes of the same and of different planes in each order, a waiting and an applied clear before a read of its plane, and an identical against a changed depth-only binding. 184 checks pass with direct and deferred encoding; the tree before the port fails 8 of them.
- **Found on:** Orbitals, upstream [issue #2806](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2806) (no NX 18 profile).

### A render area for passes on very large targets

- **Symptom:** a pass on a very large target costs about the same however little it draws; `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` shows time proportional to the target's area.
- **Cause:** a pass with no render area covers its whole attachments. A synthetic pass on a flat 6400x6400 target cost about 0.1 ms, so the cost depends on what the target holds or how the host creates it; which of the two was not found.
- **Fix:** a draw pass on a target of 16 M pixels or more that loads what it keeps stops at the far corner of its scissor when that is at most half the target (`renderTargetWidth` / `renderTargetHeight`, `g_rp_aw` in `nvn_mtl.m`; `DM_NVN_RP_AREA=0` turns it off). Only the draw path opens such a pass. A draw whose scissor leaves the area ends it, and so does anything else that needs the pass (`rp_begin`).
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md): each ink stamp's pass, scissored to 1952x2940 at (0, 3460), about 0.95 → 0.32 ms. Metal keeps what lies outside the area (a 6400x6400 target checked texel by texel on an M4 Pro, with API validation on).
- **Note:** the same stamps sample the map they write, with a game barrier each, so each still takes a whole snapshot of it ([Same-pass feedback read through a snapshot](#same-pass-feedback-read-through-a-snapshot)), about 0.76 ms; a copy of the stamp's rectangle takes 0.09 ms, but nothing proves a stamp samples only inside it. **Open.**

### Variable rate shading ignored

- **Symptom:** `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferShadingRateImagePalette`, or `nvnCommandBufferBindShadingRateImage` or `nvnCommandBufferSetShadingRatePaletteMode`, every frame.
- **Cause:** NVN2 lets a game shade tiles of a pass at a coarser rate. Metal's render passes have no per-tile rate: a rasterization rate map resamples the whole target instead, which is another thing.
- **Fix:** `hle_nvn.c` accepts the three calls and records nothing. Shading every pixel is the most any rate asks for, so the picture is the same or sharper, and the only cost is GPU time where the game meant to save it. This drops a feature rather than emulating it, and the choice is a platform limit.
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md), every frame; [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md), the palette.

## Blend, depth and stencil

### Ten attachment records for eight colors and depth-stencil

- **Symptom:** angle-dependent character smearing, or vertex-only pipeline failures with `invalid depth/stencil pipeline formats 16843012/260`, although the pass began with valid `260/260` formats.
- **Cause:** the store-decision ledger `g_sd` had nine entries, but eight color targets plus separately tracked depth and stencil need ten. The tenth `sd_att` overran the array. On the observed iPad build it overlapped `g_pass`: `udc_note_draw` wrote the overflow entry's `wrote` and `wwhy` bytes into the high bytes of the cached depth format. A later vertex-only skinning pipeline was rejected, so its dependent draws consumed stale bone matrices. The lookahead's nine-byte result array had the same capacity error.
- **Fix:** one `SD_ATT_MAX` constant sizes the ten ledger entries and both store-lookahead result declarations. Both aspects retain their own write/store decisions; no optimization or shader is disabled.
- **Check:** `t_attachment_capacity` replays eight distinct color targets with combined depth/stencil and dependent vertex-only producer/consumer draws. Literal GPU readbacks verify all 8,192 storage values, all eight color clears, and both depth/stencil clears; it also checks formats remain `260/260` and neither pipeline fails. The test runs through both pipeline compilers, both encoding modes and both TBDR settings. AddressSanitizer reproduces the old 56-byte write past the 504-byte ledger and passes after the fix (renderer/test TU instrumented; NVN linker-section registration helpers kept uninstrumented).
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 2](../../dm/games/0100E95004038000/README.md), iPad runtime watchpoint and fixed-camera diagnostic comparison. Testing the rebuilt permanent correction on iPad remains open.

### Combined depth-stencil formats stay paired in passes and pipeline keys

- **Symptom:** Metal aborts at pipeline creation: `depthAttachmentPixelFormat (MTLPixelFormatInvalid) and stencilAttachmentPixelFormat (MTLPixelFormatDepth32Float_Stencil8) must match`.
- **Cause:** the renderer treated the two aspects of combined storage as independent attachment formats. `rp_begin_ex(3)` attached only stencil; `pso_predict_rest_` also enumerated a stencil-only `0/260` alternative, even with TBDR lookahead off. Historical prewarm keys retained those formats. macOS accepts that pipeline and pass; the reported iPadOS legacy pipeline validator rejects it. The original log has TBDR off and no attachment capture, so the exact live pass behind its synchronous `pso_get` stack is still unidentified; the speculative producer is established from the source, not inferred from the cutscene.
- **Fix:** a combined texture occupies both physical attachment slots whenever either aspect is needed. The unused plane is loaded and preserved, with its test and writes still disabled through the logical `g_pass.hd/hs` flags. Native depth-only and stencil-only textures occupy only their valid slots; sampling through `X32_Stencil8` is unchanged. `pso_ds_pair` gives live, predicted and parsed prewarm keys the same effective pair, without replacing any nonzero conflicting format. Repeated alternatives coalesce in the pipeline cache. `pso_build_ex` rejects conflicting or non-renderable depth/stencil formats with an error before either compiler path can assert. `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` includes `J` lines with the source NVN/view format, physical Metal formats, logical use, pass mode, pipeline key, cache hit and draw/prediction origin.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=0100E95004038000 check_t_pso_depth_stencil`: synthetic shaders, native depth-only/stencil-only, both combined NVN formats, attachment-free draws, historical keys, format transitions, cache hits, speculation and invalid descriptors. Literal GPU readbacks check the depth and stencil writes (the stencil-only, depth-only and omitted-plane passes it also checked went with the TBDR lookahead that opened them). Both pipeline compilers and both encoding modes run under Metal validation.
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 2](../../dm/games/0100E95004038000/README.md), [issue #702](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/702) (the reporter's log, not independent confirmation). The reporter confirms the descriptor-guard build reaches cutscenes and gameplay on iPad; its subsequent character smear is traced to [the attachment-ledger overflow](#ten-attachment-records-for-eight-colors-and-depth-stencil). Playing and skipping the opening with the complete correction remain separate device checks. Attaching the unused plane can add traffic in opt-in TBDR single-aspect passes; the default full-attachment path does not acquire another attachment.

### No writes to colour outputs the shader does not write

- **Symptom:** undefined values blended into attachments the fragment shader leaves alone.
- **Cause:** Maxwell leaves targets outside the shader's output map untouched; on Metal such an attachment gets undefined values blended in.
- **Fix:** pipelines get a zero write mask and no blending for colour outputs the shader does not write (reflection `colors`, `color_known` in `nvn_refl.m`).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario 3D World + Bowser's Fury](../../dm/games/010028600EBDA000/README.md): volume draws after the G-buffer (`50d813761ca3f1f9` and others) write only outputs 0 and 2, with blending and all channels enabled on targets 1 and 3.
- **Check:** all five games' regressions were unchanged by it.

### No writes to colour components outside the shader's output map

- **Symptom:** undefined values in the components of a target the fragment shader does not write: a NaN or garbage alpha (or G, B) that later passes, such as bloom, spread.
- **Cause:** the SPH's output map (word 18, 4 bits per colour output, R G B A from the low bit) names the components the GPU writes; it leaves the others of a bound target as they were. The translator stores only the mapped components, but Metal writes the whole attachment under its write mask, the unstored components undefined. [The entry above](#no-writes-to-colour-outputs-the-shader-does-not-write) covered only outputs with no component in the map.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` `ps_omap` records a pixel shader's output map in its reflection (`raster.omap`, Maxwell's 0x50-byte SPH and NVN2's 0x80-byte one alike); `pso_desc` ANDs each target's write mask with its output's nibble, and `first_draw_covers` proves a load skippable only when the map covers every channel the target's format stores (`pf_chan_bits`). Libraries of an older chain have no map and keep the channel mask alone. `t_omap_mask` checks both Metal pipeline paths.
- **Found on:** reported by Lichii, a Switch 2 emulator, on [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (their commit `79c1516`): a depth-only visibility draw with target 0 still bound left a band of NaN and red / blue above the horizon, which bloom spread into violet halos. Ocarina's agl_common depth pass maps output 0 RGBA and output 2 R only (map 0x10f, `TranslatorContext.GetDepthRegister`). Not reproduced here (open).

### The alpha test through a library of its own

- **Symptom:** foliage, hair and fences drawn as solid quads (the parts the texture leaves transparent filled with its colour or black), dissolve effects solid instead of breaking up; once per run `SetAlphaRef …: the host has no alpha test`.
- **Cause:** `nvnColorStateSetAlphaTest` and `nvnCommandBufferSetAlphaRef` select Maxwell's fixed-function alpha test: the alpha a pixel shader writes to colour 0 is compared with the reference before blending, and the fragment is dropped when the comparison fails. Metal has none, and the host recorded the function without applying it and only logged the reference.
- **Fix:** every pixel shader whose colour 0 has a float alpha gets an alpha test entry point beside each of its own in its side library `<key>_bx` ([One compile for a pixel shader's side entry points](shaders.md#one-compile-for-a-pixel-shaders-side-entry-points), `dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_alpha.py`): `main0_a`, and `main0_d_a` where the shader has it (the typed-output entry point `main0_u` holds the same test under a function constant since 2026-11-16: [Typed outputs for every pixel shader](shaders.md#typed-outputs-for-every-pixel-shader)), each running the body, taking the function and the reference from fragment buffer `DMSH_ALPHA_BUFFER` (30) and testing colour 0's alpha, `discard_fragment()` when it fails. `SetAlphaRef` is recorded (`OP_ALPHA_REF`). A pipeline drawn with a function other than `ALWAYS` has `pso_key.atest` set and takes its fragment from that library (`atest_key`, `at_fn`, loaded on first need); its draws bind the function and reference, a copy in the frame arena per value and pass (`at_bind`). The function and the reference are data, so one pipeline serves every function and a reference that changes every draw keeps it; draws without the test keep the base library, whose size and load time do not change. As in OpenGL, there is no test when colour 0 goes to an integer target. `[[early_fragment_tests]]` stays in the variant: such a shader has its depth written before the test, on Maxwell too. Alpha-tested draws count as discarding for the first-draw load elision (`first_draw_covers`). Prewarm rows written before the key field read as alpha test off (`pso_skey_parse`). Not covered: a readable-tile merged draw (`bf_fn`) draws without the test, with a red line naming the shader. `t_alpha.m` replays every function against references 0, 0.3125, 0.5, 1 and NaN over an alpha ramp, and `test_dmsh_alpha.py` checks the entry points and compiles them for the Mac and the iPhone.
- **Found on:** reported on [DARK SOULS: REMASTERED](../../dm/games/01004AB00A260000/README.md) (grass and ferns in Firelink Shrine and Darkroot Garden, PR #32), [ASTRAL CHAIN](../../dm/games/01007300020FA000/README.md) (the death effect's tendrils, `EffectOpacityBlood`, solid black) and [Animal Crossing: New Horizons](../../dm/games/01006F8002326000/README.md) (the island map, issue #73, PR #430), each with an implementation of its own; not yet checked in those games with this one.

### Alpha-test comparisons without an alpha export

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader ... draws with alpha function 5 but has no alpha test entry point (its colour 0 has no float alpha): drawn without the test` on depth-pass programs. An RGB-only colour output can also lose fragments despite exporting no alpha.
- **Cause:** alpha-test eligibility checked only the state and the translated shader's side entry points. Maxwell's original output map, SPH word 18 bit 3, says whether output 0 exports alpha ([NVIDIA's SPH specification](https://nvidia.github.io/open-gpu-doc/Shader-Program-Header/Shader-Program-Header.html#OmapTarget)). Relational/equality comparisons consume that export; an MSL `float4` may contain padding where the original program exports only RGB. The existing translator applies the same export gate in `EmitterContext.PrepareForReturn`; `NEVER` is unconditional and needs no alpha input. Depth-pass programs can instead clip their own fragments without exporting any colour.
- **Fix:** `atest_key` uses the recorded original output map to leave comparisons off when output 0 exports no alpha. This avoids both the false missing-variant warning and comparisons of padding. `NEVER`, an unknown output map, and genuine missing variants retain their existing paths. The shader's own discard and side effects still run. This is general host binding; no shader rewrite, chain change or game rule is needed.
- **Check:** `t_alpha` tests absent colour, RGB-only output 0, alpha at output 1, missing/unknown metadata and `NEVER`, and GPU readback of all comparison functions and references. Its RGB-only fixture has a padded alpha ramp: comparisons leave every fragment, while `NEVER` drops them. Before the fix, the expanded replay fails 168 checks.
- **Found on:** Divinity: Original Sin 2 - Definitive Edition, upstream (not a profile in this tree).

### Logic operations through a library of its own

- **Symptom:** data a game builds in a target by combining draws comes out wrong; in Xenoblade Chronicles 2 indoor lighting has hard-edged dark boxes (world-space boxes in perspective, darker with the time of day) and lighter translucent rectangles elsewhere; in Skyward Sword HD a brown cast covers the distance and near objects turn into dark silhouettes, coming and going with the camera. A pass dump shows `lo=7` (or any value other than 3) on the `D` lines of the draws that write the target.
- **Cause:** `nvnColorStateSetLogicOp` makes the hardware combine the bits a pixel shader writes with the bits already in the target (CLEAR, AND, …, OR, …, SET) in place of blending; as in OpenGL and Vulkan it acts on integer and normalized targets. Metal has none, and the host recorded the operation without applying it, so every draw wrote with COPY. Xenoblade Chronicles 2 builds a per-pixel list of up to four light probes in an RG32Uint target: each probe's box writes its index into the next free byte with OR, and every other fragment writes 0 and relies on OR to leave the list as it is. With COPY each fragment replaced the list, so inside every probe volume the indirect-lighting pass read the wrong probes or none. Skyward Sword HD clears its RGBA8Unorm frame's alpha to 0, then `Dof_FogToAlpha` draws `(0, 0, 0, fog)` with OR and every channel enabled, to put fog in the alpha for the depth of field: OR with 0 keeps the colours, and COPY blacked them out.
- **Fix:** every pixel shader with colour outputs gets logic operation entry points in its side library `<key>_bx` ([One compile for a pixel shader's side entry points](shaders.md#one-compile-for-a-pixel-shaders-side-entry-points), `dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_logic.py`): `main0_l` (and, until 2026-11-16, `main0_u_l`; since then the typed-output entry point `main0_u` holds the operation under a function constant, and a shader with it has no `main0_l`: [Typed outputs for every pixel shader](shaders.md#typed-outputs-for-every-pixel-shader)) runs the body, reads each output's attachment (`[[color(n)]]`, framebuffer fetch; in `main0_u` as a float or an unsigned integer, as its colour is) and combine it with the output by the operation and the target's channel masks from fragment buffer `DMSH_LOGIC_BUFFER` (29). A float output goes through its normalized target's stored bits: rounded to them as the target would store it, combined, and normalized again (`dm_logic_norm`); masks of zero, on a target with no such bits, pass it through. Every shader has the entry points, not a listed few, because which ones draw with an operation shows only in play (Skyward Sword HD: a second shader, `eb1648acad4e38e3`, in the Knight Academy); the side library is loaded only on first need. Each read depends on the function constant `DMSH_LOGIC_FC + n` (Metal refuses a pipeline that reads an output whose target the pass lacks); the host specializes the entry per set of targets and keeps each (`lo_fn`). A pipeline drawn with an operation other than COPY into an unsigned integer or normalized target has `pso_key.logic` set (`lop_key`; the field was padding, so older prewarm rows read it as off), blending off, since the operation takes its place, and its draws bind the operation and the masks (8, 16 or 32 bits a channel, RGB10A2's 10 and 2, the 5, 6 and 1 of the packed formats), a copy in the frame arena per value and pass (`lo_bind`). The operation is data, so one pipeline serves every operation; draws with COPY keep the base library and read no attachment. Logic-op draws count as reading the target for the first-draw load elision (`first_draw_covers`) and end a readable-tile merged pass before them. Not covered, with a red line: a floating-point, sRGB, signed normalized or signed integer target, a draw with an alpha test as well in a shader without `main0_u` (which does both), and a merged pass's first phase. `t_logic.m` replays all 16 operations into RG32Uint, RGBA8Uint, RGBA8Unorm and RGBA16Unorm targets; `test_dmsh_logic.py` checks the entry points and compiles them for the Mac and the iPhone.
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles 2](../../dm/games/0100E95004038000/README.md#rendering): the probe-list writers, `shd_lgt.wishp` var3 (`ef07fd3d5046164f`, `aa9a0923524b7e46`, `2717a0aa47361bbd`, `d95874c161deb77d`, `f24b58784a33adea`), read by the indirect-lighting pass `93d0e7a7c14cdfb0` (GitHub issue #643). Normalized targets: [The Legend of Zelda: Skyward Sword HD](../../dm/games/01002DA013484000/README.md#rendering), `f11c15a99b0c1790` (GitHub issue #928; the cause and the approach were first reported by Zatteo in issue #497).

- **Typed-output binding regression (2026-11-17):** `c75f0126` moved the operation into `main0_u`, whose generated reflection has `entry_u_logic` without the legacy `logic` dictionary. Pipeline selection accepted `u_logic`, but drawing bound buffer 29 only for `has_logic` (the dictionary), leaving the operation and masks unbound. Draws now bind for either capability. `t_logic` loads reflection and libraries made by the production build finisher through `shader_load`, then reads back all 16 operations on unsigned and normalized targets; it previously set `has_logic` by hand and hid the missing bind. Skyward Sword HD's reported return of the dark cinematic cast bisects to this change, but a matching pass capture and gameplay retest are still needed to confirm that connection.

### Source-only blending on floating-point targets

- **Symptom:** the title background and gameplay render black while the HUD and text remain visible. A pass dump shows the scene rendered before tone mapping, but the bloom input holds positive infinity.
- **Cause:** a bloom blur enables ADD with source ONE and destination ZERO for both colour and alpha, a state that replaces the destination. With that blend state enabled in Metal, all 90,400 texels of the R11G11B10F target held infinity (M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1) though the input and the blur weights were finite. An isolated draw with the same fragment code gave finite values either way, so the lower-level trigger in the live pipeline remains open.
- **Fix:** `pso_desc` leaves blending off on float attachments for exactly this state (a direct write). Blends that read the destination or alter the source keep their factors and operations. `check_t_blend_zero`'s `replace_float_case` checks the pipeline descriptors.
- **Found on:** LEGO Star Wars: The Skywalker Saga upstream (no profile in NX 18). Ported from upstream NXNative `ce41059a4`; not measured in NX 18.

### Advanced blend modes as Porter-Duff factors

- **Symptom:** `nvnBlendStateSetAdvancedMode 8` (SRC_OVER) logged and blended with the state's own functions.
- **Cause:** the hardware ignores the blend state's own functions while an advanced mode is set.
- **Fix:** the blend state keeps the mode, the premultiplied-source flag and the overlap (`blend_st` in `nvn_obj.h`), and the pipeline (`adv_blend` in `nvn_mtl.m`) blends the Porter-Duff modes (ZERO, SRC, DST, SRC/DST_OVER, _IN, _OUT, _ATOP, XOR, the PLUS modes) with the fixed-function factors they amount to under uncorrelated overlap, with premultiplied colours as NV_blend_equation_advanced defines them; a source that is not premultiplied is multiplied by As where the source factor is ONE. Multiply, screen, overlay, the HSL modes and the other overlaps need the destination colour in the shader and keep the state's functions, with a red line naming the mode.
- **Found on:** [Mario vs. Donkey Kong](../../dm/games/0100B99019412000/README.md) (SRC_OVER, on one blend state in the levels).

### Float colours rounded into normalized targets as NVIDIA's colour unit does

- **Symptom:** surfaces out of direct light nearly black, as if there were no ambient light (upstream: Sonic Frontiers' shadowed grass, cliffs and character in Cyber Space 1-1). More generally, data a game packs into an 8-bit (or 16-bit, RGB10A2) normalized target as a half step, such as material flags written as (n + 0.5) / 255, reads back one higher than on the console. No red line.
- **Cause:** NVIDIA's colour unit converts a float colour into a normalized target by multiplying in float32 and rounding that product to nearest even; Apple's rounds the exact product to nearest. Upstream measured an M6 (65,536 colours into RGBA8Unorm): 128 differ from the float32 rule, all within rounding of a half step. Sonic Frontiers writes its G-buffer flags as 66.5 / 255 in float32: 66 on the Switch, 67 on a Mac, and bit 0 sends its lighting to an ambient the G-buffer leaves black.
- **Fix:** general, in the chain and the host. The guard every pixel shader calls before it returns ([A NaN colour under a zero blend weight](shaders.md#a-nan-colour-under-a-zero-blend-weight), `dmsh_rewrite.py` `blend_zero`, `dm_blend0`) takes a second word at fragment buffer `DMSH_BLEND_BUFFER`, `round`, `DMSH_BLEND_ROUND_BITS` bits per colour output (`DMSH_ROUND_8`, `_16`, `_10_2`), and makes such an output `rint(float32(saturate(c) × scale)) / scale` (`dm_rop`), which Apple's conversion stores as NVIDIA's integer. The host (`nvn_mtl.m` `rop_word`, bound with the zero word by `bz_bind`) sets it for R8, RG8, RGBA8, BGRA8, the 16-bit and the 10/2-bit normalized targets whose stored value is the colour's conversion: blending off for that output, MIN or MAX on both colour and alpha, or a logic operation. `DM_NVN_ROP_ROUND=0` turns it off for A/B runs.
  - NX 18: the words are worked out for the draw's own targets on every path that binds them: a draw (`draw_map_take`, by a merged pass's mapping), a face-merged draw (`fsm_encode`) and a pass run as a compute dispatch (`pcm_run`, from its own targets' formats: no pass is open there). Before this port the last two bound the previous draw's zero word.
- **Cost:** a draw into float targets only, or blended ones, pays the second word's load and the existing test. A rounded output pays a clamp, a multiply, a round and a divide per channel, in every pixel of a non-blended RGBA8 / RGBA16 / RGB10A2 target (most G-buffers and post-process passes). Upstream measured GPU 4.32-4.46 ms against 4.43-4.56 ms a frame on an M6 (Sonic Frontiers, within noise); nothing measured on the iPhone, where fragment ALU is the scarcer resource. `DM_NVN_ROP_ROUND=0` is the A/B.
- **Not covered:** a blended sum (rounded after the blend, out of a shader's reach), sRGB targets, signed normalized targets, the packed 16-bit formats (RGB565, RGB5A1, RGBA4), and shaders from libraries of an older chain (no word).
- **Found on:** Sonic Frontiers upstream (no profile in NX 18). Ported from upstream NXNative `a4b590976`.
- **Check:** `check_t_rop_round` (`t_rop_round.m`: 256 colours through the replay into RGBA8, RGBA16 and RGB10A2 targets with blending off, MAX and a blended sum, then the same with `DM_NVN_ROP_ROUND=0`; `rop_word` by format, blend state, logic operation and a merged pass's mapping; 9,244 checks a mode), `test_dmsh_blend_zero.py`.

### Float colours stored into 16-bit float targets toward zero, as NVIDIA's colour unit does

- **Symptom:** on the iPhone or the iPad only, solid patches or a halo of one flat colour over a character or an object, where an effect compares a depth or a value it wrote into an RG16F / RGBA16F / R16F target against the float it came from: the Mac draws the same scene right. Super Mario Bros. Wonder: Elephant Luigi on the course's back layer with dark-blue (the "seen through" silhouette colour) patches over his body and a blue halo around him; which pixels changes with the camera. No red line.
- **Cause:** NVIDIA's colour unit converts a float colour into a 16-bit float target toward zero and clamps finite overflow past 65504 to ±65504 (Direct3D's rule for a float into a narrower float, [Data type conversion](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/uwp/graphics-concepts/data-type-conversion): the hardware implements it for every API), and games rely on it. Apple Metal targets differ: macOS on an M5 Pro (32,731 of 65,536 random floats into RG16F, RGBA16F and R16F, every one truncated) and an M4 Pro (macOS 27.2.1: normal midpoint, subnormal boundary, underflow and overflow past 65504 all stored toward zero / clamped to ±65504) already match NVIDIA's rule, while an iPad Pro M4 (iPadOS 26) and an iPhone 17 Pro Max (iOS 26, A19 Pro) round to nearest even (raw dumps of the game's targets on the iPad, `DM_NVN_SNAPRAW`: 3,459 of 7,161 character pixels held the half above the float, none below it by more than half an ulp). Wonder's `agl` back-layer pass draws the player's mesh with its linear depth `−(z + c) × s` into an RG16F target (fs `2c927e2dc9a90b34`, depth write on, into the scene's depth buffer), linearises the scene depth into an R32F target (`87865c894711df52`), and the composite (`fd47d027af5670f9`, `ps_agl.bin`) paints the silhouette colour wherever the half is at or above the float plus 1e-5: at the player's depth (about 0.109) the half ulp is 6.1e-5, so a half rounded up by up to 3e-5 is "hidden", a half truncated never is.
- **Fix:** general, in the chain and the host: the pixel shader's rounding guard ([Float colours rounded into normalized targets as NVIDIA's colour unit does](#float-colours-rounded-into-normalized-targets-as-nvidias-colour-unit-does), `dm_rop`) takes a fourth class, `DMSH_ROUND_F16` (`DMSH_BLEND_ROUND_BITS` is 3 bits an output now), and converts such an output toward zero by its float32 bits: the top 10 mantissa bits in the half's normal range, fewer below it (the half denormal grid), a signed zero below that grid, the largest finite half past 65504 (the conversion rule clamps), NaN and Inf kept; the result is a half's exact value, which the GPU's own conversion stores as it is. At start-up (`mtl_init_once`), `rop_f16_probe` draws four floats into a 1×1 `RGBA16Float` target using the precompiled `clear` library on a one-off Metal 4 command buffer (normal midpoint, subnormal boundary, underflow, finite overflow) to check whether hardware already matches NVIDIA's rule. Where hardware already truncates (macOS M4 Pro / M5 Pro), `rop_class` leaves `R16Float`, `RG16Float` and `RGBA16Float` at 0, as before (no added cost); where it does not (iPad Pro M4, iPhone 17 Pro Max), `rop_class` returns `DMSH_ROUND_F16` under the normalized classes' conditions (blending off for the output, MIN or MAX on colour and alpha, or a logic operation), so a blended sum keeps Apple's conversion. `DM_NVN_ROP_F16=0/1` overrides the probe for testing; `DM_NVN_ROP_ROUND=0` turns both off for A/B runs.
- **Not covered:** the packed float formats (RG11B10F, RGB9E5), whose NVIDIA conversion is presumably the same rule but was not measured; blended sums; shaders from libraries of an older chain.
- **Cost:** none on GPUs/OSes whose hardware already converts 16-bit float targets toward zero (the guard's word stays 0 for those targets, as before); on GPUs that round to nearest even (the probe's line in the log says which), a draw into a 16-bit float target with blending off, MIN or MAX pays about nine vector integer operations per `float4` colour output in shaders that already call the guard.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_rop_round` (probed default, `DM_NVN_ROP_F16=1`, and `DM_NVN_ROP_ROUND=0`: an `RGBA16F` target beside the normalized ones, including subnormal, underflow and finite overflow edges, plus `rop_word`'s cases), `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_blend_zero`, `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_blend_zero.py` (`test_f16_rule`: CPU model over 3,000+ floats with exact float32 bit checks including `-0.0`, denormals, overflow, NaN and Inf).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md) (an iPad Pro M4 and an iPhone 17 Pro Max; Macs on M4 Pro / M5 Pro never showed it). Found by comparing the pass dumps of both (identical passes, states, binds and stores) and then the raw targets at the character's pixels.

### Depth clamp

- **Fix:** `SetDepthClamp` is recorded (`OP_DEPTH_CLAMP`) and replayed as Metal's depth clip mode (it used to be ignored).
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem: Three Houses](../../dm/games/010055D009F78000/README.md) (the game turns it on).

### Depth bounds

- **Cause:** `SetDepthBounds` was an empty function (`g_nops`), so every fragment a game meant to reject by its stored depth was shaded anyway (no picture problem has been tied to it). The Switch's GPU tests the depth already stored at a fragment's sample against [min, max], as GL's `EXT_depth_bounds_test` and Vulkan do. Metal has the same test (`setDepthTestMinBound:maxBound:` on the render encoder, which also tests the stored depth) only on Apple10 GPUs and later; on the M4 Max (Apple9) Metal's validation stops the process at the call.
- **Fix:** `SetDepthBounds` is recorded (`OP_DEPTH_BOUNDS`, the enable and the bounds as the game passed them) and kept in the replay state, so it survives pass splits and new encoders. Each draw resolves it (`dbnd_resolve` in `nvn_dbnd.h`) and sends the bounds to the encoder when it has others (`enc_dbnd`, cached with the other dynamic state in `g_ed`; a new encoder is known to start with the test off). Bounds outside [0, 1] are clamped, which changes nothing since the stored depth is in [0, 1]; (0, 1) is Metal's "off". Bounds that no stored depth meets (min above max, min above 1, max below 0, or a NaN, which fails every comparison) pass no fragment, and Metal refuses them: on a GPU with the test such a draw is skipped as one with both faces culled, unless rasterization is off. A pass without a depth attachment has nothing to test, as on the Switch. The depth bounds test reads the stored depth, so a draw with bounds on keeps it attached even with the depth test and write off (a light volume's stencil pass). The TBDR lookahead, its load elision and its pass merges treated bounds the same way until that mode was removed (upstream NXNative ad1ee5bfa). A clear drawn inside the pass (`clr_tri`) turns the test off first, since clears are not tested. Nothing goes to an encoder until a draw first has bounds: the GPU is asked once then (`MTLGPUFamilyApple10`), with one log line saying the test is applied, or a red line saying this GPU lacks it and every draw goes as if it were off. A GPU without the test (the M4 Max, Apple9) therefore draws every draw as before the test was applied, those whose bounds pass nothing too; a game that never turns the test on makes no call at all. The deferred encoder's stand-in records the call (`RO_R_DBND`, `nvn_rec.h`). `DM_DEPTH_BOUNDS=0` ignores the bounds as before, to tell a picture problem from the test. The test is on by default on the iPhone 18 Pro Max, where no run has checked it yet, so the switch is named outside the DM_NVN_ prefix: a player build drops those lines from `env.txt` (`player_env.h`) but keeps this one. `t_depth_bounds.m` replays the calls with the encoder call taken by the test (this Mac's GPU cannot make it): bounds at the first draw and after a pass split, none repeated, (0, 1) when turned off and around a clear in the pass, none without depth, the bounds sent in a pass whose draws neither test nor write depth, a draw with bounds after a same-target continuation, skipped draws' pixels, the switch, and no call at all on the Apple9 GPU under `MTL_DEBUG_LAYER=1`, where bounds that pass nothing are drawn.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md), [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md), [Pokémon Scarlet](../../dm/games/0100A3D008C5C000/README.md) (calls counted in their usage routes; what the bounds change on screen is not yet checked on an Apple10 device).

### Depth on the far plane

- **Symptom:** blocks of another sky layer in the sky, in stair-stepped squares and half squares that follow the dome's mesh. In Mario Kart 8 Deluxe's Sky-High Sundae, salmon-red blocks of the sunset panorama show through the blue sky, the same blocks every time from the same camera.
- **Cause:** a vertex shader that stores one value in gl_Position z and w puts every vertex at the viewport's far value, the usual way to draw a sky. NVIDIA's GPU fills such a triangle with exactly that value. Apple's rasterizer does not: a triangle whose three vertices have the same depth comes out up to one step short of it at many pixels, whatever the depth (z = 0.5w gives 0.49999994; only 0 is exact). In Metal tests of random z = w triangles into a `Depth32Float` target, 43 % of the pixels came out below 1.0 on an Apple M5 and 9 to 27 % on an M4 Pro, down to 1 − 2⁻²³; the GL convention's z' = z/2 + w/2, which keeps z = w, does the same. Two layers drawn there then fight. Sundae draws a red panorama and then a blue dome over it, and where the panorama landed one step in front, the blue failed its depth test. The rounding is in the rasterizer, not in the translation: z and w are bit-identical at every vertex.
- **Fix (a workaround for the platform's rasterizer, which cannot be changed):** the build marks such vertex shaders (`dmsh_reflect.py` `far_plane`: the last stores to z and w are one register defined once, or one constant other than 0, with no branches; reflection `raster.far`, `nvn_refl` `pos_far`). The host draws them with a constant depth bias of 16 units past the far value and depth clamping (`nvn_mtl.m` `draw`), which puts every pixel exactly on it: in the same tests 2 units already left none short, with depth ranges 0..1, 0..0.5 and 0.25..0.75. A draw that sets the game's own polygon offset keeps it, and a program with a geometry or tessellation stage is left alone (NX 18 draws no wide lines, upstream's other exception). Only the far plane can be pinned this way, because there the exact value is also the clamp's bound; other constant depths stay as Metal draws them.
- **Open: a reversed depth range** (near value above the far one, so z = w lands on the lower bound). Metal scales a float target's bias by the depth's exponent, so near 0 the bias is nothing: with −16 units and clamping, 24 to 75 % of the pixels stayed 2⁻²⁴ above 0 on the M4 Pro, and the host leaves such draws alone. Moving z past w in the vertex shader (z = w · (1 + 2⁻²⁰)) with clamping put every pixel on the far value in every range, reversed included, but it changes what the vertex stage outputs, which a geometry stage or a capture reads, and needs clamping tied to the shader; no game is known to draw z = w with a reversed range.
- **Found on:** [Mario Kart 8 Deluxe](../../dm/games/0100152000022000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (245 of its 12,530 vertex shaders: the skies, and full-screen passes at depth 1). Also marked in Sonic Superstars (117), Mario Kart World (32) and Mario Tennis Fever (5).

### Telling depth rejection from no rasterization

- `DM_NVN_DEPTHOVR` (depth test forced to `ALWAYS`) with `HILITE` tells "rejected by depth" from "never rasterized". `TEXSNAP` copies used to run before the pass they were meant to show (classic-queue command buffer committed ahead of the Metal 4 one); they now go through the replay's copy encoder.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md), tracing the tile lighting: the G-buffer class byte selects per-class half-resolution passes through a depth buffer of class values (`gl_FragDepth = mix(0.5, 1, nibble-swapped class / 255)`, then full-screen or per-tile quads with depth `EQUAL`).

### Front and back stencil reference values

- **Symptom:** a two-sided stencil draw whose back faces test against their own reference value passes nowhere (or everywhere) on the back faces; nothing in the log. In Mario Golf: Super Rush the ground decals are missing: the driving range's scoring target in Golf Adventure (a ring with a blue centre on the fairway) is not drawn, in the normal view or the top view.
- **Cause:** `SetStencilRef` takes a face, and the replay keeps both values, but sent Metal the front one for both faces (`setStencilReferenceValue:`), while the depth-stencil state (`dss_get`) already gave each face its own compare function, operations and masks. A back face therefore tested the front value. Mario Golf's decals are volumes drawn twice: front faces mark stencil bit 0x80 where the volume lies behind the ground, then back faces draw the colour where `stencil & 0x90` equals the back value 0x10 (the ground's mark, written by the terrain draws) while the front value is 0. With 0 for both, the colour draw passed nowhere on the ground.
- **Fix:** `enc_r_stencil_ref` sends both values (`setStencilFrontReferenceValue:backReferenceValue:`), the per-encoder state cache (`g_ed.sref`, `g_ed.sref_b`) compares both, and the deferred recorder (`nvn_rec.h` `RO_R_SREF`) carries both. `t_pso_depth_stencil` checks that a back face tests its own value, directly and through the recorder, and through the replay (`OP_STENCIL` per face, then draws in one encoder where only the back value changes).
- **Found on:** Mario Golf: Super Rush (`0100C9C00E25C000`, 4.0.0, issue #814; no profile in the repository), Golf Adventure's driving-range lesson, the PR author's runs (PR #2351): pass with fragment shader `6f91c05c60985a33`, four draws of 384 indices (two decals, two draws each); the target shows in the top view and from the tee.

### Stencil-mode texture views read through `X32_Stencil8`

- **Symptom:** striped lighting: black and white bands that follow depth on every lit surface, as if every pixel were in or out of shadow at random.
- **Cause:** a shader that reads a depth-stencil texture through a view whose `nvnTextureViewSetDepthStencilMode` is `STENCIL`: the host ignored the mode and bound depth, so a stencil test read bits of the depth value's float bits.
- **Fix:** `tex_by_id` binds an `X32_Stencil8` view for such a view. The shader declares a float texture (the translator has no integer texture type) and takes the raw bits with `as_type<uint>`, and a read of the integer view gives the stencil value in those bits. Metal allows such a view only of a texture with the pixel-format-view usage: Apple GPUs read it correctly without, but the API validation layer aborts at the first one (PR #106, Luigi's Mansion 3). Upgrading the texture when the view appears recreated the depth buffer while it was attached, and the next occlusion-counted draw crashed inside `setVisibilityResultMode`, so every depth-stencil texture has that usage from the start (`tex_desc`; Vulkan depth-stencil images too, `vkCreateImage`), which costs nothing measured (on an M4 Pro the same allocated size and GPU time); `t_stencil_view` checks it under validation.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Legends: Z-A](../../dm/games/0100F43008C44000/README.md): the deferred lighting pixel shader (`ead6e2a32540b19a`, 31 bindings) first reads the depth-stencil buffer through such a view, tests stencil bit 6 and discards. [Luigi's Mansion 3](../../dm/games/0100DCA0064A6000/README.md) (PR #106, the validation abort).
- **Note:** inputs ruled out on the way in Z-A: the G-buffer albedo, the normals (12-bit pairs packed into 3 bytes, noisy by design), the screen-space shadow mask, the scaled depth copies and conditional rendering (`render_cond` changes nothing in this game and stays off).

### Stencil-mode textures from the texture builder

- **Symptom:** a character drawn as a dithered silhouette inside a dithered square (DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined's hero).
- **Cause:** Unreal's stencil shader resources are second textures on the depth buffer's storage built with `nvnTextureBuilderSetDepthStencilMode(STENCIL)`, which the host only logged, so the character occlusion mask read depth where it tests stencil bits.
- **Fix:** the builder's mode now goes to the texture (`tex_t.stencil`), whose sampling view is the `X32_Stencil8` one a stencil-mode texture view already got.
- **Found on:** [DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined](../../dm/games/0100A9D01C446000/README.md) (the character occlusion mask, `edbfc7a088be545e`).

### The provoking vertex is the first

- **Symptom:** `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferSetProvokingVertexMode`, once.
- **Cause:** the call was not implemented. NVN2's driver writes its mode into the 3D class's `SET_PROVOKING_VERTEX` (method 0x1684), 0 for the first vertex of a primitive and 1 for the last.
- **Fix:** mode 0 needs nothing: Metal's flat values come from the first vertex. Mode 1 has no Metal setting and is reported once (`[RED] nvnCommandBufferSetProvokingVertexMode 1 (the last vertex)`); open until a game sets it.
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md), which sets mode 0.

### Conservative rasterization remains unimplemented

- **Symptom:** `[nvn] SetConservativeRasterEnable on: ignored by host for now`, followed by `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferSetConservativeRasterDilate`.
- **Cause:** `hle_nvn.c` ignores the enable setter and has no implementation for the dilation setter. The requested raster coverage state never reaches Metal.
- **Fix:** **Open.** Neither setter has been implemented. The opening indoor scene renders and accepts input, but the visual contribution of the missing coverage state has not been isolated. The diagnostic remains visible.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Scarlet](../../dm/games/0100A3D008C5C000/README.md#open) (1.0.0, the first playable bedroom). [Super Mario RPG](../../dm/games/0100BC0018138000/README.md) calls the dilation setter and `nvnCommandBufferSetSubpixelPrecisionBias` repeatedly and did not turn the enable on along the route tested, so the dilation has nothing to act on there.

## Vertex input and draws

### Integer vertex inputs

- **Symptom:** characters (skinned models) invisible or stretched into streaks while the level renders; `vertex attribute format … does not match shader input type …` for a remaining mismatch.
- **Cause:** integer vertex inputs (bone indices) translated as float: without PC reflection the translator declares every vertex input `float4`. Skinned models read their bone index (`R8UI` / `RG8UI` / `RGBA8UI`) as raw integer bits (`as_type<int>(in.m_N.x) * 48`); Metal converted the integers to floats, and every bone index was garbage.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` (`int_inputs`) now re-translates a vertex shader with `uint` inputs when every use of an input is an integer bit cast (529 of 4,854 in Mario), and the host makes the Metal vertex format agree with the declared type (`vfmt_for_ct`; signedness swaps of 32-bit components and 32-bit float→int reinterpretation keep the bits, while float-type formats and 8- and 16-bit integers of the other sign go through [shadow vertex inputs](#shadow-vertex-inputs), next entry). A remaining mismatch is reported as `vertex attribute format … does not match shader input type …`.
- **Check:** finding the draws: log model file ranges with `DM_PROBE=main:480f0` (x1 = file data, x2 = size), match the vertex/index buffer addresses in `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP_ALL` against them, then `DM_NVN_HILITE` their fragment shader.
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md) (skinned characters).

### Shadow vertex inputs

- **Symptom:** red lines 89 and 96 (`vconv_bind`, the CPU conversion every draw); about a quarter of Age of Calamity's replay thread in battle.
- **Cause:** an input that `int_inputs` declares an integer may still be fed a format of another class. A float-type format (half, unorm / snorm, 10:10:10:2, 11:11:10) leaves the converted float32 bits in the register; an 8- or 16-bit integer of the other signedness is widened by the format's own sign (Fire Emblem: Three Houses, [CPU conversion of vertex attributes the input type cannot take](#cpu-conversion-of-vertex-attributes-the-input-type-cannot-take)). Metal reads a format only into an input of its own class, so the replay used to convert those attributes on the CPU every draw (`vconv_bind`).
- **Fix:** shadow inputs (`<key>_bv`, `dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_vin.py`): the build also writes a side library for each such vertex shader. In it, every integer input is declared again as a `float4` and as the integer of the other sign, at Metal attribute indices 16–30 (NVN uses 0–15), and the body starts with `in.m_N = dm_in.m_N | as_type<T>(dm_in.m_N_vf) | as_type<T>(dm_in.m_N_vo)`. The host sends each attribute to the index of its format's class and the zero buffer to the others (`vin_ct`, `vin_on` in `nvn_mtl.m`; pipeline keys unchanged), so a pipeline with any mix of formats reads Maxwell's register bits. The float shadows are assigned first: with more than 7 integer inputs the last ones get only the float shadow (169 of 8,201 inputs in Age of Calamity).
- **Note:** a format with no shadow keeps the CPU conversion, as do transform feedback draws (`main0_tf` has no variant) and `DM_NVN_VCONV_CPU=1`. The host loads a side library only for a pipeline that routes an attribute to a shadow, but the build cannot tell which shaders will need one (an input used only as an integer may hold bone indices or float bits), so it writes them all. Shadows cover only integer inputs, so 8- and 16-bit integer formats feeding float inputs still go through the CPU.
- **Found on:**
  - [Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity](../../dm/games/01002B00111A2000/README.md): 2,259 libraries, 77 MB each for Mac and iOS. Its Mac battle at 60 fps against the CPU conversion: 0 vertices converted instead of 545,000 per submission, submit + present 10.0 → 5.7 ms a frame, CPU 3.97 → 2.44 W, GPU unchanged, the same picture.
  - Other games have 0–2,000 libraries (at most 31 MB on iOS), except [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md): 16,781 libraries, 344 MB on iOS (21% of its shader libraries, 1.5% of its IPA), and its Mac and iPhone runs so far load none. Its 8- and 16-bit integer formats feeding float inputs (red line 99, about 60,000 vertices per submission on its Mac route into the opening) still go through the CPU.

### Pulling vertex inputs

- **Cause:** a pipeline's vertex descriptor fixes the vertex layout it is drawn with, so a shader pair drawn with a layout it has no pipeline for waits until one is compiled.
- **Fix:** pulling inputs (`<key>_bp`, `dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_pull.py`, decoder `dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_pull.metal`): a side library of every vertex shader with stage inputs, whose `main0` / `main0_z` have no `[[stage_in]]`. They read their inputs themselves from a per-draw layout block at buffer 16 (`dm_vlayout`): per stream the base address, stride and divisor, and per NVN attribute, all 32 of NVN2's, the raw attribute state word. The base instance comes from `[[base_instance]]`, so indirect draws are right too. `dm_fetch` decodes the NVN format at run time into the register words Maxwell's fetch gives the shader, and the input takes them with `as_type`. A pipeline made with it has no vertex descriptor, so it serves every vertex layout a shader pair is drawn with; it is meant for drawing while the pipeline for the draw's own layout is still being built.
- **Note:** the words are bit-identical to Metal's vertex-descriptor fetch for every format `vfmt` knows, and to the host's CPU conversion for the integer-read-as-float formats. unorm / snorm go through the `unpack_*` builtins, because a plain division under fast math is off by one unit in the last place for some values; NaNs of R11G11B10F come out canonical, as from Metal's fetch. With a divisor the element is `base instance + (instance - base instance) / divisor`, Metal's own rule. Positions match the vertex-descriptor pipeline's (`[[invariant]]`, preserved invariance).
- **Note:** the run-time format decode costs GPU time: on an M2 Max, 8 attributes in a vertex-bound draw of tiny visible triangles took 65 % longer (about 1.0 ms per million vertices) than with the vertex descriptor. With the formats as compile-time constants it was 9 %, and with the whole layout compiled in, 0. So these pipelines only stand in until the fast one is built. Other entry points (transform feedback, geometry capture, layer) get no variant; shaders without stage inputs need none, since their pipelines have no vertex descriptor.
- **Symptom:** an unsigned iOS build reports `[RED] pulling library ... not usable (no library ...)` and its draws wait for pipelines, either with the profile's `pso_mtl4 on` or with **Metal 4 pipelines** (a launcher setting then) and **Pipeline prebuild** turned on in the launcher for a game whose profile leaves `pso_mtl4` off. Then the prebuild ends at once as if finished (`pipeline prebuild done: 7495 pairs, 0 bases built, 0 already archived, 7495 left to the draws, 0 failed, 0.6 s`), and the shader image holds `_bp_left_out` instead of the `_bp.metallib` files. Or packaging with `IOS_PULL=1` fails with `SKIPS[@]: unbound variable`.
- **Cause:** the IPA packer used to strip `_bp.metallib` unless `IOS_PULL=1`, independently of the runtime's profile default. It later followed the profile's `pso_mtl4` (`build_ipa.sh`, and the NSP script's `ios_wants_pulling` with `DMSH_IOS_PULL=0`, so `dmsh_build.py` did not compile them either), as if nothing could read them with the key off. But the launcher offers the **Metal 4 pipelines** toggle for every NVN game, with the profile's key as its default only, and with it the device's prebuild and hybrid pipelines read the pulling libraries. The inclusion path also expanded an empty array under `set -u`, which macOS Bash 3.2 treats as unset.
- **Fix:** an iOS build keeps the pulling libraries for every NVN game (`build_ipa.sh`, `ios_wants_pulling`); a profile's `pso_mtl4`, then the switch, is now retired and read by nothing that packs. `IOS_PULL=0` leaves them out, for a smaller IPA whose device prebuild then builds no bases. An IPA that packs a [pipeline archive made offline](../development.md#the-pipeline-archive-made-offline) (`pso/`), or an Xcode project, always carries them, whatever `IOS_PULL` says, since the archive's bases are made of them. An image built without them (`_bp_left_out`) is built again at the next shader step. Packing options always contain `--store`, so both inclusion and exclusion work under Bash 3.2. A device that already wrote its prebuild stamp from an app without the libraries, on the same chain version, runs the prebuild again once the app carries them: [A prebuild stamp written without the pulling libraries](shaders.md#a-prebuild-stamp-written-without-the-pulling-libraries).
- **Measured:** the pulling libraries are 4–10 % of a game's library bytes (in the Mac images: Fire Emblem: Three Houses 10 MB, Luigi's Mansion 3 22 MB, OCTOPATH TRAVELER II 62 MB, Minecraft Dungeons II 137 MB). On the device they are worth the size: Echoes of Wisdom with both toggles on, without them on an iPhone 17, compiled 564 pipelines during play (23.3 s, the worst frame 2.6 s); with them on an iPad mini 6, its prebuild built 7,488 bases in 513 s once, and play compiled none.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/t_pull.m` (the words against Metal's fetch and the CPU conversion), `t_pull_inv.m` (a depth prepass with one pipeline and depth-equal with the other), `test_dmsh_pull.py`; `dm/ios/tests/test_profile_pack.py` packs tiny fixtures through the actual archive step and checks runtime-prebuild library inclusion, explicit overrides and an IPA with a pipeline archive in the ZIP.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md): draws that waited for their pipeline on a cold start. [The Legend of Zelda: Echoes of Wisdom](../../dm/games/01008CF01BAAC000/README.md#found-on-this-game): an iPhone build whose launcher toggles built no bases (issue #1684).

### Absent vertex attributes lose W

- **Symptom:** a projectile paints the floor but its airborne mesh is invisible, without a missing-shader error (upstream: Splatoon Raiders' Slosher ink).
- **Cause:** the native vertex descriptor and the pulling decoder supplied four zero words for an absent attribute (format 0). NVN / Maxwell give the default float vector `(0, 0, 0, 1)` as raw register bits, including when the Metal declaration uses integers. An alpha-tested depth pass whose opacity comes from that W discards every fragment, and the colour pass with depth EQUAL then draws nothing.
- **Fix:** general. `vertex_constants` holds the default vector at offset 16 of slot 30, which `pso_desc` gives active absent inputs; inactive shadow declarations and unknown formats still read zeros at offset 0. `dm_fetch` (`dmsh_pull.metal`) supplies the same default for format 0, and `pull_layout_fill` keeps non-zero formats whose stream has no buffer, so the decoder reads zeros for those. Ported from upstream NXNative `ef8b25d2c` (PR #3237).
  - NX 18: slot 30's layout stride is 32 (was 16), at run time (`ZERO_ATTR`) and in `pso_offline.py`, so the default at offset 16 fits the stride: metal-tt refuses an attribute past its buffer's stride, and every offline row with an absent attribute would have been left to the device. The pipeline descriptors with a zero-buffer attribute change, so the shipped archives and prewarm lists are recorded again with the shader batch.
- **Check:** `t_default_attrib` (float, signed and unsigned inputs beyond `S.nattr`, explicitly absent and of an unknown format; a shadow-input pipeline with an active shadowed and an absent attribute), `t_pull` (the pulling default, missing buffers, invalid streams, unknown formats), `test_pso_offline.py`.
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md) upstream (#3181); not checked on NX 18 hardware.

### Float32 vertex padding through integer inputs

- **Symptom:** a fullscreen post-process draw writes no color, then a later blur composite darkens scenery while the character remains visible.
- **Cause:** reinterpreting Float1/2/3 as UInt1/2/3 preserves supplied bits but pads missing `w` with integer `1`. A shader that bitcasts the input back to float receives `w=1.4e-45`, rather than `1.0`, and can clip the whole draw. Float4 has no missing components.
- **Fix:** short float32 vectors use the existing float shadow input, or CPU expansion with float `(0,0,0,1)` defaults when shadows are unavailable or disabled. Float4 keeps direct reinterpretation. `t_float_pad` checks signed/unsigned GPU fetch, CPU fallback and Float4 controls.
- **Found on:** [Xenoblade Chronicles: Definitive Edition](../../dm/games/0100FF500E34A000/README.md) (the game profile is a separate, pending contribution).

### CPU conversion of vertex attributes the input type cannot take

- **Symptom:** `Vertex attribute m_36(5) of type uint4 cannot be read using MTLAttributeFormatHalf2` (draws dropped: black silhouettes); UVs collapse onto one texel (flat tan characters); vertices far out of view (negative 16-bit values widened with zeros).
- **Cause:** `int_inputs` declares an input `uint` when its first translation only bit-casts it, and the shader then takes it back with `as_type<float>`. Maxwell's fetch converts by the format and the register holds the float32 bits, and widens integers by the format's signedness, whatever type the shader gives the register. Metal refuses a float-type format for an integer input; reading the raw bytes as integers gives denormals. For a signed format on an unsigned input `vfmt_for_ct` swapped `Short4` for `UShort4`, which keeps the 16 bits but widens them with zeros, so every negative value read as 32,768–65,535.
- **Fix:** the replay converts such attributes (half, unorm / snorm 8 and 16, 10:10:10:2, 11:11:10, BGRA8 into an int / uint input) on the CPU into float32 bits, four components with the fetch's (0, 0, 0, 1.0) defaults, in the frame arena, and binds them as UInt4 / Int4 on buffer slots 1–12 (`vconv_bind`, once per range and layout per submission; red line 89). 8- and 16-bit integer formats whose signedness differs from the input's go through the same conversion (`vconv_int` in `nvn_mtl.m`, red line 96 once): 32-bit values extended by the format's own sign, missing components (0, 0, 0, 1) as integers.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem: Three Houses](../../dm/games/010055D009F78000/README.md): KTGL's character and soldier shaders, fed a Half2 (UVs); also [Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity](../../dm/games/01002B00111A2000/README.md)'s soldiers (flat tan). The ground under close-up battle cameras was flat steel blue: the terrain's vertex shader (`56778feb1cfdee7e`) reads its grid position from an `RGBA16I` attribute (x and z the grid cell, y the height, which spans the whole int16 range), and so does the side pass that draws the river surface into targets of its own (`db725c85ca060403`).
- **Note:** [shadow vertex inputs](#shadow-vertex-inputs) (`dmsh_vin.py`) have since replaced both conversions; they remain for the formats shadow inputs cannot take (and for transform feedback draws and `DM_NVN_VCONV_CPU=1`), and the profile-side answer for one shader is [Integer-typed vertex inputs fed normalized formats](shaders.md#integer-typed-vertex-inputs-fed-normalized-formats).
- **Note:** how the steel-blue ground was traced, since two steps misled: where the ground was missing nothing wrote the depth (reversed-Z, cleared to 0), the lighting takes depth 0 for sky and paints the fog colour, and the never-cleared G-buffer colour targets kept an old image there (cobblestones of a mesh under the terrain, which from the map camera showed through the gaps like a road). A `HILITE` colour over the gap was stale too (the never-cleared albedo), and the end-of-frame depth showed 0 there. Recomputing every draw's clip positions from `CBDUMP` data showed that no draw of the shader reached the gap; then the side pass's layer, which showed only scattered tiles of the river, led to the vertex format. Ruled out on the way: depth clamp (Metal's clip and clamp modes gave the same depth), the pass without a depth attachment (142595 is a screen-space decal pass: it samples the depth and writes albedo and normals), polygon offset (none), dropped draws.
- **Note:** to confirm in Three Houses: 64-pixel rectangles in the albedo where decals land. The decal pass (`417a523053457352`, slots 16 and 18) and the lighting (`094a2d11253f126c`, `f54830a75e07e14a`, slots 18–20) read their decal and light lists from texture buffers that the translation declared `texture2d`, because those handles have no PC type (`texture type mismatch: shader declares 2, texture is 9`); skipping the decal pass removed the rectangles. Since row wrap ([Buffer textures read by 1D integer fetches (row wrap)](#buffer-textures-read-by-1d-integer-fetches-row-wrap), general) they read the real lists: slot 18 holds 1,052,640 elements, 65 rows. On the Mac prologue route the torches and burning houses now light the battle map and the close-up ground (they lit nothing before), and the blood decals take the scene's light instead of showing as dark blue patches. The rectangles did not appear on that route in either build (no 64-pixel structure in the close-up frames), so the scene where they were seen still has to be checked.

### More than 16 vertex attributes
