- **Cause:** a full clear with no draw after it waits on the current targets for the next pass that attaches them, as its load action. Copies land such clears first, but a dispatch only ended the open pass. So a clear before a dispatch landed after it. The game resets its auto exposure by clearing the exposure image to 1 and dispatching the shaders that read and write it as an image: the late clear overwrote the exposure they had just written, and adaptation then climbed back from 1.
- **Fix:** `dispatch` calls `rp_flush` as `copy_t2t` does. A dispatch moved ahead of a pass (`cs_hoist_try`) already waits for no pending clear. The cost is one clear-only pass where a game clears targets and dispatches before drawing to them.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md): 7 s after boot on the same route, exposure 3.035 and still climbing with 63 % of the frame near white, then 5.555 and settled with 0.1 %. The user's battle scenes, blown white before, look right after it.

### `[[primitive_id]]` in a specialized Metal 4 pipeline

- **Symptom:** a regular lattice of dots over the ground, and the light wrong: three of four layers of an interleaved ambient occlusion array stay empty, while the draws and their bindings look right.
- **Cause:** a Metal 4 pipeline specialized from a base with unspecialized colour state gives the fragment shader `[[primitive_id]]` 0 for every primitive. The same shader built whole counts properly. Measured in small test programs (16384 0 0 0 pixels per layer, against 4096 each), M6, macOS 27.2. The game writes the layer from the primitive ID, drawing four triangles. Ruled out: `gl_Layer`, view layers, clears, and the classic and Metal 4 API alone. This is a platform behaviour the host cannot change.
- **Fix:** the reflection records `reads_prim_id` (`raster.primitive_id`, `dmsh_build.py`), and `pso_build_ex` builds such a pipeline whole. Such pipelines give up the specialization's compile time, for these shaders only.
- **Prebuild:** `pre_desc` (the prebuild of a game's shader pairs) and `pso_offline.py` (the offline archive) leave pairs with such a fragment shader out, as no draw uses their base.
- **Check:** `t_image_primitive_id` draws four full-screen triangles that store into the layer of their primitive ID through the host's draw path (an attachment-less pass, a metallib loaded as game shaders are). Each of the four layers holds its own primitive's value; without the flag, layers 1 to 3 stay empty (macOS 27.0.1, M3 Pro). `test_pso_offline.py` checks that such a pair is left out.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md): `b6726be4afc44691`.

### Every start after play captures the whole pipeline archive again

- **Symptom:** the start after a play session spends minutes in the prepare step ("Preparing shaders") with MTLCompilerService at 700-980% CPU, and only a few bases are new: `pipeline prepare: 30838 lines (…) in 713.42 s on 10 workers before the first frame, progress screen shown; bases 262 compiled, 8346 from the archive, 637 reused`, then `pipeline archive compacted: 14 files of bases with a vertex layout gave 8543 bases to the files just written and were removed`.
- **Cause:** play writes a run file per flush (the timer's, 10 s after a burst of new bases; 13 to 273 in an Ocarina of Time session), and a compaction leaves a file per `ARCH_PERIOD_MAX` bases, 5 here. A few files of play bring the count over `PSO_ARCH_COMPACT`, and with every run file in the compaction its prewarm captures every base it takes from them again from the original shader functions ([the #342 workaround](shaders.md#a-compaction-flush-that-crashes-then-crashes-every-start)), a full compile each: 9 files of play beside a compaction's 5 made the next start capture 8,543 bases again. A start 6 minutes after a compaction captured the same bases again in as long (654 s against 669 s). From the code, not seen: a compaction of more than 16,000 bases writes more than `PSO_ARCH_COMPACT` files itself and compacts again at every start.
- **Fix:** a run file's name holds how many pipelines it holds (`<seq>-<count>.mtl4a`), and compaction takes files by size tier, `PSO_ARCH_COMPACT` times the one below (under 8 pipelines, 64, 512, then up to a full period): when a tier holds more than `PSO_ARCH_COMPACT` files, its files and those of every smaller tier are compacted (`arch_compact_pick`), and a base found in another file is used as it is (`arch_compact_takes`). Only the compacted files are removed. A full period's file is never compacted, since a compaction would write it again as it was. A file named before, or with a count that is not all digits, counts as the largest tier below a full period, so an earlier compaction's full files stay; a pile of more than 8 such files is compacted once, as before. Cost, from the rule alone (a test runs 200 sessions of 30 files of 6 pipelines): a start captures again about what the last session added, 506 pipelines on average, where the old rule took the whole archive; now and then the tiers above it fill and merge: 16,380 pipelines at sessions 91 and 191 in that run, about 19 minutes of compiler work on this Mac's 10 workers by the 0.7 s figure (not measured). How large such a start can be depends on what play brings: a start takes every file of the tiers it compacts, however many. At most 17 files below a full period stayed open after any start in that run (a merge can leave 9 in the tier above until the next start), each about 0.06 ms of a lookup that misses in it. A compaction never removes a full file, so one whose binaries a system update makes miss stays open until the archive folder is removed (from the code, not seen).
- **Check:** `t_pso_archive`: which files a start compacts, by tier, from their names (5 full files beside 9, 30 and 273 files of play, 9 full files, old and malformed names, each tier boundary), and 200 sessions by the rule alone; a compaction of two full periods' files and nine of one base captures only the nine again and keeps both full files, and the next start takes every base with none compiled; a compute pipeline in a file left out is not captured again. a merge that makes a ninth file in the tier above, taken at the next start. `test_pso_archive.py`, in fresh processes: the same compaction and the start after it; nine files named before the count compacted once, then not again. In the game (Ocarina of Time on an M4 with 16 GB, macOS 27.2, main bd979ced with eed7c429, copies of the device archive with a 30,838-line list, stopped at the title): a compaction's 5 files and 9 files of play (16, 19, 4, 7, 12, 5, 7, 6 and 11 pipelines, 5 under 8 and 4 under 64, so no tier overflows), with 262 bases new to the list: 713.42 s capturing 8,543 bases again before, 23.58 s compiling only the 262 with the change, and 2.35 s the start after. An earlier revision of the rule, the same for these files: after a real 17-minute session (29 files) the next start took 24.9 s capturing 255, the one after 3.0 s; an archive of 547 files named before compacted once, 669 s, then 3.5 s.
- **See also:** [Metal's archiver store filled by one flush period](#metals-archiver-store-filled-by-one-flush-period).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on an M4 (16 GB, macOS 27.2 26B123).

### Metal's archiver store filled by one flush period

- **Symptom:** hundreds of `[RED] background render pipeline creation failed: error: cannot commit transaction no. '50463' on '…/com.apple.gpuarchiver/gpuarchiver-…/PersistentState': MDB_MAP_FULL: Environment mapsize limit reached` (or `cannot write 'UNITSMAPPINGS' database: MDB_MAP_FULL`), then `pipeline archive: compaction left the old files (the flush failed)`; the draws of those pipelines are missing and the frame rate falls (Ocarina of Time's Kakariko Village at 30-70 ms a frame, 1,502 such lines in one session).
- **Cause:** a pipeline the run captures for its archive is compiled through the flush period's Metal 4 compiler, bound to that period's pipeline data set serializer (`ep->comp`, `arch_capture`). Metal keeps each serializer's state in an LMDB environment of its own, a `gpuarchiver-*` folder under `com.apple.gpuarchiver` in the user cache folder, with a fixed map size of 10 MB (the `PersistentState` meta page). Once it is full every compile through that compiler fails, not only the capture, and the host took the error as the pipeline's: the pipeline was never built, and the period's file was not written either. A period had no size limit while a compacting prewarm ran: the timer's flushes waited for the prewarm's end so that the recaptured bases would make one file, and a prewarm of 40,000 rows put 8,693 bases into one period. Periods of 3,508 bases were written whole.
- **Fix:** a period is handed over at its 2,000th capture (`ARCH_PERIOD_MAX`, `arch_flush_from`), during a compacting prewarm too, so each gets a fresh environment; a compaction now writes a file per period and removes the old files once every period that may hold recaptured bases is written, and keeps them when one was not written or a base was not captured again. A pipeline the capture refuses is built by the plain compiler (`g_comp4`), since the archive is a cache, never a condition of drawing; the failing period is handed over once, and a failure in a period that has captured nothing (so not a full store) stops capturing for the run, said in red. A pipeline both compilers refuse returns the plain compiler's error, and costs a second compile.
- **Check:** `t_pso_archive`: concurrent bases with a failing capture compiler all get a pipeline and the period is handed over once; a failure in a new period stops the captures; both compilers refusing returns the error; a compaction with a period of 4 writes a file per period and keeps the old files until its last period is written. `t_pso_stall`: a base Apple's compiler cannot build fails on both compilers and leaves capturing on. In the log, `pipeline archive: capturing a render pipeline failed after N in its flush period`.
- **See also:** [Metal's build folders left by pipeline archive flushes](shaders.md#metals-build-folders-left-by-pipeline-archive-flushes): the folders stay on the Mac; they take disk space but do not fill later runs' stores, each period making a new one.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on an M3 Pro.

### An archive period written after the compiler service died

- **Symptom:** every start on an archive aborts a few seconds in, during the pipeline prewarm, with `failed assertion 'Mesh Render Pipeline Descriptor Validation meshFunction must not be nil.'` and `CRASH sig=6`; the backtrace runs `mtk_build` → `arch_lookup` → Metal's archive lookup → `-[MTLMeshRenderPipelineDescriptor validateWithDevice:error:]`. The run that wrote the archive had `Compilation failed due to an interrupted connection: XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED` lines and `[RED] pipeline archive: capturing a render pipeline failed after N in its flush period`, and `~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports` has `MTLCompilerService-*.ips` reports.
- **Cause:** Apple's compiler crashed on one shader, and the compiler service took every compile it held down with it, a capture of the current flush period among them. Since the hand-over of a failed capture's period ([Metal's archiver store filled by one flush period](#metals-archiver-store-filled-by-one-flush-period)), that period was written: its serializer holds pipelines whose functions never came back, their keys in its index. A later start's lookup of such a mesh pipeline made Metal's archive build a descriptor with no mesh function, and Metal aborts on it instead of failing the lookup.
- **Fix:** upstream NXNative `9bd8bb386`, adapted. A capture marks its period `crashed` before leaving the period, independently of its plain fallback's result. The host's other compiles (plain pipelines through `plain_rps` / `plain_cps` / `plain_spec`, the host's own through `host_cps` / `host_rps`, the geometry mesh pipelines) go through `compile_done`, which calls `arch_died` on a compiler-service interruption even when the caller ignores the error (`err_compiler_died`: the GPU-family error domain and generic code 2 do not identify it, so the XPC interruption text does). `arch_died` marks and hands over the current periods (the draws' and the prebuild's) and advances the death count; `arch_write_file` never writes a crashed period or one made before the latest death, including periods already handed over. Its unwritten counts are drained and a compaction keeps the old files; the next start captures its pipelines again. Not ported: upstream's drop of a period while a plain compile is still unresolved at its serialization gate's bounded wait (NX 18's serialization gate, [A pipeline compile crashes while an archive period is written](#a-pipeline-compile-crashes-while-an-archive-period-is-written), came later without it: its bounded wait lets the period be written all the same), so a plain compile that reports the death only after its period was written cannot recall that file. The compiler crash itself remains open in Apple's compiler. Previously poisoned files need separate recovery: remove the period file named in the old failure log (`<seq>-N.mtl4a` and its `.keys`), or rebuild the archive.
- **Check:** `t_pso_archive` (`crashed_period`): a capture the service died under has its period marked, handed over and not written, its counts drained, and the next period captures and is written; a death reported through `compile_done` by a compile that is not a capture does the same, and an ordinary error does nothing.
- **Found on:** upstream's Ocarina of Time on an M2 (the archive without the bad period starts and plays; that period alone aborts at every start).

### Two captures of one shader function crash in Metal's binary cache

- **Symptom:** the pipeline prebuild dies with SIGSEGV (the NSP script's tool prebuild: `[RED] dmhost exit code -11`) and no `CRASH` line of its own, sometimes seconds into the run and not on every run. The crash report has `objc_msgSend` ← `MultiLevelAirntBinaryCache::getElementAtDestination` (+60) ← `-[MTLCompiler compileFunctionRequestInternal:…]` ← `AGX::Compiler::compileProgram<AGX::VertexProgramKey…>` ← the host's capture (`arch_capture_ex` before the fix) ← `pre_pair`; other prebuild workers wait in `-[_MTLBinaryArchive getBinaryDataForKey:reflectionType:]` under the same function, and no thread writes an archive (`airntSerializeToURL`).
- **Cause:** in Metal. A capturing compile looks its program up in its flush period's archive and messages the entry `getBinaryDataForKey` returned without holding it; another compile that captures the same program at that moment replaces and frees the entry. The prebuild handed its pairs out in list order, and consecutive pairs often share a vertex shader (Ocarina of Time's lists: 18,308 of 34,649 pairs use the previous pair's, up to 996 in a row; Super Mario Odyssey's up to 201), so several workers compiled one vertex program at once. The prewarm and prepare step, the background builds and the draws could also capture one vertex function on several threads at once (its bases with other pixel shaders or vertex layouts).
- **Fix:** a workaround for Metal's race, ported from upstream ([NXNative 75758b940](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/75758b940277673609384d5b7a0957c055b60d59)): `fn_claim` in `nvn_mtl.m`. Captures that share a vertex-stage function (a render pipeline's vertex function, a mesh pipeline's object and mesh functions, a compute pipeline's kernel; by its library's bytes and entry name) never compile at the same time (`arch_capture_try`, `arch_capture_cs`), whichever flush period they capture into: the prebuild's own (`pb` files) and the draws' alike, since nothing shows Metal's binary cache is not shared between them. The fragment function is not claimed: every crash compiled a vertex program, and claiming Octopath Traveler II's few shared pixel shaders held its prebuild to about one compile at a time upstream (17 s → 118 s). A capture that cannot claim its functions never goes ahead beside the other:
  - The prebuild does not wait beside it: it puts the pair back with its functions and takes another, and a worker takes a pair it put back, before a new one, as soon as none of its functions is under way (`pre_later_take`). Once the list is handed out, the workers wait for the functions of the pairs still put back, with no time limit: what they wait for is a compile under way, which holds its own worker as long.
  - The prebuild hands a vertex shader's pairs out one after another, the shader with the most pairs first (`pre_order`), so each shader's run starts at once and falls in few flush periods.
  - The price: a shader's pairs are captured one after another, so a prebuild takes at least as long as its largest shader's pairs one at a time. A list where one vertex shader has most pairs is prebuilt about one capture at a time: STEINS;GATE RE:BOOT's 474 of 483 pairs, Rhythm Heaven Groove's 32 of 33, Baba Is You's 55 of 115 (with captures of equal cost, STEINS;GATE's prebuild takes 474 captures' time where an even share is 483 / 16: 15.7 times as long on an M4 Max's 16 workers, 5.9 on the iPhone's 6). Among the large lists, Kirby and the Forgotten Land's largest shader has 3,148 of 21,409 pairs, more than an even share on 16 workers (1,338) but not on 6; the others' longest runs stay below it (Ocarina of Time 205 of 28,178, Super Mario Odyssey 201 of 20,740). `t_pso_race` with STEINS;GATE's list shape, grouped (synthetic shaders, M4 Max, 16 workers, other builds running): 3.4-3.6 s against 0.6 s unguarded with `--work 20`, 5.6 s against 0.9 s with `--work 60` (where 6 of the 429 captures that waited, on the draws' path, gave up after 2 s). It falls on the Mac tool prebuild of these games' NSP Mac builds and on a first start's prepare step when the bundled archive does not cover the list; no game's prebuild has been timed with it.
  - Every other capture (the draws, the prewarm and prepare step, the background builds) waits up to 2 s and then builds the pipeline with the plain compiler, which captures nothing; the archive gets it at a later start. So a compile that does not return ([A compile that does not return stalls every flush and draw](shaders.md#a-compile-that-does-not-return-stalls-every-flush-and-draw)) holds back the captures of its own functions by 2 s each and races none of them. NX 18's own: the wait lends its thread's QoS to the threads whose captures hold the function (`fn_lend`, as `pso_pend_take` lends it to a build it waits for), so a draw's capture on the submit thread does not wait at user-interactive QoS for a later draw's capture running at utility QoS on `pp_bg_queue` (NX 18 puts the look-ahead's later builds there), which iOS tends to run on the efficiency cores, and then build its pipeline again with the plain compiler.
  - NX 18's own: a compaction's recapture and a harvest (a compute or mesh pipeline captured after its first use) have their pipeline already, from the old file or from the plain compiler, so one that cannot claim its functions builds nothing again and is only not captured. Upstream built it again with the plain compiler, and in a compaction that base then failed the whole compaction: the old files stay and NX 18's `compact_failed.tsv` holds the next compactions back on that system build ([A compaction that fails is tried again every start](shaders.md#a-compaction-that-fails-is-tried-again-every-start)). In a prewarm task and on the harvest queue such a capture waits as long as the captures of its function keep returning, and gives up only once none has returned for 10 s (`FN_PATIENT`, `g_fn_patient_ms`; a compile that does not return): a prewarm's pair tasks that share a vertex shader run at once on the prepare step's workers (Super Mario Odyssey's lists: runs of up to 11) and take turns at about 0.8 s a capture (Ocarina of Time: 8,543 bases captured again in 713 s, [Every start after play captures the whole pipeline archive again](#every-start-after-play-captures-the-whole-pipeline-archive-again)), so with 2 s in all the fourth of them gave up. A draw's recapture (a draw that takes a base from an old file while a compacting prewarm runs) still waits 2 s. The compaction's red line then says how many bases fn_claim held back (`…; N bases because another capture of their shader function did not return in time (fn_claim)`).
  - NX 18's own: a library counts by a digest of its file's bytes (`lib_sum`, taken when a capture first claims one of its functions, once per file), not by its name: Metal keys a program by its content, and two shader keys whose libraries are the same bytes (shared sources compile alike, from standard input: [A library that depends on its source's file name](shaders.md#a-library-that-depends-on-its-sources-file-name)) are one program to it. Upstream's library name (`lib_tag`) is that digest; NX 18's names the file, for the archive's index, so by name such keys were two claims, and the prebuild, which groups pairs by shader key, captured both at once on two workers.
  - NX 18's own: the prepare step waits for the pairs put back too, not only for every pair to be handed out, but once every pair is handed out and no pair has finished for 10 s (`PRE_PREP_IDLE_MS`), it leaves them to the background with the compile they wait for, so such a compile still never holds the game's start. A prebuild the process's end cuts short writes no `prebuilt.tsv` and removes none of its earlier files (`pre_trim`: the pairs it did not reach were not looked up in them), so the next start builds what is left. Once the process's end stops it (`mtl_warm_stop`), no worker takes a pair put back, even one whose function has just come free: upstream's `pre_later_take` looked at the stop only after the list, so each capture that returned freed its shader's next pair and the workers built the whole run put back beside the archive's write, into a period the exit had handed over (a quit during Ocarina of Time's "Preparing pipelines" could then end with `not all written in time`).
  - The log: `pipeline prebuild done: …; N times a pair was put back while another capture had one of its shader functions, M captures so far waited for one, K of them built without the archive (fn_claim)` (and `cut short by the process's end` when it was); the prebuild tool's last line the same two counts; the first capture built without the archive, one line (`a render capture waited 2000 ms for another of the same shader function: built without the archive`); the prepare step's `no shader pair finished for 10.0 s` when it left pairs to the background.
- **Check:** upstream, Ocarina of Time's tool prebuild (`DM_PSO_PREBUILD=tool`) from a cold archive on an M4 Pro (macOS 27.2.1, 34,649 pairs, 14 workers): main 644-664 s and 9.2 GB of archive in four runs (it crashed with this stack in one of six), the change 493-496 s and 8.5 GB in two runs, no crash, every base built. Octopath Traveler II in `regress.py check`: prebuild 18.7 → 18.8 s, its prepare step's prewarm lists 0.93 → 1.63 s (captures of one vertex function waiting for each other), the same frames and pictures. Tried there: claiming the fragment function too (113 s for Octopath Traveler II); the pairs spread across the list (707-713 s and 10 GB, every period holding nearly every shader); giving a pair up after 2 s (223-282 pairs left to the draws). In NX 18 (M4 Max, macOS 27.3): `t_pso_stall` checks the claim, the wait, the fallback, the putting back, a prebuild waiting for a capture under way and stopping without a stamp or a trim, the order, and the prepare step waiting for the pairs put back and stopping after its limit; `t_pso_archive`, a base with a held compile's vertex function built by the plain compiler after the wait; `t_pso_race` stresses the capture path with real Metal and measures the waits (it has not reproduced the crash, with shaders this small); `t_pso_archive` (`compaction_claim`), a compaction's recapture whose vertex function other captures hold: in a prewarm task it waits through three that return 150 ms apart (limit 200 ms) and is captured, outside one it keeps the old file's base after 200 ms with nothing built by the plain compiler, and the compaction keeps the old file and writes `compact_failed.tsv` (9 of its checks fail with upstream's recapture); `t_pso_stall`, a copy of a vertex library under another shader key is the same claim (6 checks fail by name). A game's prebuild with the change has not run on the Mac or the iPhone yet.
- **Found on:** upstream: [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on an M2 Max, three prebuilds of three ([#815](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/815)), and one of six on an M4 Pro; [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md) 1.9.0 once on an M4 Pro (macOS 27.2.1). Not seen in NX 18's own runs.

### A cold start leaves a file per timer flush, and the next start compacts them all

- **Symptom:** the start after a cold start spends minutes in the prepare step ("Preparing shaders"), with MTLCompilerService at 700-980% CPU, yet the log says no base was compiled: `pipeline prepare: 30838 lines (…) in 761.61 s on 10 workers before the first frame, progress screen shown; bases 0 compiled, 8608 from the archive, 637 reused`, then `pipeline archive compacted: 80 files of bases with a vertex layout gave 8859 bases to the files just written and were removed` ([issue #1747](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1747): a cold start of 457 s, then a warm one of 309 s with 0 compiled).
- **Cause:** the timer's flush (10 s after a burst of new bases) ran during a prewarm that does not compact, so a cold start wrote a file every 10 s: 80 files on Ocarina of Time here, 43 in #1747. More than `PSO_ARCH_COMPACT` run files make the next start a compaction, and its prewarm captures every base it takes from them again from the original shader functions ([the #342 workaround](shaders.md#a-compaction-flush-that-crashes-then-crashes-every-start)), a full compile each. A capture counted neither as compiled nor apart from the lookups, so the prepare line read 0 compiled.
- **Fix:** the timer's flushes wait for the prewarm's end whether it compacts or not (`arch_flush_from`), as they already did during a compacting one, so a prewarm writes a file per `ARCH_PERIOD_MAX` bases. The prepare line says how many bases were captured again, `pipeline bases so far` also their time. What a prewarm holds unwritten is written when the player quits (`mtl_arch_save` from `atexit`, for ⌘Q, closing the window and `exit()`: it waits up to 10 s for the period's compiles and the writes, a file of 900 bases having taken 1.8-4 s; `dm_gfx_quit` in the graceful exit, within its 1.5 s) and starts writing when the app goes to the background or the window is minimized (`dm_gfx_suspend`; on iOS a background task holds the app up to 25 s for those writes, `archive_background` in `front_ios.m`). A write the exit cuts short leaves no `compacting` marker, which would turn compaction off on that system build (only this run's marker goes; a crashed run's stays); on iOS no write arms one while the app is in the background (from `dm_gfx_suspend(1)` to `dm_gfx_suspend(0)`, `arch_mark_bg`), and one armed when it goes there is removed, since iOS may end a suspended app with `SIGKILL`. A start removes `*.mtl4a.tmp` files older than ten minutes, left by writes cut short; their sequence numbers count meanwhile, so no later write takes the name of one still there. Only a process killed in its prewarm (a crash, `SIGKILL`) still leaves up to a period's captures unwritten (`ARCH_PERIOD_MAX`, plus compute pipelines still queued), built again at the next start. A cold start of more than `PSO_ARCH_COMPACT` x `ARCH_PERIOD_MAX` (16,000) bases still writes more than `PSO_ARCH_COMPACT` files, and the next start compacts them (from the code, not seen).
- **Check:** `t_pso_archive`: no `compacting` marker while in the background (`arch_mark_bg`), one again in the foreground, and going to the background removes a write's; the `.tmp` sequence numbers and leaving unopened files out of `compact_failed.tsv` are from the code, untested. Nine new bases in a prewarm with a timer flush after each make one file per full period of 4 and one for the rest, 3 files, no more than `PSO_ARCH_COMPACT`; a compaction counts its recaptured bases and their time. `test_pso_archive.py`, in fresh processes: that cold start, then a next start that compacts nothing and builds nothing; a cold start killed in its prewarm after 6 bases (`_exit`), which leaves the full period's file alone, so the next start takes those 4 and builds the other 5; and one that quits there (`exit()`), which writes the other 2 as well, so the next start takes all 6 and builds the other 3, leaving an earlier run's `compacting` marker in place. In the game (Ocarina of Time on an M4 with 16 GB, macOS 27.2, main bd979ced with eed7c429, copies of the device archive's prebuilt files with a 30,838-line list, stopped at the title): before, the cold start took 861.94 s writing 80 run files and the next start captured 8,859 pipelines again, 761.61 s before the first frame; with the change the cold start took 716.20 s writing 5 files (4 full periods and the rest) and the next start 3.30 s, with no compaction (one run each, nothing else running; an earlier pair with a build overlapping both cold starts: 854 and 725 s, then 736 and 3.67 s).
- **See also:** [Metal's archiver store filled by one flush period](#metals-archiver-store-filled-by-one-flush-period).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on an M4 (16 GB, macOS 27.2 26B123); #1747 on an M4 Pro.

### SIGTERM during the first Metal initialization

- **Symptom:** SIGTERM during startup logs `received SIGTERM: graceful exit`, then `CRASH sig=5` in `_os_object_release` from `mtl_init`. The caller can be the graphics drain or the guest's first NVN memory pool registration.
- **Cause:** the shutdown worker calls `mtl_drain` while pool registration can call `mtl_init` on the guest thread. Both callers can see a null `g_dev` and enter initialization. Replacing `g_feedback_q` releases the queue the other initializer published, and the trap occurs at that release. A non-null device also became visible before the queues and residency set were ready.
- **Fix:** guard the complete shared Metal initialization with `dispatch_once` (`mtl_init_once`). Every caller of `mtl_init` waits for the initialized state before proceeding. Ported from upstream (`4f4bfb6a7`).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_mtl_init` pauses the first device request while the real graphics suspend path runs. It checks for a single device request and waits for both callers to finish with the graphics state ready. Upstream's classic-fallback case is left out: NX 18 has no classic fallback.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata The End of YoRHa Edition](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md), upstream.

### A run that ends in its prewarm loses what it compiled

- **Symptom:** a run without a window (headless, or `DM_PSO_PREPARE=0`) compiles the same few dozen to few hundred bases again at every start, and area loads stall on them (a draw waits in `pso_get` → `arch_capture` → `MTLCompiler`), although each run compiled them before. The run's last lines say `pipeline archive at exit: 425 pipelines not written yet, not all written in time: those left are built again at the next start (968 ms)`, or `[RED] graceful exit not done in 1.5 seconds, exiting at once` with no archive line at all.
- **Cause:** since [A cold start leaves a file per timer flush](#a-cold-start-leaves-a-file-per-timer-flush-and-the-next-start-compacts-them-all), the timer's flushes wait for the prewarm's end, and a period is written only when full (`ARCH_PERIOD_MAX`, 2,000) or at exit. With a window the prewarm is the prepare step, which ends before the first frame; without one it runs beside the game, and Ocarina of Time's (31,663 lines) outlives a play session, so everything a session compiled waited for the exit. The graceful exit (`SIGTERM`, and `oe::ExecuteProgram`'s hand-over) gave the archive what was left of 1.5 s, while a quit gives it 10 s and writing takes 1.8–4 s per 900 bases. When a draw was still compiling its pipeline, the drain (`dm_gfx_suspend`) waited on that draw, and the 1.5 s fallback ended the process before anything was handed over. A harness that stops a run for "no new frame" sends `SIGTERM` exactly then. The prewarm also went on compiling during the exit, beside the write and into a period no one wrote.
- **Fix:**
  - During a prewarm the game plays through (not the prepare step, not a compaction), the timer's flush hands the period over once it is `DM_PSO_ARCH_WARM_FLUSH_MS` old (60 s by default, `0` turns it off). Each later one waits twice as long, up to 16 times, so a cold start adds about one file per doubling, not one per 10 s. While captures wait, the timer looks again every 10 s. There is no cap by count: a period handed over at 512 captures would be a file of the top compacted tier, and a cold start's 8,600 bases in 16 of them would make the next start compact them all again.
  - Every end the host controls stops the prewarm and the pairs' prebuild first (`mtl_warm_stop`: what they had not built is built at the next start) and gives the archive the same time, `DM_QUIT_ARCH_MS` (10 s, `MTL_QUIT_ARCH_MS` on the graphics side): atexit (⌘Q, closing the window, `exit()`), and the graceful exit (`hle_lifecycle.c`). The graceful exit starts the write first (`dm_gfx_quit_begin`), drains graphics on another thread and waits for that at most 1.5 s, then waits for the write; a red line says when the drain did not end, and the archive is written all the same. `oe::ExecuteProgram` starts the write before the iPhone's 1.5–3 s notice (`hle_oe.c`).
  - A draw that is still compiling its pipeline holds its period, so that period is written only if the compile returns within the time; the periods before it are written either way. Past the time plus 0.5 s, a deadline ends the process, and starts the next program if the exit had not, so a stuck exit does not turn a program switch into a quit; one flag lets only one of the two run the continuation (`execve`).
  - Files are still written as `.tmp` and renamed after their index, so a write the deadline cuts short leaves the files already there valid; a start removes a `.mtl4a.tmp` a stopped write left once it is ten minutes old (a younger one may be another copy's write under way), and an index whose file never came.
- **Check:** `test_pso_archive.py`, in fresh processes. Five new bases in a prewarm beside the game, then the timer's flush after `DM_PSO_ARCH_WARM_FLUSH_MS=300`, then `_exit`: one file, and the next start takes all five and compiles none (before: no file, five compiled); the same in the prepare step leaves no file. `SIGTERM` in a prewarm, with and without a draw that holds the graphics layer (`DM_TEST_STUCK_DRAW`: `dm_gfx_enter` with no leave): one file, all five taken at the next start (before: none with the draw held). A hand-over (`dm_host_shutdown_then`) with that draw held: the next program starts after the write and finds the file. A torn write's `.tmp` and an index without its file, beside a file that is not an archive: the leftovers are removed (the `.tmp` dated eleven minutes back), the bad file is skipped, and all five bases come from the good file (before: the `.tmp` stays). The deadline's path (past 10.5 s) has no test. In upstream's run (not measured with this host), Mario Golf: Super Rush headless on an empty archive, each run ended by `SIGTERM` (2026-11-15): during a cold pair prebuild, 1,890 bases written at the exit in 5.3 s; with `DM_PSO_PREPARE=0`, 929 in 4.7 s; with `DM_PSO_ARCH_WARM_FLUSH_MS=5000`, the timer's flush handed the period over at 10 s and the exit waited for its write (367 bases, 6.1 s), the prewarm stopped at 1,396 of 2,573 lines, and 1 capture made after the hand-over stayed unwritten; the next start took 360 bases from those files and compiled only the 360 of lines the run had not reached, and its exit wrote 381 pulling bases in 8.5 s. The 1.3 s before would have written none of them.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) on an M4 Pro (macOS 26.6), headless: every warm-up run ended with 400–500 bases not written, and the next runs compiled 57–167 bases and missed the archive 57–256 times ([PR #2625](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2625)).

### A pipeline compile crashes while an archive period is written

- **Symptom:** the pipeline prebuild (the NSP script's tool prebuild, `[RED] dmhost exit code -11`), or a run that captures pipelines, dies with SIGSEGV and no `CRASH` line of its own. The crash report has `objc_msgSend` ← `MultiLevelAirntBinaryCache::getElementAtDestination` ← `-[MTLCompiler compileFunctionRequestInternal:…]` ← the host's capture, and a thread on the `MTLBinaryArchive_Serialize` queue in `airntSerializeToURL` ← `arch_write_file` (unlike [Two captures of one shader function crash in Metal's binary cache](#two-captures-of-one-shader-function-crash-in-metals-binary-cache), where no thread writes).
- **Cause:** in Metal. Serializing a flush period's archive frees entries of Metal's compiled-binary cache that a compile under way through another compiler (the next period's, or the plain one) still reads. Upstream's three crashes (Super Mario Party's prebuild of 39,111 pairs on an M4 Pro, macOS 27.2, after 4,000 to 22,000 pairs) each had a serialization under way. NX 18 had no guard: each period has its own compiler, so a period's write never waited for the next period's compiles.
- **Fix:** ported from NXNative cc8f44906 ([#2513](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2513)): `ser_begin` / `ser_compile_begin` in `nvn_mtl.m`. A serialization waits for the compiles under way and holds new ones back until it is written, the writer first. It waits at most 2 s (`SER_WAIT_MS`) for those under way: a compile that does not return must not stop every later one behind the waiting write, so the write then goes ahead beside it, with a line in the log. Every compile of the archive's paths goes through it (`arch_capture_try`, `arch_capture_cs`: the capture, and the plain compiles when the archive does not capture, when a capture gives up its function claim, or after a capture fails); NX 18's claim of a shader function (`fn_claim`) comes first. The write's line counts the compiles held back and their time (`compiles held back by the writes so far: N, M ms in all`). The price, upstream: the prebuild 15 to 25% slower; in 90 s of play one write of 199 bases took 474 ms and held nothing back. A write in play no longer stalls a draw: [A timed archive write stalls active presentation](#a-timed-archive-write-stalls-active-presentation).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pso_archive` (`t_pso_archive --ser-gate`): a period's write waits for a compile under way and is written once it returns; a capture started while a period serializes waits, with no compile under way beside the write, and goes on once it is written, counted as held back; a compile that does not return holds a write back about 2 s, then the write goes ahead. The default run's 429 checks and `check_t_pso_warm` as before.
- **Found on:** upstream's Super Mario Party (`010036B0034E4000`, [#2513](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2513)); not seen on NX 18. **Credit:** ported from NXNative cc8f44906 (MIT).

### A timed archive write stalls active presentation

- **Symptom:** with the serialization gate above, the title screen or a menu stops for over a second: a pipeline archive write, and compiles waiting for it, even where the frame did little new pipeline work (upstream: Mario Kart World's longest frames after ten seconds, 1,340 and 1,609 ms).
- **Cause:** the timer flushes a partial period 10 s after a burst of captures. Serialization and compilation must run apart, so a timed write in play blocks a compile the current frame needs.
- **Fix:** ported from NXNative f539b2dcd (PR #3060), upstream's change on top of the gate, with it. With a window (`mtl_has_window`), the timer (`arch_note_new` → `arch_hand_over(…, ARCH_FLUSH_TIMER)`) leaves the captures in the current period and looks again 10 s later while a frame was presented in the last 10 s (`dm_present_ns`) or a compile is under way; once both are idle it takes the gate at once (`ser_try_begin`, never waiting) and writes the period on the archive's queue there and then. NX 18's other paths are as they were: a full period (`ARCH_PERIOD_MAX`), a save (going to the background, the background archive write the iPhone gives its background time, quitting: `mtl_arch_save`, `ARCH_FLUSH_NOW`), the prebuild's own period, the compaction's close, the prewarm's timed flushes (`arch_warm_flush_due`) and a run without a window flush as before; archive admission is unchanged. Captures kept for a later write are lost only if the process ends with no save, as before with a write under way.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pso_archive` (`t_pso_archive --active-flush`, the timer driven by the test): idle with a compile under way, the period stays; presenting again, nothing is written and the capture stays pending; idle with no compile, written at once, with no retry after; a deferred write holds no compile back; a save and a full period write while presenting; without a window the timer writes as before.
- **Found on:** upstream's [Mario Kart World](../../dm/games/0400C3F00006E000/README.md) (title screen and menus). **Credit:** ported from NXNative f539b2dcd (MIT; co-authored upstream by Arthur tinseau).

### Archive lookups asked every file, so a few files made a start compile every base again

- **Symptom:** a start after some play spends minutes in the prepare step and the log says `pipeline archive compacted: N files of bases with a vertex layout gave M bases to the files just written and were removed`: Ocarina of Time on an M4, 9 files of play beside a compaction's 5 made the next start capture 8,543 bases again, 713 s before the first frame (PR #1880); after a cold start's 80 files, 762 s ([A cold start leaves a file per timer flush](#a-cold-start-leaves-a-file-per-timer-flush-and-the-next-start-compacts-them-all), [issue #1747](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1747)).
- **Cause:** a lookup asked every file of the pipeline archive in turn, about 0.06 ms for each file the pipeline is not in, so every flush's file made each later lookup dearer, and more than `PSO_ARCH_COMPACT` (8) run files made a start a compaction. A compaction compiles every base it takes from the old files again from the original shader functions, because importing them through lookup archives crashes Metal ([the #342 workaround](shaders.md#a-compaction-flush-that-crashes-then-crashes-every-start)), a full compile each. So a rule that saved a few seconds of lookups (80 files: about 4.8 ms per miss, 2-4 s over a 9,000-base prewarm on 10 workers) cost minutes, and play, writing a file per timer flush, brought it on after a session or two. [Every start after play captures the whole pipeline archive again](#every-start-after-play-captures-the-whole-pipeline-archive-again) limits what a compaction takes; this entry is the lookups that made the file count matter.
- **Fix:** an index beside each archive file (`<file>.keys`, `arch_write_file`): the keys of the pipelines in it, and whether every pipeline in it has one. A key digests what the pipeline's Metal 4 descriptor sets (`arch_key`): its functions by library (the file it came from, under the shader folder: `lib_from_file` names it), entry name and constant values (by value: Metal has no getter for them, but their class describes and hashes them by value), the vertex layout, the colour attachments and every other field. A start reads every index into one table, newest file first, and opens the files in parallel (`pso4_init`); a lookup (`arch_lookup`: render bases, compute, task / mesh pipelines, the prebuild) asks the file the index names alone, and a miss asks none. A pipeline with no key (a library with no name: embedded kernels, diagnostic overrides) and a file with no index (written before it, or renamed) go through the files without a complete index, as before. Which files a compaction takes stays the size-tier rule (`arch_compact_pick`; `arch_compact_takes` now finds a file among every run file, `g_arch_run`, indexed or not), and a compacted file's index goes with it: the file count no longer costs lookups, only the start's opening of the files (in parallel) and the disk, which the tiers bound. A key the index names a file for is looked up there alone: a miss (an OS update that changed the GPU compiler, a library changed under the same name with a new chain) compiles it, and the new, newer file holds it from the next start on. A field the host starts setting on a pipeline descriptor must go into the key too, or two pipelines that differ only there share one, and one of them is compiled and written again at every start. The key names no chain, so the index also carries the chain each file was written under, and a start removes a file of another chain without opening it ([Pipeline archive files of an older shader chain opened at every start](memory.md#pipeline-archive-files-of-an-older-shader-chain-opened-at-every-start)).
- **Check:** `t_pso_archive`: the first file's index holds its base's key, complete; the same pipeline gives the same key, another layout another, constant values by their values whatever the order they were set in, and a library with no name no key. `test_pso_archive.py`, in fresh processes: after a cold start's 3 indexed files the next start asks one file for each base it finds and none for the 5 it does not (also after a cut-short and a quit cold start); a compaction of 9 one-base files leaves one file with its index and removes the 9 with theirs; files renamed to the names of before lose their index and are asked in turn (the tier tests). In the game (ŌKAMI HD on an M4 Pro, macOS 27.2, its package split again for keyed records, a 152-line prewarm list, a fresh archive, two starts each): the first start compiled its 41 bases into one indexed file; a second start that asked for the same pipelines took all 41 from the archive (twice; and a third start, after a second had written the 4 it lacked to a file of its own, took 41 of 41). Some second starts compiled 2, 4 or 8 again: those pipelines paired the same pixel shaders with another vertex library (`a7537d4f1786cfda` where the first start used `c59f90…` and `b555f7…`), so no file held them and asking every file would have missed them too; which vertex library ŌKAMI HD's prewarm takes varies between starts (open, not from the index).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (PRs #1879 and #1880, issue #1747).

### A compacting start captured every old base again before its first frame

- **Symptom:** a start after some play takes 9-10 s on an iPhone, most of it the prepare step, and the prepare line counts bases captured again: `pipeline prepare: 4963 lines (…) in 8.41 s on 6 workers before the first frame, progress screen shown; bases 83 compiled, 1804 from the archive (447 of them captured again for the compaction), 320 reused`, then `pipeline bases so far: … (447 of them captured again for the compaction, 37241.4 ms)`, and later `pipeline archive compacted: 46 files of bases with a vertex layout gave 447 bases to the files just written and were removed`.
- **Cause:** a compaction ([size tiers](#every-start-after-play-captures-the-whole-pipeline-archive-again)) captures every base the prewarm takes from the files it replaces again from the original shader functions ([the #342 workaround](shaders.md#a-compaction-flush-that-crashes-then-crashes-every-start)), a full compile each, and it did so inside the prepare step's lookups, before the first frame: 37.2 s of compiles over 6 workers for 447 bases, 38.4 s for 866, where the lookups alone take 1-2 s. Metal 4 has no way to copy binaries between archives: `MTL4Archive` only looks pipelines up, a `MTL4PipelineDataSetSerializer` captures only what its compiler builds, and the one way to feed it an archive's binaries, `MTL4CompilerTaskOptions.lookupArchives`, is the import that crashed Metal (#342). So the captures stay; only their time moves.
- **Fix:** the prewarm takes those bases from the old files as any lookup does and only notes them (`compact_note`: the descriptor, one per index key; compute and task / mesh pipelines too, which the harvest queue used to capture again during the prewarm). The captures run after the start, in the background (`compact_step`, on one serial queue at utility QoS): once the first frame is presented (or after `PREPARE_LATE_SUBS` submissions in a run that never presents), the shader-only lines after it are loaded, no prebuild is under way, the bundled archive's crash guard is past its window (a Metal crash in a capture must not count against that archive), and at least 10 s after the prewarm (`COMPACT_START_MS`). It captures 8 at a time (`COMPACT_BATCH`) and then rests as long as the batch took, 1 s at least, so it uses at most half of one core and makes no burst in the first minute of play. It holds while the device is thermally serious or critical, in Low Power Mode, or in the background (iOS, `arch_mark_bg`), and goes on after. Everything the compaction guarded stays: the captures go into the draws' flush periods, so `ARCH_PERIOD_MAX` still hands a full one over; every period handed over from the pass's first capture to its close is a compacting one (`arch_flush_slot`, the crash marker around its write); the old files go only once the last of them is written (`compact_close`, `arch_compact_finish`), and a base not captured or a period not written keeps them and writes `compact_failed.tsv`; each capture claims its vertex-stage function (`fn_claim`, `FN_PATIENT`), so it never compiles beside another capture of it, and a draw that wants that function waits for the one capture under way, lending it its QoS. The timer's flushes wait while it captures, as they wait for the prewarm, so its bases make as few files as they can, and still hand a period over once it is `DM_PSO_ARCH_WARM_FLUSH_MS` old (60 s, then doubling), since a pass runs minutes into play beside the draws' own new bases; while it is held they go on as usual. A quit stops it (`mtl_warm_stop`; the exit writes the period under way); the old files stay, nothing is lost, and the next start notes what is left (the bases already captured are found in the newer files first). The log: `pipeline archive compaction after the first frame: capturing N bases again from M files (…)`, `… held: …` and `… goes on after … s`, `… N of N bases captured again in … s (… s of captures), the old files go once its last period is written`, then `pipeline archive compacted: …` as before; the prepare line and `pipeline bases so far` say `(N of them noted for the compaction after the first frame)`.
- **Check:** `t_pso_archive`: a compacting prewarm of 9 one-base files captures nothing (no capture before the first frame, the prepare line counts 9 noted), the pass waits for the first frame and holds while hot, captures original shader functions (no archive imports), hands a full period of 4 over, holds the timer's flush while it captures and lets it through in Low Power Mode, keeps the old files until it closes and then removes them, the new files holding every base; compute and task / mesh pipelines are noted and captured by the pass; the size-tier compaction captures the small files' bases alone; a pass capture whose function another capture holds waits while they keep returning and, held past `g_fn_patient_ms`, keeps the old file and writes `compact_failed.tsv`. `test_pso_archive.py`, in fresh processes: nine files, a start that quits after 5 of its captures leaves the nine beside two new files with no failure note or marker, the next start notes the 5 that are left (the 4 in the full period's file are in no tier) and removes the old files, and the one after compacts nothing; the compaction with a fresh base in its period, the old-name and the tier restarts as before. On the phone, not measured yet: from the two logs, the prepare steps would drop from 8.41 s to about 2.2 s (Fortune's Weave: 37.2 s of the 48.4 s of base work over 6 workers) and from 7.70 s to about 1.3 s (Smash: 38.4 of 39.9 s), the starts from 9.32 s and 9.74 s to about 3.1 s and 3.3 s, with the captures afterwards on one utility thread for a few minutes.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md) and [Super Smash Bros. Ultimate](../../dm/games/01006A800016E000/README.md) on an iPhone 18 Pro Max (build `29dcbb4d`, X2.0 round of 2026-11-16).

### A specialization descriptor iOS 26.0 returns autoreleased

- **Symptom:** with Metal 4 pipelines (then `DM_PSO_MTL4=1`, now always on), the iOS app crashes before its first frame, right after `pipeline prewarm list … lines`: `CRASH sig=11 pc=objc_release+0x10`, `bt: objc_autoreleasePoolPop warm_task __warm_prepare_block_invoke`, on several prepare workers at once. The Mac runs the same pipelines.
- **Cause:** `newRenderPipelineDescriptorForSpecialization` promises a +1 object by its name, so ARC releases it once at the end of `pso_build_ex`. On iOS 26.0 (23A341) the driver returns it autoreleased. On an iPad Pro M4 the method is `-[AGXG16GFamilyRenderPipeline newRenderPipelineDescriptorForSpecialization]` in `AGXMetalG16G_B0`. So ARC's release freed it, and the warm task's pool released it again. Under LLDB on the iPad:
  - the descriptor sits in the autorelease pool when the call returns (`_objc_autoreleasePoolPrint`);
  - `NSZombieEnabled=YES` reports `-[MTL4RenderPipelineDescriptor release]: message sent to deallocated instance`.
  That one release freed it, so the driver keeps no reference of its own. macOS 27.0 (26A428, M4 Pro) returns it retained. There, the direct call made the same prewarm's 34 specializations without a crash.
- **Fix:** `spec_desc` (`nvn_mtl.m`) finds out on its first call how the driver returns the descriptor. A weak reference survives the call's own autorelease pool only if the caller owns the descriptor. After that it takes the descriptor the way the driver gives it, so the caller owns exactly one reference either way. The answer holds for the process: every base comes from the one device's compiler or its archives. The `pipeline bases so far` line names it: `their descriptors returned retained` or `returned autoreleased`. `t_pso_stall` specializes through it too.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=01005CA01580E000 check_t_pso_archive` runs `spec_desc` against two stand-in bases, one that returns the descriptor retained and one that returns it autoreleased. On the first call and on later ones, the descriptor outlives every pool and is freed once dropped. The previous direct call dies on the autoreleased stand-in. Axiom Verge with `DM_PSO_MTL4=1`:
  - on the iPad, it crashed on every start before the fix. Now it runs at 60 fps: the cold start compiled 18 bases and made 25 specializations, and later starts take the bases from the archive. The log says `returned autoreleased`;
  - on the Mac, it says `returned retained`.
- **Found on:** Axiom Verge 1.0.3 (`0100052004384000`, its profile a separate change) with the launcher's **Metal 4 pipelines** on: an iPhone 16 Pro Max (iOS 26.0, LiveContainer) and an iPad Pro 11-inch (M4, iPadOS 26.0, from Xcode and in LiveContainer) crashed the same way.

### A specialized Metal 4 pipeline keeps its base's fixed state in its descriptor

- **Symptom:** with Metal's API validation on (`MTL_DEBUG_LAYER=1`), the game stops at a draw in a multisampled pass: `-[MTL4DebugRenderCommandEncoder setRenderPipelineState:]: failed assertion 'Render Command Encoder Set Render Pipeline State Validation The color sample count (4) does not match the renderPipelineState's color sample count (1) The raster sample count (4) does not match the renderPipelineState's raster sample count (1)'`. With `MTL_DEBUG_LAYER_ERROR_MODE=nslog` the game plays on and logs the pair at every such bind. Without validation nothing is wrong on screen.
- **Cause:** `pso_build_ex` specializes a base, whose colour state is left open, with the descriptor `newRenderPipelineDescriptorForSpecialization` returns. That descriptor holds the colour state open, but its fixed state is at its defaults (1 sample, alpha to coverage off, no input primitive class, amplification count 1), whatever the base was built with. The specialized pipeline is described by that descriptor, so the debug layer reads a pipeline of a 4x pass as a 1-sample one. The pipeline key, the pass and the guest's state were right: NVN rasterizes at the target's sample count, as the host's key does (`g_pass.samples` from the attachments).
- **Fix:** `pso_fixed4` (`nvn_mtl.m`) writes a pipeline's fixed state (sample count, alpha to coverage, rasterization, input primitive class, amplification count) into a Metal 4 descriptor. `pso_desc4` builds the base with it, and `pso_build_ex` writes the same state into the specialization descriptor before the colour state (NX 18's `pso_col4_of`, which gives a pipeline with no pixel shader no colour writes), so a specialization always says what its base was built with. Ported from upstream `5b71bf9bd`.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=0100E95004038000 check_t_pso_msaa`: the production pass setup and pipeline lookup in a 4x depth-only pass, a 4x colour and depth pass (also with alpha to coverage) and a 1x pass, through MTL4Compiler in both encoding modes (`DM_NVN_ENC_THREAD=0` and `1`), each bound and drawn under API validation, with the pipeline archive off (every pipeline compiled in the test). The pipeline reads as the pass's sample count and keeps the draw's alpha to coverage, and a 4x pipeline with a triangle input class and amplification count 2 (a layered, slice-merged pass's) keeps all three through `pso_build`; without the debug layer, which alone reports a pipeline's descriptor, those checks print a skip line. Without the fix the Metal 4 runs stop at the first bind; with only the sample count and alpha to coverage copied, the input class and amplification count checks fail. NX 18 ran it on the Mac (34 checks a run); a run on the iPhone is still to do.
- **Found on:** [PRAGMATA](../../dm/games/0400C76024710000/README.md), in upstream's run: three depth-only pipelines of its 4x depth passes (fragments `36a5f8e07d4b90d2`, which writes depth from a texture, `ccfad64e9842a35c` and `f2ff0a146037ecae`) stop validation at the first gameplay frame; 11,067 such binds in a 128 s nslog run.

### NVN2's DLSS on MetalFX

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game calls `nvnCommandBufferEvaluateDlss` and its builder functions, which the host did not have.
- **Cause:** NVN2 has DLSS as a part of the API, a neural network that runs on NVIDIA's GPU, with no counterpart on the Mac.
- **Fix:** `hle_nvn.c` takes the builder, the network and the parameters (the network's texture IDs and storage are the driver's own needs and are dropped), and answers `GetOptimalSettings` from the method (0: 2x, 1: 3x, 2: 1x). The replay's `OP_DLSS` runs MetalFX's temporal scaler (`dlss_eval`, `nvn_mtl.m`) over colour, depth and motion vectors, with the jitter and a history reset, falling back to a bilinear draw when there is no scaler. NGX's motion-vector flags set the scaler's (macOS / iOS 27): without `MVLowRes` (1 << 1) the vectors are at the output's size (`outputResolutionMotionVectorsEnabled`), and `MVJittered` (1 << 2) has the scaler subtract the jitter from them (`jitteredMotionVectorsEnabled`); a Mac on 26.x takes them as unjittered at the input's size, with a red line. This replaces the upscaler, so the picture is close to the game's, not the same.
- **Encoding thread:** with `DM_NVN_ENC_THREAD` (the iOS default) the replay's command buffer is the recording's stand-in, which has none of MetalFX's calls: the host stopped at the first evaluation (`deferred encoding: DmRecCB has no stand-in for -useResidencySet:`). The scaler's state and its encode are a block run on the encoding thread with the real command buffer (`rec_cb_block` in `nvn_rec.h`), as for [a game's own upscaler](#a-games-own-upscaler-drawn-by-metalfx). Check: `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=040074A01BF12000 check_t_dlss_mfx` (two evaluations through one scaler in a submission, the thread off and on). Not run on a game with the thread. The scaler's encoders take no barrier: `mfx_order` orders them after the work before them and the next draw pass after them ([An encoder of barriers alone orders nothing](#an-encoder-of-barriers-alone-orders-nothing)).
- **Bias mask:** DLSS's bias-current-colour mask (`nvnDlssParametersSetBiasCurrentColorMask` and its subrectangle base) is MetalFX's reactive mask: both run from 0, the temporal history as usual, to 1, the current colour alone. The scaler is made with `reactiveMaskTextureEnabled` and the mask's format (a part of its key), and each evaluation gives it the texture and its corner; a mask too small for the input is not used, and a format MetalFX makes no scaler for is dropped with a red line, keeping the scaler. Before, the mask was only logged (`[RED] DLSS: the game's bias mask (a reactive mask for MetalFX) is not passed on`). Upstream NXNative 23790f8dc (PR #1904): Star Fox's mask is R8 at the input's size, 960x540, with 0 or 255 only: 21-27% of the picture across its prologue, a band at the top and the bottom (about 57 rows each, the 2.39:1 frame inside 16:9) and the near cockpit and ship edges that cross into it, and 0 on every character; shots on an M4 Pro with the mask passed and not show no visible difference, and neither shows trails. Check: `check_t_dlss_mfx` moves a checkerboard one pixel with motion vectors of 0, not reset: on an M4 Max 0.171 from a reset's output with the mask at 0, 0.054 at 1, and 0.171 at 1 with the mask dropped (upstream's Mac: 0.164, 0.021). Not yet run in NX 18 on a game.
- **Open:** the jitter's sign has not been checked; no ghosting has been seen. A game whose bias mask covers what it draws, not only a band, has not been run.
- **Found on:** [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md): `gfxa::TaskDLSS`, 1024x576 to 2048x1152. [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md): the bias mask.

### The DLSS network resized

- **Symptom:** `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnDlssNetworkResize (x0=… x1=0x300 x2=0x1b0)` at the title.
- **Cause:** NVN2 lets a game resize a DLSS network instead of making a new one. The replay takes each evaluation's sizes from its parameters and textures, so the network keeps nothing of it.
- **Fix:** `nvnDlssNetworkResize` succeeds, logging the first size (`hle_nvn.c`).
- **Found on:** [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md): 768x432, once; its evaluations run 768x432 to 768x432.

### MetalFX's DLSS stand-in preserves alpha

- **Symptom:** a later pass that composites an RGBA DLSS output sees opaque alpha, whatever alpha the input colour held. RGB is correct and no validation or compilation error is logged.
- **Cause:** MetalFX's temporal scaler writes alpha 1.0. The host used its output directly as NVN2 DLSS's RGBA result, so an input alpha of 0.25 or 0.75 became 1.0.
- **Fix:** upstream NXNative `1db4bcb28`. When the output format has alpha, `dlss_eval` gives the input shader-read usage and the output render-target usage, then draws the input alpha over MetalFX's RGB with an alpha-only write mask (`dlss_alpha_pso`, `copy.metal` `fsa`), on the real command buffer after the scaler. It uses the evaluation's input rectangle and the scaler's own output extent: the record's output size is the original network builder's and stays as it was after `NetworkResize` (upstream: taking it skipped the alpha in Star Fox's resized network). `fsa` clamps its taps to the input rectangle's texel centres, so padding around a dynamic or offset input never blends into its alpha. If the alpha pipeline cannot be made, the bilinear stand-in, which keeps every channel, is drawn instead. Output formats without alpha are unchanged.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_dlss_mfx`: alpha 0.25 and 0.75 through two evaluations, a padded 768x432 input in a 1024x576 texture (every output pixel, edges and corners) and an original builder size larger than the output, with the encoding thread off and on and with `DM_NVN_BARRIER=game`.
- **Cost:** upstream measured the alpha draw on an M6 at RGBA16F 960x540 to 1920x1080: a median 0.246 ms GPU and 5.7 microseconds of CPU encoding per evaluation. Not measured on an iPhone.
- **Found on:** upstream's Fast Fusion (the vehicle selector's background and interface hidden) and [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md) (the resized network).

### DLSS scalers released under their encoders

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game using DLSS crashes after minutes of play, at a moment it makes a DLSS network again or evaluates one at new sizes (`[nvn] DLSS network …` and a new `DLSS: MetalFX temporal scaler …` line, for example a 1920x972 output), with `*** Terminating app due to uncaught exception 'NSInvalidArgumentException', reason: '-[AGXG17XFamilyMLContext executable]: unrecognized selector sent to instance …'` in `-[_MTL4MachineLearningCommandEncoder encodeToCommandQueue:]` under `rec_commit` / `mtl_sub_commit` (the host exits with 134). The class named varies with what took the freed memory, and many runs pass.
- **Cause:** `dlss_scaler_for` originally kept one MetalFX scaler per network in 4 slots. `nvnDlssNetworkFinalize` told the replay nothing, so the slots were never freed, and the fifth network always took slot 0, dropping its scaler at once (`e->sc = nil`). The same happened when a network's sizes changed. A Metal 4 command buffer does not retain what is encoded into it: when that scaler had been encoded earlier in the same submission, the commit used a freed object. A standalone program that releases a scaler between its encode and the commit fails the same way, and keeping the scaler until completion makes it pass. The networks were also named by their addresses, so one made again in the same memory with the same sizes kept the old network's scaler and history.
- **Fix:** a retired or evicted scaler is held until the submission completes (`dlss_scaler_drop`, `defer_obj_release`), and when the cache is full a newcomer takes the slot used least recently. `nvnDlssNetworkInitialize` gives each network a serial, which its evaluations carry instead of its address; `nvnDlssNetworkFinalize` now retires every cached scaler of that serial on the replay thread behind the next submission's fence (`mtl_dlss_forget` / `dlss_retire_drain`). `dm/gfx/tests/t_dlss_scaler.m` checks the slots, LRU eviction, finalize retirement, holding and serials; `t_dlss_mfx.m` drops encoded scalers before commit under zombie objects, which fails every time without the holding.
- **Found on:** [Fast Fusion](../../dm/games/040041E0212AE000/README.md), at its 8th network, after about 9 minutes of play (PR #1479).

### MetalFX temporal scaler async initialization aborts and dynamic-resolution texture switches

- **Symptom:** on an M2 Pro (macOS 27.3 beta), a Switch 2 game whose dynamic resolution creates multiple DLSS scalers aborts during early evaluations with MPSGraph's ANE region asserting `ANE inference operation failed` (`Code=-19`, `inferenceCount 96` on the 97th evaluation); with `MTL_ERROR_MODE=nslog` the GPU command buffer waiting on that inference times out and macOS revokes GPU access. Separately, games whose dynamic resolution switches between separate input textures of different sizes stall for ~80–88 ms every few seconds on every resolution step.
- **Cause:** two issues in `dlss_scaler_for` (`nvn_mtl.m`). First, `MTLFXTemporalScalerDescriptor.requiresSynchronousInitialization` defaults to `NO`, so MetalFX returns an interim scaler and swaps in a compiled ANE/MPSGraph scaler asynchronously; that external MetalFX/MPSGraph background swap failed on M2 Pro / macOS 27.3 beta. Second, keeping only one slot per network evicted and rebuilt the scaler on every size step. NX 18 keys a scaler on the evaluation's input content size (`iw`, `ih`; upstream keys the textures' common box and scales the content inside it, which NX 18 has not ported), so in NX 18 every content size a dynamic resolution steps to is a key of its own.
- **Fix:** set `d.requiresSynchronousInitialization = YES` so MetalFX builds the final scaler synchronously (avoiding the broken async swap path). Key the cache by `(network serial, key)` across `DLSS_KEEP` LRU slots (12 on the Mac as upstream; 6 on an iPhone, where each kept scaler's history stays resident) instead of one slot per network, tracking the network's active slot (`cur`) so returning to a kept size sets `fresh = 1` and resets temporal history (matching a rebuild without paying compilation). `nvnDlssNetworkFinalize` calls `mtl_dlss_forget` so finalized networks' slots are retired via `defer_obj_release` in `dlss_scaler_for` and `mtl_sub_commit` instead of pinning GPU memory across the larger cache.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_dlss_scaler check_t_dlss_mfx`: `t_dlss_scaler` (17 checks) checks `DLSS_KEEP` slots, LRU eviction, keeping and returning to an earlier size with a history reset, staying without re-resetting, bias-mask keys, and `nvnDlssNetworkFinalize` retirement; `t_dlss_mfx` (29 checks) checks keeping an earlier size beside a new one and mid-submission finalize retirement under `NSZombieEnabled=YES` with the encoding thread off and on.
- **Open (NX 18):** with synchronous builds and NX 18's content-size keys, a game whose dynamic resolution steps through more content sizes than the slots hold stalls the replay at each new one (upstream measured about 80 ms a build on an M2 Pro); not measured on the phone. Upstream ported from `e9bcc5f35`.
- **Found on:** Xenoblade Chronicles: Definitive Edition – Nintendo Switch 2 Edition (`0400FF500E34A001`, 2.0.0; M2 Pro, macOS 27.3 beta, [PR #3373](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/3373)): synchronous init ran 2,697 evaluations to the time limit where async init aborted at evaluation 97; caching by `(network, descriptor)` reduced a 5-minute field run from 191 scaler builds (190 frames of ~88 ms) to 4 builds and zero stalls.

### A game's own upscaler drawn by MetalFX

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's frame in its heavy scenes is dominated by one compute dispatch between the render-resolution passes and the output-resolution ones: Donkey Kong Bananza's upscaler takes 3.5-5 ms of a 24 ms GPU frame on an M1 in handheld mode (960x540 → 1920x1080, `DM_NVN_GPUPROF`), and the game cannot reach its 60 fps interval there.
- **Cause:** the upscaler is the game's own network (agl's NDSR, `agl_ndsr.pck`), written for the Switch 2's tensor cores: its HMMA matrix products are emulated ([Ampere HMMA through simdgroup_matrix](shaders.md#ampere-hmma-through-simdgroup_matrix)), and its shared-memory matrix loads move their row addresses between lanes with shuffles (41 % of its time on the M1). MetalFX's spatial scaler does the same job, the same sizes, in 0.97 ms.
- **Fix:** a swap the player chooses, since the look changes; the iPhone and iPad launcher picks MetalFX by default (2026-11-15: Bananza's GPU time a frame from 13.5-15.5 ms to about 10.7 ms on an iPhone 18 Pro Max, and Star Fox, Wonder, Mario Kart World and Bananza checked by eye), the Mac launcher and the host without a launcher keep the game's. The game's upscaler shaders are the compute shaders its shader map found in an NDSR pack, a source ending in `#NDSR@<offset>` (`upscaler_keys` in `dm/games/game.py`, the header's `DM_GAME_UPSCALER_KEYS`). The pack is a library several engines ship under their own names, so the rule names none: agl's `agl_ndsr.pck` (Bananza: 24 variants, each one texture in at slot 0 and one image out at slot 1; Splatoon Raiders, Nintendo Switch Sports Resort: 20; Mario Kart World: 30) and its siblings `agl_ndsr_vr.pck` and `agl_ndsr_vr_light.pck` (Ocarina of Time: 37 more), Kirby Air Riders' and Fast Fusion's `.ndsr` files (the same 20 networks in both), Star Fox's pack nested in a `VIPR` container (Splatoon Raiders' 20), and the pack inside nn::ndve's movie file (Ocarina of Time: 2; the movie's own networks, `#NDVE@`, are not named). In NX 18's shader maps: Ocarina of Time 22 → 61 keys, Star Fox 0 → 20, Bananza 24 and Mario Kart World 30 unchanged. Shaders with the same key are the same code, so a pack shared by several games classifies once for all of them. Naming a shader only lets the host read its colour; the colour and the dispatch checks below decide whether MetalFX draws it; the launcher shows **Game's upscaler** (`DM_UPSCALER`: `game` by default, `metalfx`) only for games that have some. With `metalfx`, `up_mfx_encode` reads the two rectangles from push-constant bank 24 (`S.push[1]`). Each rectangle contains an origin and an inclusive last texel relative to that origin. The Metal 4 spatial scaler processes the input in the colour mode the shader's code shows ([An upscaler's colour read from its code](#an-upscalers-colour-read-from-its-code)). Each side is the game's own texture where MetalFX can take it as it is: the rectangle covers the whole texture, its format is not sRGB, and the texture has the usages MetalFX asks for. The input goes in its own format (the scaler is made in it; a format MetalFX refuses falls back to RGBA16F; `DM_NVN_MFX_DIRECT=0` keeps RGBA16F only), unless its colour is square-rooted, which only the copy squares. The output must be private and RGBA16F: MetalFX's own R11G11B10F writes come out about two steps of the format's mantissa low (2.0 as 1.94, 0.5 as 0.484, where the RGBA16F output drawn back gives 1.98 and 0.496), about 2.4 % darker everywhere. Any other side goes through the scaler's own texture, made on first use: `copy_t2t_draw` draws the input rectangle into an RGBA16F texture at the origin, or a second draw places the result in the output rectangle, a render pass each. Ocarina of Time's network reads square roots in RGB9E5 (format 93) and writes R11G11B10F (1440x810 → 1920x1080 handheld), so both its copies stay: the input's squares the colour, and the output's avoids MetalFX's dark R11G11B10F writes. Upstream measured on an M6 (a harness of 1,200 such dispatches through `up_mfx_encode`, four alternating runs, medians) 340 µs a dispatch with both copies, 316 µs with the input straight, and 271 µs with both straight; [PR #2559](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2559) measured both going in game on an M1 at 0.64 and 0.97 ms a frame less, so the input alone is an estimated 0.2-0.4 ms there. Not yet measured on NX 18. Since Ocarina of Time's network is square-rooted, `DM_NVN_MFX_DIRECT` changes nothing there: its A/B needs a named network with linear colour and a whole input that is not RGBA16F, and none of the owner's games is confirmed to have one yet. Ocarina of Time's own phone A/B is `DM_UPSCALER=metalfx` against `game`: picture and battery. On the encoding thread the recording holds a block that encodes the scaler into the real command buffer (`rec_cb_block` in `nvn_rec.h`; [NVN2's DLSS on MetalFX](#nvn2s-dlss-on-metalfx) goes the same way). Four scalers (sizes, colour mode and formats) are cached for dynamic resolution and refused formats. Upstream keeps eight; NX 18 keeps four, since each slot can hold an input and an output texture of its own and eight could keep twice the scratch memory. A slot whose sides are the game's own textures holds none, so straight paths now keep less than before. Invalid rectangles, shrinking or equal-sized pairs, indirect dispatches and unsupported bindings or scaler sizes keep the game's shader.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=040074A01BF12000 check_t_upscaler_mfx`: a named shader's 960x540 → 1920x1080 dispatch fills the image through MetalFX. A second dispatch converts a cropped R11G11B10F input with a highlight above 1.0 and checks every pixel inside and outside an offset output rectangle. A slow pass writes the input just before the scaler and a pass copies the image right after it, 8 rounds ([An encoder of barriers alone orders nothing](#an-encoder-of-barriers-alone-orders-nothing)). It runs with the encoding thread off and on, once with `DM_NVN_BARRIER=game`, and once with `DM_NVN_MFX_DIRECT=0`. Whole R11G11B10F textures: the input is taken as it is, and the R11G11B10F output is drawn back with its mean within 1.5 % of the colour (MetalFX's own write fails that), into an RGBA16F image both sides go straight, and into an offset rectangle the output is drawn back. Equal sizes and an unnamed shader keep the shader, and without `DM_UPSCALER=metalfx` nothing changes (Metal validation stops MetalFX's Metal 4 scaler on a barrier setting of its own with no public API, so that run is without it). `test_game_profile.py` checks the keys taken from a shader map and `test_settings.py` that the launcher offers the choice to Bananza and not to a game without the network. **Open:** a standalone Metal 4 MetalFX HDR test on M1 intermittently returned non-finite pixels at small input sizes (64x36 and 320x180). The packed-format regression also saw an intermittent colour error at handheld resolution. Check picture quality and frame time against the game's network in a heavy area. The network's own cost is the translator's LDSM / MOVM emulation, open in [Ampere HMMA through simdgroup_matrix](shaders.md#ampere-hmma-through-simdgroup_matrix).
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): its networks are in `agl_ndsr_vr.pck` and `agl_ndsr_vr_light.pck`, which `UPSCALER_SOURCE` did not match, so the option did nothing there, not even a log line ([issue #1911](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1911)); with them named ([PR #1459](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1459)), `fa3033d05c0a41d6` is drawn by MetalFX, 1440x810 → 1920x1080, and upstream measured on an M2 Air the upscale went from 2.50 to 1.31-1.38 ms and the frame p50 from 27.5-28.2 to 25.5-26.7 ms (handheld, scripted Kokiri Forest route, two runs a side, on a host before the straight input of PR #2559; not yet run on NX 18). [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md): NDSR `7629d857a0a93833` in the heavy areas, 1676x943 → 2304x1296 docked (after three passes of its own at the render resolution, which stay), 960x540 → 1920x1080 handheld.

### A scaler the ring gave up left the residency set at once

- **Symptom:** none reproduced. Expected from it: a `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault` in a frame's last command buffer, with `DM_UPSCALER=metalfx` and dynamic resolution, as the resolution steps to a size not seen lately.
- **Cause:** the game's upscaler drawn by MetalFX kept `UP_MFX_SCALERS` scalers (4 in NX 18) in a ring filled in order of creation (`up_mfx_scaler_for`, `nvn_mtl.m`). A size the ring did not hold took the oldest slot, and the scaler there lost its input and output textures. They were released behind the next fence but taken out of the residency set at once (`res_remove`), so a command buffer recorded, held by the encode thread or on the GPU could still write and read textures outside the set.
