With dynamic resolution the oldest scaler made could be the one used a frame ago.
- **Fix:** evicted textures are retired through the deferred-release queue alone (`defer_obj_release`), which takes them out of the residency set once the submissions encoded up to then have completed, as every other retirement does. The scaler object goes the same way: it was released at once, and a Metal 4 command buffer does not retain what is encoded into it ([DLSS scalers released under their encoders](#dlss-scalers-released-under-their-encoders)). The cache also evicts the least recently used slot (`used` counter, matching `dlss_scaler_for`) rather than creation order so an active size is not evicted under dynamic resolution. Ported from upstream NXNative be38b7dad.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=040074A01BF12000 check_t_mfx_evict`, in both encoder modes: checks LRU retention of a recently used scaler, that both scaler-owned input and output textures stay resident and the scaler stays alive while a submission is open, and that the textures leave the residency set and the scaler is released after that submission completes. It never hands the GPU a texture outside the set. Fails without the fix (3 of 10 checks on NX 18). The test turns off the profile's bilinear stretch (`DM_NVN_UP_BILINEAR=0`) and touches the first scaler by its own key, since NX 18 keys a scaler with its own input on the output's size (`DM_NVN_MFX_CONTENT`).
- **Found on:** code reading for [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md)'s GPU address faults with this upscaler and dynamic resolution ([GitHub issue #1810](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1810)). Open: whether this is what faulted there, as it was not run on the game and a fault was not provoked (one restarts the GPU). An earlier report on the same game faulted with `DM_UPSCALER=game`, so other causes exist. NX 18's one scaler per output (`DM_NVN_MFX_CONTENT`) already made evictions under dynamic resolution rarer.

### An upscaler's colour read from its code

- **Symptom:** with `DM_UPSCALER=metalfx`, a game's picture is washed out wherever it upscales: lifted shadows and low contrast, the scene only, with the UI drawn after it correct; with `DM_UPSCALER=game`, or at a size the game does not upscale, it is right.
- **Cause:** the swap ran every variant of agl's NDSR network in MetalFX's HDR mode, taking input and output as linear. That is true only of some. Each variant shows its colour in its sRGB curves: Bananza's pack (header word `0x300`) encodes its linear input right after reading it (0.0031308, 1 / 2.4, as floats); Splatoon Raiders' pack (`0x40100`) has no such step, because the colour it reads is encoded already. Within each pack the output differs by variant: no decode at the end, so the output stays encoded (half of Splatoon Raiders', a third of Bananza's), or a decode to linear (0.04045, 2.4; in Splatoon Raiders' half-float code 0.0404358, 2.3984375 and 0.0774536 for 1 / 12.92). So MetalFX passed Splatoon Raiders' encoded colour through, into an image the following passes read as linear. Ocarina of Time's networks (`agl_ndsr_vr.pck`, `agl_ndsr_vr_light.pck`) have no sRGB curve and still convert: a pass before them square-roots the scene colour (gamma 2, `MUFU.SQRT`, into RGB9E5), and the network writes its result squared with its sign kept, `x * |x|` (FMUL in `_vr`, HMUL2 in `_vr_light`), into R11G11B10F. Taken as one colour space, MetalFX left that result root-encoded: about the square root of the right picture, pale and low in contrast (the picture's mean 146 of 255 against 92, its darkest 5 % at 63 against 10).
- **Fix:** the host reads the colour from each named shader's code when the program loads (`up_mfx_read_color` in `prog_get`, `up_mfx_color`): the curves' constants are immediates, whole bytes in the code, and count from 8 uses, far fewer than a curve per channel of every output tile. No profile key, no chain change, no shader rebuild. An encode and a decode in floats are linear input and output: HDR mode, as before. A half-float decode with no encode is sRGB input and linear output: perceptual mode into RGBA8Unorm (the network clamps to 0-1 before its decode, so the range is the same), and the draw into the game's image reads it through an RGBA8Unorm_sRGB view, which decodes it; always through the scaler's textures. Square-rooted input: at least 8 sign-keeping squares (`up_mfx_squares`: FMUL or HMUL2 of a register by itself, `|x|` on one side only; the real networks have 32 to 108), no power (`up_mfx_has_pow`: no `MUFU.EX2` or `MUFU.LG2`, which every curve takes wherever its constants come from) and no constant of a curve, each counted alone from 8 uses. The copy into the scaler's RGBA16F texture squares the input (`COPY_T2T_SQUARE`, `copy.metal` `fsq`), never the game's texture straight, and MetalFX runs in HDR mode, so values above 1 keep their range. Any other shape keeps the game's shader, with a log line: a variant whose output stays encoded, a network that squares and then encodes, and a variant whose code shows neither a curve nor a square, since nothing in it says what its colour is (Ocarina of Time's pre-passes square-root with `MUFU.SQRT` alone). Each colour has its own scaler in the size cache.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=0400380020056000 check_t_upscaler_mfx`: the classifier on synthetic code in each shape, and an sRGB input, full and as a packed subrectangle, comes out decoded, with linear input at the same sizes not decoded. The classifier against the translated shaders of all 44 variants of the two packs: no mismatch (Bananza 16 linear, 8 kept; Splatoon Raiders 10 sRGB, 10 kept). The same test checks the square-rooted shape on synthetic code (signs, lanes, RZ, a power or a curve's constant beside the squares), a square-rooted input in R11G11B10F and in RGBA16F that comes out squared through the copy. Upstream also ran the rule against the raw code of Ocarina of Time's 37 `_vr` and `_vr_light` compute shaders: 18 square-rooted (10 and 8; `fa3033d05c0a41d6` and `d99fbeeb53d38847`, which the game runs, among them), 12 that encode after the square kept, 7 pre-passes kept. Mario Kart World's 30 by the same rule, from their raw code: 20 linear, 10 kept. **Open:** Kirby Air Riders' and Fast Fusion's 20 (one set, another version of the library) have not been read, and Star Fox dispatches one of Splatoon Raiders' variants with no curve (`ddc729bcdb4c8df9`, [PR #2340](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2340)), which keeps its shader; an unknown shape keeps its shader, so the choice may do nothing there yet.
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md) ([#1156](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1156)), the first level with `c1b8edc09e3313b1` and `30d8ee33de9dee85`: the washed-out picture reproduced, and with the fix the sky and scene means match the game's upscaler within 0.6 of 255 per channel, against about 50 too high before. The square-rooted shape on [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md), upstream's runs, not yet repeated on NX 18: washed out on an M5 Ultra with the option on ([PR #1459](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1459)), reproduced on an M5 Pro, and with the squares the picture's means within 1.3 of 255 of the game's upscaler on every channel (darkest 5 %: 9.7 against 10).

### An encoder of barriers alone orders nothing

- **Symptom:** MetalFX scales a picture whose drawing has not finished (stale or half-drawn input), or the pass after a scaler reads its output before the scaler wrote it: one frame now and then where the GPU is busy. No game has been seen to show it; the tests below show it in every round.
- **Cause:** MetalFX's Metal 4 scalers open encoders of their own with no barrier, before or after their work, so `dlss_eval` and `up_mfx_encode` put an encoder before them with a consumer barrier (after the queue's stages, before Blit) and a producer barrier (after Blit, before the queue's stages). Those barriers order the encoder's own blits: with no work between them they order nothing. Upstream measured on an M4 Pro (macOS 27.2) a kernel reading what a slow kernel just before it wrote: stale in 20 of 20 rounds behind an empty encoder of Blit barriers, in none behind the same barriers around a one-word copy. On the replay's command buffer it rarely shows, because the encoders the host ends there carry a producer barrier of their own (`m4_end`, `enc_r_end_pass`); work that ends with no barrier (a scaler's own encoders, a pass ended bare) was not ordered. The present's scaler (`fx_encode`) already waited behind its real copy of the crop, and NX 18 already cleared the relaxed end (`g_last_end_relaxed`) after both scalers, so the pass after a scaler takes its consumer barrier.
- **Fix:** `mfx_order` (`nvn_mtl.m`, ported from upstream NXNative `b25b651b`), before the scalers of `dlss_eval` and `up_mfx_encode`: the encoder between the barriers holds a copy of one word of a 16-byte scratch buffer, which the barriers carry, and it clears the relaxed end. Cost: one 4-byte copy and one encoder per evaluation, nothing in games without DLSS or `DM_UPSCALER=metalfx`.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=040074A01BF12000 check_t_dlss_mfx check_t_upscaler_mfx`: a slow draw pass that ends with no barrier writes the scaler's input just before each evaluation or dispatch, and a draw pass copies its output right after (`t_mfx_passes.h`), 8 rounds, the encoding thread off and on, and once with `DM_NVN_BARRIER=game`. With the empty encoder both checks fail in every run (Apple M4 Max, macOS 27.3); with `mfx_order` none.
- **Found on:** no game; upstream's review of [PR #1541](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1541). It touches [Star Fox](../../dm/games/0400E88025B1A000/README.md) and [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave](../../dm/games/04007EF01F8AE000/README.md) (DLSS) and [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) with `DM_UPSCALER=metalfx`.

### Metal 4 does not order operations inside one encoder

- **Symptom:** tall blue / textured panels that flickered frame to frame: the fountain's vertex shader pushed the water surface to the top of the screen.
- **Cause:** the host batches consecutive copies, fills and inline uniform updates (`UpdateUniformBuffer`) into one compute encoder, and Metal 4 does not order operations inside one encoder. Mario writes the same uniform slot twice in a row each frame (4 bytes of −1, then 16 bytes of wave coefficients); without an intra-encoder barrier the writes occasionally landed in reverse order, the fountain's GPU wave simulation (a 512×1 RGBA16F texture updated by render → copy back → splash pass every frame) ran with −1 as its self coefficient and diverged to ±65504.
- **Fix:** `blit_buf` now inserts `barrierAfterEncoderStages` when an operation reads or writes a guest range an earlier operation in the same encoder wrote; texture copies always get one. Render passes have their own encoder with full barriers, so they were not affected; compute dispatches share the copy encoder and always get a barrier, as texture copies do (Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity, [Compute dispatches share the copy encoder](#compute-dispatches-share-the-copy-encoder)).
- **Check:** without the barriers in `blit_barrier` the stripes come back (the switch that turned them off, `DM_NVN_BLIT_ORDER=0`, has been removed).
- **Found on:** [Super Mario Bros. Wonder](../../dm/games/010015100B514000/README.md).

### Buffer copies through aliased Metal addresses

- **Symptom:** a buffer copy, inline update or fill completes, but a following copy through another Metal buffer over the same allocation reads stale bytes.
- **Cause:** Metal buffers over one allocation have distinct GPU addresses. Device visibility alone does not flush their alias caches. Sparse virtual pools also give the same physical bytes distinct guest addresses, so the copy encoder's guest-range dependency checks can miss a conflict entirely; barriers at encoder boundaries cannot order copies within that encoder.
- **Fix:** `blit_buf` marks encoders containing buffer copies, updates or fills; their start, intra-encoder and end barriers include `MTL4VisibilityOptionResourceAlias`. Operations touching a sparse virtual range (`nvn_sparse_alias`) receive conservative barriers against prior and subsequent operations, since a mapped run can span unrelated physical allocations. Ordinary buffers retain range-based batching. Texture-only and helper encoders keep their existing visibility options.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=010015100B514000 check_t_copy_alias` exercises two no-copy buffers over one allocation, physical-to-virtual, virtual-to-physical and virtual-to-virtual sparse aliases, copies, inline updates and both fill paths, within one encoder and across encoder boundaries, with direct and deferred encoding and Metal validation. On an M4 Pro, the original host failed 37 of 101 checks; [PR #200](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/200) alone left 24 failures from missed intra-encoder sparse dependencies; the complete fix passes all 101 in both encoding modes.
- **Found on:** synthetic regression using the sparse mapping mechanism of [Monster Hunter Rise](../../dm/games/0100B04011742000/README.md); the game and iPhone have not been rerun for this change. The no-copy buffer failure was reported by MacNativeGames (`803128e`) in PR #200 and reproduced here.

### Inline uniform updates inside a render pass

- **Symptom:** many render passes with one draw each and high submit time: Metroid Dread's cutscene frames had about 180 passes with one draw each (about 5 ms of submit time per frame on the Mac).
- **Cause:** the game updates uniforms inline (`CommandBufferUpdateUniformBuffer`) between draws, and every copy or update ended the host's render pass. This was correct but cost submit time.
- **Fix:** an inline uniform update inside a pass no longer ends it: it is deferred to the pass end (`DM_NVN_UB_DEFER=0` restores the old behaviour), and the draws after it read a shadow ([Partial-cover uniform shadows keep passes open](#partial-cover-uniform-shadows-keep-passes-open)); see [development.md, Power](../development.md#power).
- **Found on:** [Metroid Dread](../../dm/games/010093801237C000/README.md).
- **Note:** 04's Metroid Dread section (at `6a06877`) still described the old behaviour ("every copy or update ends the host's render pass"); the deferral came later (development.md's Power section), and it is now the default (`ubd_on` in `nvn_mtl.m`, off with `DM_NVN_UB_DEFER=0`).

### Partial-cover uniform shadows keep passes open

- **Symptom:** hundreds of render passes a frame and heavy attachment traffic when a game updates a small part of a large uniform block before each draw group.
- **Cause:** a draw whose uniform binding overlaps an update deferred in the open pass reads a shadow; when the deferred updates cover only part of what the shader can read, the rest must come from somewhere, and the pass used to end there (`nvn_mtl.m`, the comment above `ubd_on`).
- **Fix:** the shadows take the rest from memory (`ubp_get`, `DM_NVN_UB_PARTIAL`) and the per-shader read extents of `cb_extent.tsv` (written by a full `dmsh_build.py` run) keep those passes open. `DM_NVN_UBP_CHECK=1` compares every shadow with the GPU's memory.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md) (2026-10-21, the user's Great Plateau save, Mac M4 Pro): a frame had about 386 render passes and loaded and stored about 900 MB of attachments (on an iPhone at 60 fps about 55 GB/s, near the memory's bandwidth); the GPU waits for each pass to finish before the next. 220 of the passes ended only because the game updates the first 64 bytes of a 64 KB vertex uniform block (`0x70c38c0000`) before each draw group while its shaders read up to 544 bytes of it (the terrain pixel shader `eb0afe555b86da79`, which indexes the block with an integer made from an interpolated value, reads the whole block). With the fix: 386 → about 165 passes, GPU p50 about 9.3 → 6.5 ms, submit 5.7 → 4.9 ms; `DM_NVN_UBP_CHECK=1` found every shadow equal to the GPU's memory (968,000 checked).
- **Note:** a storage buffer bound with size 0 once made every partly covered shadow after it in the submission refuse ([A storage buffer bound with size 0 counted as a write anywhere](#a-storage-buffer-bound-with-size-0-counted-as-a-write-anywhere)). What still splits BotW's big passes (a `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` frame dump): the NintendoWare VFX GPU particles run one compute dispatch per emitter between draws (ten 800x450 half-resolution passes and two G-buffer passes, about 190 MB a frame; the G-buffer ones read the depth being drawn, so they cannot move ahead of the pass), and decals that sample the G-buffer right after the game sets it again. The decals sample targets of their own pass (Metal leaves that undefined; the Mac reads the stored content); `DM_NVN_FB_SNAP=all` would snapshot them at a large cost. Tried and dropped: `DM_NVN_TBDR=1` (since removed; 10–15% fewer stores, more CPU, red ledger lines, and it turns the deferred uniform updates off), `DM_NVN_BARRIER=game` (no gain), same-target continuation outside TBDR mode (the decals end the pass anyway).

### A storage buffer bound with size 0 counted as a write anywhere

- **Symptom:** a scene runs at 20–27 fps where similar ones hold 60, with the GPU time about three times its usual (draw counts unchanged). The `residency set` line shows `passes ended to land them for a read` in the tens of thousands, every one `refused … other GPU writes`, and about three times the usual `load-all store-all passes`. A burst of 1,200 such refusals at each world load is the short form.
- **Cause:** the partly covered uniform shadows ([Partial-cover uniform shadows keep passes open](#partial-cover-uniform-shadows-keep-passes-open)) refuse while another GPU write of the submission may have touched the block. The writes are kept by 4 KB page (`sbw_note`). A writable storage buffer whose size the host does not know counted as a write anywhere: it set `g_sbw_over`, and every partly covered shadow after it in the submission was refused, each refusal ending the pass. A size of 0 counted as unknown, so did a write over 16 MB. Breath of the Wild's GPU particle dispatch `9f8116baffddaf81` (`cs_effect.bin` variant 3, 16 bytes a particle) binds its three outputs with `nvnCommandBufferBindStorageBuffer(…, address, 0)`, inside a 10 MB memory pool; the uniform block the draws update (`0x70c38c0000`) is in another pool.
- **Fix:** such a binding now counts as the rest of the Metal buffer its address falls in (`sbw_len`): a device pointer that runs past its buffer is out of bounds on Metal, and the host could not give it the Switch's meaning anyway. A write over 64 pages is kept as an interval (up to 64 a submission, overlapping ones merged; the next submissions see them through `gw_commit`), not by page. A write of no known size still stops the tracking.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md) (GitHub issues #534 and #598, shrines, MacBook Air M2 and M3). The Shrine of Resurrection save shows the load burst: the 300 frames around the world load had 1,200 refusals and 107 load-all store-all passes, now 0 and 47; `DM_NVN_UBP_CHECK=1` found 146,000 shadows equal to the GPU's memory, 0 different. The reporter's log shows the same burst at each load, and 240 refusals a frame in its slow stretches.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_sbw` (16 checks: the size-0 outputs of that dispatch do not hit the uniform block's pool, a write of no known size still stops the tracking, the interval list's limit, and the next submission's view through `gw_live`).

### Inline updates of a running submission refused uniform shadows

- **Symptom:** the `residency set` line shows `passes ended to land them for a read` in the tens of thousands, every one `refused … earlier submission running`. The slower the GPU, the more passes, and each pass makes it slower: a scene stays at a very low frame rate once it falls in.
- **Cause:** a partly covered uniform shadow ([Partial-cover uniform shadows keep passes open](#partial-cover-uniform-shadows-keep-passes-open)) takes the rest from memory, which refused it while a running submission could still write those pages. Two kinds of write counted. The inline updates of the frame before: a game that updates a block every frame refused it for as long as that frame ran, though the CPU knows those bytes. And any write of 16 MB or more, which counted as a write anywhere.
- **Fix:** inline updates of running submissions are remembered by byte, each with its fence, and laid over memory in the shadow (`um_put`, `um_overlay`; `DM_NVN_UBP_RUNNING=0` refuses as before); a slot a running page holds leaves the other page refused as before. A byte counts only while its fence runs, so what the game writes later is not overwritten. Writes of 16 MB or more are kept whole, by range (`gww_put`, 32 of them; beyond that a write anywhere).
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md): its ink query block is updated at 0x00..0x14 and 0x18..0x70 while the shader reads 0x00..0x70, so one unused word leaves it partly covered, before each of about 150 queries a frame; `7866f36373e7` writes a 56 MB buffer each frame. 90,000 refusals every 300 frames became none. `DM_NVN_UBP_CHECK=1` on the first level: 592,000 shadows equal to the GPU's memory, 0 different.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_ubp_running` and `check_t_sbw` (fails with `DM_NVN_UBP_RUNNING=0`).

### Counter reports of a running submission refused uniform shadows by page

- **Symptom:** a scene that holds 60 fps with the GPU at about 5 ms a frame drops for seconds at a time to 35–40 fps, with the GPU at 20–26 ms, after a slow frame such as a load. The `residency set` line shows tens of thousands of refusals per 300 frames, every one `a report still to land` (before this fix they were counted as `earlier submission running`).
- **Cause:** the host writes a counter report (a timestamp, or a samples-passed result) on the CPU when its submission completes (`post_run`): 16 bytes, which the GPU never writes. The report counted as a GPU write of its whole 4 KB page (`sbw_note` → `gw_commit` → `g_gw` / `g_gwx`), so every partly covered uniform shadow ([Partial-cover uniform shadows keep passes open](#partial-cover-uniform-shadows-keep-passes-open)) over that page was refused while the submission ran, and each refusal ended a pass. A game whose reports share a page with its uniform blocks loses nothing while the GPU keeps up. Once one frame falls behind, the extra passes keep the next ones behind too. Samples-passed results did not count at all, so a shadow over one could read it before it landed.
- **Fix:** each report is kept by its own 16 bytes, with its submission's fence, in a ring of 4,096 (`hw_note`, `hw_live`). Its page goes into a table of its own (`g_gwr`), only so that every other page skips the ring. A shadow is refused only when it overlaps a report still to land; the vertex-stream and conditional-render readers (`gw_live`) check the same. A submission's reports that do not fit in the ring's finished slots go into the page tables as before. `DM_NVN_REPORT_EXACT=0` refuses by page as before (still counted as `a report still to land`).
- **Found on:** [Ori and the Will of the Wisps](../../dm/games/01008DD013200000/README.md#frame-rate), GitHub issue #1660 (Unity writes two timestamp reports a frame into the page of its uniform blocks). The issue's reporter found the cause and an exact-range fix on an M3 Max, where one start in three stayed at 15–18 fps. On main (Apple M6, windowed, the new-game route into Swallow's Nest, 2026-11-11), the by-page refusal gave 625,330 refusals in 15,000 frames, all over reports, none over other GPU writes; up to 133,767 per 300 frames, with the frame time at 25–28 ms and the GPU at 25–26 ms for about 15 s. The same route with the fix: no refusals, frame time p50 16.67 ms in every 300 frames, GPU p50 at most 6.0 ms.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_ubp_running` (two of its checks fail with `DM_NVN_REPORT_EXACT=0`: a shadow beside a running report on its page is made, one over its bytes is refused, as one over a samples-passed result is, and reports past the ring refuse their page).

### A submission past 8,192 queued writes counted as writing everywhere

- **Symptom:** one scene of a game drops to 1-2 fps and stays there, with the GPU busy 400-600 ms a frame and about ten times the render passes of the scenes before it, many of one draw each on the same target. The `residency set` line shows `passes ended to land them for a read` in the tens of thousands, nearly all `refused … earlier submission running`, with some `unreadable`. A pass dump (`DM_NVN_PASSDUMP`) names `draw_` → `ubd_conflict` as the end of those passes (`E <pass> pass ends in <function>:<line>`).
- **Cause:** the host lists every buffer write a submission queues (copies, fills, inline uniform updates: `pend_add`), so that a partly covered uniform shadow ([Partial-cover uniform shadows keep passes open](#partial-cover-uniform-shadows-keep-passes-open)) knows what memory does not hold yet. The list held 8,192 writes. Past that the submission counted as a write anywhere (`g_pend_over`), `gw_commit` carried that to later submissions while it ran (`g_gw_all`), and every partly covered shadow was refused, each refusal ending the pass. The extra passes made the GPU slower, so the overflowing submissions were still running at the next frame's draws and the refusals went on: the drop did not recover until the scene ended.
- **Fix:** the list has no fixed size: its entries and page nodes grow as a submission needs them (`pend_room`) and keep that size for the next submissions, so a steady frame allocates nothing, and the update index (`g_pend_upd`) is 32-bit. The hash has 8,192 buckets instead of 2,048. A write still counts as one anywhere only when the list cannot grow (no memory) or when more than 256 writes over more than 8 pages each are queued (`PEND_WIDE`). The fixed limit had been raised once before (256 → 8,192, Monster Hunter Rise); raising it again would only have moved the cliff. The first submission of a run to fill the wide list logs `pend: a submission queued more than 256 writes over 9+ pages each …` once, so a device log shows whether a game ever needs upstream `2acc593b5c0` (PR #2801: large writes indexed by granule, with no count limit; not ported).
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md#rendering), the third stage of the first boss fight (16 GB MacBook Air M2, docked, 2026-11-05). The stage measures its ink map with about 1,550 occlusion queries a frame, each after 7 inline updates of the query block: 11,010 writes in one submission, against about 2,800 in the game's other fights. Before the fix, frames took 400-1,100 ms, with up to 1,025 render passes, 1,008 of them one-draw passes on the 6400x6400 map, and 173,452 refusals (161,812 `earlier submission running`, 11,640 `unreadable`). After it, the stage ran at a median of 17.7-19.9 ms a frame, with at most 118 render passes and no refusals. The Mac's swap and the game's footprint (13-14 GB) did not change across the drop: it looked like memory pressure but was not.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_ubp_running` queues the stage's 10,850 updates in one submission (3 of its checks fail with the old limit), then 43,400 (past 32,768, the next fixed limit the first version of the fix had).

### Every uniform lookup walked a page's whole queue of writes

- **Symptom:** the game stops presenting while it keeps running: a few frames in 30 s, then none, with the GPU idle and the submit thread at full CPU (S2Arm: 133 s of user time on that thread).
- **Cause:** `pend_since` collects the writes queued in a submission over a range by walking the page's hash chain (or, for a range over more than 8 pages, the whole list), and the walk had no bound. S2Arm saw one submission queue about 116,000 inline updates on one page; every uniform block lookup on it (`ubp_get`, at each draw) walked the whole chain and sorted what it found.
- **Fix:** a lookup takes at most `PEND_SINCE_MAX` (8,192) steps, nodes of the chains or entries of the whole-list scan, and past that answers -1, cannot tell, as for an overflowing list (`g_pend_over`): `ubp_get` makes no shadow (the draw's pass ends, counted in `g_ubp_why[4]`) and `htab_written` reads its table again. A shadow taken after each burst of updates looks up only the writes since the one before, so a game that draws after every few updates keeps its shadows however many the page queues; a block first read after more than 8,192 writes on its page, or one whose cached shadow was evicted, is refused. `pend_last`, whose walk also has no bound when nothing matches, is not capped: `vbuf_unaligned` reads its answer as `>= c->npend` and would take a cannot-tell for no write, and `pend_read` would drop an inline update's data. Ported from S2Arm 8cdae876 (Apache-2.0).
- **Found on:** upstream S2Arm, one open-world action game on an iPad (their #640); not seen here. [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md#rendering)' boss stage queues about 11,000 writes in a submission ([A submission past 8,192 queued writes counted as writing everywhere](#a-submission-past-8192-queued-writes-counted-as-writing-everywhere)), most of them on its query block's page: a shadow there taken without a recent one of the same range is refused past the cap. Not measured in the game.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pend_since_cap`: 100 and 8,192 updates on one page are all found and the shadow under the cap holds each slot's newest bytes; 8,193 and 20,000 answer -1 (20,000 within 5 ms) and the shadow is refused; a range over more than 8 pages finds its writes under the cap and answers -1 past it. `check_t_ubp_running` takes a shadow after each of 6,200 queries' updates (43,400 in one submission) and refuses a block first read after them.

### An unbound uniform slot reads the zero bank

- **Symptom:** `stage 0 cb5 has no uniform buffer bound` (or another stage and slot, such as `stage 0 cb0`), once per run.
- **Cause:** the argument buffer kept a null pointer for the unbound slot. The game draws with nothing bound there; the host misses no bind. What the Switch reads from an unbound slot is not known.
- **Fix:** the slot now reads the all-zero bank, as an unbound texture slot reads the zero texture.
- **Found on:** [Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door](../../dm/games/0100ECD018EBE000/README.md) (at the title); [Batman: Arkham Knight](../../dm/games/0100ACD0163D0000/README.md#files-and-shaders) (`stage 0 cb0`, at boot: the first command buffer draws before the game has bound any uniform buffer).
- **Check:** a pass dump of the frame (`DM_NVN_PASSDUMP=<frame>`, frame 0 for the boot) shows the draw (`j` line) and its stage's `K` line with no `B … cb` line, and the frame's `[nvn]` call counts show whether the game called `CommandBufferBindUniformBuffer` at all.

### A uniform slot the game binds empty

- **Symptom:** `[RED] stage 5 cb1 has no uniform buffer bound: shader … address 0 size 0 (frame 0)`, once per run; now `stage 5 cb1 is bound empty by the game (address 0, size 0): shader … reads it as 0`, once, not red.
- **Cause:** none in the host. The game binds the slot itself with address 0 and size 0: Resident Evil 4 binds compute slot 0 and then slot 1 empty before every dispatch of `4e06df4bbdf9b7c9` (a log of each bind's payload at replay, frames 0 to 2), whose cb1 reads it does not use. Every read of a size-0 binding is past its size, which reads 0 on Maxwell as for a binding shorter than the shader's reads (`ub_padded`), so the zero bank the host binds is what the console reads.
- **Fix:** the replay state keeps which uniform slots a bind has set (`S.ubo_set`), and `cbuf_slot` notes an empty binding the game made once, without `[RED]`. A slot never bound ([An unbound uniform slot reads the zero bank](#an-unbound-uniform-slot-reads-the-zero-bank)) and an address in no pool stay red.
- **Found on:** Resident Evil 4 (Switch 2) upstream (`stage 5 cb1`, frame 0); NX 18 has no profile for it.

### Compute dispatches share the copy encoder

- **Symptom:** GPU time per frame doubles with many small dispatches (Age of Calamity: GPU 9 → 18 ms a frame on the Mac; the iPhone's 15 → 51 ms, 17 fps).
- **Cause:** each dispatch had a compute encoder of its own with full queue barriers on both sides, a GPU drain per dispatch: about 10 µs of GPU each on the Mac.
- **Fix:** dispatches now go into the open copy encoder, each after a barrier inside it (as texture copies are, `blit`), with the compute argument table set once per encoder (`dispatch_enc`): about 1.3 µs each.
- **Found on:** [Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity](../../dm/games/01002B00111A2000/README.md): after its first cutscene the first battle runs about 900–1,500 small dispatches a frame on the Mac (an iPhone log's call counts suggest more), each after a game barrier, at the same draws and passes; GPU 7.3 → 8.1 ms across the cutscene with the change.
- **Note:** `DM_NVN_DISPATCH_OWN=1` gives each dispatch its own encoder again.

### Compute bindings prepare texture copies before the dispatch encoder

- **Symptom:** with `DM_NVN_DISPATCH_OWN=1` the host crashes in the Metal driver (`CRASH sig=11 addr=0x98` in `AGX::ComputeContext<…>::prepareForEnqueue(bool)`) at a compute shader's first read through a texture view of another format; by default (dispatches sharing the copy encoder) that read can see the texture's old pixels. Metal validation reports `Command buffer already has an open command encoder` for the first.
- **Cause:** resolving a sampled texture binding can upgrade the texture to allow format views and copy the old pixels into the new one (`tex_upgrade`, through `blit()`). `dispatch` opened its encoder before `encode_args` resolved the bindings. With an encoder of its own, the copy opened a second encoder on the same command buffer. With the shared encoder, the copy came after the dispatch's barrier (`dispatch_enc`), so nothing ordered it before the shader's read.
- **Fix:** `dispatch` fills the compute argument table before it opens an encoder of its own or places the shared encoder's barrier, as `image_prepare` already does for image writes. Compute bindings only write the argument table, so they need no open encoder; any copy a binding issues then lands before the barrier. `dm/gfx/tests/t_dispatch_own.m` checks the pixels and the driver group counts for direct and indirect dispatches, first and cached views, both encoder modes and both encoding threads.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) with `DM_NVN_DISPATCH_OWN=1` ([issue #1743](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1743)), during the Kokiri Forest load: on an M2 Pro 5 of 5 runs crashed before the fix and 3 of 3 loaded the forest after it. The stale read on the default path is reproduced by the test (M2 Max, M6).

### Compute dispatches ordered by the memory they touch

- **Symptom:** none seen on NX 18: a GPU cost. Every game dispatch in the copy encoder had a barrier before it ([Compute dispatches share the copy encoder](#compute-dispatches-share-the-copy-encoder)), so a frame of small independent dispatches (one per particle emitter) paid the barrier's fixed cost each time, about 1.3 µs on the Mac.
- **Cause:** `dispatch_enc` put a barrier before every dispatch and recorded it as writing all memory, while copies in the same encoder already follow their ranges (`blit_buf`, `bw_hit` / `br_hit`).
- **Fix** (`dispatch_ranges`, `dispatch_hits`, `dispatch_enc_r`): when every binding of a game's compute shader can be bounded, the dispatch takes the copies' rule. Its storage buffers count as written (read-only ones too) and its uniform slots (up to `ub_bound`, as the other hazard checks) and the storage of the textures it samples as read (linear textures are made on their pool's buffer). A barrier comes before it only when it reads a range written since the encoder's last barrier, writes one read or written since then, or follows an operation recorded as writing everything (a texture copy, so a binding's own `tex_upgrade` copy still lands first). It is recorded with its ranges, so a later copy or dispatch that reads its output or overwrites its input gets the barrier. Everything else keeps the old rule, a barrier and "everything written": images, global memory, buffer textures, handles read from uniforms or pushed (bindless, `dyn`, pool, `htab`, `SBUFU`, `SBUFQ`, `TEXA`), a storage or uniform binding of size 0, a range in a sparse pool, a texture the replay cannot resolve, any binding kind not listed, more ranges than the tables hold, every indirect dispatch, and the host's own kernels (`rb_swap_gpu`, `gsb_swz`). Draws, the helper compute passes and `DM_NVN_DISPATCH_OWN=1`'s encoders are unchanged: every encoder ends with a full barrier (`m4_end`). Between two dispatches with ranges the game's barriers now decide by default ([Compute dispatches ordered by the game's barriers](#compute-dispatches-ordered-by-the-games-barriers)); `DM_NVN_DISPATCH_RANGE=1` keeps this rule between them too, `=0` restores the old rule (kept in player builds). The first dispatch that skips a barrier under this rule logs `compute dispatches: no barrier between ones whose memory does not overlap`.
- **Not covered:** a storage binding the shader indexes past its bound size (counted as the bound size, as `csh_cs` and the storage write notes do), which Metal would not keep in bounds either.
- **Found on:** the S2Arm project's change (ported from S2Arm 116fe90e); NX 18 has not measured it on a game. Hyrule Warriors: Age of Calamity's battles (about 1,000 dispatches a frame) are the case to try.
- **Check:** `t_dispatch_range` with `DM_NVN_DISPATCH_RANGE=1` and `=0`, on the GPU: a dispatch whose storage buffers miss an earlier one's gets no barrier, one that reads an earlier dispatch's output as a storage or a uniform buffer gets one, one that overwrites what an earlier one read gets one, a copy reading a dispatch's output gets one, an indirect dispatch keeps it and orders the next, and so does a texture copy; with `DM_NVN_DISPATCH_RANGE=0` every dispatch after the first has one. With the uniform ranges left out, the uniform read loses its barrier and reads the wrong words. `t_dispatch_own` (texture upgrade before the dispatch) passes with the rule on.

### Compute dispatches ordered by the game's barriers

- **Symptom:** none seen on NX 18: a GPU and encode cost. Particle systems run many small dispatches a frame in the copy encoder that share the buffers they write, so the range rule ([Compute dispatches ordered by the memory they touch](#compute-dispatches-ordered-by-the-memory-they-touch)) still put a barrier before almost every one, and the GPU ran them one at a time.
- **Cause:** NVN makes a compute dispatch's writes visible to later work only when the game calls `nvnCommandBufferBarrier`; ordering dispatches by overlapping ranges orders them where the game asked for no order. S2Arm measured Mario Kart World's particles, about 230 dispatches a frame with 91 % meeting an earlier one's range: encode 6.6–9.8 → 0.4–1.2 ms, GPU p50 9.0 → 5.3 ms.
- **Fix** (`dispatch_range_mode`, `dispatch_hits`, `dispatch_enc_r`, `blit_buf`, `OP_BARRIER` in `mtl_exec`): a game dispatch with ranges (`dispatch_ranges`, unchanged) gets a barrier only when the game issued a barrier since the dispatch before it (`g_dsp_game_bar`), when it touches a range a copy wrote or read since the last barrier or follows an operation recorded as writing everything, or when the dispatch tables (256 ranges each, a range already there not added again) are full. Its ranges go into tables of their own (`g_dspw` / `g_dspr`), which later copies, updates and fills are checked against (`blit_buf`) and later dispatches are not. Any barrier, and a new copy encoder, empties them; any barrier also settles a pending game barrier. Dispatches without ranges (indirect ones, images, global memory and the rest listed in the range rule's entry) and the host's own kernels keep the old rule on both sides. `DM_NVN_DISPATCH_RANGE=2` (or unset) is this rule, the default; `=1` (or `range`) checks dispatches against each other's ranges as before; `=0` puts a barrier before every dispatch; `=ab` with `DM_AB=<frames>` alternates `1` and `2` (phase 0 is `1`), for the iPhone A/B (kept in player builds). The first dispatch that skips a barrier logs `compute dispatches: no barrier between ones with no game barrier between them`.
- **Not covered:** a game that relies on dispatch order without calling a barrier; NVN does not promise that order either.
- **Found on:** the S2Arm project's change, ported from S2Arm a51b43f7 (Apache-2.0), after NXNative 547c5db6 (MIT); NX 18 has not measured it on a device. To measure: `DM_NVN_DISPATCH_RANGE=ab` with `DM_AB=<frames>` on the iPhone, frame time and battery, on a game with many particle dispatches.
- **Check:** `t_dispatch_range` unset and with `=2`, on the GPU with Metal validation: two dispatches whose storage overlaps get no barrier without a game barrier; after an `OP_BARRIER` replayed through `mtl_exec` the next one gets one and reads the earlier output; a copy reading a dispatch's output gets one, and a dispatch reading the copy's; the same dispatch twice keeps one table entry a range; the dispatch that finds the table full gets one. With the game-barrier line in `dispatch_hits` taken out, the dispatch after the game's barrier loses its barrier and reads the wrong words.

### Fragment-ordering barriers between draws that write images

- **Symptom:** dialog boxes with holes the size of their text, and white specks where text faded.
- **Cause:** each UI element is drawn into its own layers and then composited into the window image by one draw per layer, all in one attachment-less pass, each a read-modify-write of the image, with `nvnCommandBufferBarrier(ORDER_FRAGMENTS | INVALIDATE_TEXTURE)` (bits 0x12) between them. The host took a barrier only as an order for the next pass, and Metal orders nothing between draws of one render pass for shader writes: the text element's composite read the window before the parchment had landed and wrote the old pixels back.
- **Fix:** a barrier with `ORDER_FRAGMENTS` or `ORDER_FRAGMENTS_TILED` now ends the open pass when a draw in it wrote an image or a storage buffer (`swrite_note` in `nvn_mtl.m`), so the next draw opens a pass behind the full consumer barrier; reflection marks an image binding written when its type has write access (`nvn_refl.m`, which left `rw` unset for images).
- **Found on:** [FINAL FANTASY TACTICS - The Ivalice Chronicles](../../dm/games/010038B015560000/README.md).

### A segment committed after a buffer → texture copy

- **Symptom:** a texture copied from a staging buffer shows another texture's blocks (Catherine: Full Body's cutscene subtitle band a hard-edged dark box with a magenta stroke through it).
- **Cause:** the GPU copy read the next texture's blocks: the source was right when recorded and when encoded, but the game's resource thread wrote the next texture into the same staging block before that first submission had been committed, because the host spent a long time building pipelines inside it. On the Switch the GPU runs a submission's copies as soon as the submit returns.
- **Fix:** a segment is committed at the first pass boundary after any buffer → texture copy (`g_cut_copy`, `sub_cut` in `nvn_mtl.m`), so the copies run while the host encodes the rest; the `residency set` line counts these cuts (`N after buffer → texture copies`). This cut always happens, whatever `DM_NVN_CUT` says.
- **Found on:** [Catherine: Full Body](../../dm/games/0100BF00112C0000/README.md): the band is a 32x512 BC3 gradient copied with `nvnCommandBufferCopyBufferToTexture` (recorded at the start of a frame, `main+0x305e64`); the resource thread wrote the next texture into the staging block 64 ms later, and the host spent 300 ms building pipelines inside the submission; a few cuts per 300 frames in this game, only while it loads.
- **Check:** found with `DM_NVN_HILITE` on the band's pixel shader, `DM_NVN_TEXRAW` of the texture (decoded: opaque black and magenta blocks, not a fade) and `DM_PROBE_WATCH` on the staging address beside a log of the copy's submission completing.

### Copies a blit cannot do, drawn

- **Symptom:** a scaled texture copy skipped with a red line (Metal blits cannot scale); format-changing, depth-stencil and mirrored copies.
- **Fix:** such copies (a format change, a scaled region or a mirrored one) are drawn (`copy_t2t_draw`, Depth16Unorm as well since Kirby Air Riders' far shadow mips), one render pass per layer; a destination made without render-target use is upgraded first ([Render-target use added on demand](#render-target-use-added-on-demand)); depth and stencil are written from a fragment shader, stencil through `[[stencil]]` export from an `X32_Stencil8` view of the source.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md): RG11B10F → RGBA16F every frame, half-size depth-stencil copies, and a scaled `MIRROR_Y` copy of the frame to 1280×720 RGBA8. [Super Mario 3D World + Bowser's Fury](../../dm/games/010028600EBDA000/README.md): one scaled copy when leaving the title (1920×1080 → 1280×720, RGB10A2, flags `ENGINE_2D`; probably the captured frame for the transition): 04 listed it as open (skipped with a red line); `copy_t2t_draw` now draws scaled copies too (its comment in `nvn_mtl.m` names this one), so it is no longer skipped.

### More texture copies drawn: depth, integer, past the edge, into a 3D slice

- **Symptom:** `[RED] texture→texture copy case not implemented: …` for copies between depth formats (a scaled Depth16Unorm or Depth32Float copy, Depth32Float_Stencil8 into Depth32Float at another size), between depth and a float colour, between integer textures of another size or format, for a destination region that runs past the destination's edge, or for a 2D source into a slice of a 3D texture; the destination keeps its old content. A scaled depth copy into a packaged depth texture was dropped too: at an internal resolution other than 1x, S2Arm's D-HT4P copied its scaled depth into a full-size packaged depth texture each frame, and its fog read stale depth (a white veil every few frames).
- **Cause:** `copy_t2t_draw` took float, unorm and sRGB pairs, and depth only into the same depth format; it refused a destination region past the texture; `copy_t2t_enc` sent no 3D destination to it, and gave render-target use only to a non-depth, non-integer destination, so a packaged depth destination was refused for lack of it.
- **Fix:** `t2t_kind` in `nvn_mtl.m` picks what the draw reads and writes (`copy.metal`): any depth format into any other (`fsd`; a combined destination has its stencil cleared to 0 when the source has none), depth into a float colour's first channel (`fsdc`) and the first channel back into depth (`fscd`), and unsigned or signed integer texels read as they are and unfiltered (`fsu`, `fsi`, one signedness only). Integer to float or depth has no defined conversion and keeps the red line, as do stencil-only formats and 3D sources. A destination region past the texture is clipped to it (viewport and scissor), the source still mapped by the two whole regions, as the 2D engine writes only within the surface. A 2D source into a 3D destination draws into the slice's `depthPlane`. Every pair the draw takes gets render-target use first ([Render-target use added on demand](#render-target-use-added-on-demand)), depth too. Same-size depth-plane copies between Depth32Float_Stencil8 and Depth32Float keep the exact staging-buffer copy, which leaves the destination's stencil alone.
- **Found on:** ported from S2Arm c5eb1fd7 and 52452def (Apache-2.0), from its players' diagnostics (19 games, 94 reports of such copies); no game here was run with it.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_stencil_view` (`copy_kinds`: Depth16Unorm halved into Depth32Float, Depth32Float_Stencil8 → R32Float → Depth32Float exactly, R32Uint halved, R16Sint → R32Sint, R32Uint → R32Float refused, a region past the edge clipped, a 2D source into slice 2 of a 3D texture, and a scaled Depth32Float copy into a depth texture without render-target use through `copy_t2t_enc`). Without the fix the first seven fail and the 3D slice aborts under Metal validation.

### `Downsample` of a single-sample texture

- **Symptom:** `[RED] Downsample case not implemented: 320x176 1 samples format 70 → 80x48`; a missing bloom.
- **Cause:** the host took `nvnCommandBufferDownsample` only as a multisample resolve and skipped the rest.
- **Fix:** a single-sample source is drawn into the destination as a whole-level scaling copy with bilinear filtering, converting the format (`copy_t2t`, through `copy_t2t_draw`; [Copies a blit cannot do, drawn](#copies-a-blit-cannot-do-drawn)).
- **Found on:** [Bayonetta 2](../../dm/games/01007960049A0000/README.md): its bloom from a 320x176 target into 80x48 and 48x32 textures, and its 1280x720 BGRA8 frame into an RGBA8 texture of the same size.

### Tiled multisample resolves

- **Symptom:** `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferTiledDownsample`; menus render but the stage is black or incomplete.
- **Cause:** the tiled resolve entry points reached the unimplemented-call stub, leaving their destination textures unchanged. Ordinary `Downsample` already recorded a resolve.
- **Fix:** `TiledDownsample` and `TiledDownsampleTextureView` use the same recorded resolve as their ordinary counterparts. Texture descriptors and the selected views are copied when recorded; the resolve runs in submission order. Tegra tiled-cache scheduling hints remain host no-ops. This adds no new format, size-conversion or sample-count support to the existing resolve path.
- **Found on:** Ikaruga 1.0.2, reported by [hada0127 in issue #592](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/592), with a [source-only patch](https://gist.github.com/hada0127/5cf963267ae4e0da47ecf8e150c7bdf6). The report's independent SDK audio-layout failure is separate. No repository game profile or current gameplay validation is provided by this change.

### Depth-stencil copies carry the stencil

- **Symptom:** no ambient occlusion (Mac); on a device the result depends on whatever that memory holds.
- **Cause:** the depth downsample copy left the stencil as the destination's previous content (at the time, new depth-stencil textures were not zero-filled either).
- **Fix:** the copy now carries the stencil.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening](../../dm/games/01006BB00C6F0000/README.md): the occlusion pass (`aa05724ffa62cb82`, a 480×270 R8 target cleared to 1.0) only writes where stencil bit 1 of the quarter-size depth-stencil copy is clear, so on the Mac the mask kept 1.0 everywhere.

### Periodic GPU profiles close at frame boundaries

- **Symptom:** `DM_NVN_GPUPROF=every:1` prints no per-frame reports and fills its timestamp heap across frames.
- **Cause:** reporting waited for a submission in an unselected frame. Every frame is selected at interval 1, so that condition never holds.
- **Fix:** before the next frame's first encoder, `gp_periodic_finish` snapshots the sampled frame's labels and registers a shared-event notification at its last committed fence. The report resolves the heap after all of that frame's segments complete, without a GPU wait or an extra command buffer. The heap cannot be reused while the report is pending.
- **Found on:** review of the measurement tools for [The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md); reproduced with synthetic counters and completion events, without game execution. `t_gpuprof` checks consecutive and sparse frame selection, completion ordering and heap ownership; it runs in CPU CI.

### GPU profiles stamp only the encoder they began

- **Symptom:** with `DM_NVN_GPUPROF`, the host aborts minutes in on `-[AGXG16XFamilyComputeContext_mtlnext writeTimestampWithGranularity:afterStage:intoHeap:atIndex:]: unrecognized selector`, `up_flush` on the stack; or a profiled render pass with a wrong GPU time.
- **Cause:** every encoder end goes through `m4_end`, which called `gp_end` for any encoder, including the upload, diagnostics and present encoders, which are not profiled. `gp_end` closed whichever main-command-buffer encoder `gp_begin` had last opened and sent its end stamp to the encoder it was given. An upload flush inside a profiled draw pass sent the render encoder's timestamp selector to the upload compute encoder (the abort); where the kinds matched, the pass lost its own end stamp.
- **Fix:** `gp_begin` records the encoder it stamped, and `gp_end` ignores any other encoder, so the open pass keeps its label until its own end.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md) with `DM_NVN_GPUPROF=every:300` on the Mac (issue #531; one run in four, about frame 23,000). `t_gpuprof` ends an upload encoder inside a profiled pass; it runs in CPU CI. Not rerun in the game.

### An argument block reused after another shader of its stage encoded

- **Symptom:** none seen in a game. A draw could bind a texture, sampler, storage or uniform buffer from before a binding change: the draw's shader had written its block earlier in the pass, another shader wrote a block on the same stage after the change, and the first shader drew again. Upstream [PR #2249](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2249) found the mechanism by reading.
- **Cause:** a stage's binding changes were flags (`g_args_dirty`, `g_args_ubo`) that whichever shader wrote the stage's next block cleared, so they told the next shader on that stage nothing about its own block. Every path that puts another shader on a stage marks the stages first, and a program switch marks them all, so the one route found is a combined program (`bind_stages`, stages bound from different programs): when the bound combination was the next of the `NCMP` ring slots to be replaced, a new combination took that slot and came back as the same pointer, and `OP_BIND_PROGRAM` (`np != S.prog`) saw no change. It needs more than 64 combinations in use; [AI: THE SOMNIUM FILES](../../dm/games/010089B00D09C000/README.md) made more than that in the precompile prediction.
- **Fix:** in `nvn_mtl.m`, for every NVN game. A block is valid by generation: each binding change takes the next number of one sequence for every stage (`g_args_gen` for any binding, `g_args_ugen` for what the uniform slots point at only), each block keeps the numbers it was written at (`ab_gen`, `ab_ugen`), and it is reused only while both hold, patched when only the uniform one moved. Shaders that take turns on a stage with no binding change keep their blocks. `bind_stages` never gives a new combination the slot `S.prog` names. Ported from upstream `f31276ada`; the patch path keeps NX 18's block address read before the slots are written (`ag`: without it the NieR:Automata GPU hang returns) and `ubd_dirty` keeps the readable size (`ub_bound`).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_args_gen` (under API validation): the sequence (a binding change, another shader's encode, the first shader again), shaders taking turns, a uniform-only change, and the ring slot; without the `bind_stages` change its slot checks fail. `t_geometry_hazards` and `t_arena_trim` read the generations.
- **Found on:** reading; no game failure reported.

## Conditional rendering and queries

### Conditional rendering evaluated at replay

- **Symptom:** the 3D scene black (targets cleared again before they are read); `[RED] SetRenderEnableConditional: the host draws anyway`; with a game that skips clears under render conditions, scenes black behind the HUD (Mario vs. Donkey Kong's levels, 1.0.1).
- **Cause:** by default the host draws everything a `SetRenderEnableConditional` would skip. A game that wraps clears in `SetRenderEnableConditional(RENDER_IF_NOT_EQUAL, rec)` … `SetRenderEnable(true)`: the condition compares the 64-bit counters at `rec` and `rec + 16`. A timestamp report puts 0 in the counter field (the time goes in the second field). The host ignored the condition and wrote the time into both fields, so every clear ran.
- **Fix:** profile key `render_cond`: `OP_RENDER_COND` is evaluated at replay from the records in memory. A timestamp report replayed earlier in the same submission counts as 0. A condition on a samples-passed result (an occlusion query, written when its submission completes) keeps drawing while that report is in flight, and once it has landed is evaluated from it, as the GPU would at that point in the queue ([Occlusion conditions read once landed](#occlusion-conditions-read-once-landed)). A pass dump shows the switches as `E` lines (`conditional render … → not drawn`) and a skipped clear as `c … skipped by conditional rendering`.
- **Found on:**
  - [The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom](../../dm/games/0100F2C0115B6000/README.md): TOTK wraps nearly every `ClearColor` / `ClearDepthStencil` this way; each clear is followed by `ReportCounter(TIMESTAMP_TOP, rec)`, the CPU sets `rec` to 1 when a target needs a clear, and `rec + 16` is never written, so after one clear the two are equal and later conditional clears of that target are skipped. The first tile-lighting round, the half-resolution scene depth the light volumes test against (back faces `GREATER` against a buffer just cleared to 1.0 can never pass) and the light accumulation were all cleared before the composite read them.
  - [Mario vs. Donkey Kong](../../dm/games/0100B99019412000/README.md) (it skips clears under render conditions, as Tears of the Kingdom and Animal Crossing: New Horizons do).
  - Drawn unconditionally with no `render_cond`, nothing looked wrong in [Big Brain Academy: Brain vs. Brain](../../dm/games/0100620012D6E000/README.md) (no screen played looked wrong) or [DARK SOULS: REMASTERED](../../dm/games/01004AB00A260000/README.md) (nothing seen).
  - [Portal 2](../../dm/games/0100ABD01785C000/README.md) needs no `render_cond` and must not get it: NVIDIA's occlusion culling in its D3D9 layer (`IDirect3DDevice9::SetOcclusionShape`, `materialsystem.nro+0xc1130`) puts a mesh under `SetRenderEnableConditional(RENDER_IF_NOT_EQUAL, pair)` when its shape has a query, else `SetRenderEnable(true)`, so drawing everything only costs GPU time. With the key on, those conditions read 0 / 0 at replay (no report in the submission writes the pairs; memory later held `0xffff` / 0) and visible furniture vanished. **Open:** who writes those pairs and when.

### Occlusion conditions read once landed

- **Symptom:** with `render_cond`, GPU time spent on objects the game's occlusion queries culled: a pass dump shows `conditional render … (unknown, drawn)` on query records whose submission completed long before.
- **Cause:** every address a samples-passed report ever wrote counted as unknown at replay, landed or not (`rc_occ_has`, later the report's `conservative` mark), so every draw under an occlusion condition was made. The report table already knows when a report has landed: its submission's fence against `g_rc_complete`, with later buffer writes and GPU writes tracked.
- **Fix:** only counter kinds the host does not implement stay conservative (`rc_note`); a landed samples-passed result is read like a landed timestamp one (`rc_value`), and a report still in flight keeps the block drawing.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): it reports its queries a submission ahead of the conditional draws, so all of them had landed at replay (1.48 million conditions on the Kokiri Forest route, 0 in flight, 42% false). GPU p50 17.06 → 16.44 ms and p95 18.74 → 18.01 ms a frame on a 20-core M4 Pro (two runs each), picture unchanged. Skipping every unknown block instead (the visible ones too) whitened the scene for good: only the evaluated condition is a valid skip.

### Report tables that dropped entries

- **Symptom:** with `render_cond`, a 3D scene black or missing behind the HUD, only in scenes with many occlusion queries; a pass dump shows conditions reading `values 1 / 0` on a record a report of the same submission has just written, and clear-only passes on targets just drawn to. Scenes with fewer queries are fine.
- **Cause:** the reports of a submission were kept in a table of 256, queries and timestamps alike. Once full, a timestamp report was forgotten, its condition read the 1 the CPU wrote, and the clear ran ([Conditional rendering evaluated at replay](#conditional-rendering-evaluated-at-replay)).
- **Fix:** only timestamp reports are kept per submission, and the query addresses, which are kept apart, are enough for those; both tables grow (`rc_note`, `rc_occ_add`).
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md): 285 ink queries a frame in its dialogue scene came before the conditional clears, and the lit scene was wiped: a black screen with only the text on it. The opening cutscene has no such queries, and the pier about 160.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_sbw`.

### Query slots running out inside a pass

- **Symptom:** `[RED] visibility slots exhausted`.
- **Cause:** the visibility buffer holds 8,192 slots and is bound for a whole pass. A new one came only when a pass began, so a single pass with more queries than were left counted nothing for the rest.
- **Fix:** the draw that needs a slot when none is left ends the pass, and the next begins with a new buffer.
- **Found on:** [Splatoon Raiders](../../dm/games/0400380020056000/README.md): at the first level's load, one submission with about 9,000 draws.

### Samples-passed reports with no reset before them

- **Symptom:** characters a few metres away drawn as their hats and props only, their bodies missing from the main view while their shadows are there; near characters draw fully. A log line or a probe shows every samples-passed report reading 0.
- **Cause:** the host started counting samples passed only at `nvnCommandBufferResetCounter(SAMPLES_PASSED)`, and a report before any reset wrote 0. On the GPU the counter runs from the queue's creation, so a game that never resets it reads end − begin of two reports around each query, and gets the samples between them. Red Dead Redemption never resets it: every query read 0, every character counted as hidden.
- **Fix:** in `nvn_mtl.m` (Occlusion queries), for every game, one model of the counter: a running total that `post_run` keeps in submission order under `g_post_mu`. A report hands the visibility slots since the previous report to its submission's completion, which adds them to the total and writes it; the slots left at a submission's end are added at its completion (so each slot is read once, while its buffer still belongs to that submission); a reset drops the slots before it and zeroes the total at that point in the queue. Counting starts at the first reset or, with none before it, at the first report (the pass is split there if its encoder had no visibility buffer); the log says `occlusion queries: samples passed reported with no ResetCounter before it` once. Games that reset read the same values as before; each report used to sum every slot since the reset, reading slots of earlier submissions whose visibility buffer the ring could already have handed to a later one: in `t_render_enable_queue`, a reset and a draw in one submission and a half-sized draw and the report in the next read 64 samples instead of 96, the second draw's count twice. The test also checks end − begin across submissions with no reset.
- **Check:** on the change's first version (a separate mode for games that never reset; the same values for them), Red Dead Redemption 1.0.4 (Apple M4, macOS 27.0.1), a recorded replay out of the saloon in Armadillo: the distant characters have their bodies at frames 2,280–2,340, frame time unchanged at 33.3 ms, no `[RED]`; played by hand, the same.
- **Found on:** Red Dead Redemption (`01007820196A6000`): `rage::CharacterVisQueries::TestVisiblity` takes each character's result from `GetOcclusionQueryData`, end − begin of two reports around its box (about 35 queries and 324 reports a frame, no reset), and puts a character at or below its threshold in the occluded list, which `renDrawBucketContainer::AddToDrawList` uses to drop its low-detail body.

## Graphics APIs: NVN only

NX 18 is Metal 4 only and renders NVN games only. The Vulkan layer (`dm/gfx/vk/hle_vk.m`: Hades, Super Mario Galaxy and Super Mario Galaxy 2, Super Mario 64 and Super Mario Sunshine in Super Mario 3D All-Stars) and the OpenGL layer (`dm/gfx/gl/hle_gl.m`, NVIDIA's desktop OpenGL through EGL: Balatro, Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition, Well Dweller) drew on classic Metal queues of their own, and were removed in 0.5 with their shader translators (`dm/gfx/tools/vk_shaders.py`, `gl_shaders.py`), their run-time shader captures and their tests; a later release ports them to Metal 4. Their code, and the notes on them this section held, are in git at `93c34371` (`git show 93c34371:docs/reference/rendering.md`, sections "Vulkan layer" and "OpenGL layer").

- **The NSP script** still tells the graphics APIs apart (`profile_guess.py` `shader_sources`, below) and stops right after the extraction for a game it finds to render with Vulkan or OpenGL: `[RED] <name> renders with Vulkan; NX 18 supports NVN games only` (or `OpenGL`). `build_ipa.sh` stops the same way for `GFXAPI` other than `nvn`.
- **The host** binds an NVN game's EGL, GL and Vulkan imports to named stubs, as any import it does not implement, so a game that only imports them (Absolum: SDL3's EGL and Vulkan support; Balatro's LÖVE links `vkGetInstanceProcAddr`) runs as before. Only a call to one stops the game, with `[RED] this game renders with OpenGL/Vulkan, which NX 18 does not support (Metal 4 only; the layer was removed in 0.5): it called <function>` (`dm/host/hle.c` `unimpl_c`; `make -C dm/host/tests GAME=<id> symbols` tests it).

### Telling a GL game from an NVN or Vulkan one

- **Symptom:** (GL layer, removed in 0.5; history) the NSP script's provisional profile said Vulkan, and the capture boot crashed in the GL layer.
- **Cause:** `main` imports `vkGetInstanceProcAddr` for SDL's Vulkan loader, which nothing uses; the game draws with EGL and GL.
- **Fix:** `profile_guess.py` decides `graphics gl` when `main` imports `eglCreateWindowSurface` and names `glShaderSource`, and now leaves `vkGetInstanceProcAddr` out of its Vulkan count.
- **Found on:** [Digimon Story Cyber Sleuth: Complete Edition](../../dm/games/010014E00DB56000/README.md): `main` imports `nvnBootstrapLoader` and 13 EGL functions. Its graphics setup (`main+0x5ed8f0`) makes an NVN device first, which only a helper beside the movie player uses (`main+0x528000`–`+0x52c400`: a memory pool and four textures next to the movie's GL textures). Then `eglGetDisplay`, `eglBindAPI(EGL_OPENGL_API)`, a 4.5 core context, a window surface on `vi::CreateLayer(w, h)` and a glad loader (`main+0x52c460`). Of the 22 games extracted here (at the time), only Digimon matches the rule. [Balatro](../../dm/games/0100CD801CE5E000/README.md) (SDK 18.3.2, LÖVE 11.4 on OpenGL with SDL and LuaJIT; one boot): `main` imports the EGL functions and `vkGetInstanceProcAddr`, SDL's Vulkan loader, which nothing calls (`subsdk1` is NVIDIA's Vulkan driver); Hades and both Galaxies import 99 or 100 Vulkan functions, `vkCreateInstance` among them.
- **Note:** [Absolum](../../dm/games/0100A83022A22000/README.md) imports the same EGL functions (SDL3) but renders with NVN and names no GL function.

### NVN in NROs under an SDL main that names GL

- **Symptom:** the NSP script took the game for an OpenGL one (`main imports EGL and names glShaderSource`), the GL shader capture found no shader, and the capture boot stopped with `[nvn-mtl] FATAL cannot read the shader map` from `h_nvnQueueSubmitCommands` called in an NRO (`nro8+0xc10d0`).
- **Cause:** Portal's `main` is SDL with its EGL video backend; it imports 20 EGL functions and names `glShaderSource` twice (SDL's GL renderer), but imports no NVN and draws nothing. The renderer is in the Source engine NROs: `materialsystem.nro` (with the D3D9 layer linked in) and `video_nx.nro` import `nvnBootstrapLoader` and draw with NVN.
- **Fix:** `profile_guess.py` `shader_sources` now reads the imports of the loose NROs in the RomFS (`romfs_nro_imports`): when one imports `nvnBootstrapLoader`, the game is an NVN game whatever `main` imports. Digimon keeps the GL rule: it has no NRO. The survey, which has only a RomFS manifest, called Portal 2 an OpenGL game and left GLSLC out of its modules until `nx_extract --manifest` also wrote the RomFS's NROs (`romfs_nro/`, 60 MB for Portal 2's 21) for it to read the same way.
- **Found on:** [Portal](../../dm/games/01007BB017812000/README.md); [Portal 2](../../dm/games/0100ABD01785C000/README.md) (the survey).

### NVN and its own GLSLC under a main that also links EGL

- **Symptom:** the NSP script took the game for an OpenGL one (`main imports EGL and names glShaderSource`) and translated 2 GL shaders; the game boots with sound and answers input, but the window stays black, and the log has `[nvn-mtl] [RED] shader … is not in the shader map` for every shader it makes, with no EGL call at all.
- **Cause:** the engine links both paths. `main` imports 14 EGL functions and names `glShaderSource`, but also imports `nvnBootstrapLoader` and 8 GLSLC functions (`glslcCompile` among them, defined in `subsdk2`), holds 4 GLSL sources, and keeps the rest in its RomFS `.shader` files (RIFF `SHDR`, a `gl43` chunk with `#version 450` GLSL). On the Mac it draws with NVN and compiles every shader at run time with GLSLC; taken for OpenGL, it got no GLSLC capture.
- **Fix:** `profile_guess.py` `shader_sources`: a `main` that imports `nvnBootstrapLoader` and `glslcCompile`, holds GLSL sources, and loads a module that defines `glslcCompile` is an NVN game whatever else it links, and gets the GLSLC capture as Three Houses does. Digimon keeps the GL rule (no GLSLC compile of its own); of 16 games extracted here (Super Mario Odyssey, Super Smash Bros. Ultimate, Metroid Dread, Hollow Knight: Silksong, Cuphead, DRAGON BALL FighterZ and others), only Rayman's decision changes. `test_nro_graphics.py` has both cases.
- **Found on:** Rayman 30th Anniversary Edition (`01007C6025688000`, 1.0.0, SDK 20.5.15, Digital Eclipse): the capture gets 21 shaders in 30 s, the split makes 42, and it plays with picture and sound at 60 fps.

## Dual-source blending

### An unwritten secondary blend source

- **Symptom:** Metal refuses a pipeline with "Fragment shader does not write to render target color(0), index(1) that is required for blending"; the Metal 4 specialization path can instead abort after `MTLCompilerService` crashes in `AGCLLVMStatelessFragmentObject::buildBlendFactor`.
- **Cause:** NVN blend state reads source 1, but the fragment shader's output map writes only colour 0. Passing that state directly to Metal asks its blend compiler to read an absent secondary output.
- **Fix:** for a known output map without colour 1, `pso_desc` chooses zero for the unwritten secondary source, consistently with the translator's dual-source output initialization (`Translator.EmitOutputsInitialization`). Source-1 colour / alpha factors become ZERO, and their complements become ONE. The shader, blend equation, blending enable and write mask remain intact. A written secondary source, an unknown output map or a separately specialized fragment is left alone. `t_missing_src1` compares both Metal pipeline paths against a shader explicitly writing a zero secondary source.
- **Open:** the original GPU's value for an unwritten secondary source has not been measured; zero is the host's defined choice for that missing value.
- **Found on:** [Mario + Rabbids Kingdom Battle](../../dm/games/010067300059A000/README.md).

### A written secondary blend source (NVN dual-source blending)

- **Symptom:** the host aborts on Metal's compiler queue (`findOrCreateComputeFastClearPipeline`, `[AGX] Failed assertion "error == MTLCompilerErrorNoError"`) seconds into the game, with or without the pipeline prebuild, while `MTLCompilerService` crash reports show `EXC_BAD_ACCESS` in `llvm::ExtractElementInst` under `AGCLLVMStatelessFragmentObject::buildBlendFactor` (`AGCLLVMStatelessPSODynamicLibrary::compile`).
- **Cause:** target 0 blends with a `SRC1` factor and the pixel shader writes outputs 0 and 1. NVN, as Maxwell, takes output 1 as target 0's second source; the translator declares it `[[color(1)]]`, and Metal reads a second source only from `[[color(0), index(1)]]`. With no such output, Metal 4's blend compiler crashes building the pipeline's colour state ([An unwritten secondary blend source](#an-unwritten-secondary-blend-source) left a written output 1 alone).
- **Fix:** `dmsh_entry.py` `fs_dual` gives a pixel shader listed in the profile's `dual_want.tsv` that writes exactly colours 0 and 1 a second entry point, `main0_d`, the same body with output 1 as `[[color(0), index(1)]]` (`entry_d` in its reflection). `pso_desc` takes it for a pipeline whose output-0 target blends with a `SRC1` factor (`pso_reads_src1`); output 1's attachment, if the pass has one, is then left unwritten, as on Maxwell. A shader without the entry point blends with zero for the second source, as an unwritten one, with a red line naming it, and `DM_NVN_DUAL_WANT=<file>` appends every such key to a file for the profile's list. Only listed shaders get the entry point: 5,474 of Arceus's 6,156 pixel shaders write exactly colours 0 and 1, nearly all for a second render target. `t_missing_src1` checks both Metal pipeline paths with the entry point.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Legends: Arceus](../../dm/games/01001F5010DFA000/README.md): `f4f36a03b0c6691d` (`system_resource/gfx2/shader/env/system/ha_post_effects.bnsh`, variation 4: RGBA16Float, destination factor `SRC1_ALPHA`) and `ff45da02e225f961`, in the first minute.

### A shader added to `dual_want.tsv` keeps its cached build

- **Symptom:** adding a pixel shader to the profile's `dual_want.tsv` leaves the build reporting cache hits, the reflection without `entry_d`, and the host logging `[RED] shader ... has no dual-source entry point`.
- **Cause:** `dmsh_build.py` selects the extra `main0_d` entry point through `DUAL_WANT`, but the per-shader cache key did not include membership in that set. The packing script noticed the table change and reran the builder, which restored the old variant.
- **Fix:** `shader_key` includes the shader's membership in `DUAL_WANT`. Adding or removing a key invalidates that shader's cached variant; unrelated entries leave its key unchanged. The existing paired-pixel dependency also updates paired vertex keys. No cache deletion is required.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Skyward Sword HD, issue #928](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/928), `01002DA013484000`: Link's eye shader and three other dual-source shaders. The fog reported there had another cause: [Logic operations through a library of its own](#logic-operations-through-a-library-of-its-own).

## Attachment stores and the picture's layout

### Image stores into an attachment of their own pass

- **Symptom:** an image-only fragment draw's writes disappear from the subresource also bound as its color attachment when the pass ends; other image layers retain their writes.
- **Cause:** a writable image or storage alias marked every attachment written, which retained the tile store. For a loaded color attachment with no raster output or clear, that store writes the old tile contents over the shader's image writes to the same texture. Disabling the unwritten-store optimization did not avoid this correctness fault.
- **Fix (resolved in synthetic GPU tests; game validation open):** track possible shader writes separately from color-tile writes. A loaded color attachment with possible image/storage writes and no tile writes uses `DontCare` at pass end, independently of the optimization switches. Record physical color writes before resetting a merged pipeline's logical-output map; specialized fragments have conservative unknown outputs. Clear load actions and explicit clears executed in the current pass count as tile writes. A clear already landed in a standalone pass does not: marking it as a tile write retained a stale store over the following image-only draw (found while landing #808). Depth/stencil and non-loaded attachments retain their existing decisions. Mixed raster/clear and image writes to the same subresource are outside this proof and still retain the tile store.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_image_attachment_store` executes literal shaders through production draw replay and reads all four image layers on the GPU, with the unwritten-store optimization on and off. It also checks raster outputs, an earlier raster draw followed by an image-only draw, pending and explicit clears, a standalone clear before an image-only draw, unknown output reflection, a zero channel mask, a native `DontCare`-load control, and physical/logical output mapping. The standalone-clear case failed four checks before tracking the actual clear load action; all 120 checks pass with it. `t_unwritten_store` additionally preserves depth writes with a possible image alias.
- **Found on:** [DARK SOULS: REMASTERED](../../dm/games/01004AB00A260000/README.md#rendering), reported and diagnosed by Jacob-Tate in [#187](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/187#issuecomment-6519569185), with the original store fix in [34c7453](https://github.com/Jacob-Tate/nxnative/commit/34c7453e081203627752edd877dde40452afe7b2). Independently reproduced with synthetic shaders on an Apple M2 Max, macOS 26.6; no local game or iOS validation.

### Unwritten attachment stores without lookahead

- **Symptom:** read-only depth/stencil attachments are written back after every pass, and the periodic unwritten-store savings counter remains zero with the default configuration.
- **Cause:** `udc_note_draw` and `sd_finish` returned early when `DM_NVN_TBDR` was off (the TBDR mode has since been removed). This also disabled the local unwritten-attachment proof, although it does not depend on future sampled-texture readers. The broader lookahead remains disabled because it does not understand bindless readers.
- **Fix:** collect per-pass write facts independently of the lookahead switch and omit only stores of attachments that were loaded and provably unchanged. Clears, depth writes, stencil writes and possible writable image/atomic/storage aliases conservatively retain stores, except for the mandatory [image-only color-attachment rule](#image-stores-into-an-attachment-of-their-own-pass). Lookahead, pass merging and cross-submission invalidation remain disabled. `DM_NVN_UNWRITTEN_DC=0` disables optional unwritten-store savings; the image-write preservation rule stays active.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_unwritten_store` performs real GPU readback against a store-all control and literal color/depth/stencil values, with lookahead disabled.
