It covers read-only depth/stencil, depth writes, stencil writes, pending clears, and writable images (including native image atomics) and both storage-buffer binding forms. `t_unwritten_store --bench` runs a synthetic 32-pass 1080p workload with balanced on/off order; it is not a game FPS measurement.
- **Found on:** [NieR:Automata US 1.0.3](../../dm/games/010056B015FE8000/README.md#factory-combat-capture-and-9s-texture-follow-up), during iPhone factory-combat performance investigation. The change passes the GPU proof and rendering regressions on Mac; iPhone gameplay benefit remains to be measured.

### Masked color targets store back during depth-only draws

- **Symptom:** a depth prepass keeps a color target loaded with its channel mask set to zero. The attachment store ledger in `DM_NVN_GPUPROF` still includes the color target.
- **Cause:** `udc_note_draw` counted a shader output or an enabled channel mask as a write. A raster write needs both.
- **Fix:** use the draw's physical attachment write mask for the existing [unwritten-store rule](#unwritten-attachment-stores-without-lookahead). `draw_color_writes` maps each physical attachment to its logical shader slot before the draw clears the pipeline's mapping. Unknown or specialized shader outputs are treated conservatively. `DM_NVN_UNWRITTEN_DC=0` provides the store-all control.
- **Check:** `check_t_unwritten_store` reads back color, depth and stencil from production draw replay. It checks store bytes and pixels for a masked output, a shader with no color output and a remapped color export.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game).

### iOS layouts: portrait, a half-folded Duo, iPad windows

- **Symptom:** portrait gameplay overlaps the touch controls, touch coordinates land outside the game picture, or a TV's picture renders at 4K again after a layout pass.
- **Cause:** the front end assumed a landscape screen with the controls over the whole view, so its picture and touch geometry did not follow a portrait screen (an iPad, an iPhone Duo's inner display) or a Duo's fold. On iPadOS the deprecated `UIRequiresFullScreen` kept the game out of resizable windows, and a native rotation A/B on the iPhone Duo iOS 27.1 simulator showed that it also blocked upside-down portrait.
- **Fix:** `dm/ios/Sources/DmLayout.h` computes the picture and controls rectangles from the view's bounds, its safe area and UIKit's division region (`UIHingeInteraction`, iOS 27.1); front_ios.m lays the views out from them and DmGamepad.m its controls. Landscape keeps the overlay unless the hinge is half open over a horizontal division; portrait puts a fitted 16:9 picture above the controls, at the division or the midpoint. On a Duo in portrait only the top safe-area inset applies. Game touches map through the picture rectangle (with [Fill](../settings.md#picture), larger than the view), and a layout change releases held inputs. The phone's orientation list stays landscape (the Duo's inner display follows its bounds), the iPad's has all four and no `UIRequiresFullScreen`. While a TV shows the picture the phone is all controls (`dm_layout_pad_only`). The drawable's scale is set in one place, `DmGameView layoutSubviews`: the screen's pixels, at most `maxPixelWidth` across, which the TV sets to 1920, so a later layout pass cannot undo the TV's 1080p cap.
- **Check:** the [Duo harness](../../dm/ios/tests/duo/README.md) tests layout, hit targets, touches, the in-game menu's live settings and the drawable cap in the Simulator with synthetic host state. A physical Duo was tested by the PR #683 author on their fork; full-game tests on a physical iPad and a Duo with this repository's build are open.
- **Found on:** [Pokémon Brilliant Diamond](../../dm/games/0100000011D90000/README.md).

### The picture laid out in the shape the game presents

- **Symptom:** with *Picture shape* at 1.43:1 (`DM_SCREEN_ASPECT`) the game renders 1920x1344, but on an iPhone Duo in Fit (the default) the picture comes out smaller than the 16:9 one, with black bars on all four sides; it fills the screen only in Fill. On the Mac a click lands off the point under the pointer (it was mapped on a 16:9 rectangle of the window).
- **Cause:** the front ends assumed a 16:9 picture. iOS sized the game's view to the largest 16:9 rectangle (`DmLayout.h` `dm_aspect_fit`, `dm_aspect_fill`) and the present then fitted the 1.43:1 frame inside that 16:9 drawable (`present_viewport`), so the picture was fitted twice; the Mac's mouse-to-touch mapping (`front_mac.m`) used the same 16:9 rectangle.
- **Fix:** the present reports the shape of the frame it draws ([the layer's](#a-crop-only-picks-texels-the-picture-has-the-layers-shape)) whenever its width / height changes (`nvn_mtl.m` `present_now` → `front.h` `front_picture_shape`; every graphics API presents there). The iOS front end lays the game's view out in that shape (`DmLayout.h` takes it as a parameter: the overlay, the split, Fill and the TV alike), so a 1.43:1 picture fills a 1.43:1 screen in Fit and touches are normalized on the picture as drawn; the Mac maps the mouse on the picture's own rectangle. A 16:9 game reports 16:9 and keeps its layout, and a size change of the same shape (1280x720 handheld, 1920x1080 docked) is not reported.
- **Check:** the [Duo harness](../../dm/ios/tests/duo/README.md) tests the taller picture in Fit, in Fill and on a wider screen, and the production root view following a reported shape and returning to 16:9. Not yet run on a device.
- **Found on:** [Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#screen-shape-dm_screen_aspect-current) and [Animal Crossing: New Horizons](../../dm/games/01006F8002326000/README.md#screen-shape-dm_screen_aspect-current) with `DM_SCREEN_ASPECT=1.43`.

### The window textures' passes limited to the presented crop

- **Symptom:** on the iPhone 18 Pro Max, Donkey Kong Bananza's per-pass profile (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF=every:600`) showed a 2880x1620 pass with no draws, clearing and storing the window texture and its Depth32Float_Stencil8 depth-stencil, at 0.97 ms a frame at the phone's lower GPU clock and 40 MB of stores, and a one-draw composite at 2880x1620 storing 17.8 MB, while every present showed a 1920x1080 crop of that texture (44% of it). Fortune's Weave, Star Fox, Mario Kart World and Ocarina of Time make window textures larger than the crop they present too.
- **Cause:** NVN clears and stores the whole target; the host opened those passes at the texture's size, so every tile outside the crop, which never reaches the screen, was cleared and written to memory each frame.
- **Fix:** general, in the host (`nvn_win_area.h`; `nvn_mtl.m` `present_enqueue`, `rp_begin_ex_`). Each present records its window texture's largest crop (its far edge, x + width by y + height). Once that largest crop has held for 60 presents of the texture and covers at most 90% of it, a pass whose colour attachments are all such window textures with the same area (level 0, slice 0; a depth-stencil attachment no smaller than the area) opens with `renderTargetWidth` / `Height` at the area: tiles outside it are not cleared, drawn or stored, and draws' and clears' scissors are clamped to it. A crop that grows past the area turns it off at once until the new largest has held again, so only frames already in flight can be clipped at the edge. A smaller crop that has held `WA_SHRINK` (600) presents becomes the largest (added 2026-11-18: Bananza's composite window texture got a full-size present at boot and was never limited after it); a dynamic resolution's moving crop does not hold that long. Not for passes that must cover the whole target (`rp_begin`), layered passes, or passes already limited to their scissor (`DM_NVN_RP_AREA`). The bandwidth ledger still counts the whole attachments. `DM_NVN_WIN_AREA=0` turns it off; one `window textures: passes on a 2880x1620 window texture limited to the presented crop's 1920x1080` line when it starts; `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` adds a `W` row per limited pass.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> t_win_area` (the hold, the growth, the 90% rule, the pass rule, slot reuse); `check_t_present_crop` (the real QueuePresent sets the area for a held crop and drops it for a larger one, with and without the present snapshot).
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md) (NX18-X2.0 gpu-clock-collapse.md). Not yet run on a device.

### A pass drawn every other frame

- **Symptom:** on the iPhone 18 Pro Max, Donkey Kong Bananza draws a 128x128 view (RG11B10Float colour + Depth32Float) with 700 to 1,700 draws in some areas, 1.35 ms a frame at the phone's lower GPU clock, about a tenth of the frame (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF`; NX18-X2.0 gpu-clock-collapse.md).
- **Fix:** a picture trade, one game, by the owner's preset plan (Balanced and Battery Saver take it, Quality does not): the profile key `pass_alternate <W>x<H>:<colour>/<depth>` (`nvn_pass_alt.h`, `nvn_mtl.m` `rp_begin_ex_` and the draw path). A pass of exactly that size and those formats is drawn on one frame and kept the next: its clears become loads (a clear landing in a pass of its own is that pass too), and its draws are dropped except those that count an occlusion query or may write memory (storage buffers, images or global memory bound for writing, bindings the reflection cannot list, transform feedback; a binding the shader only reads does not count: the first version counted Bananza's read-only instance buffers and dropped none of the view's draws), so queries the game reads still run. It is kept only when the same texture was drawn the frame before; a view that comes and goes, or a new texture, draws at once, and a pass reopened within a frame keeps that frame's decision. `DM_NVN_PASS_ALT=0` turns it off (the Quality preset sets it); one `pass drawn every other frame: 128x128 ...` line at the start, then a count of kept and drawn passes and dropped draws; `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` adds an `A` row per kept pass.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=040074A01BF12000 check_t_pass_alt` (draw, keep, a reopened pass, a new texture, a gap in frames; the per-target table: a cube's levels and a second cube alternating in step, a face updated once in six frames always drawn, a cube spread over three frames drawn on every other visit, two cube textures on alternate frames each on every other visit, a replaced target drawn when it comes back; the shapes matched and refused); `t_nvn` compiles the draw path with the key.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md), where the view turned out to be the game's visibility test: its every draw counts an occlusion query, so no draw is dropped, and a kept frame's loaded depth tests this frame's objects against last frame's as well, so the game judges more of them hidden. Off for a day, then **on again as a deliberate picture trade** (owner: some pop-in is fine on the phone): same area, otherwise the same code, on 59.7 fps mean with 60 held 9 min, off 47.7 fps with 60 held ~150 s, no pop-in seen. Quality keeps the view exact (`DM_NVN_PASS_ALT=0`).
- **Cube shapes (2026-11-19):** `pass_alternate` takes up to 4 shapes joined by commas, and `cube:<colour>` names every pass into a cube or cube-array texture (the NVN texture's own target) in that colour format with no depth attachment, whatever its size: an environment cube's mip chain and its filters, never a scene capture into it (which has depth). Each target is the texture's level and first layer (`pa_table`, 32 targets), so the levels of a chain alternate in step, and a face or level the game updates once in several frames (a time-sliced filter) never ran the frame before and always draws; a face-split pass ends at its dropped draw as it does after a drawn one, and one with face-split draws joined from a texture that is not a cube draws (counted). `DM_NVN_CUBE_ALT=0` draws them every frame (the Quality preset), separately from `DM_NVN_PASS_ALT`. Donkey Kong Bananza `cube:92`: its environment cube (128x128, 8 levels, RG11B10Float; [Mip-chain pass sampling the level it renders](#mip-chain-pass-sampling-the-level-it-renders)) and any RG11B10Float cube it filters into. On screen: reflections and the cube's ambient light follow the scene one frame late on alternate frames (a sixtieth of a second at 60 fps); the analysis put the filter at about 0.05-0.15 ms a frame on average (NX18-X2.0 bananza-alldata-analysis.md, ranked fix 6). Not yet run on a device: which passes match, and the saving, wait for the phone's log (`cube passes drawn every other frame` line) or a pass dump (`A … cube pass` lines).

- **Cubes spread over several frames (2026-11-23):** Ocarina of Time's rule was on but skipped 36 of 144,001 cube passes on the Mac (and about as few on the iPhone): the game redraws faces 0-1, 2-3 and 4-5 of its environment cube (each with its level 0 and mip levels 1-7) on three frames in turn and prefilters the second cube every third frame, so no target came back on the next frame, and its 8 levels x 6 faces of two cubes overflowed the 32-slot table besides. A cube target now keeps when it was drawn on its last visit no more than `PA_CUBE_GAP` (3) frames before, so each target draws on every other visit, half its update rate however the game spreads it (a face at most 6 frames old there); a six-frame round robin still always draws, and the table holds 128 targets. `<W>x<H>` shapes keep the next-frame rule. Ocarina on the Mac, the same save and spot: 45% of cube passes kept (34,074 of 75,601), the picture as before; the GPU saving is to be read on the iPhone. `t_pass_alt` has the three-frame case.

### The game's upscaler as a bilinear stretch

- **Symptom:** on the iPhone 18 Pro Max, Donkey Kong Bananza's upscaler drawn by MetalFX (`DM_UPSCALER=metalfx`, agl's NDSR network replaced by the spatial scaler, 1048x590 to 1280x720 R11G11B10F into its 1920x1080 image) took 0.6-1.4 ms a frame of GPU time, plus a render pass that draws MetalFX's RGBA16F output back into the game's R11G11B10F image (NX18-X2.0 bananza-alldata-analysis.md, ranked fix 2).
- **Fix:** a picture trade, one game, by the owner's preset plan (Balanced and Battery Saver take it, Quality does not): the profile key `upscaler_bilinear on` (`nvn_mtl.m` `up_bil_on`, `up_mfx_encode`). Where MetalFX would draw the game's upscaler, one bilinear draw from the input rectangle to the output rectangle (`copy_t2t_draw`, `linear`) draws it instead, reading the game's texture and writing its image; square-rooted input is squared in the same draw (`COPY_T2T_SQUARE`). sRGB-encoded input keeps MetalFX, which decodes it; an image that cannot be a render target keeps MetalFX too (a red line). The stretch reaches the game's image, not the present: the game draws its UI at the output size afterwards, so text and HUD stay sharp. On screen: the 3D picture is softer, edges a little blurrier, and MetalFX's own sharpening is gone (the present's Sharpening row applies to it again). `DM_NVN_UP_BILINEAR=0` lets MetalFX draw it (the Quality preset), `=1` turns the stretch on in any game; the switch is read once a frame, so a preset picked in game applies at the next frame. `DM_UPSCALER=game` (the Mac's default) runs the game's network in every preset.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=040074A01BF12000 check_t_upscaler_mfx`: with the switch on, the named shader's dispatch fills the 1920x1080 image with the input's colour through the stretch (no MetalFX scaler made), a packed HDR subrectangle lands in its destination rectangle only, square-rooted input comes out squared, and sRGB-encoded input still goes through MetalFX and comes out decoded.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md). Not yet run on a device: the saving (about 0.4-0.8 ms a frame at full clock, more at the low clock, by the analysis) and the look wait for the phone.

### Colour loads no draw of the pass needs

- **Symptom:** Donkey Kong Bananza's developer-build data showed 38-90 MB a frame of colour attachment loads that no draw of the pass writes over (G-buffer passes whose later draws mask some targets off, targets bound beside depth-only draws), each a full read of the attachment into tile memory (NX18-X2.0 bananza-alldata-analysis.md, fix 4).
- **Cause:** every colour attachment a pass did not clear was loaded; an attachment no draw writes is already stored as DontCare ([unwritten attachment stores](#unwritten-attachment-stores-without-lookahead)), but its load still ran.
- **Fix:** general, in the host (`nvn_mtl.m` `ldc_plan`, `ldc_scan`, `ldc_draw_mask`): when a draw opens a pass, a scan of the replay up to the pass's certain end (a SetRenderTargets to other targets, or the submission's end; a same-target one is scanned past) finds the colour attachments no draw or clear may write (by the bound fragment shader's outputs and the channel mask; a geometry or mesh program or an indexed multi-draw counts as writing all). Those are loaded as DontCare and, never written, stored as DontCare: their memory is left exactly as it was. The scan is a prediction only: before a draw joins the open pass, `draw_` checks what it may write or read from the tile (all attachments for a tile read, a remapped or specialized fragment, an unknown output set, a geometry or mesh draw), and one that would touch an attachment loaded as DontCare ends the pass first and opens its own, which loads it; a clear drawn as a triangle does the same, and a write the check missed is drawn again in a new pass with a red line. Not for clear-only, face-split, slice-merged, layered, kept-frame (pass_alternate) or multisampled attachments, nor passes not opened by a draw. Attachments that a draw overwrites whole before any read are not detected (left as Load). `DM_NVN_UNWRITTEN_LOAD=0` loads them as before; the `residency set` line counts the loads skipped (and their MB) and the pass ends a draw forced.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_unwritten_load` (two RGBA8 targets and a depth target through the production replay: target 1 not drawn, masked off, depth-only draws, drawn later in the pass, a same-target SetRenderTargets, a draw the scan could not see, a clear triangle into it; on and off read back byte for byte under the validation layer's load-action fill); not yet run on a device.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md).

### Colour-less geometry draws join an open pass

- **Symptom:** in Donkey Kong Bananza's developer-build pass dump on the iPhone, about 2 splits a frame between passes on the same attachments were made by the geometry emulation: a geometry draw whose colour writes are masked off ended the open pass with colours and opened one without them, and the next ordinary draw ended that and reloaded every attachment again (NX18-X2.0 gpu-clock-collapse.md).
- **Cause:** a colour-less geometry draw (`geo_color_omit`) leaves the colours out of the pass it opens, to save their load and store; at a pass already open with its colours there is nothing to save, but the draw path ended it all the same (`g_enc_geo_nocolor != g_geo_omit_colors`).
- **Fix:** general, in the host (`nvn_mtl.m` draw path): such a draw joins an open pass with colours, drawing with its colour writes masked off as it would anywhere; only a pass it would open itself goes without the colours. `DM_NVN_GEOMETRY_JOIN=0` ends the open pass as before; a `colour-less geometry draws drawn in an open pass with colours` line counts them.
- **Check:** `t_nvn` compiles the draw path; not yet run on a device (the next developer run's pass dump should show the draw_ splits gone).
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md).


### A sampler registered again between two draws of a submission

- **Symptom:** draws encoded before a sampler ID was registered again, in the same submission, sample with no sampler at all: a clamped read past the edge comes back 0, with the encode thread on (`DM_NVN_ENC_THREAD=1`); with it off the same draws happened to read right.
- **Cause:** `mtl_reg_samp` released the ID's Metal sampler and its border twin at once. Argument blocks hold a sampler's resource ID, not a reference, and a block encoded earlier in the submission still named it when its command buffer ran.
- **Fix:** general, in `nvn_mtl.m` `mtl_reg_samp`: the old sampler and twin go behind the open submission's fence (`defer_obj_unref`), as a texture ID's views do (`treg_drop_views`).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_args_levers` (the registration-check draws re-register the bound sampler mid-submission and read every pixel back, with the encode thread off and on); `t_border`.
- **Found on:** the unit test written for argument blocks checked by ID after a registration (`DM_NVN_ARGS_REGCHK`); not seen in a game.

### Clears a texture already holds

- **Symptom:** Donkey Kong Bananza clears a 2880x1620 RGBA8 target and its D32S8 depth (ser 5 / 6) every frame and nothing reads or writes them otherwise: a clear-only pass of its own each frame, about 42 MB of stores and 0.25-0.35 ms of GPU time at full clock, about 1 ms at the clock iOS caps it to (NX18-X2.0 bananza-alldata-analysis.md, fix 1b).
- **Cause:** a clear with no draw after it lands in a pass of its own (a waiting lazy clear at the list's end, `lc_land`, or `rp_flush`), which stores the whole attachment, even when the texture already holds exactly that clear.
- **Fix:** general, in the host (`nvn_mtl.m` `sc_note`, `sc_skip_held`): a clear-only pass records what each attachment it clears whole now holds (the texture, the format it was cleared through, each plane's value); a later clear-only pass leaves out an attachment whose record holds the same value through the same format, and opens no pass when none is left. The proof rests on writes alone: every GPU write into a game texture forgets its record first (every attachment a pass attaches, every blit copy's destination, image bindings, texture clears, MetalFX's DLSS and NDVE outputs; the background purge and memory trims forget them all), so whatever reads the texture later (an attachment load, a sample, a copy, the present, a readback) reads the bytes the clear would have stored. Only textures no other write can reach take a record: a CPU_NO_ACCESS pool's private single-level, single-layer, single-sampled 2D texture, not over a buffer, not sparse or lazily backed, not block-compressed, without the shader-write usage (one that gets it is a new texture, `tex_upgrade`); the record names its texture weakly. The skipped attachment keeps the write stamp of the drawn-over render target rule (`tu_stamp`). This is not an owed clear that lands at the texture's next use: no read path needs a hook, so a missed reader cannot see stale content. `DM_NVN_SAME_CLEAR=0` opens every clear-only pass as before; the `residency set` line counts `clears the target already held left out` (with the stores they would have made) and `clear-only passes not opened`.
- **Clear triangles (`DM_NVN_SAME_CLEAR_TRI`):** that Bananza pass was never clear-only: the game clears depth whole and the stencil through a write mask of `0x1`, and a masked stencil clear was drawn as a triangle (`clear_ds_tri`) in a draw pass it opened at once, which records nothing. Such a clear with no pass open, over the whole target, on a target that can take a record now waits like the other clears (`R.clear_sm`; a newer masked one folds in, a full stencil clear waiting or newer folds it into a load-action clear) and its triangle is drawn as its pass begins, before anything else (`clear_s_tri_pass`: compare always, replace through the mask, no depth write, no blending, the whole target as viewport and scissor, so the result is `(old & ~mask) | (value & mask)` whatever the old content). A stencil record keeps which bits it knows (a load-action clear knows all 8); the bits known before the pass (read before its attachments forget them) plus the triangle's mask are recorded after a clear-only pass (`sc_note_tri`), and a later clear-only pass whose triangle writes only bits the record holds with the same values leaves the stencil out, the whole pass when its colour and depth are held too. A pass a draw opens takes the triangle the same way and records nothing. Colour clears drawn as triangles (a partial channel mask or scissor) still open their pass at once and are never left out. `DM_NVN_SAME_CLEAR_TRI=0` draws the masked clear at once as before; the `residency set` line counts the passes not opened that had a stencil triangle and the loads and stores they would have made.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests check_t_same_clear` (readbacks with the switch on and off match byte for byte through a load, a copy, a sampling copy, and after each kind of write between two identical clears; the Bananza shape, colour and depth cleared with a stencil triangle of mask `0x1`, repeated, then after a draw, a copy, an image store and a sampling draw, with another stencil value or mask, a partial scissor, and a blended draw); not yet run on a device.
- **Found on:** [Donkey Kong Bananza](../../dm/games/040074A01BF12000/README.md).

### One-draw passes as compute dispatches

- **Symptom:** in Breath of the Wild and Tears of the Kingdom the GPU time of a frame hardly follows the render resolution (Mac, DM_CTL `scale`: TotK the same at 0.5, BotW 8 % less). A frame holds about 150 render passes, about half of them one full-screen triangle or quad into a small colour target (blur, luminance and mip chains, lighting quads), each 20-40 µs whatever its size: 1.6-3.9 ms a frame on the Mac's per-pass profile (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF`).
- **Cause:** Apple GPUs. A render pass costs a fixed 15-40 µs however little it draws: plain Metal on an M4 Max, a dependent chain of 8x8 to 256x256 one-draw passes, 27-42 µs a pass, against 2.5-7 µs a step for the same chain as compute dispatches (NX18-X2.0 tinybench). The host's barriers are not it: `DM_NVN_BARRIER=game` (no full barrier before each pass) left the passes' time as it was.
- **Fix:** general, on for every game while each is tested with it (profile key `pass_compute`, `off` for a game that shows a fault; `DM_NVN_PCM` overrides: `0`, `1`, `ab`): such a pass runs as one dispatch of a kernel made offline for its vertex / pixel shader pair (`dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_pcomp.py`, built by `dmsh_build.py` as `<vertex>_<pixel>_pc` for the pairs of the pipeline record's K rows and the profile's `pass_compute.tsv`). A threadgroup is 8x8 pixels; lanes 0-5 run the vertex shader's pulling entry once per index into threadgroup memory; each lane finds its pixel's triangle, interpolates the inputs (perspective, linear, flat), runs the pixel shader's body (its implicit-LOD samples take their gradients from the 2x2 lane quad), and writes the targets with the colour attachment's blending and write mask, after a depth test against the depth target when the pass has one; an occlusion query's slot gets the passing pixels. The host (`nvn_mtl.m` `pcm_plan`, `pcm_scan`, `pcm_samples_rt`, `pcm_run`) takes a pass whose one draw has one or two triangles, no depth or stencil write, stencil test, depth bounds or polygon offset, no discard, alpha test, logic operation, advanced or second-source blending or storage writes, samples no level it writes, and is the pass's only draw or clear (a scan of the recorded commands); it attaches the targets as the pass would and dispatches in the copy encoder behind its barrier. A pair with no kernel keeps its render pass and is written to the pipeline record as a K row; a kernel's pipeline is built in the background (and by the prewarm for the lists' K rows), the render pass drawing until it is ready. Colour targets up to `DM_NVN_PCM_MAXPX` pixels (1 MP) are made shader-writable from creation (`pcm_usage`): made so on first use instead, a target left its family's shared heap. A resolution family gets the usage on every member when its largest size is within that limit and on none when it is over (`fam_usage`), as a heap is shared only among members of one usage and the usage takes lossless compression away (an 8192x8192 RGBA8 target on the Mac read flat in 0.254 ms with render-target and read usage, 0.48 ms with shader write, the same as noise): a larger family stays compressed, and its passes keep their render pass (`pcm_run` declines at 5). With `DM_NVN_FAMILY_NOCOMPRESS=1` every member gets it, as before. A family member whose heap holds another size is declined too (16), so its render pass clears the load ([valid-size tracking](#a-resolution-family-member-reads-another-sizes-compressed-blocks-as-garbage)).
- **Faults found on the way (Breath of the Wild, by bisection over pairs, `DM_NVN_PCM_SKIP`):** an RG11B10 target took negative values a render pipeline would store as 0, which darkened the scene through the exposure chain (the kernel clamps unsigned float targets at 0); level views of the targets were freed before the GPU ran the kernel, and the ID went to another texture the kernel then wrote (the hearts as a red block: views go through the deferred release).
- **Measured:** Breath of the Wild, Great Plateau, Mac (`DM_NVN_PCM=ab`, `DM_AB=600`): render passes about 145 → 92 a frame, GPU mean 10.9 → 9.8 ms, p50 11.95 → 11.2.
- **Check:** the picture against a run without kernels (hearts, scene brightness); `t_nvn`; `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_pcomp.py` (the composer on a synthetic pair, compiled for both platforms). Not yet run on an iPhone or on Tears of the Kingdom.
- **Found on:** [Breath of the Wild](../../dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md).
